# The Assayer Code-Conformance Audit · `rep:assayer:code-conformance-campaign`

This is the working record of the code-conformance campaign opened under the ruling *Audit before conformance* ((`dec:assayer:conformance-audit-first`) in [the campaign backlog](backlog.md)). The documentation campaign that preceded it rewrote the specification, derived the record set from it, and put both under a linter. This campaign asks the question that work made askable for the first time: does the code do what the corpus says it does?

The campaign opens with an audit and nothing else. No code change lands before the audit is complete, with two standing exceptions — the labelled to-do notices may be improved as notices, and the harness clock injection is carved out of the freeze as roadmap-specified infrastructure. The worklist below is therefore a register of what conformance would mean, item by item, and never a licence to make it so.

The document practices the discipline it serves. The label at each heading or environment head is that environment's mint; a parenthesized label in running text is a citation; material in fenced blocks is displayed without participating. Every label minted here has area `assayer`, and every kind is drawn from a fixed registry of environment kinds. The linter's carrier covers package documentation, so this file participates in the repo check and its grammar is enforced mechanically on every run.

The record no longer displays a label it means to point at. While the audit ran it had to: a notice minted in code and a claim minted over a test were not reachable from prose, so this record showed their labels in double-backtick spans and said in words what a citation would have said mechanically. The capability landed with the notice-citation wave — one resolution space across kinds and surfaces, so a label minted anywhere is citable from anywhere — and the close-out converted every one of those spans into a citation that resolves (`rem:assayer:closeout-conversion`). The consequence is worth stating plainly, because it is the whole reason the wave was worth doing: a notice this record cites can no longer be silently renamed or retired out from under it, and the repair waves below are planned against notices the check will keep honest.

## Method · `sec:assayer:audit-method`

**Desideratum (What a complete audit looks like)** · `goal:assayer:audit-complete`

Every module of `packages/assayer/src` has been read against the records and specification that govern it, and every checkable claim either stands with its evidence or has become a worklist item with a disposition. The audit is complete when the scope map (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`) has no unaudited cluster, when every worklist item carries a disposition an independent reader could recompute from the citations given, and when the deferral register's every entry has been pinned against the code rather than against its own history. Completion is not conformance: an audit that ends with a long worklist has succeeded exactly as much as one that ends with a short one.

**Convention (The specification is the measure)** · `conv:assayer:audit-measure`

The audit measures the code against the corpus, never the corpus against the code. This is the standing ruling (`dec:assayer:spec-authoritative`) applied as a working rule: where the two disagree, the presumption is that the code is the party in the wrong, and the burden of argument falls on any finding that would instead call the document stale. That burden is real and is discharged by evidence — a later decision that superseded the text, a mechanism the text describes that no longer exists — and never by the observation that changing the document would be cheaper than changing the code.

The corpus, for this purpose, is `docs/spec.md` and its parts under `docs/spec/`, the landed records under `adr/`, and the repo-level records the package's prose imports. Rustdoc is not the corpus: a doc comment that contradicts the specification is a finding against the comment, and a doc comment that says something the specification does not is unspecified rather than binding.

**Convention (The disposition grammar)** · `conv:assayer:audit-dispositions`

Every audited item closes with exactly one of four dispositions, and the grammar is deliberately narrow so that a disposition carries information rather than tone.

| Disposition | What it asserts |
| --- | --- |
| **conformant** | The corpus states a requirement and the code meets it. |
| **divergent-code-wrong** | The corpus states a requirement, the code does something else, and the code is the party to change. |
| **divergent-spec-stale** | The code is right and the corpus text is the stale party. Requires an argument, per (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`). |
| **unspecified** | The corpus settles nothing here, so what the code does is neither conformant nor divergent — it is a gap in the corpus. |

An item that cannot be adjudicated without work the audit has not done is not given a fifth disposition; it stays open and says what evidence would settle it. The distinction that matters most in practice is between **divergent-code-wrong** and **unspecified**, because a notice in the code often reads as the first when it is really the second — a wish about what the code ought to do, with no corpus text behind it. Sorting those two apart is a large part of what this audit is for.

**Convention (What counts as evidence)** · `conv:assayer:audit-evidence`

An item's evidence is a path and a line in the code, and a label in the corpus. Both halves are required. A finding that cites only the code has not shown a divergence, because it has not said from what; a finding that cites only the corpus has not shown one either, because it has not said the code does otherwise. Where a claim rests on a numeric value, the evidence names both numbers. Where it rests on control flow, the evidence states the sequence of calls under which the divergence is observable, so that a reader can construct the failing case rather than take the finding's word for it.

Nothing is carried on faith from an earlier wave. Every item entering the worklist from a prior campaign's findings was re-verified against the tree as it stands, and any item that did not survive re-verification is recorded here with the reason rather than quietly dropped.

**Convention (Tranche discipline)** · `conv:assayer:audit-tranches`

The audit proceeds in tranches, each a bounded module cluster taken end-to-end against the records that govern it, and each recorded in the tranche register (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`) with its dispositions. A tranche is not a sampling pass: within its cluster every checkable claim the governing records make is checked, and the tranche's entry says which records it read, so that a later reader can tell a cluster that was audited from one that was glanced at. Clusters are sized so that one worker can hold the cluster and its records at once, which in this package has meant one source directory and the one or two records that govern it.

## Scope map · `sec:assayer:audit-scope`

**Table (The package's clusters against the record set)** · `tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`

The audit's unit of work is the cluster: a source directory of `packages/assayer/src`, or a file at its root, together with the landed records and specification chapters that govern it. The mapping is not one-to-one in either direction — a record may govern several clusters and a cluster may answer to several records — and the table says what it is rather than tidying it.

This table was rebuilt at the close of the read phase, by the method the glue tranche prescribed (`rem:assayer:glue-scope-map`): its rows are an enumeration of the source tree, and its two columns are the derivations the tranches performed for themselves, harvested from their own scope-map remarks and from the register's records-read column (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`). Nothing in it is carried over from the map it replaces. Every row but one is therefore governing text a tranche read and measured against rather than governing text somebody assigned. When it was built, one row was the exception: `resonance/`, which no tranche had taken, and whose columns were marked underived rather than guessed. That row is now derived like the rest (`rem:assayer:resonance-scope-map`), so every row of this table is governing text a tranche read and measured against.

| Cluster | Governing records | Specification | Derived by |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| `ledger/` | memory | the Outcome Ledger chapter | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-ledger`) |
| `owner/` | ownership, concurrency, ordering, construction, calibration, durability, clock | the runtime, assessment, label and registry operations chapters | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-owner`) |
| `risk/` | calibration, challenge, posterior | the risk, companion, monitoring and warm-up chapters | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-risk`) |
| `feature/` | vector, substrate | the feature-vector, dimension-map and standardisation chapters; the configuration reference | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-feature`) |
| `extraction/` | vector | the structured-extraction chapter, its reference index, and the assessment operations chapter | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-extraction`) |
| `health/` | health, degradation, metrics | the health-monitoring chapter; the warm-up chapter for the stage set and the concordance bootstrap; the feature chapter for the recalibration | (`rem:assayer:health-scope-map`) |
| `metrics/` | metrics | the health-monitoring chapter | (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-metrics`) |
| `persistence/` | durability, clock, temporal governance | the guarantees appendix and the temporal operations chapter; the specification is silent on the mechanism itself | (`rem:assayer:persistence-scope-map`) |
| `snapshot/` | concurrency, ordering, retention, durability | the concurrency chapter; the feature-vector chapter for the version invariant | (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-scope-map`) |
| `pending/` | retention | the label-pipeline chapter, the concurrency chapter, and the configuration reference | (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-scope-map`) |
| `api/` | surface, construction, durability, degradation, ordering, clock | the host-contract, label-pipeline, assessment-operations and concurrency chapters; the configuration reference | (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`) |
| `guidance/` | surface | the guidance landscape chapter; the configuration reference | (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`) |
| `model/` | posterior, substrate, degradation | the risk core-model chapters for the update and the leverage bound; the Gaussian foundations chapter for everything else | (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-scope-map`) |
| `linalg/` | substrate, posterior | the Gaussian foundations chapter | (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-scope-map`) |
| `identity/` | memory, degradation, concurrency, clock | the key-space identity chapter; the host-signals chapter for the cached modes | (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-scope-map`) |
| `signal/` | retention, degradation, metrics | the host-signals chapter; the identity chapter's cache table; the configuration reference | (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-scope-map`) |
| `report/` | construction, calibration, clock | the architecture chapter's assumed-properties table and report-validation precondition; the encoding chapter for the coordinate lift | (`rem:assayer:report-config-scope-map`) |
| `config/` | construction, calibration | the configuration reference | (`rem:assayer:report-config-scope-map`) |
| `numerics.rs` | calibration | the configuration reference's calibration table; the risk core-model chapter for the regime transition | (`rem:assayer:report-config-scope-map`) |
| `lib.rs`, `assessment.rs`, `error.rs`, `types.rs`, `serde_codec.rs` | degradation, surface, clock, ordering, concurrency, construction | the assessment operations chapter whole; the feature chapter for the batch initialisation; the companion chapter for the challenge prior | (`rem:assayer:glue-scope-map`) |
| `resonance/` | derivation, ordering, surface, ownership | the decision-landscape Part whole — its derivation-interface, channel-policy, crossover and exploration chapters — and the resonance-rendering appendix, which governs most of the shipped code; the configuration reference for the display constants | (`rem:assayer:resonance-scope-map`) |
| `testing/`, `tests/` | — | the testing plan, which is not corpus | no tranche |

`resonance/` was the one unaudited cluster and its row said so in both columns rather than repeating what the map it replaces asserted. The unblocking condition was the derivation record's landing, and the record landed; the tranche followed it the same day and the row above is what it derived (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`).

The restraint paid, and it is worth saying how, because the row is the campaign's one controlled test of the method. Had a tranche filled this row before the record existed it would have named the derivation-interface chapter, which is the chapter the cluster's own doc comments name. The reading found that the chapter governing most of the cluster's lines is the resonance-rendering appendix, that three further records reach into it, and that the whole of the decision-landscape Part is in scope rather than one chapter of it. That is the sixth wrong specification column the campaign did not write.

`guidance/` illustrates the same restraint on a cluster that *was* audited. Its record column names one record where the map it replaces named the label-guidance-api record, which is legacy and therefore not corpus; the tranche measured against the guidance chapter and the surface record instead and lost no authority doing it (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`).

**Remark (What the map it replaces got wrong, and how)** · `rem:assayer:audit-scope-map-history`

The rebuild is not tidying and the reason should survive the map that occasioned it. Six of the old map's rows were wrong on the specification side and they were wrong in four distinct ways, each found by a tranche going to the named document rather than by anyone auditing the map: a chapter that mentions the cluster's subject in no casing at all (`persistence/`, `pending/`), a chapter about an unrelated subject that enumerates nothing the cluster does (`api/`), a chapter that is real and about the cluster and states a true half while the governing table sits in a different chapter (`signal/`), and a named document that is not prose at all but the build manifest (`report/`). Five rows were wrong on the record side, and those failed in three ways of their own: a record named that governs no line of the cluster (`model/`, `signal/`), a record omitted that decides the cluster's central question (`snapshot/`, `linalg/`, `api/`), and a record named that is legacy and so is not corpus (`guidance/`). One row bundled two files under a record that governs neither of them together (`linalg/`, `numerics.rs`). And one cluster — the crate glue, the highest-fan-in code in the package and the one the specification writes a nine-step algorithm for — had no row whatever, which is the failure the register's completeness check is structurally unable to see, because a check that asks whether every row appears cannot ask whether every file has a row.

Two things follow for whoever maintains this table. The first is that an amended row is evidence about that row and nothing else, which is why this rebuild derived every row afresh instead of keeping the eight a tranche had already corrected. The second is that the completeness check needs its complement: the register verifies that every row here is audited, and only an enumeration of `src/` verifies that every cluster here has a row. Both directions are cheap and only one of them was ever run.

## The founding worklist · `sec:assayer:audit-worklist`

**Convention (How a worklist item is written)** · `conv:assayer:worklist-grammar`

Each item is one entry, and the entry carries four things in a fixed order: its **locus**, a path and line; its **disposition** under (`conv:assayer:audit-dispositions`); its **severity**, argued rather than asserted; and **what conformance would mean**, one sentence naming the concrete change that would close it. Severity is read off the consequence and not off the effort: an item corrupts a number a host acts on, or it degrades a diagnostic a host reads, or it affects a surface nothing reaches — high, medium, low in that order — and the argument for which is part of the item.

An entry may carry a fifth thing and carries it last: its **sequencing**, naming the entries whose repair it depends on or which depend on it. The close-out added this and the reason is a defect the read phase kept producing. A tranche that discovered a dependency wrote it into its own prose, where the entry it binds does not mention it, so a reader planning from the entries alone would schedule two repairs independently that cannot be. Sequencing is therefore recorded on both entries a relation joins, never on one, and the register consolidates them (`reg:assayer:audit-sequencing`).

Where an item already stands as a labelled notice in the code, the entry names the notice's own label rather than restating its summary, because the notice is the defect's identity under the to-do profile and a second description would be a second identity. It cites that label, which the record could not do while the audit ran and can now (`rem:assayer:closeout-conversion`).

An entry that has been repaired carries a sixth thing and carries it last, after its sequencing: **repaired**, naming the wave that closed it and the commit that landed the repair. Nothing else in the entry changes. What the audit measured stands as it was measured, because an entry rewritten to describe the repaired code would destroy the evidence its own repair answers to, and a reader asking why a wave landed would find only the wave's own result. The repair line is therefore an addition and never an amendment, and an entry with no repair line is open whatever the code now does.

One amendment is forced rather than chosen, and it is the same rule read backwards: where the entry cited a labelled notice and the repair removed the notice with the defect it disclosed, the citation goes too. A notice is the defect's identity under the to-do profile, so a repair that ends the defect ends the identity, and an entry citing a label that no longer mints would fail the check that makes these citations worth writing at all. The locus sentence records that the notice stood and that the repair took it.

**Data (What the founding worklist came to)** · `data:assayer:worklist-census`

The founding worklist drew on two sources. They overlap, and the overlap is worth recording because it is the first evidence that the two campaigns found the same code: the claim waves reported two code defects, and one of them — the axis registration that under-extends standardisation — was already standing as one of the twelve labelled notices. The founding worklist therefore holds fewer distinct items than the two sources' counts add up to, and (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`) is registered once.

| Source | Items registered | Divergences | Refuted |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The twelve labelled notices | 11 | 16 | 1 |
| The claim waves' catalogue | 3 | 3 | 4 |
| The first tranche | 7 | 5 | 2 |
| The clock lane's measurements | 3 | 3 | 0 |

Twenty-four entries, and the divergence count exceeds the entry count because one entry — (`entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`) — carries six departures that share a notice and a locus. By severity the divergences stand at eight high, thirteen medium, and six low, with the high ones concentrated unsurprisingly: every one of them either sets the width of the feature vector or multiplies the gradient of a model update.

Two of the twenty-four are not this campaign's to close. (`rem:assayer:out-of-package-item`) belongs to an upstream package's corpus, and (`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`) is the carve-out removed from the freeze, so its repair is already in another lane's hands and this record holds only its conformance facts.

**Remark (A to-do label could not be cited from prose, and now can)** · `rem:assayer:todo-labels-uncitable`

The to-do profile's stated purpose is to give a deficiency "a name that a register can count, a document can cite, and a checker can verify". When this audit opened, the first and third held and the second did not. A parenthesized to-do label in a participating document was read as a citation and failed to resolve, because the mint stands in a Rust comment and the prose carrier did not reach it — established by writing one into this document and watching the check report it as resolving nowhere. The consequence was that this audit, the first document the profile was built to serve, had to display every notice label it referred to, and so got none of the resolution checking that is the whole point of a citation.

The finding was against the tooling rather than against the profile's decision, and it belonged to the campaign's tools-over-discipline standing rule: a name a document is promised it can cite, and cannot, is a discipline held by care. Two repairs suggested themselves — teaching the resolver to reach code-side mints from prose, or minting a prose-side identity per notice that the notice's own label is checked against — and neither was taken in the form offered: what landed instead is one resolution space across every kind and both surfaces.

That capability landed while the audit's last tranche ran, and the close-out converted this record's every displayed label into a citation that resolves (`rem:assayer:closeout-conversion`). The remark is kept rather than deleted because the campaign's own convention is that nothing is quietly dropped, and because what it records is not the defect but the shape of it: a promise a document was given and could not use, found by a document trying to use it. That is the cheapest way this campaign has found defects in its own tools, and it is worth a reader knowing it worked.

**Entry (Per-Sentinel informativeness is exported as a constant)** · `entry:assayer:wl-sentinel-informativeness`

**Locus** `src/api/health.rs:217`, a literal zero on every Sentinel's health. A labelled notice stood over the literal, and the wave-nine repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **The locus is one line out and is corrected in place by the public surface tranche** (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-cross-cites`), which owns the file: the literal stands at `src/api/health.rs:217` and the notice above it opens at `src/api/health.rs:213`. Everything else here was confirmed at those lines. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:monitoring:encoding-effectiveness`), which defines informativeness as the mean absolute weight over the Sentinel's own slot and records that neither it nor the indicator beside it exists. Every input is present: the slot ranges are in the dimension map and the weights are on the model. **Severity** medium. No decision consumes the value, but it leaves the package as a live gauge, so a host alerting on low informativeness sees a permanent zero and cannot tell an uninformative Sentinel from an unmeasured one. It is also the designated early warning for silent encoding failure, which is therefore disabled. **What conformance would mean** the field is computed from the mean absolute operational weight over the Sentinel's slot, or is made optional and left empty rather than filled with a number that is not a measurement. **Coverage** none, and worse than none: the export test constructs the zero as its fixture input, so it pins the wrong value rather than catching it.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `7d8414df`, by both halves of the conformance line at once: the field is computed — the mean absolute operational weight over the slot the published dimension map carries — and it is optional, so a Sentinel the published map does not yet hold reads as unmeasured rather than as zero, with the export mapping absence to NaN as the report-age gauge already does. The notice retired with the defect. The engine falsifier recomputes the slot fold from the very published snapshot the report read and requires it nonzero after thirty labels through the slot (`test:crate:informativeness-reads-the-operational-slot-weights`); the export test that pinned the zero as its fixture now carries a measured value and an absence and asserts each arrives as itself (`test:crate:informativeness-export-carries-measurement-and-absence`).

**Entry (The embedded health snapshot omits its own join key)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:462`. A labelled notice stood over the snapshot type, and the wave-nine repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`schema:output:health-snapshot`), which fixes nine fields and names the version as the field the design turns on. Absent are the version, the snapshot age, the drift flag, and the uncertainty inflation factor. The spec-stale defence was considered and fails on its own terms: the legacy assessment-contract record's argument for a narrow snapshot is a size argument aimed at per-model and per-Sentinel detail, and the four missing fields are twenty-odd bytes of scalars that the argument does not reach — and that record expects a sixth field which has never shipped either. **Severity** medium and trending. Nothing computed is wrong, but the forensic join the schema exists to establish is impossible, because an assessment log and a health-report stream share no key. The inflation field compounds with (`entry:assayer:wl-empirical-coverage`): even were the field present, nothing computes its value. **What conformance would mean** the four scalars join the snapshot, the version taken from the published model state the assess path already reads. **Coverage** none.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `af9298db`. The four scalars join the snapshot: the version of the published snapshot the assessment read — the join key — the age in seconds the uncertainty correction was computed at, the drift flag as the fold over the published accumulators at half the auto-reset threshold (the full value resets to zero in the same update, so half is the largest observable reading of the schema's "near"), and the most recent inflation factor, absent until a refit has computed one. The notice retired with the defect. The engine falsifier verifies each field by recomputation against the same published state the assessment read, with the inflation present after the two-hundred-label refit (`test:crate:snapshot-carries-version-age-drift-and-inflation`), and the half-threshold semantics are pinned on either side of the boundary (`test:crate:drift-near-threshold-at-half`). One count the entry did not carry is recorded with the wave's findings: the schema's anchor-frozen row is also absent from the snapshot and was not among this entry's four.

**Entry (Six shipped defaults disagree with the parameter tables)** · `entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`

**Locus** `src/config/types.rs:68` and the six sites the notice reached. A labelled notice stood at the configuration head, and the wave-two repair took it with the six departures it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong on all six, verified individually. The configuration chapter is unusually self-aware and names every one of the six departures in its own prose, which makes this the one worklist item whose authority argues the audit's case for it rather than merely permitting it.

| Departure | Shipped | Specified | Authority | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Pending-buffer capacity | a fixed million | a computation on rate and latency | (`tab:config:pending-buffer`) | medium |
| Pending-entry expiry horizon | one hour | twenty-four hours | (`tab:config:pending-buffer`) | high |
| Chain-length normaliser | 128 at both sites | 16 | (`tab:config:extraction`) | medium |
| Drift noise allowance | 0.5 | 0.1 | (`tab:config:monitoring`) | medium |
| Minimum class count for the discrimination statistic | 15 | 20 | (`tab:config:monitoring`) | medium |
| Recent discrimination window | a quarter of the buffer | a fixed five hundred | (`tab:config:monitoring`) | low |

Two of the six need their severity argued rather than asserted, and the argument changes the picture the notice paints. The expiry horizon is the worst thing in this worklist that is only a default: a label arriving more than an hour after its assessment finds its pending entry gone, so any deployment whose feedback arrives on a human timescale silently loses most of its training signal. Against that, the recent-window row is nearly harmless, because the calibration buffer ships at the specified two thousand and a quarter of two thousand is exactly the specified five hundred — the divergence is semantic, biting only on a partly-filled buffer or a retuned capacity, and both the notice and the spec's prose overstate it by missing the coincidence.

The minimum class count is the closest thing in the whole worklist to a defensible spec-stale claim, and it still does not clear the bar: the code argues its own deviation in a comment, but the argument has no landed record behind it, and an argument in a comment is not corpus.

The two pending-buffer departures were re-verified individually by the snapshot and pending tranche at their configuration loci and both stand. The capacity ships as a fixed million (`src/config/types.rs:543`) against a table that states a computation on rate and latency; the expiry horizon ships at thirty-six hundred seconds (`src/config/types.rs:544`) against the tabulated twenty-four hours, the factor of twenty-four the configuration reference names in its own prose. Both are read once at construction and handed to the buffer (`src/lib.rs:417`, `src/lib.rs:418`), so neither is reachable by any later path and the repair is at the two constants.

**What conformance would mean** each default moves to its tabulated value, and the capacity becomes the specified computation rather than a constant. **Coverage** none of the six is guarded, and one is anti-guarded: the builder suite asserts the shipped million as the expected capacity, so a test now defends the divergence against repair.

The thirteenth tranche took `config/` and found the notice this entry names had misattributed its first two departures. The notice's own text called them the Ledger's capacity and the Ledger's horizon; both are the pending buffer's, and `LedgerConfig` has neither a capacity field nor an hour-scaled horizon — the Ledger table (`tab:config:ledger`) closes "All six ship as tabulated" and the four fields the structure carries do. The notice text was corrected at the value's own site under the freeze's notice-text exception; the entry's own table was right and is unchanged. Only two of the six departures live in `config/` at all, which is the correction to this entry's shape: the module is the notice's home rather than the defects' home, and the remaining four are repaired in the extraction, monitoring and calibration clusters.

**Repaired** in wave two at `132e35e6`, all six departures in one commit because they shared one notice and one disclosure. Each default moved to its tabulated value; the capacity became the computation — the configuration now carries the rate and latency the requirement computes from, 720,000 at the defaults it ships, with the builder validating the factors. The anti-guarded builder test pins the computation instead of defending the million; the recent-window and class-count diagnostics read the table's constants; and the drift suites' fixtures were restated so that residuals the claims call inside the allowance are inside the tabulated one.

**Entry (The named-pair interaction template is implemented nowhere)** · `entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`

**Locus** `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:364` and the template enumeration at `src/feature/interaction.rs:164`. A labelled notice stood at the allocating match, and the wave-seven repair took it — with the correction that the notice understated what it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong, and the notice's own framing understates it. The notice says two template types are swapped; what is actually true is that the specified named-pair template — two Sentinels named explicitly, expanding to exactly one feature (`def:feature:template-named-pair`) — exists in no arm at all, the wildcard (`def:feature:template-wildcard`) occupies the named pair's position so that declaring a named pair buys a quadratic block, and the arm left over duplicates the per-Sentinel template rather than implementing a fifth kind. **Severity** high. This sets the feature vector's width and therefore the model dimension: at the reference configuration's eight Sentinels a mistakenly-declared named pair allocates twenty-eight coordinates where the corpus allocates one, which moves every learned weight, the factorisation cost, and the dimension identity the reference tables state. **What conformance would mean** a named-pair variant carrying two Sentinel identifiers and expanding to one feature, the wildcard renumbered into the third position, and the duplicated per-Sentinel arm removed. **Coverage** worse than none, and this line is corrected in place by the feature tranche (`rem:assayer:feature-pins`). The named-pair variant indeed does not exist to be tested, which is what the line as first written recorded; what it missed is that the arm standing in the named pair's position *is* tested, that the test asserts the quadratic count as the expected one, and that its authored claim attaches the corpus's argument for the wildcard's cost to the type the corpus defines as costing exactly one dimension. The duplicated per-Sentinel arm is pinned a third time. Repairing the templates fails two tests whose claims must be rewritten with them.

**Repaired** in wave seven at `a2828bfd`, and the notice's framing was an understatement the repair had to correct as the entry said. Type 2 carries two Sentinel identifiers and expands to one feature, present while both are registered; type 3 is the wildcard; the duplicated per-Sentinel arm is gone, its second operand being an aggregate the per-Sentinel arm can already address. The width is counted in three places and all three moved together: the count function takes the registered set rather than its size, the rebuild allocates the pair's single index only when both are present and in registration order, and the registration handler's delta arm reads the identifiers because a count of the population cannot express whether *this* registration completes a named pair. The builder refuses a named pair naming the same Sentinel twice. The two tests that pinned the swap are rewritten with their claims, and the named pair gets the value-level test it never had.

**Entry (Empirical coverage of the reported uncertainty is computed nowhere)** · `entry:assayer:wl-empirical-coverage`

**Locus** `src/health/published.rs:248`. A labelled notice stood over the discrimination computation, and the wave-nine repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:monitoring:empirical-coverage`), which specifies the standardised residual per buffer entry, two reported coverage fractions, and the inflation factor as a quantile ratio — and which notes that no additional per-request state is needed, the calibration rows this function already reads being the only input. **Severity** medium. The diagnostic is report-only by construction, so nothing acted on is corrupted; what follows is that the conservatism of every reported interval is unmeasured in every deployment, the guarantee that discrimination is visible has nothing behind it, and the honest-notes register names it. **What conformance would mean** the two fractions and the factor are computed from the calibration rows the function already holds and join its result type — **and this line is corrected in place by the health tranche** (`rem:assayer:health-cross-cites`), which found it an underestimate. The rows do not hold the uncertainty the standardised residual divides by, because the calibration entry never carried one; the quantity is computed per request and sits one field access from the site where the entry is built, so conformance means threading it there. **The health tranche's correction went one clause further than the code supports, and that clause is corrected in place by the persistence tranche** (`rem:assayer:persistence-cross-cites`): the calibration entry is not serialised into the checkpoint, the payload declaring no calibration field and the payload's own notice listing the buffer among what it does not yet carry, so widening the type costs nothing at the persistence boundary today. What replaces that clause is an ordering rather than a cost — the buffer is required to join the payload eventually, and should carry the uncertainty when it does, so the two repairs are sequenced rather than independent. The absence itself stands exactly as registered. **Coverage** none.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `fd273603`, the computation half, on the persisted uncertainty row wave five threaded at `0f3dcd10`. The two coverage fractions and the inflation factor join the discrimination metrics, computed per refit from the rows the function already reads: the prediction formed at the current regime parameters through the shared transition, the residual divided by the row's stored assessment-time uncertainty, the 0.975-quantile of the absolute residuals over its nominal width, and the calibration minimum applied per regime partition as the sample gate. The discrimination computation moved after the fit so the residuals are formed at the parameters being published; the persisted metrics widened, so the checkpoint format bumped nine to ten with the version-guard pair refusing nine by name. The hand-derived falsifier reads 0.5, 0.8 and 2.5/1.96 from forty rows whose residuals were placed at exactly those quantiles (`test:crate:empirical-coverage-fractions-and-inflation-hand-derived`), and the gate falsifier shows an under-minimum regime contributing nothing with the diagnostic withheld entire when both partitions sit under the floor (`test:crate:empirical-coverage-gates-each-regime-at-the-calibration-minimum`). The factor reaches the embedded snapshot through the sibling repair at (`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`).

**Entry (One shared class-rate tracker matches neither model)** · `entry:assayer:wl-p-plus-tracker-rates`

**Locus** `src/owner/label_path.rs:470` and `src/owner/label_path.rs:475`, both passing the same rate. A labelled notice stood on the shared field, and the wave-two repair took it — with two satellite notices at the call sites and one on the wiring — together with the defect they disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:weighting:trackers`), which gives the global tracker the operational rate and the eligible tracker the inherited one so each reports class balance over the same horizon as the models it feeds — and which says in terms that no new parameter is introduced, so the shared field should not exist. **Severity** high. These trackers set the class rate, which sets the balancing weights, which multiply the posterior gradient of every model update. The shipped rate gives a horizon roughly a fifth of the sister's, and a tracker faster than its model applies a correction the model's own memory does not warrant. This corrupts learned weights, not a report. **What conformance would mean** each call passes the rate the table assigns it; both rates are already threaded onto the same configuration and correct. **Coverage** none. The pipeline tests exercise that the trackers move and never assert a decay rate, so they pass under either.

**Repaired** in wave two at `96c6bb31`. The shared field is deleted rather than retuned — the table says no new parameter is introduced — and each call passes its models' rate. The coverage line was almost right: the pipeline tests pass under either rate except one, the convergence scenario, whose liveness bound encoded the retired rate's horizon; it is restated by recomputation at the eligible tracker's own rate, with the derivation beside the assertion, and the defaults test now pins the two rates as the forgetting rates the table assigns.

**Entry (Label-time reconstruction leaves four of six blocks at zero)** · `entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`

**Locus** `src/owner/label_path.rs:1234`. A labelled notice stood here, and the wave-six repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:runtime:reconstruction`), which enumerates six sources and whose own conformance prose names what ships line for line: the bias, the frozen signal features and the frozen per-Sentinel slots, and nothing else. **Severity** high, and by recomputation the highest-leverage item in the founding worklist. Every model update is applied to a vector that differs systematically from the one its own prediction was computed from. Against the reference configuration's dimension summary the zeroed blocks — identity per-dimension and cross-dimension, aggregate, interactions, competitive — come to a hundred and thirty-odd of six hundred and thirty-eight coordinates, so roughly a fifth of every training vector is structurally zero and the weights over that fifth receive gradient only through the prior. They never learn. **What conformance would mean** the four blocks are re-derived from the current working copy while the Sentinel and signal blocks stay frozen — and the feature tranche has since found that most of this is written already and exported, in a function of `src/feature/` that nothing in production calls, so the repair is largely a call rather than an implementation (`rem:assayer:feature-census`). The reconstruction registered here is the label path's own private second version, not the package's helper. **That correction is itself corrected in place by the snapshot and pending tranche** (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`), which owns the buffer the reconstruction reads from: the call is necessary and is not sufficient, because three of the algorithm's six sources read values the corpus requires to be frozen at assessment time and the pending entry does not store any of the three (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`). Calling the helper closes two of the four zeroed blocks; the other two need the buffer widened first, so the two repairs are ordered rather than independent. Everything the feature tranche established stands — the helper is written, exported, tested and uncalled. **The severity argument is widened at its consuming end by the posterior and substrate tranche** (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-cross-cites`), which owns the update the reconstructed vector is handed to. The entry argues the damage as a gradient applied along the wrong vector, and that is the smaller half. The update reads the same vector twice before it applies anything: the covariance product and the leverage are both computed from it in the update's first two steps (`alg:update:sherman-morrison`), and the leverage sets the effective weight through the bound. So a systematically wrong vector perturbs the update's magnitude as well as its direction, by an amount and in a direction that depend on the covariance's off-diagonal structure over the zeroed blocks and are therefore not signed in general. The consequence for the entry is that the zeroed fifth cannot be reasoned about as a fifth of the gradient lost: the remaining four fifths are applied at a weight computed from the wrong geometry, so the whole update is affected rather than the zeroed part of it. Nothing in the model cluster can detect this, and nothing in it should — the update receives a standardised vector and has no second copy to compare against, which is the same structural reason its coverage line gives. **Coverage** none. Nothing compares a reconstructed vector against the assessment-time vector, and the function's unused configuration parameter is itself evidence that no caller-level assertion constrains its output.

**Sequencing** after (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`), and the order is forced rather than chosen. Three of the algorithm's six sources read values the corpus requires to be *stored*, and they are three of the four fields the pending entry does not carry (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`). Calling the reconstruction helper — which is most of what this repair was registered as — cannot supply them from any state the label path holds. The buffer must store them first.

**Repaired** in wave six at `2a2c60b2`, second of the ordered pair and after the widening, exactly as the sequencing requires — the reconstruction's commit follows the widening's. The label path's private two-of-six version is deleted and the path calls the package's label-time assembly, completed to all six sources: sources one, three and four read the widened entry's frozen values, and sources two, five and six are re-derived at the moment of labelling — the structural triples, indicators and cross-dimension aggregates from the identity infrastructure, and the aggregate block from the stored extractions under the current thresholds and registered count. The falsifier the coverage line said was absent now stands: under unchanged state the reconstruction reproduces the assessment-time raw vector exactly, every position (`test:crate:label-reconstruction-matches-assessment-under-unchanged-state`). The slot guard inside the helper is deliberately untouched: its per-axis placement defect is wave seven's (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-frozen-slot-truncation`).

**Entry (Axis registration under-extends standardisation past one dimension)** · `entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`

**Locus** `src/owner/lifecycle.rs:440`. A labelled notice stood here, and the wave-four repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`req:standardisation:class-assignment`), which requires every position to carry a class derived from the dimension map so that a class and an index never drift apart. The models are extended by three coordinates per identity dimension; the class vector is pushed three coordinates once, however many dimensions there are. **Severity** high, and higher than the notice implies. The notice calls the defect live at two or more identity dimensions, which is correct; what it does not say is that the reference configuration itself specifies two, so the defect is live at the documented reference deployment rather than at an exotic edge. From that registration on, the standardisation vectors are shorter than the model dimension and positions past the truncation are standardised against the wrong class or not at all. **What conformance would mean** the class push becomes a loop over the identity dimension ranges. **Coverage** none. The integration suite registers two identity dimensions and registers no axis afterwards; where an axis and a dimension both appear the axis comes first, so the one ordering that trips this is never run.

**Repaired** in wave four at `db8f6c6b`, as the conformance line specified: the class push is a loop over the identity dimension ranges. The falsifier runs the one ordering the coverage line found missing — the reference configuration's two dimensions, then an axis — and before the repair read the second dimension's appended positions carrying ZScore and the neutral resize fallback where the corpus assigns the axis classes at their priors; after it, one class triple per dimension and the vectors exactly as long as the models the same event extended.

**Entry (Companion estimates never decay on read)** · `entry:assayer:wl-companion-read-decay`

**Locus** `src/risk/challenge.rs:426`, and the same defect on the sibling report path at `src/risk/challenge.rs:488`. A labelled notice stood at the first site and the wave-three repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:companion:inference`), which says a read returns the posterior at the moment of reading, the pseudo-counts being first decayed to the present as a pure function of elapsed time without mutating anything. **Severity** medium to high. The estimate is a third input to the derivation a host acts on, and an idle channel returns both a stale mean and a stale variance — the second too tight, which the corpus flags as the one direction in which staleness is not conservative. The magnitude is bounded by the decay table, so it matters on genuinely idle channels. **What conformance would mean** the read takes a clock and computes the decayed counts into locals. **The notice's prescribed remedy is itself wrong and should be corrected**: it asks for read access to be made clocked *and mutable*, and the corpus forbids the second half in terms — a read must not be a write. This is a repair the freeze's notice-improvement exception permits. **Coverage** none. Decay is exercised only through the write path. **Sequencing** the derivation tranche confirms this entry's severity argument at the crossing it names (`rem:assayer:resonance-cross-cites`): the point estimate enters the derived constants on every derivation call and the variance enters the kernel input, so one stale read moves every crossover location and every crossover variance at once. The direction the entry flags as the unconservative one is confirmed there too — a variance too tight narrows every action bandwidth through the same denominator and releases the display floor early. It shares its consumers with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`), which is the same boundary read from the far side.

**Repaired** in wave three at `64f1afda`, as the conformance line specified and against the notice's own remedy: every read — the estimate, the raw beta estimate, the effective sample size, the sufficiency verdict and the health report — takes the present and decays the pseudo-counts into locals, mutating nothing, and the pure read is pinned equal to what the mutating decay would have answered. The World harness reads and updates its tracker on its virtual clock, which closes the wall-clock seam the batch suite's decay budget was named for; retiring that budget is left to the harness lane that owns it.

**Entry (The importance weight returns the odds ratio)** · `entry:assayer:wl-importance-weights`

**Locus** `src/risk/challenge.rs:622`. A labelled notice stood here, and the wave-two repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:weighting:balancing-weights`), whose form makes each weight the reciprocal of twice the relevant class rate. The squaring claim was checked by recomputation at the corpus's own tabulated class rate of one tenth: the specified pair stands in a ratio of about nine, the shipped pair in a ratio of eighty-one, which is that ratio squared exactly. **Severity** high. These weights multiply the posterior gradient of every update, and the specified form's defining property — each class contributing exactly half the gradient at any class rate — is lost at every rate but one half. The ceiling also binds at roughly twice the specified rate, so every row of the corpus's table describes behaviour the code does not have. **What conformance would mean** the expression becomes the reciprocal form, and the doc comment stops stating the odds ratio as intended behaviour. **Coverage** worse than none, and the founding entry's own coverage line is corrected here by the risk tranche (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-risk`). It read the neighbouring class-rate tracker test — which does assert that the rate moves past one half and does not check a weight — and took it for the weight's coverage; three further tests call the weight function directly and were missed. Two of the three cannot tell the two forms apart, which is why they pass: at a balanced rate both forms return unity, and at a hundredth against the test's ceiling of ten both forms saturate to that ceiling. The third pins the divergence outright, asserting the odds ratio of nine at a one-in-ten positive rate where the specified form gives five. It pins it twice over, because its authored claim names the odds against the class as what the weight is for, so the divergent formula stands as this corpus's stated expectation in the one place a reader would look to learn the function's purpose. Repairing the weight now fails a test whose claim must be rewritten with it.

**Repaired** in wave two at `a636d10d`. The expression became the reciprocal form and the pinning test was restated with it: it now asserts the specified pair at the tabulated rate — five against five ninths — and the equal-halves property outright, rate times weight one half on both sides, under a claim arguing the balance rather than the odds. The two tests that could not tell the forms apart keep their assertions, with their comments corrected; the doc comment states the balancing form; the notice left with the defect.

**Entry (The eligibility predicate inverts two specified rows)** · `entry:assayer:wl-eligibility-predicate`

**Locus** `src/risk/challenge.rs:658`, with its only call site at `src/owner/label_path.rs:455`. A labelled notice stood here, and the wave-two repair took it with the defect it disclosed. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:eligibility:training`) on both rows: the table makes the slow action causally confounded and admits a failed challenge by default, and the predicate admits the slow action unconditionally and admits a challenge only under the ground-truth flag. The policy field the table's default belongs to ships, defaults as tabulated, and is read by nothing — the predicate is a constant function that cannot consult it. **Severity** high. This is the corpus's causal-inference contract. The sister and anchor models — the deployment's inherent-risk estimate — are trained on the confounded population, learning about the host's own intervention behaviour, and are simultaneously starved of the population the default policy admits. The corruption is in learned parameters and is silent. **What conformance would mean** the predicate accepts the policy, refuses the slow action without ground truth, and defers to the policy on a failed challenge; the policy is threaded to the call site. **Coverage** none on the two inverted rows. The suite covers exactly the two rows the corpus agrees with.

**Repaired** in wave two at `b7f1ab91`, as the conformance line specified: the predicate accepts the policy, refuses the slow action without ground truth, defers to the policy on a non-ground-truth challenge — pass and fail alike, since the Core cannot see which — and the policy is threaded from the configuration to the call site, so the field that was read by nothing is read. The two inverted rows gained the pins the coverage line found missing, each under a claim arguing its row's causal judgement rather than restating the predicate.

**Entry (Standardisation accepts a desynchronising extension without complaint)** · `entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-unchecked-extension`

**Locus** `src/feature/standardisation.rs:304`, which pushes one mean, one variance and one class per element handed to it and never compares the resulting length against the model dimension it tracks. **Disposition** unspecified. The corpus requires the class assignment to hold (`req:standardisation:class-assignment`) and does not say who enforces it, so the absence of a check is a gap rather than a violation. **Severity** medium, as an amplifier rather than a defect of its own. It is the reason (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`) is silent instead of loud, and a length assertion here would convert that item and any future sibling from a quiet corruption into a failing test, covering all four extension call sites at once. **What conformance would mean** the extension asserts its post-condition against the dimension it is extending alongside. **Coverage** not applicable; the point is that the check does not exist.

**Repaired** in wave four at `2def99cf`, as the conformance line specified: the extension takes the model dimension it extends alongside and asserts the vectors end exactly that long, at all four extension call sites through the one function. The silence this converts to a failing test is on record as the sibling entry's before-run — a desynchronising extension accepted without complaint — and the guard's own test hands the extension one class fewer than the dimension and reads the refusal.

**Entry (Two configuration knobs reach no behaviour)** · `entry:assayer:wl-inert-knobs`

**Locus** the guidance investigation flag at `src/guidance/mod.rs:80` and the initial calibration constant at `src/risk/calibration.rs:83`. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong for both, and the two differ in an important way. The investigation flag is inert and *disclosed*: the scorer takes it with an underscore and ignores it, and the legacy guidance record says in terms that the implemented scorer is the same proxy in both modes, so the finding is that a public surface advertises a control it does not have. The calibration constant is inert and *undisclosed*: it is read into a convergence tracker whose only reader is a health method with no production caller, while every shipped surface reads the published snapshot instead, where the value is hardcoded. **Severity** low for the first and medium for the second, on the disclosure difference: an undisclosed dead knob is one a host will tune and believe. **The disclosure argument for the first is narrowed in place by the public surface tranche** (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-cross-cites`), which owns the guidance cluster: the disclosure stands in the legacy record alone, and the specification conceals it — the interface signature tabulates the parameter set as two members and closes by saying the interface is implemented as specified down to the field names, against a shipped type carrying three (`sig:guidance:interface`). The severity is unchanged and the contrast drawn against the calibration constant is smaller than it was written. **What conformance would mean** each knob is either wired to the behaviour its name promises or removed from the public configuration. **Coverage** the calibration constant's own builder test states in its comment that the refit uses the cold-start value rather than the tracker's, and then asserts a value sourced from the snapshot — so the corpus's own test documents the knob's inertness while appearing to exercise it.

Two further inert knobs are recorded in the ledger tranche rather than here, because they are inert for a different reason — the code that would read them is never called (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-sweep-unscheduled`).

**Entry (The test corpus is weaker than its count suggests)** · `entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`

**Locus** distributed. The substantiated total is forty items over a corpus of roughly thirteen hundred tests, reached by three scripted passes over every test body with the results then read by hand; about forty-seven mechanically flagged candidates were removed as false positives, so an unfiltered scan would have reported ninety. **Disposition** unspecified as against the specification, which says nothing about test strength; the authority is the test documentation policy, under which every test carries an authored claim, and the finding is that a substantial minority of those claims are not established by the bodies beneath them. **Severity** medium, and structural rather than local. Its consequence is visible throughout this worklist: of the sixteen confirmed divergences the audit's two verification lanes returned, not one was caught by a test, and in two cases a test actively pins the divergence as its expected value.

| Class | Substantiated | Clearest shape |
| --- | --- | --- |
| No oracle | 30 | a test named for an ordering or a non-blocking property whose body observes neither |
| Tautological | 6, and two added by the identity and signal tranche | a test that re-implements the production formula and asserts on its own copy |
| Byte-identical bodies | 1 pair, three further pairs added by the health tranche, and a fifth added by the posterior and substrate tranche | two differently-named drop tests with the same two-line body |
| Conditionally vacuous | 1 | the only assertion nested inside a loop that may execute zero times |
| Inert-knob tests | 2 | see (`entry:assayer:wl-inert-knobs`) |
| Aimed at unreached code | 6, opened by the identity and signal tranche | a complete suite over a type no production path constructs |

**What conformance would mean** each item gains an oracle independent of the code under test — which is authoring work under the test documentation policy, not repair work, and belongs to a wave of its own rather than to the conformance fixes. **Coverage** not applicable.

The byte-identical class is enlarged by the health tranche and its shape is sharper than this entry recorded (`rem:assayer:health-pins`). Three further pairs stand between an inline test module and an external suite over the same module, and they matter beyond tidiness because the pairs share their authored claims: a claim the corpus counts as established by two tests is established by one test written twice. That makes the duplication a defect in the *register's* arithmetic rather than only in the suite, and it falls on the drift accumulators, whose specified triggers the same tranche found half unbuilt and wholly uncovered.

**The tautological row is enlarged and a sixth class is opened by the identity and signal tranche** (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-pins`). The two tautologies are the sharpest the campaign has met, because the module they stand in contains no update code for them to have tested and their presence is why that absence went unnoticed. The new class is a test whose oracle is real and adequate and pointed at code no production path constructs: it belongs under none of the five rows, since it is neither missing an oracle, nor circular, nor vacuous, nor a duplicate, and its consequence — the register counting coverage the shipped path does not have — is reached by a route the duplication row does not describe.

**The class is enlarged a second time and its excuse is removed by the posterior and substrate tranche** (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-pins`). A fifth pair stands inside a single file, over the storage constructor's rejection of an asymmetric matrix: the same fixture, the same perturbed element, the same tolerance and the same assertion, with the second test citing the claim the first mints. The health tranche's three pairs could be read as an artefact of the inline-and-external split, which is a structural excuse this pair does not have, so the shape is duplication rather than layout.

**That tranche also opens a class this entry does not carry**, and it belongs under none of the five rows: a test whose oracle is conditional on the build profile. The symmetric wrapper's audit test has no assertion in its body at all, its oracle being the debug assertion inside each operation it calls, so it is a real test under `cargo test` and an empty one under `cargo test --release` while its authored claim asserts the same thing in both. It is not a no-oracle test — under the profile the suite normally runs in, the oracle is present and is adequate — and it is neither tautological nor vacuous, so filing it under any existing row would mis-describe it. What conformance would mean for it differs in kind from the rest of this entry: not a new oracle, but a claim that says which profile establishes it, and the observation that none of the test profile's carriers currently has a place to say so.

**The derivation tranche adds four items and one class** (`rem:assayer:resonance-pins`). Two glosses claim bit-for-bit agreement over oracles that are absolute tolerances of one machine epsilon, which at the magnitudes those tests compare is tens of units in the last place for one assertion and tighter than one for another in the same body; one of the two also compares two of the three fields it says it compares, the omitted field being the one that scales every crossover's bandwidth. A third pins its property of a helper rather than of the function the claim is about, the transfer holding by argument and not by the test. A fourth is a conditional oracle of a shape the five rows do not cover and the sixth class does not either: **an oracle weaker than the property, by disjunction**. The corpus states that a display floor binds for two named actions; the test asserts that it binds for one *or* the other, and recomputation at the test's own inputs shows it binds for one. A disjunctive oracle is not missing, not circular, not vacuous and not duplicated — it is present, adequate for something, and adequate for something weaker than the claim above it, which is the hardest of these to see by reading a test body and the easiest to see by recomputing the corpus's number.

**Two authored claims in that cluster are false rather than weak**, which no row of this entry covers because the entry is about oracles and these are about statements. One asserts an inequality over the entropy gauge that the module's own first fixture refutes (`entry:assayer:wl-exploration-subadditivity-false`), and one describes the gauge as a measure of what a label would buy, which is what the specification writes a disclaimer to deny (`entry:assayer:wl-exploration-gauge-named-information-value`). Both must be restated with the repairs they belong to and neither can follow them, for the reason wave two's restatement gives: a claim explaining the wrong behaviour in the corpus's own vocabulary is the document a maintainer consults to decide the repair is wrong.

**Entry (Report staleness leaves the timestamp interface)** · `entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`

**Locus** `src/extraction/mod.rs:273`, where the staleness of a Sentinel's report is computed by taking a duration directly between two intra-process instants of the standard library's own type, with the ceiling reapplied by hand; the instant it measures from is stamped at ingestion, `src/report/ingestion.rs:133`, and the value becomes the seventh feature of the chain-structure block at `src/extraction/structure.rs:115`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong, and the authority is sharper than it first appears. The clock record does not merely prefer the timestamp interface; it states as a structural property that neither domain exposes an unclamped reading, so that a caller cannot obtain one without leaving the timestamp interface altogether (`dec:clock:embedded-clamp`). This call site leaves it. That the ceiling is reapplied by hand from the same constant makes the computed number right and the record's stated property false, which is the more serious of the two: a guarantee the record offers as unwritable is written here.

**Severity** medium for the feature and high for the harness. The number itself is clamped correctly, so nothing is corrupted. What the raw instant costs is injectability: the clock lane measured the package's suite red under concurrent machine load at this campaign's base commit — ten or eleven multi-channel failures in parallel runs from cold and warm alike, and one surviving even single-threaded — with the failures falling exclusively on the enrichment cells that compare reported values, because the elapsed interval between ingestion and assessment is whatever the machine's load made it. That measurement is the clock lane's and is recorded here as theirs rather than re-taken. A suite whose failures track machine load is a suite that cannot witness a conformance repair, which is why this item's fix is the one thing carved out of the freeze.

**What conformance would mean** the interval is measured through the monotonic domain's own accessor, which the harness can drive, so the comparison becomes exact rather than budgeted.

**Coverage** inverted: the enrichment comparisons are the coverage, and they are what the defect breaks.

**Sequencing** partly overtaken, and the close-out's locus sweep is what noticed (`rem:assayer:closeout-locus-sweep`). The carve-out lane has repaired the stamping half: report arrival is now taken from the injected clock at `src/report/ingestion.rs:133` rather than from the standard library's own call, so the interval's near end is drivable. The far end is not. The subtraction at `src/extraction/mod.rs:273` still takes its duration between two raw instants and still reapplies the ceiling by hand, which is the half this entry is actually about and the half the record's structural property turns on. The entry's severity is unchanged on both counts — the feature's number is still right, and the suite still cannot witness a repair until the far end is drivable too — and what has changed is that the remaining work is one subtraction rather than two readings.

**Entry (A timestamp's default is a hidden clock read)** · `entry:assayer:wl-timestamp-default-reads-clock`

**Locus** `src/types.rs:371`, where the persistent timestamp's default implementation returns the current reading.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:clock:embedded-clamp`) and against the separation the same record builds (`dec:clock:two-domains`). The record's case is that the domains are held apart by type so that a mistake becomes unwritable; a default that reads the wall clock reopens the mistake from a direction no type can guard, because it is reachable from every derived default of every structure that contains one, including in a test fixture that meant to construct a neutral value.

**Severity** medium. Nothing is currently known to compute a wrong number through it, but it is an unbounded time read with no call site to inspect, which makes it exactly the shape that defeats clock injection: a harness can control every explicit reading and still lose to a derived default. It is a plausible contributor to the suite's load sensitivity and should be closed alongside (`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`).

**What conformance would mean** the default is the epoch or the type carries no default at all, so that obtaining the present requires saying so.

**Coverage** none; a defect of this shape is invisible to a test that does not already suspect it.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `b24b21c2`, beside the field seal, by the conformance line's stronger option: the type carries no default at all, so obtaining the present requires saying so — through the explicit reading or an injected clock. The crate-test that pinned the defaulted timestamp as the moment of its creation is retired with the impl it pinned, and the absence is held by the same source scan that holds the field seal (`test:crate:timestamp-fields-and-default-stay-sealed`). No derived default of any containing structure survived to argue for the weaker option: the compiler found no site.

**Entry (A batch shares one clock and the code reads two)** · `entry:assayer:wl-batch-timestamp-split`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:801`, where the per-request assessment takes a fresh persistent reading, in a function that already receives the batch head's monotonic instant as a parameter.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:ordering:batch-timestamp`), which says a batch is answered against one timestamp captured at its head and concludes that two requests in one batch therefore cannot disagree about the present. The letter of that promise is kept for the monotonic domain, which is threaded from the head; it is broken for the persistent domain, which is what the Ledger and identity decay paths actually read. So two requests in one batch can and do disagree about the persistent present.

The record's own care makes the divergence pointed rather than incidental. Three lines below the reading, the same function refuses to load the model snapshot a second time, with a comment explaining that a second load could see a different version — the code is deliberate about consistency of view and accidental about consistency of time, in adjacent statements.

**Severity** medium. The disagreement is bounded by the batch's own duration, so the decayed values two requests read differ by microseconds and no assessment is meaningfully wrong today. The finding's weight is that this is a production behaviour change to repair, not a comment fix: threading the head's persistent reading changes what every request in a batch computes, and the record and the claims that rest on it are implicated. It should therefore be scheduled as a conformance change with its own evidence rather than folded into the clock lane's harness work.

**What conformance would mean** the persistent reading is taken once at the batch head beside the monotonic one and threaded, or the record is amended to say that the batch shares its monotonic clock only — and the first is what (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) presumes, the second being available only on an argument nobody has yet made.

**Coverage** none. Nothing asserts that two requests in one batch see the same persistent reading.

**Re-verified at the crate glue** (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-glue`). The finding stands and its locus has moved: the per-request persistent read is `src/assessment.rs:801`, not `:795`. The batch head is confirmed at `src/api/assess.rs:44`, which takes one monotonic reading under a comment citing the decision by label. The exculpation the chapter would supply — that a batch carries no consistency guarantee beyond each request seeing whatever state was current when it ran (`req:runtime:assessment-interface`) — is refused by the decision itself, which separates the two: the batch shares a clock and does not share a view. The per-request snapshot load at `src/api/assess.rs:52` is the permitted half. **Falsifier** a corpus text licensing a per-request reading in the persistent domain specifically, which this tranche looked for and did not find.

**Remark (What did not survive re-verification)** · `rem:assayer:worklist-refutations`

Four handed items were refuted, and they are named here rather than dropped, because an audit that quietly discards what it disproves is not measuring anything.

The notice (`todo:code:only-tests-construct-the-challenge-effectiveness`) is **conformant** and should be retired rather than fixed. It reports as a defect exactly the property (`inv:guarantee:companion-independence`) requires: the Core holds no Companion state and nothing in it routes a label to the tracker, and the corpus names the Companion among the surfaces that are deliberately not Core state. A Core call site would be the violation, not the repair. Adjacent to it there is a real unmet requirement the notice does not raise — the replacement interface the companion chapter asks for does not exist — and that is worklist material where the notice was not.

Of the claim waves' catalogue, three items did not survive. The byte-identical bodies were reported in the report and validation surface and are not there: those files hold thirty-two tests between them and all are distinct, the earlier finding having been a scanner artefact that enumerated one file twice and matched its tests against themselves. The genuine duplicate pair is elsewhere and is registered above. The operational decay rate was reported inert and is live: it is validated at build time and consumed on the label path, and what is actually inert is a neighbouring tracker rate, which is (`entry:assayer:wl-p-plus-tracker-rates`). And the vacuous-pass class as handed — assertions on constants, self-comparisons, a container checked non-empty just after being filled — is absent from this corpus entirely: none of the three shapes occurs in any of the roughly thirteen hundred tests. One vacuity of a different kind survives and is registered under the weak-test entry.

The handed count of roughly forty weak tests is correct at forty, but the agreement is a coincidence of two errors rather than a confirmation, and it is recorded that way.

**Remark (Three defects in the corpus's own prose)** · `rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`

The audit measures code against the corpus, so a defect in the corpus is outside its disposition grammar; these three are recorded for the record pipeline rather than adjudicated here.

The configuration chapter states that the tests exercise the specified chain-length normaliser. Most of them do not — the majority of the extraction tests pass the shipped value — so a reader trusting that sentence believes a regression guard exists where none does, and none of the tests asserts the default itself. This entry was overstated as first written and is corrected by the extraction tranche (`rem:assayer:extraction-prose-defects`), which counts them. The assessment chapter and the snapshot notice both say the snapshot carries five of the nine specified fields; against the schema's nine rows it carries four, the fifth structure field not being one of the nine, so both understate the gap by one. And the legacy assessment-contract record cannot state its own arity: it says six fields in one place, five in another, and prescribes a verification of six, while the sixth has never shipped. The third is the reason the spec-stale defence for (`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`) fails: a record that contradicts itself on the contract's size is a weak authority to weigh against a specification that does not.

**Remark (One worklist item lies outside the package)** · `rem:assayer:out-of-package-item`

The claim waves' stale-documentation finding was handed to this audit as an Assayer item and is not one. Its subject is the upstream graph package's handle semantics, and its record is live rather than retired — the renumbering the campaign performed was of this package's records, not that one's. The finding itself survives and inverts: the documentation in several places still says a stale handle yields an empty result, the code asserts and panics in release as well as debug, and three tests prove the panic.

The disposition is **divergent-spec-stale**, which is the one such verdict this wave records, and the argument is what the grammar demands. A later record of that package supersedes the earlier two on precisely this question and rules that a stale handle panics in both build profiles because its use is a logic error; that record names the empty result as the defect it was written to remove, since a handle whose generation has been reused resolves to an unrelated node and yields a silently wrong answer; and that package's own API document already agrees with the code in one place while contradicting it in another some hundreds of lines later. The code implements the later ruling. Reinstating the empty result would restore a documented silent-corruption hazard.

The consequence for this campaign is a scope question rather than a work item: the repair belongs to the upstream package's own corpus, and the Assayer's consumption surface carries no such claim and needs no change. It is recorded here so the founding worklist's arithmetic is honest about what it holds.

## The deferral pass · `sec:assayer:audit-deferrals`

**Convention (What pinning a deferral means here)** · `conv:assayer:deferral-pinning`

The deferral register was audited once already, by the wave that gave every item a label and migrated it into the record that owns the deferred decision.
