In that band the refit is attempted and abandoned every time, and the bookkeeping that records a completed refit never advances — so a full search runs on every label rather than on the specified cadence, and the count of completed refits drives the convergence state to its terminal value after a handful of labels.

**Severity** high. It is the one finding in this cluster that is both a wrong number and a wrong amount of work: a diagnostic a host reads reports convergence that has not happened, and the assessment path pays a full fit per label.

**What conformance would mean** the floor is compared against the undiscounted count, which is the quantity the pre-check already computes, leaving the recency weights to the fit where they belong.

**Test coverage** none on the band; two construction tests pin buffer and cadence values below the specified floors, so validating those floors would turn both red.

**Repaired** in wave two at `678fcb57`. The floor is compared against the undiscounted weighted counts — the quantity the pre-check already computed — and the recency factor stays in the objective. The pre-check's comment asserting a deliberate mismatch is replaced by the agreement it now has, and the band has a pin: a buffer built inside it — undiscounted census above the floor, discounted reading below — is fitted, with the reported effective samples matching an independent recomputation. The two construction tests the coverage line names pin configuration floors this repair does not touch and stand for the wave that validates configuration.

**Entry (The replacement surface the companion chapter requires does not exist)** · `entry:assayer:wl-risk-replacement-trait`

**Locus** distributed: the shipped tracker lives inside the package and is exported from its root, and no trait stands anywhere in the workspace.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`req:companion:replacement-trait`). This is the gap the founding worklist flagged when it refuted the neighbouring notice — the notice reported as a defect the very property (`inv:guarantee:companion-independence`) requires, and the real unmet requirement beside it was left unregistered. It is registered here. The record concedes the same gap and assigns the fault the same way (`cav:challenge:arrangement-open`), so this entry adds the evidence rather than the judgement: the chapter asks for a two-method surface, a scalar estimate and a posterior, and neither the surface nor any implementation of it exists.

A second reading of the same chapter has no carrier either: the inference algorithm speaks of the posterior's two counts, and the type the package hands out is a bare moment pair, so nothing can return the counts even if a trait existed to ask for them.

**Severity** medium. Nothing computed is wrong, and by (`cav:challenge:unwired`) nothing runs at all; what is absent is the extension point the chapter requires, so a host wanting its own tracker must fork rather than implement.

**What conformance would mean** the trait is declared with its two methods and the shipped tracker implements it.

**Test coverage** not applicable; the surface does not exist to be tested.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `3c816d9d`. The trait is declared with its two methods and exported beside the shipped tracker, which implements it: a provider answers a scalar estimate, and the posterior method defaults to a moment-matched Beta built from that estimate, so a provider of a scalar and a variance participates at first-order fidelity without implementing anything further. The second reading's missing carrier exists too — the posterior crosses as its two pseudo-counts, the type the entry found nothing able to return — and the tracker overrides the default with its exact decayed counts. A scalar-only provider's default posterior reproduces its mean and variance exactly, with a pair no Beta can carry answered from the uniform prior (`test:unit:replacement-trait-default-posterior-moment-matches`), and the tracker as a trait object answers its genuine conjugate counts (`test:unit:shipped-tracker-implements-the-replacement-surface`).

**Table (The risk cluster's remaining divergences)** · `tab:assayer:risk-remaining`

| Divergence | Authority | Locus | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The calibration record ships five of the seven tabulated fields, and the absent effective uncertainty is what blocks empirical coverage | (`constr:platt:buffer`) | `src/risk/calibration.rs` | medium |
| Nine of the eleven tabulated calibration constraints are unvalidated, so a recency factor above unity silently inverts the weighting and one below zero yields negative weights that pass the guard | (`tab:config:calibration`) | `src/risk/calibration.rs:547` | medium |
| The initial calibration constant is read into a tracker no production surface reads, while every shipped surface takes the value from the snapshot, where it is a literal | (`cav:calibration:pre-calibration`) | `src/risk/calibration.rs:83` | medium |

The third row is the second half of (`entry:assayer:wl-inert-knobs`), re-verified and corrected in one particular: the constant's own wiring *does* have a production caller, and what has no production reader is the convergence tracker's health method. The registered coverage note verifies exactly as written — the builder test states in its comment that the refit uses the cold-start value rather than the tracker's, and then asserts a value taken from the snapshot against a predicate true for every admissible setting.

**Remark (A spec-stale verdict was proposed here and did not survive)** · `rem:assayer:risk-spec-stale-refused`

One reading of this cluster returned the only **divergent-spec-stale** verdict either tranche produced, and it is recorded here because it failed, and because how it failed is the clearest demonstration in the campaign of what (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) costs the party proposing one.

The claim was that the risk chapter's sentence saying the blend weight moves with elapsed time describes a mechanism the code does not have. A second lane was set to break it, and found every one of its factual grounds true: the correction is not read on any path reaching the weight, the two weights the module's own fixture produces are bit-identical, and the sister and the anchor carry the same forgetting rate, so nothing in the weight varies with time.

None of that discharges the burden. The grammar asks for a later decision that superseded the text or a mechanism the text describes that no longer exists, and neither is available: no record supersedes the definition the sentence belongs to, the corpus's own register still marks it as binding and shipped, and the mechanism it names is described in the present tense elsewhere in the specification. The decision that looked like the superseding one cannot be — its argument is that the raw and corrected forms produce the *same* number, and claiming equivalence is the opposite of claiming supersession. Measured against this campaign's one successful spec-stale argument (`rem:assayer:out-of-package-item`), which names the later record and quotes it naming the defect it was written to remove, this claim reaches nothing like the bar.

The right answer was a third one. The sentence is imprecise rather than stale: worked through the specification's own algebra the weight's entire dependence on time sits in an unscaled guard term, so the clause is a non sequitur from the corpus's own formula rather than evidence the corpus has gone out of date. The substantive requirement — that the uncertainties are time-corrected and the point estimates are not — is met, and is recorded as a conformant row. The clause itself belongs with the corpus's other prose defects (`rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`), which sit outside the disposition grammar by construction. Its one live hazard is that a reader repairing the code to match it would break a correct computation.

Two consequences are worth stating. The audit still records exactly one spec-stale verdict, the upstream one, which is what the founding worklist already claimed and which is now claimed against an attempt to add a second. And the record's own word for the cancellation — *exactly* — is wrong by the same algebra, the guard term leaving a residual some twelve times the tolerance the module's fixture asserts. No test fails today, because both weights are built from raw forms and the observed difference is zero, which means the test named for the cancellation would pass unchanged if the cancellation were false. That is a third pinned shape: a test tautological with respect to its own claim.

**Remark (Asking who calls a Companion is asking the wrong question)** · `rem:assayer:risk-census`

The census question needs restating before it is asked of this cluster, because the answer the ledger tranche taught us to look for is here a requirement rather than a defect. The whole Companion surface has no production call site, and (`inv:companion:boundary`) demands exactly that while (`cav:challenge:unwired`) records it. A Core call site would be the violation. That is why the founding worklist's refutation of the notice at the tracker stands, re-verified here by an exhaustive census of every construction: all of them are tests or test support. The lesson the ledger tranche drew — ask who calls this — survives, but with a second half attached: having found that nothing calls it, ask next whether the corpus wants anything to.

Asked that way the census still returns results. Two functions on the tracker have no call site anywhere in the workspace, not even a test, which no corpus text requires and which is the ordinary uncalled shape rather than the mandated one. Two configuration constants on the challenge block reach no reader at all and belong to the configuration cluster's tranche rather than this one. And the four helpers the label path does use are the cluster's only live surface, which is what concentrates this cluster's two worst divergences into so few lines.

**Remark (Two of this cluster's registered items were recorded as uncovered and are not)** · `rem:assayer:risk-pins`

The test-pin question returns the strongest result of the wave here. Two founding entries in this cluster carry a coverage line reading *none*, and both lines are wrong in the same direction — the tests exist, and they defend the divergence rather than catching it.

The importance weight's case is corrected in place above. The eligibility predicate's is the same shape: the Challenge row is pinned twice, once in the predicate's own suite and once in an integration test, so the row the corpus calls unconditionally eligible is asserted as requiring ground truth in two places. Only the Slow row is genuinely uncovered. Adjacent to both, the predicate's suite carries a comment acknowledging that the policy is not threaded — the suite documenting the gap rather than failing on it.

A third pin was found in the blend, on a quantity the corpus defines against one input and the test asserts against another, and it is tautological besides, re-implementing the production expression and asserting on its own copy. Its risk is directional and worth naming: a maintainer reconciling the code against the documentation would change the production quantity and break a passing test, which is the failure mode a pinned divergence creates and the reason this question is asked at all.

Taken with the ledger tranche's find and the owner cluster's two, the audit has now met five tests that assert a divergence as their expected value and two that stand hollow over a central guarantee. That is enough instances to say the pattern is structural rather than incidental, and it strengthens (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) considerably: the corpus's own suite is not merely weak in places but is in several places the thing that would have to be rewritten first before a conformance repair could land.

## Fourth tranche: the feature vector · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-feature`

The cluster is `src/feature/` — the dimension map, the aggregate block, the interaction templates, the assembly of the vector at assessment and at label time, the standardisation statistics, and the two warm-up accumulators — taken against the two records the scope map assigns it (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`), vector and substrate, and against the feature data-model chapters, which is the largest single body of checkable claims the specification holds: eight blocks in a fixed order, a dimension formula in eight terms, fifteen tabulated aggregate features, five template types, three map invariants, a three-stage compilation pipeline, a nineteen-row table of class priors, and four algorithms with numbered steps.

The cluster was taken fourth because the tranche register asked for it. The owner tranche left the extension-order question open and named this directory as the place that owns the dimension map; the founding worklist's highest-severity feature item has its locus here; and three of the corpus's notices prescribe a class-assignment function whose home is this cluster's standardisation module. It is also the cluster where the ledger tranche's census question pays best, for a reason recorded below: more of this directory is uncalled than of any cluster audited so far.

**Table (What the feature cluster conforms to)** · `tab:assayer:feature-conformant`

Recorded first, because an audit that lists only its findings misrepresents what it read. Fifty-one rows were checked across the cluster and hold; the selection below is the load-bearing part of that set, and the nineteen class priors are summarised in one row rather than tabulated one by one.

| Checked | Authority | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The rebuild walks the eight blocks with one cursor, in the specified order, and assigns every index exactly once | (`inv:dimension:covering`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:285` |
| A rebuild is a total recomputation from the current registries, consulting no prior map state | (`alg:dimension:compaction`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:276` |
| The cross-dimension block is present exactly when a dimension is registered | (`def:feature:vector-structure`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:311` |
| The per-dimension block is eight fixed features plus three per registered axis | (`tab:feature:dimension-formula`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:302` |
| The axis offset within a dimension block is eight plus three times the registry position | (`conv:dimension:axis-index`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:299` |
| The slot is the occupancy indicator plus sixty extraction positions and two per spatial axis | (`def:extraction:slot`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:327` |
| Every map in the record is insertion-ordered, and competitive cells are grouped by dimension in registration order | (`schema:dimension:map-record`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:408` |
| Competitive indicators occupy the final block, after the interactions | (`def:dimension:competitive-range`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:406` |
| The fifteen aggregate features are computed in the tabulated order, over reporting Sentinels only | (`tab:feature:aggregate-block`) | `src/feature/aggregate.rs:128` |
| Concordance is the fraction of reporting Sentinels above that axis's own threshold | (`tab:feature:aggregate-block`) | `src/feature/aggregate.rs:171` |
| Breadth counts the axes whose fleet maximum is above threshold, and the cross-axis product is novelty against displacement | (`tab:feature:aggregate-block`) | `src/feature/aggregate.rs:175` |
| Thresholds start at one half and are recomputed every thousand assessments from the observed window | (`alg:feature:concordance-recalibration`) | `src/health/concordance.rs:195`, read at `src/assessment.rs:934` |
| Interaction products are formed from raw values, before standardisation, and the interaction slot is standardised afterwards as a feature in its own right | (`mot:feature:interaction-overview`) | `src/feature/assembly.rs:389` |
| Assembly addresses every block through the map rather than through a computed position | (`inv:dimension:contiguity`) | `src/feature/assembly.rs:327` |
| A slot's extraction is upcast from single to double precision at assembly | (`rem:extraction:single-precision`) | `src/feature/assembly.rs:367` |
| The occupancy indicator is one when the Sentinel reports and zero otherwise, and an absent Sentinel leaves its whole slot zero | (`def:extraction:slot`) | `src/feature/assembly.rs:364` |
| Sub-score extraction reads cell z-scores at the first four extraction positions and cell cumulative sums at the twenty-fifth through twenty-eighth | (`tab:extraction:chain-z-scores`) | `src/feature/assembly.rs:472` |
| The bias position is never standardised, on either the read or the update path | (`alg:standardisation:label-time-procedure`) | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:231` |
| The statistics update clips for the update alone and takes the variance toward the squared deviation from the previous mean | (`alg:standardisation:label-time-procedure`) steps 2 and 5 | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:279` |
| The tracking rate, the clip width, the variance floor and the initialisation count ship as specified | (`alg:standardisation:label-time-procedure`) | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:195` |
| All nineteen class priors match the tabulated pair, recomputed one by one | (`tab:standardisation:class-priors`) | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:126` |
| Compaction retains the surviving positions of all three statistics vectors together | (`alg:dimension:compaction`) | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:328` |
| Both accumulators are one-pass incremental updates rather than sums of squares, and both reset at the new width when the dimension changes | (`alg:standardisation:batch-initialisation`) step 2 | `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:139`, `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:250` |

**Entry (The anchor projection gathers the two positions the corpus computes)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-anchor-gathered-pair`

**Locus** `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:447` and `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:448`. A labelled notice stood over the projection's construction, and the wave-seven repair took it with the defect it disclosed.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:dimension:anchor-projection`), which fixes the fifteen-position anchor input and says in terms that thirteen positions are gathered directly and two are computed: the fourteenth is the maximum over the four per-axis maximum z-score positions, and the fifteenth is the logarithm of one plus the reporting Sentinel count. The code gathers all fifteen. The last two are taken as the fourteenth and fifteenth positions of the aggregate block, which by (`tab:feature:aggregate-block`) are coverage and the maximum cumulative sum — two quantities the projection table does not name at all, one of them already a fraction and the other a cumulative sum where the corpus asks for a maximum and a logarithm.

The projection is live on both paths that read it: the assessment path gathers through it at `src/assessment.rs:1040` and the label path at `src/owner/label_path.rs:596`. The six cross-dimension entries above them are correct against the block's own order, and the count of six from an eight-wide block is what the corpus asks for, so the divergence is exactly the two computed positions and nothing else.

**Severity** medium. The anchor is the deployment's inherent-risk estimate and reads a fixed fifteen-position vector, so two of its fifteen inputs carry a quantity the corpus did not choose for it. Nothing is corrupted in the sense of a wrong arithmetic result — the anchor learns weights on whatever it is shown — but the model is a different model from the specified one, its reference to the per-axis maxima is absent, and it cannot see the reporting count at all, which is the one input the corpus gives it for judging how much of the fleet the estimate rests on. It is medium rather than high because the anchor is never extended or marginalised and the substitution is stable, so no learned weight is ever invalidated by it.

**What conformance would mean** the fourteenth position becomes the maximum over the four per-axis maximum z-score positions and the fifteenth the logarithm of one plus the reporting Sentinel count, both computed at projection time rather than gathered — which is what the notice already asks for.

**Coverage** worse than none, and doubly so. Two tests stand on the projection and both pin the gather as the design. The first, at `src/tests/dimension_map.rs:354`, asserts that every one of the fifteen entries is present and in bounds, and its authored claim states that "the projection is a gather by index" — the divergence named as the mechanism, in the one place a reader would look to learn what the projection is. The second, at `src/tests/dimension_map.rs:385`, asserts that the fourteenth and fifteenth entries are present when no identity dimension is registered, which is an assertion that they are gathers rather than computations. Repairing the projection turns both red and requires both claims to be rewritten.

**Repaired** in wave seven at `01472af9`. The fourteenth position is the maximum over the four per-axis maximum z-score positions and the fifteenth the logarithm of one plus the reporting Sentinel count, both computed at projection time. The two entries hold no index, which is also what settles the sister model's projected quadratic form: a computed position names no sister coordinate, so contributing nothing to the shared subspace is right rather than an omission. Both live paths go through one helper and each supplies the reporting count it holds — the assessment counts extractions carrying features, the label path reads the set frozen at assessment time. The two tests that pinned the gather are rewritten with their claims and a third is added for the computed pair.

**Entry (Every position the package starts with is classed as a z-score)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-cold-start-classes`

**Locus** the cold-start construction, `src/snapshot/working.rs`, with the statistics vectors beside it. A labelled notice stood there, and the wave-seven repair took it with the defect it disclosed.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`req:standardisation:class-assignment`) and against (`tab:standardisation:class-priors`). The requirement derives every position's class from three authorities — the map for the block and the slot, the fixed extraction layout for the class within a slot, the signal schema for the signal block — and the priors table gives each class its pair. The steward's construction assigns the generic standardising class to every position at once and initialises the means to zero and the variances to one directly, rather than from any class's prior.

This is the same requirement the owner tranche registered at its resize fallback (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-absent-class-assignment`), and it is registered again rather than folded into that entry because the two are different failures of it. That entry is a gap-fill: positions a lifecycle event added arrive without classes and the resize picks one. This is the initial state: at construction the vector is the bias, the fifteen aggregates and the whole signal block, and every one of them is classed as a z-score for the life of the process, because nothing ever revisits a class once assigned. Against the priors table that is wrong at six aggregate positions by inspection — the four concordance features and coverage are rates and fractions at three tenths and a twentieth, and the maximum cumulative sum is a cumulative sum at two and twenty-five — and wrong at every signal position by construction, since the schema's declared shapes are what (`req:standardisation:class-assignment`) makes the authority there and nothing consults them.

The class-assignment function the corpus requires is absent, as the owner tranche found, and this cluster adds a second absent function beside it. The notice at `src/feature/standardisation.rs:368` names two — a signal-class expansion and the class assignment itself — and neither exists. The signal expansion's own three references stand at that notice, at `src/owner/thread.rs:153`, and at `src/signal/schema.rs:67`, the last being a file the owner tranche's census did not reach; so the corpus's derived class rule is unimplemented on both of its dynamic authorities rather than on one.

**Severity** medium, and its argument is a compounding one rather than a local one. The generic class carries neutral moments, so a mis-classed position is standardised as though already standardised, and the corpus's own estimate is that priors matter for about one standardisation half-life, some three thousand five hundred labels. Two things stop that decay argument from being the whole story. The maximum cumulative sum's specified prior standard deviation is five where the shipped one is unity, so that position enters five times too loud against an isotropic model prior for the whole of that half-life. And the mechanism that is supposed to end the wait early is (`alg:standardisation:batch-initialisation`), whose completion the owner tranche found can be discarded permanently and silently when a channel is full (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-batch-init-drop`) — after which every position stays on the generic prior for the life of the process rather than for a half-life. The two entries are independent defects with a shared worst case, and neither's severity can be argued without the other.

**What conformance would mean** the class vector is derived from the map and the signal schema at construction and recomputed after every rebuild, and the statistics are initialised from the resulting classes' priors — which is the two absent functions existing and `init_from_priors` being called.

**Coverage** none, and none is possible from the outside: no test observes the class vector the steward builds, and the one class-related assertion in the cluster's own suite constructs its classes by hand.

**Repaired in part** in wave seven at `3c363821`, and the remaining half is recorded here rather than claimed. The two absent functions exist: `expand_signal_classes` reads a declaration's shape and gives the same class to each position that shape occupies, and `assign_feature_classes` derives every position's class from the map's own position enumeration, so it follows the canonical layout by construction rather than keeping a second copy of it. The rebuild recomputes rather than padding, which discharges the resize fallback (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-absent-class-assignment`) in passing.

What is **not** done is the clause that reads "and the statistics are initialised from the resulting classes' priors". `init_from_priors` still has no call site. Calling it makes the cold-start vector non-degenerate, and a non-degenerate vector reaches a discontinuity in the assessment path's uncertainty that an all-zeros one does not: a thousand pure repeated reads of one request step their uncertainty from 0.79 to 2.52 around the hundredth, which `thousand_identical_derivations_stable` catches and which no drift band accommodates. The step was isolated to the prior initialisation and not to the rebuild's recomputation, by disabling each half in turn. It is a defect in its own right — a read that changes what a later read reports — and it is not this entry's to repair, so the priors wait on it. The holding is recorded at `33f1b406`.

**Entry (A specified warm-up algorithm ships complete and never runs)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-bootstrap-unrun`

**Locus** distributed across the four steps: the accumulator at `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:98`, the slot that would hold one at `src/report/slot.rs:94`, and the blend that would consume it at `src/owner/thread.rs:273`. A labelled notice stood at the last and the wave-three repair took it with the defect it disclosed.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:standardisation:sentinel-bootstrap`), which fixes four steps: open a per-Sentinel accumulator of capacity one hundred on registration, accumulate that Sentinel's slot values with occupancy included from each assessment it reports in, blend the empirical statistics into its standardisation entries at the specified weight leaving the remaining fifth on the priors, and release the accumulator. Every step was censused separately and none of the four runs.

The accumulator is implemented and correct. Its opener has exactly one call site in the workspace, `src/tests/report.rs:296`. Its observation method has no call site outside its own test module at all, so step two never executes. The command that carries a completed set of statistics to the model owner is declared at `src/owner/commands.rs:96` and *handled* at `src/owner/thread.rs:273`, where the handler logs and returns; its only sender in the workspace is `src/tests/owner.rs:758`. So step three is a no-op reached only from a test, and the notice standing in that handler concedes it in terms. The health event that would announce a completed bootstrap is emitted nowhere but a test either.

The census question's second half is asked and answered against it: the corpus does not merely permit this surface to run, it specifies it as one of the three mechanisms that feed the standardisation statistics (`req:standardisation:timing`), and names the deficiency it exists to remove (`cav:limitation:late-standardisation`). Nothing here is a met requirement.

**Severity** medium to high. Nothing computed is wrong. What is lost is that every Sentinel registered after the deployment has warmed up is standardised against priors for a full standardisation half-life across the whole of its slot — sixty-one positions and more — where the corpus shortens that to a hundred assessments. It compounds with the entry above, because the priors it waits out are not even its own classes' priors but the generic one, and it compounds with the batch initialisation's discard for the same reason: of the three mechanisms (`req:standardisation:timing`) names, one is unwired, one can be lost silently, and the third is a tracking average that a deployment receiving no labels does not move at all.

**What conformance would mean** registration opens the accumulator on the slot, the assessment path observes the new Sentinel's slot values into it, and the handler blends at the specified weight and publishes — which is the blend function the notice names existing and the three call sites being made.

**Coverage** the accumulator's arithmetic is well tested and that it is ever opened, observed or completed outside a test is not. This is the ledger tranche's uncalled-sweep shape met a second time, in a second cluster, and the two should be read together.

**Repaired** in wave three at `a07e04e5`, the three call sites exactly as the conformance line named them: registration's synchronous phase opens the accumulator on the slot at the configured capacity, the extraction loop observes each reporting assessment's raw occupancy-and-features slot values through the shared state on the batch-initialisation pattern, and the owner's handler blends a completed accumulator into that Sentinel's slot range at the configured weight, emits the completion event, and publishes. A completion arriving at a stale slot width is dropped rather than blended misaligned, since the live accumulator's own reset rule would have restarted it. The two configuration knobs' descriptions now state what the algorithm reads them as. An engine-level test pins the occupancy entry's published mean at exactly the blend of the observed value with what the entry held — and finding that any later lifecycle publication erases the blend from the published statistics is this wave's live confirmation of the lifecycle-standardisation entry wave four carries.

**Entry (Neither accumulator skips the non-finite entries both algorithms exclude)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-nonfinite-accumulation`

**Locus** `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:148` and `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:259`. Both stood disclosed by notices, one at each loop, and the repair took the notices with the defect they disclosed.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:standardisation:batch-initialisation`) step 1 and (`alg:standardisation:sentinel-bootstrap`) step 2, which both require non-finite entries to be skipped element by element. Neither loop tests finiteness; each takes every entry into the running mean and the second moment unconditionally.

**The observable sequence.** One non-finite entry at one position, at any point in the hundred observations, leaves that position's running mean and second moment non-finite for the remainder of the accumulation, because every subsequent update is a difference against them. The completion then applies the empirical statistics *wholesale*, which is what the algorithm says it does, so the position's published mean and variance are non-finite. Standardisation divides by them at `src/feature/standardisation.rs:236` and the position's standardised value is non-finite on every subsequent assessment, after which the inner product every model takes against the vector is non-finite too. So one bad entry at one position disables every prediction the deployment makes, permanently, and the element-by-element skip the corpus specifies is precisely the guard against it.

**Severity** high. It is the only defect this tranche found whose consequence is total rather than proportional, and it is silent at the point of entry: the accumulation records nothing, and the failure surfaces later and elsewhere. The notices themselves concede the exposure, each naming the upstream audit whose miss it is meant to survive, which is the corpus's own reason for putting the skip in the algorithm rather than trusting the audit.

**What conformance would mean** each loop tests the entry for finiteness and leaves that position's running pair untouched when the test fails, which is one condition in each of two loops.

**Coverage** worse than none, and this is the cluster's clearest hollow test. `init_stats_are_finite` at `src/feature/bootstrap.rs:654` is named for exactly this property, and its authored claim states the hazard in full — that a non-finite entry "would corrupt every feature the slot ever produces". Its body feeds three finite streams and asserts the outputs are finite. A test named for non-finite safety, whose claim names the consequence, and which never presents a non-finite value, is the no-oracle class of (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) standing directly over a high-severity divergence. Nothing else in the workspace feeds a non-finite value to either accumulator.

**Repaired** in wave one at `755679c1`. The hollow test became the falsifier — it now presents a NaN and an infinity mid-stream — and its claim was restated to the guard rather than to the finite stream it used to feed; a second test carrying the same claim covers the batch-initialisation loop, which had no test of this property at all.

**Entry (The label-time slot guard truncates the position the corpus puts last)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-frozen-slot-truncation`

**Locus** `src/feature/assembly.rs:612`, the shorter-of-the-two copy length, with the comment beneath it at `src/feature/assembly.rs:616` asserting the guard is correct in both directions.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:extraction:slot`) taken with (`def:dimension:layers`). The extraction is six groups in a fixed order and the per-axis outcome-memory pairs are the *fifth* group, not the last: the batch context feature follows them. The map holds this and says so — the base outcome-memory offset is invariant at fifty-seven under every lifecycle event and the per-axis offsets give each axis's pair its own position — and the guard consults neither, copying a prefix whose length is the smaller of the stored and current widths.

**The observable sequence.** Assess a request, register or deregister a spatially-enabled outcome axis, then label that request. The stored extraction and the current slot differ in width by two. On a deregistration the copy drops the final two stored positions, which are the batch context feature and the last axis pair's second half; if the removed axis was not the last registered, every surviving later axis then reads the following axis's values and the batch context value is read as an axis average. On a registration the copy stops two short, so the new axis's pair takes zero — which the comment claims is correct — while the batch context feature is read at the position the new pair now owns and its own position takes zero. In every case the guard preserves the width and loses the alignment, which is the same failure the owner tranche found at the whole-vector scale (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-layout-misalignment`) reproduced inside one slot.

**Severity** medium. It is bounded to pending entries that outlive an axis lifecycle event affecting the spatial set, so it is not the common path; it is medium rather than low because what it corrupts is a training vector, silently, and because the comment above it is a false assurance of exactly the kind the ledger tranche registered at the absence cast — a reader who checks the guard finds it argued and stops looking.

**What conformance would mean** the frozen extraction is placed through the map's per-axis outcome-memory offsets, so that each surviving axis's pair lands at its own new position, absent axes take their class prior or zero by position, and the batch context feature is placed last on both sides.

**Coverage** none. No test in the workspace reconstructs a vector across an axis lifecycle event, and the reconstruction the label path actually runs never reaches this function at all, for the reason recorded at (`rem:assayer:feature-census`).

**Repaired** in wave seven at `cd1c6b8d`. The extraction is placed group by group: the fixed run ahead of the pairs copies as itself, each stored pair lands at the pair position of the same rank, a pair the assessment never held stays zero, and the batch context is placed last on both sides. The comment asserting the prefix copy correct in both directions goes with it. One boundary is stated rather than crossed: position cannot recover *which* axis a stored pair belonged to, the stored extraction being a flat vector carrying no axis identities, so an axis retired from the middle still shifts its successors' pairs by one rank. The entry recorded no coverage and none existed; a test now reconstructs across an axis registration and fails on the prefix copy at the batch context's own position, and the two tests that pinned the truncation are rewritten with their claims at `8b8f0a87`.

That stated boundary is closed at `9560d68e`. The spatial axis identities are in the map — `slot_axis_offsets`, the slot tail's counterpart to the `id_axis_offsets` the identity blocks already carried — and the order the stored extraction was taken under now travels with the pending entry, which is the half nothing already stored could supply: the entry's other axis-keyed fields are unordered maps ranging over every outcome axis rather than the spatial subset the tail is built from, so no arrangement of what was already there recovers the ordering. The placement matches a stored pair to a current position by axis rather than by rank. A surviving axis's pair lands at that axis's own position whatever was registered or retired before or after it; a retired axis's pair is dropped rather than donated to whoever inherited its rank; an axis registered since the assessment is named by nothing stored and reads zero; the fixed groups and the batch context are as wave seven left them. Two format generations move with the carriers — the checkpoint's, because the map it holds gained the identities the saved parameters are indexed against, and the journal's, because the context gained the order. Three tests fix the cases position could not tell apart: a middle deregistration, where the successor's pair no longer shifts and the retired axis's values appear nowhere in the vector; a middle registration, where the newcomer's pair stays blank and the later axis is carried past it; and the retire-then-register sequence in which the two widths agree exactly, so nothing but identity separates the axis that accumulated the memory from the axis that would otherwise have inherited it.

**Entry (Template operands are absolute indices that no rebuild re-resolves)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-absolute-operands`

**Locus** `src/feature/interaction.rs:174`, and the same field on two further arms at `src/feature/interaction.rs:209` and `src/feature/interaction.rs:219`. The notice is (`todo:code:migrate-this-legacy-offset-based-enum`).

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:dimension:compilation-pipeline`), whose first stage is the symbolic declaration — "two operand selectors naming a slot feature, an aggregate feature, an identity feature, a declared signal, or the owning competitive indicator" — and whose second and third stages are recomputed on every map rebuild precisely so that a declaration survives a layout change. Three of the five shipped arms carry a raw absolute index into the vector instead of a selector, and a rebuild does not touch it, because there is nothing in it to resolve.

The consequence is not hypothetical for the corpus's own recommended set. (`tab:feature:default-interaction-set`) draws every identity operand from the cross-dimension aggregate block, and that block's position moves whenever a per-dimension block is added or an axis changes the per-dimension width. A template declared against it therefore reads a different feature after the next registration — an identity feature before, a signal or a slot position afterwards — and the product is formed and standardised without complaint. The fourth arm is the counter-example that shows the fix is available: it addresses its aggregate operand as an offset within the aggregate block (`src/feature/interaction.rs:342`), and the aggregate block never moves.

**Severity** high. It corrupts a feature value that every full-dimension model reads, silently, and it does so under the ordinary event that the specification treats as ordinary. It is bounded to deployments that declare templates at all, which is a real bound — see (`rem:assayer:feature-census`) — but it is the recommended configuration that triggers it.

**What conformance would mean** the operand becomes a selector naming what it wants, resolution maps it to an index on every rebuild, and the absolute offsets disappear from the declaration.

**Coverage** none, and one test documents the hazard without testing it: the claim on `type4_product_computed` at `src/feature/interaction.rs:667` notes that the two operands use different addressing modes, "the two addressing modes resolving to the same block here" — the coincidence that makes the test pass named in the claim, and never varied.

**Repaired** in wave seven at `24eafd63`. `FeatureSelector` names the five kinds the declaration stage names — a slot feature, an aggregate feature, an identity feature, a declared signal, or the owning competitive indicator. The three block-relative kinds resolve against the map on every rebuild; the two entity-relative ones resolve at expansion time. An operand naming nothing in the current layout reads as zero, the treatment the anchor projection already gives its absent identity entries. The absolute offsets are gone from the declaration.

Two things are worth carrying. The builder now checks each operand against the block it names rather than against a flat context width, which made one of its own tests wrong: it had declared a signal so that an absolute context index one past the empty-schema bound became valid, and per-block checking makes that the wrong question — rewritten at `d11844f1`. And the canonical `{template_type, operand_a, operand_b}` struct the notice asks for is **not** built: the first operands stay block-relative, which is where the fourth arm was already the counter-example the entry cited, so the legacy-enum notice stands.

**Entry (The compilation pipeline's third stage does not exist)** · `entry:assayer:wl-feature-compiled-triples`

**Locus** `src/feature/interaction.rs:305`, the per-assessment template dispatch, and the map record that would hold the compiled form, `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:201`. Two labelled notices bore on it, and the wave-seven repair took both with the stage they asked for.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:dimension:compilation-pipeline`) stage 3 and against (`schema:dimension:map-record`), which lists the interaction pipeline's three parts as the declarations, the resolution map, and the compiled triples. The first two ship; the third does not. What the assessment path iterates is the declarations, matching on the template type and performing a lookup in the resolution map for every expanded feature — which is a name lookup and a template interpretation, the two things the corpus says stage three exists to remove from that path.

**Severity** low. Nothing computed is wrong: the dispatch produces the same products the compiled form would. What is lost is the property the pipeline is built for, and a cost that scales with the interaction block on the hot path rather than on the rebuild.

**What conformance would mean** the rebuild emits the flat triples beside the resolution map, the record carries them, and the assessment path becomes the product loop the notice describes.

**Coverage** not applicable; the stage does not exist to be tested.

**Repaired** in wave seven at `19228153`. The rebuild emits the flat triples, the record carries them, and the assessment path is the product loop — output, first operand, second operand, and no name lookup or template interpretation left on it. They are compiled from the finished map rather than from the cursor arithmetic that built it, so the triples resolve their operands through the same `resolve_context` every other reader uses: one authority for what an operand names rather than a second copy beside the first. That also answers what the four-phase notice was for at this locus — a competitive template's first operand is the cell's own indicator and lives in the block assigned after the interactions — without reordering the allocation. An instance whose operand names nothing emits no triple and its output keeps the zero the vector was initialised with.

**Table (The feature cluster's remaining divergences)** · `tab:assayer:feature-remaining`

Four further divergences were found and are recorded compactly, each having been checked against the cited authority in the same way as the entries above.

| Divergence | Authority | Locus | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The named-pair template exists in no arm, the wildcard occupies its position, and the leftover arm duplicates the per-Sentinel one | (`def:feature:template-named-pair`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:364` | high |
| Two standardisation knobs are validated at build time and read by nothing, one of them carrying the bootstrap blend weight's value under a different documented meaning | (`alg:standardisation:sentinel-bootstrap`) | `src/feature/standardisation.rs:187` | medium |
| The generic-class variant the priors table names for the bias is assigned on no production path, so the accumulator's guard against it can never fire | (`tab:standardisation:class-priors`) | `src/owner/thread.rs:453` | low |
| The rebuild's own step list numbers its eight blocks one to seven and then nine | (`def:feature:vector-structure`) | `src/feature/dimension_map.rs:240` | low |

The first row is (`entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`) re-verified at its registered locus, and it stands exactly as registered on the substance; only its coverage line changes, and that correction is made in (`rem:assayer:feature-pins`) rather than here.

The second needs its severity argued. The two knobs sit on the standardisation configuration and are refused by the builder's numerical pass at `src/api/builder.rs:309` and `src/api/builder.rs:316` when out of range, so a host is told twice that they are real: once by their presence and once by their validation. Neither reaches a consumer. They are medium rather than low on the disclosure test (`entry:assayer:wl-inert-knobs`) draws, and the second of the two is worse than undisclosed, it is *mis*-disclosed: the corpus fixes a bootstrap blend weight of four fifths at (`alg:standardisation:sentinel-bootstrap`) step 3, the shipped field carries four fifths, and its documentation says it is a bootstrap confidence level for confidence intervals, which nothing in the package computes. A host reading the field would reasonably believe the blend is wired.

**Remark (The extension-order question, settled)** · `rem:assayer:feature-extension-order`

The owner tranche registered (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-layout-misalignment`) and left one thing open: whether conformance means a permutation applied after the rebuild or an insertion at the block boundary instead of an append. The question belongs to this cluster, because the map is what the two repairs differ about, and the answer is **permutation**, on the corpus's own words rather than on cost.

The three texts in tension are these. (`def:feature:vector-structure`) fixes eight block types in a fixed order and is a definition of what the vector *is*. (`alg:dimension:compaction`) states the allocation rule in one sentence — extension appends at the end and disturbs nothing — and then draws a conclusion from it: the rebuilt map's ranges correspond exactly to physical positions, and no translation layer stands between them. (`inv:dimension:version-consistency`) is unconditional and says the map and the parameters share one index semantics.

Measured against those, the two candidate repairs are not symmetric. Insertion at the block boundary contradicts the allocation sentence flatly: it is not an append and it does disturb, since every later block shifts. Permutation contradicts none of the three. Extension still appends at the end; the rebuild then rearranges the parameters and the standardisation vectors to the canonical order it has just computed; afterwards the map's ranges *do* correspond exactly to physical positions and no translation layer stands anywhere, which is the conclusion the algorithm draws and which is false today. Under (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) the repair that leaves every sentence of the corpus standing is the one the audit must name, and only one of the two does.

A second argument makes it more than a reading of which sentence bends. The machinery permutation needs is already specified and already obligatory: the same algorithm requires that after every marginalisation the parameters and standardisation vectors are physically compacted, with every higher-indexed entry shifting down to close the gap. That is a physical rearrangement of learned parameters performed by a lifecycle event, and the code implements it — `src/feature/standardisation.rs:328` on the statistics side, and the owner cluster's gather on the parameter side. Permutation on extension is that same operation under a different index set, not a new obligation the corpus has never contemplated. Insertion, by contrast, would be a new allocation rule, and the corpus states the old one in terms.

The finding this settles is unchanged in severity and locus; what changes is the repair named in it. It is recorded here rather than by editing that entry, because the entry's own **What conformance would mean** offers both and the choice between them was explicitly deferred to this cluster.

**Remark (What the census turned up in this cluster)** · `rem:assayer:feature-census`

The register asked every later tranche to ask who calls a function, and to ask next whether the corpus wants anything to. This cluster returns the largest answer of the campaign so far, and one of its results changes a founding entry's repair rather than adding to it.

Three public functions of `src/feature/` have no production call site. `assemble_and_standardise_assessment` at `src/feature/assembly.rs:425` is a documented convenience wrapper and its absence of callers is ordinary — the assessment path calls the raw assembly directly at `src/assessment.rs:987` and standardises separately, which is what the wrapper's own documentation tells it to do. `init_from_priors` at `src/feature/standardisation.rs:355` has no call site *anywhere*, not even a test, and it is the function that would discharge (`tab:standardisation:class-priors`) at construction; it is registered above as part of (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-cold-start-classes`).

The third is the one that matters. `assemble_phi_for_label` at `src/feature/assembly.rs:543` is the package's implementation of (`alg:runtime:reconstruction`), and its five call sites are all in `src/tests/assembly.rs`. The production label path does not call it: it holds its own reconstruction at `src/owner/label_path.rs:1234`, reached from `src/owner/label_path.rs:424`, and that private function is the locus of (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`). Read side by side the two are not the same function. The registered entry says four of six blocks are left at zero, which is true of the one that runs; the one that does not run re-derives the aggregate block, the identity dimensions' structural triple, the competitive indicators and the interactions, freezes the signal block and the slots as the corpus requires, and excludes only the cross-dimension block and the identity measurement features.

So that entry's **What conformance would mean** is smaller and differently placed than it reads. Completing the four blocks is not new work: most of it is written, exported, and tested in this cluster, and the repair is for the label path to call it and for its remaining exclusions to be closed. That is the same correction (`rem:assayer:owner-no-cadence`) made for the Ledger sweep — the obstacle is not that the package lacks the mechanism, it is that the call is not made — and it is the second time this campaign has found a correct implementation and a production path that reimplements it worse alongside. The helper carried its own labelled notice at `src/feature/assembly.rs:512`, asking for three of its remaining gaps and naming one it had already closed, the competitive indicators — a notice that had drifted from its code and had to be re-read before it was acted on; the wave-six repair took it with the gaps it disclosed.

Two narrower results are worth carrying. The interaction templates have no default: the builder initialises the declaration list empty at `src/api/builder.rs:68` and nothing in the package constructs (`tab:feature:default-interaction-set`), so a deployment that does not declare its own templates by hand, out of raw offsets, has no interaction block at all and the reference dimension the corpus tabulates is unreachable without work the package does not help with. The corpus calls the set a recommendation rather than a constraint, so the absence of a constructor is **unspecified** rather than divergent — but it bounds the reach of (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-absolute-operands`) and of (`entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`) alike, and both bounds should be stated when either is scheduled. And two of this cluster's six modules carry a module-wide suppression of the dead-code warning — `src/feature/interaction.rs` and `src/feature/assembly.rs`, with two more on `aggregate` and `bootstrap` — so, as in the owner cluster, the census here was done by search and could not have been delegated to the compiler.

**Remark (Three founding coverage lines and one that was right)** · `rem:assayer:feature-pins`

The test-pin question returns results in this cluster of the same shape the risk tranche met, and one of them corrects a founding entry in place.

(`entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`) carries the coverage line "none, and none is possible: the variant does not exist to be tested". The first half is wrong and the second half is right about the wrong thing. The named-pair variant indeed does not exist, so nothing tests *it*; but the arm standing in its place is tested, and the test asserts the divergence as the design. `type2_feature_count_is_combinations` at `src/feature/interaction.rs:433` asserts twenty-eight instances at eight Sentinels, which is the wildcard's count where the corpus gives the named pair exactly one, and its authored claim says so in the corpus's own vocabulary — that a pair template "grows quadratically" and that "the quadratic growth is the cost that makes pair templates the expensive kind to declare". The corpus's reason for the quadratic cost belongs to the wildcard (`def:feature:template-wildcard`); the named pair's entire purpose is that it "buys the cross-Sentinel question at constant cost". So the claim has taken the argument the corpus makes about one type and attached it to another, in the gloss a reader consults to learn what the type is for. The duplicated arm is pinned a third time at `src/feature/interaction.rs:450`, whose claim states that what the second operand is "affects the value computed, never the shape of the block" — a sentence that is true of the shipped arm and false of the fifth template type the corpus asks that arm to be. And the module's own test-index table at `src/feature/interaction.rs:9` carries the same claim into the file's documentation. That entry's coverage line is corrected here, as the risk tranche corrected two.

The anchor projection's two pins are recorded at (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-anchor-gathered-pair`) and the non-finite hollow test at (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-nonfinite-accumulation`); together with the three above, this cluster alone contributes five tests that assert a divergence as their expected value and one that stands hollow over the property it is named for.

Against that, one founding coverage line was checked and is right. The standardisation suite's extension test at `src/tests/standardisation.rs:259` is the only test that reaches `extend_standardisation`, and it asserts what (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-unchecked-extension`) says it does not: it constructs the classes by hand and never compares the resulting length against any model dimension. That entry stands as registered, at its registered locus `src/feature/standardisation.rs:304`, with its disposition unspecified intact — the corpus still does not say who enforces the class assignment — and this tranche adds only that the amplifier argument is stronger than registered, because the entry above it shows the length invariant being restored by a resize whose class comes from nowhere at all.

**Remark (A fourth defect in the corpus's own prose)** · `rem:assayer:feature-prose-defect`

(`tab:standardisation:class-priors`) is introduced by a sentence that counts twenty classes and followed by a table that lists nineteen. The code implements nineteen and every one of them matches its tabulated pair, so the count is a defect of the prose and not of the enumeration; it is recorded here because a reader trusting the sentence would report the code as missing a class, which is a false finding this audit came close to making. It joins the three at (`rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`) and belongs to the record pipeline rather than to this campaign.

**Remark (One construction site outside the cluster concedes the invariant)** · `rem:assayer:feature-flagged-snapshot`

The census of dimension-map constructions reached one site outside both clusters this wave audits, and it is flagged here for the tranche that will own `src/snapshot/`. The published snapshot's cold-start constructor at `src/snapshot/published.rs:192` takes a model dimension from its caller, discards it at `src/snapshot/published.rs:202`, and builds a map from an empty configuration; the comment between them states that the map's dimension "may differ from `p` at cold start". (`inv:dimension:version-consistency`) requires that at every published version the map and the parameters share one dimension, and calls the invariant unconditional. A published snapshot whose own constructor documents the two as possibly disagreeing is that invariant conceded in a comment, which is the shape (`rem:assayer:owner-census`) warns about: a defect that is defensible read alone and wrong about what its neighbour guarantees.

## Fifth tranche: per-Sentinel extraction · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-extraction`

The cluster is `src/extraction/` — the ancestor-chain walk, the three chain score groups, the coordination block, the outcome-memory read, the batch context, and the alarm summary — taken against the vector record and the extraction data-model chapter (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`).

The chapter is the most mechanically checkable in the specification and says so about itself: it fixes six groups in a stated order, a width for each, a table of within-group positions, a reference index that gives every one of the sixty positions a name, and a total that the reader is invited to sum. A tranche here is therefore mostly arithmetic, and the result is correspondingly lopsided — this cluster is the most conformant the audit has read, and the three divergences it holds are all in the one group whose width depends on a registry rather than on a constant.

**Table (What the extraction cluster conforms to)** · `tab:assayer:extraction-conformant`

Thirty-eight rows were checked across the cluster and hold. The group arithmetic is recorded first, because it is what the chapter asks to be checked and it passes without exception.

| Checked | Authority | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Six groups are appended in the stated order: chain z-scores, chain cumulative sums, chain structure, coordination, outcome memory, batch context | (`setup:extraction:from-batch-report`) | `src/extraction/mod.rs:247` |
| The six group widths and the total are the tabulated ones, on the reporting path and on the no-report path alike | (`def:extraction:slot`) | `src/extraction/mod.rs:243`, `src/extraction/mod.rs:204` |
| The six chain z-score views are cell, root, maximum, mean, gradient and spread, four axes each, in that order | (`tab:extraction:chain-z-scores`) | `src/extraction/chain.rs:93` |
| The gradient is cell minus root, so a positive value means a localised anomaly | (`tab:extraction:chain-z-scores`) | `src/extraction/chain.rs:118` |
| The three cumulative-sum views are cell, root and element-wise maximum, four axes each | (`tab:extraction:chain-cusums`) | `src/extraction/cusum.rs:86` |
| The eight chain-structure features are the two ranks, the two energy ratios, the two maturities, the chain length and the staleness, in that order | (`tab:extraction:chain-structure`) | `src/extraction/structure.rs:82` |
| Report staleness enters logarithmically | (`def:extraction:report-staleness`) | `src/extraction/structure.rs:115` |
| The twelve coordination features are the two per-axis groups, the concordance, the context fraction, the peak depth and the root context maximum, in that order | (`tab:extraction:coordination`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:116` |
| Every one of the twelve coordination features defaults to zero when the report carries no coordination context | (`tab:extraction:coordination`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:111` |
| The peak context depth is divided by the maximum chain depth directly | (`tab:config:extraction`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:144` |
| The outcome-memory block is the three base averages followed by two per spatially-enabled axis, its width following the axis registry rather than the report | (`tab:extraction:ledger-features`) | `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:61` |
| The batch context is the logarithm of one plus the sample count of the containing reported cell | (`tab:extraction:batch-context`) | `src/extraction/mod.rs:289` |
| The extraction is stored in single precision | (`rem:extraction:single-precision`) | `src/extraction/mod.rs:255` |
| The extraction is deterministic given a report and identical for every Sentinel | (`setup:extraction:from-batch-report`) | `src/extraction/mod.rs:187`, by construction |
| The alarm summary ships all eight named fields and enters no model | (`def:runtime:alarm-summary`) | `src/extraction/alarm.rs:49` |
| The composite is the maximum of the normalised cell peak z-score and cell peak accumulator | (`def:runtime:alarm-summary`) | `src/extraction/alarm.rs:179` |
| Coordination features reference only cells the rest of the extraction has seen | (`tab:extraction:coordination`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:108`, by construction |
| No extraction site obtains an unbounded reading through a derived default | (`dec:clock:two-domains`) | `src/extraction/alarm.rs:152`, and the cluster by census |

**Entry (The per-axis outcome memory is grouped by kind where the corpus pairs it by axis)** · `entry:assayer:wl-extraction-ledger-axis-grouping`

**Locus** `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:69` and `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:79`, two sequential loops over the same axis list. No notice stands here, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:extraction:reference-index`), whose per-axis row reads "Compressed and raw axis means, **one pair for each** of the $m_s$ eligible axes" — an interleaved layout, each axis's two features adjacent. The code emits every axis's compressed average first, in axis order, and only then every axis's raw average. The two layouts coincide at one spatially-enabled axis and diverge at two or more, where the corpus's positions run compressed-first, raw-first, compressed-second, raw-second and the code's run compressed-first, compressed-second, raw-first, raw-second.

The finding is not that one ordering is nicer. It is that two other parts of the package already implement the corpus's ordering, so at two spatial axes the extraction disagrees with both of them. The dimension map's offset convention is built on pairs: (`def:dimension:layers`) says the per-axis outcome-memory offsets give, for each spatially-enabled axis, "where **that axis's pair** of Ledger features begins", so a reader taking an axis's offset from the map and reading two positions gets one axis's compressed value and the *next* axis's compressed value. And the standardisation class assignment pushes the classes in pairs — a compressed class then a raw class, once per axis, at `src/owner/lifecycle.rs:449` — so the second axis's compressed average is standardised against the raw class's prior and the first axis's raw average against the compressed class's, whose prior variances differ by a factor of four. Three sites, two of which agree with the corpus.

**Severity** high, bounded. It puts a wrong prior on model inputs and makes the map misreport what lives where, silently, and both consequences are on the learned side rather than on a diagnostic. The bound is real and must be stated with it: the reference configuration has one spatially-enabled axis, the dimension summary (`tab:dimension:summary`) sizing the slot at sixty-three, which is the slot width at one such axis. So the defect is invisible at the documented reference deployment. It is not exotic either — the same table sizes deployments at three and at five spatial axes as ordinary cases — so this is the mirror of (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`), whose severity the owner tranche raised because the reference itself triggers it. Here the reference itself conceals it.

**What conformance would mean** one loop over the axis list pushing each axis's compressed and raw averages together, which is the layout the map and the class assignment already assume.

**Coverage** worse than none, and pinned in four places. `multiple_spatial_axes` at `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:174` asserts the fifth position is the second axis's compressed average and the sixth is the first axis's raw average — the divergent layout asserted position by position, at the one axis count where the two layouts differ. Its authored claim states the divergence as the design in the corpus's own register: "Per-axis valences are laid out by kind and not by axis: every axis's compressed valence comes first in the caller's axis order, and only then does the run of raw valences begin." A sibling test at `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:203` pins the same layout through its zero-defaulting path, the module's test-index table repeats the claim at `src/extraction/ledger_features.rs:10`, and the cluster's own README carries the sentence a third time. This is the campaign's clearest instance of a divergence that has been written up as a design decision rather than caught, and repairing it fails two tests whose claims must be rewritten with them.

**Repaired** in wave seven at `60b9df49`. One loop replaces two, so each axis's compressed and raw averages are adjacent and the map's per-axis offset addresses the pair it says it addresses. The two tests that pinned the grouped layout position by position are rewritten with their claims, and the module's test-index table and the cluster README follow them.

**Entry (The chain-length normaliser divides by the logarithm of its parameter)** · `entry:assayer:wl-extraction-chain-length-form`

**Locus** `src/extraction/structure.rs:110`, the divisor.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:extraction:chain-structure`), whose row reads "Chain length, $\log_2(1 + d)$ normalised by the maximum chain depth" — the logarithm taken and then divided by the parameter. The code divides by the logarithm of one plus the parameter instead. The parameter is the same one whose shipped value is already registered as a departure at (`entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`); this is a second and independent finding about the same feature, and the two do not overlap: one is the value, this is the form.

**The spec-stale defence was considered and refused**, and the refusal is worth stating because the corpus text that invites it is unusually persuasive. The configuration chapter's commentary on this very parameter argues that raising it from sixteen to a hundred and twenty-eight "compresses the feature into a narrower band rather than shifting it eight-fold", because the feature "is logarithmic in depth before it is divided". That argument is true of the shipped form and false of the tabulated one: under the tabulated form the eight-fold parameter change divides the feature by exactly eight, which is the shift the sentence denies. So a passage of the corpus reasons from the code's formula. The grammar (`conv:assayer:audit-dispositions`) asks for a later decision that superseded the text or a mechanism the text describes that no longer exists, and neither is available: no record supersedes the extraction chapter's table, the mechanism it names is present, and a second sentence of the same corpus disagreeing with a table is a defect in the corpus rather than evidence the corpus has gone out of date. Measured against the campaign's one successful spec-stale argument (`rem:assayer:out-of-package-item`) this reaches nothing like the bar, and it fails in exactly the way the risk tranche's proposed verdict failed (`rem:assayer:risk-spec-stale-refused`). The prose is recorded below as a defect instead.

**Severity** low, and the argument is arithmetic. The two forms differ by a constant factor and not by a shape: both are the same logarithm of depth times a divisor that does not depend on the request, so the feature is the same monotone function of chain depth under either, and standardisation removes the scale difference within a standardisation half-life. Nothing the model can learn is lost, and the order-statistic correction the chapter relies on this feature to make learnable (`rem:extraction:order-statistic-bias`) is learnable under both. What is wrong is that a deployment setting the parameter does not get the rescaling the table says it is buying.

**What conformance would mean** the divisor becomes the parameter rather than its logarithm, at which point the configuration chapter's commentary about the shipped value must be rewritten too, since it describes the behaviour being removed.

**Coverage** worse than none. Two tests pin the shipped form outright: `chain_length_normalisation` at `src/extraction/structure.rs:203` asserts the ratio of two logarithms explicitly, and `chain_length_deep` at `src/extraction/structure.rs:313` asserts that a chain at the maximum depth normalises to exactly unity — a property that holds under the shipped form and not under the tabulated one, so it is the divergence promoted to an invariant. Both authored claims state the shipped form as the definition.

**Entry (The no-report path drops the two flags its sibling preserves)** · `entry:assayer:wl-extraction-no-report-flags`

**Locus** `src/extraction/mod.rs:206`, against its sibling at `src/extraction/alarm.rs:148`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:runtime:alarm-summary`), which says all eight fields ship and fixes the hierarchical flag as reflecting "the coordinate semantics declared at registration" — a property of the Sentinel, not of whether it filed a report this cycle. When the ancestor chain is empty the extraction returns the summary's derived default, discarding both the hierarchical flag its caller passed in and the immaturity flag. The alarm module's own empty-chain branch three files away does the opposite: it zeroes the six computed fields and carries both flags through.

So two functions in one cluster answer the same input in two incompatible ways, and a caller cannot choose between them on the documentation — which is the shape the ledger tranche registered at its routing siblings (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-rootless-disagreement`), met here in a case that is reachable on the ordinary path rather than in a state the design excludes.

**Severity** low. The summary enters no model by construction, so nothing learned or acted on is corrupted; what a host loses is that a Sentinel with hierarchical coordinates stops declaring them for exactly the cycles in which it is silent, which is when a host reading the diagnostic most wants to know what kind of Sentinel has gone quiet.

**What conformance would mean** the no-report path constructs the summary the way its sibling does, carrying both flags and zeroing the rest — or calls the sibling, which already handles the empty chain.

**Coverage** none of the drop. The preserving branch is pinned by `flags_passthrough` at `src/extraction/alarm.rs:380`, whose claim says the flags are relayed "on a populated chain as much as on an empty one"; the discarding branch is exercised by `extract_occupancy_no_report` at `src/extraction/mod.rs:694`, which asserts only that the peak z-score is zero. So the corpus's suite contains a claim asserting the property and a test walking past the place it fails.

**Table (The extraction cluster's remaining divergences)** · `tab:assayer:extraction-remaining`

| Divergence | Authority | Locus | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The active context fraction is unbounded above unity when the reported context capacity is zero | (`tab:extraction:coordination`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:140` | low |
| Coordination concordance reads the first aggregate threshold for every axis, under a comment asserting an equality the recalibration is designed to remove | (`alg:feature:concordance-recalibration`) | `src/extraction/coordination.rs:135` | low |

The second needs its authority stated carefully, because the corpus is thin here rather than contradicted. (`tab:extraction:coordination`) names a single "coordination threshold" and does not say where it comes from, so the choice to draw it from the aggregate block's per-axis thresholds is **unspecified**. What is divergent is the comment beside it, which justifies taking the first of four on the ground that they are "typically all equal": the per-axis thresholds are recomputed independently, each from its own axis's window, by an algorithm whose whole purpose is that an axis's threshold reflects that axis's own distribution. The equality the comment relies on holds before the first recalibration and is removed by it. It is registered as low because the feature is one of sixty and the substitution is stable, and as a divergence rather than a gap because a comment asserting a property the corpus removes is a false assurance of the kind the ledger tranche registered at the absence cast.

**Remark (A cluster where the census returns nothing)** · `rem:assayer:extraction-census`

The census question was asked of every public and private function in the cluster and returned no uncalled machinery at all, which is worth recording because it is the first clean negative result the campaign has had.

Every public function has exactly one production call site, and the whole cluster funnels through one entry point: the six group extractors and the alarm summary are each called once, from `src/extraction/mod.rs`, and that function is called once, from the assessment path at `src/assessment.rs:1249`. No private function is uncalled. The cluster carries a module-wide suppression of the dead-code warning at `src/extraction/mod.rs:61`, so as in the owner and feature clusters the census had to be done by search; unlike them it found nothing for the search to catch.

That is the sharpest available contrast with the tranche immediately before it. The feature cluster and this one sit on either side of the same data and were audited in the same wave, and one holds three public functions with no production caller — including its own implementation of a specified algorithm — while the other holds none. The difference is not diligence but shape: extraction is a pure compression with one caller and no lifecycle, and every function in it exists because the one caller needs it. The lesson for the remaining clusters is that the census question's yield is predictable from a cluster's fan-in, and the clusters still unaudited with the lowest fan-in — `health/`, `metrics/`, `persistence/` — are where it should be asked hardest.

**Remark (The two items that terminate here, cross-cited)** · `rem:assayer:extraction-cross-cites`

Two registered items have their locus in this cluster and are **not** re-registered; both were re-verified at their recorded lines and both stand.

(`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-root-only-read`) stands at `src/extraction/mod.rs:295`, with the depth-zero key it describes at `src/extraction/mod.rs:299` and the immaturity flag derived from the same lookup at `src/extraction/mod.rs:232`. A labelled notice stood there too — the cluster's only one, which is itself a fact about this cluster worth recording, since the package holds a dozen — and the repair took it with the defect, so it is described here rather than cited. The specification's own alarm-summary definition conceded the consequence in its closing sentences at the time this tranche was written, naming the shipped predicate as an existence test and drawing the conclusion that the flag was almost never raised; the ledger tranche reached that conclusion independently and the two agreed. Both statements have since been overtaken by the repair recorded at the entry, and the definition now states a predicate the code takes.

(`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`) stands at `src/extraction/mod.rs:273`, feeding `src/extraction/structure.rs:115`, with the ceiling reapplied by hand at `src/extraction/mod.rs:277`.
