Its repair is the carve-out and belongs to the clock lane, so this tranche adds facts and no disposition. Two facts have changed since it was written and the lane should have both. The ingestion half **is now clocked**: the arrival stamp at `src/report/ingestion.rs:133` is taken from an injected clock rather than from the standard library, so the entry's recorded line for that half has moved and half of the repair appears to have landed. The assessment half has not: the reading the staleness is measured against is still a raw standard-library instant, threaded from `src/assessment.rs:797` to `src/assessment.rs:852`. The consequence is a false assurance at the seam. The comment now standing above the ingestion stamp, at `src/report/ingestion.rs:129`, states that "Both come from the same injected clock, so their difference is a property of the scenario rather than of how busy the machine was" — and the second half of that sentence is exactly the load sensitivity the clock lane measured. A comment asserting a repair that is one half complete is worse than no comment, because the next reader checks the seam, finds it argued, and stops.

**Remark (Two further defects in the corpus's own prose)** · `rem:assayer:extraction-prose-defects`

Two are recorded for the record pipeline, and one existing entry of (`rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`) is corrected here.

The configuration chapter's commentary on the chain-length parameter reasons from a formula its own table does not state, as argued at (`entry:assayer:wl-extraction-chain-length-form`). Whichever way that divergence is closed, one of the two passages has to move, and the audit's presumption fixes which: the table is the authority and the commentary is what is wrong.

The four scoring axes are named twice and not consistently. The reference index names them "novelty, drift, spread and coordination"; the extraction and feature chapters name the second "displacement", both in the chain z-score table's own prose and in the aggregate block's cross-axis product row. The code is positional throughout and names them novelty, displacement, surprise and coherence, so nothing computes a wrong number through the disagreement and the code's naming is **unspecified** rather than divergent. It is recorded because an audit reading the appendix alone and the code alone would report a mismatch that is not one, and because a reader mapping an axis by name has two answers.

The correction is to the first entry of (`rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`), which states that the configuration chapter's claim about the chain-length normaliser's test coverage is false because "the extraction tests pass the shipped value". That is too strong and is corrected in place: two of the nine chain-structure tests do pass the specified sixteen, at `src/extraction/structure.rs:319` and `src/extraction/structure.rs:338`, and seven pass the shipped value. The corpus sentence is therefore true of a minority rather than of nobody, and the substance of the finding survives unchanged — a reader trusting it still believes a regression guard exists where none does, because none of the nine asserts the default itself.

## Sixth tranche: the health surface · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-health`

The cluster is `src/health/` — the published summary and the queried report, the degradation counters and their per-assessment context, the event channel and its three event families, the drift accumulators, the discrimination computation, and the four concrete trackers — taken against the health and degradation records and the monitoring analysis chapters (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`).

The cluster was taken with `src/metrics/` because the second exports the first and neither can be adjudicated alone: a health field's severity depends on whether it leaves the package, and the export surface is meaningless without the field behind it. Three of this tranche's findings were only visible from both sides at once.

It is also the cluster the specification is most candid about being far from, and that candour set the tranche's method. The monitoring chapter states every requirement at full strength and then closes each environment by saying where the shipped system stands, which makes it unusually easy to mistake a conceded absence for an adjudicated one. Every concession below was therefore re-measured rather than transcribed, and doing so changed the verdict twice: once where the chapter understates what is missing, and once where the chapter says a quantity is invisible to a host when it is in fact visible and wrong.

**Remark (Where this cluster's governing records are not the ones the scope map assigns)** · `rem:assayer:health-scope-map`

The scope map gives this cluster the health, degradation and metrics records and the monitoring analysis chapters, and that is right for ten of the twelve files. It is not right for two, and the divergence is recorded rather than worked around because the map is the audit's completeness check.

`src/health/concordance.rs` answers to the warm-up chapter for its bootstrap (`alg:warmup:concordance-bootstrap`) and to the feature chapter for its recalibration (`alg:feature:concordance-recalibration`), and to no monitoring environment at all. `src/health/composite.rs` answers to the warm-up chapter for the stage set it computes over (`tab:warmup:stages`). Both were audited against those chapters, which is what the cluster's own records direct — the health record cites the warm-up table rather than restating it (`dec:health:on-demand-composite`) — so no environment went unread. What the map should gain is the warm-up chapter against the `health/` row; the audit does not amend the map, and records the correction here for the reader who checks one against the other.

Recorded first, because an audit that lists only its findings misrepresents what it read. Forty-one rows were checked across the cluster and hold; the selection below is the load-bearing part of that set.

The final row records conformance with the corpus's own statement that a quantity is absent, which is a different assertion from the rest of the table and is marked as such deliberately. It is a row rather than a finding because a specification that says a thing is missing is not contradicted by its being missing.

**Entry (The concordance calibration window has the wrong population and capacity)** · `entry:assayer:wl-concordance-window-shape`

**Locus** ``src/health/concordance.rs:186`` and ``src/health/concordance.rs:574``. The observation method accepts one aggregate array per assessment and pushes one entry into the rolling window; its own documentation reserves a widened API that pushes every reporting Sentinel's sub-score. The default capacity is five thousand, and the comment over it names the deferred switch to ten thousand per-Sentinel sub-score entries.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:warmup:concordance-bootstrap`), which requires every recalibration after the first thousand assessments to read the ten thousand most recent sub-scores with one entry per reporting Sentinel. The initial value, cadence and percentile are independent parts of the algorithm and remain conformant (`tab:assayer:health-conformant`); this entry is only the window's population and capacity.

**Severity** medium. With one reporting Sentinel, the retained evidence horizon is half the specified one. With several, the aggregation first takes each axis's maximum across Sentinels and then stores one entry, so the window both shortens the evidence horizon and removes the per-Sentinel population the requirement names. The resulting percentile is a statistic of assessment maxima rather than of reported sub-scores.

**What conformance would mean** the observation boundary supplies one sub-score entry for every reporting Sentinel, the window retains ten thousand such entries, and the cadence remains indexed by assessments rather than by inserted entries. **Coverage** none for the population distinction; the circular-buffer tests cover capacity and order only. **Sequencing** the producer boundary must widen before the capacity changes, because increasing a window that still stores aggregate maxima would preserve the wrong statistic for longer rather than produce the required one.

**Remark (The monitoring legibility measurements and their limits)** · `rem:assayer:monitoring-legibility-measurements`

The synchronisation-resolution experiment drove a pair far apart and saw the reading climb through ten orders while every residual still read as being at its resolution. That is why the resolution may describe the reading but may not overrule the threshold: permitting it to do so makes the diagnostic fall silent when the pair has stopped meaning anything.

The Sentinel association calibration was driven across a sixty-four-fold range of label counts. A perfectly predictive control held between $19.65$ and $19.84$ times the null floor, a one-per-cent spread, while structureless surfaces sat between $0.37$ and $2.66$ times it, leaving a margin of at least $7.4$. The lower edge therefore stays at three times the floor across window lengths; the upper edge moves because a carrying block's raw correlation is a property of its traffic while the floor falls as the inverse square root of effective samples.

The per-dimension association case was measured separately because the frozen-window Sentinel bench collapses to between five and nineteen observations across an identity-layout rebuild. Two identity dimensions were registered on one engine and read from one publication, one keyed to the outcome and one driven by an independent half-selector, with a third dimension registered halfway through to force a rebuild. Across four schedule points the keyed block read between $8.85$ and $20.89$ times the floor and the null block between $0.86$ and $1.99$, a margin of at least $5.7$ at every point. The keyed reading rose monotonically and stood higher after the rebuild in every run. The separation is held by (`test:integration:the-association-reading-separates-a-keyed-dimension-from-a-null-one`) and continued accumulation across the rebuild by (`test:integration:per-dimension-readings-survive-a-layout-rebuild`).

On the same stream, a structureless block's contribution fell monotonically from $0.125$ towards $0.0005$ while the block carrying the outcome held, a separation of at least $6.8$ at the tightest checkpoint. The weight-mass reading over the same schedule gave the structureless block between $0.76$ and $1.05$ of the control's figure, which is the measured difference between what a model carries and what its score owes. The per-Sentinel contribution separation is held by (`test:integration:contribution-separates-a-sentinel-the-score-leans-on-from-a-hash-fed-one`), and direct removal cost by (`test:unit:removing-a-block-the-score-leans-on-costs-loss-and-removing-an-idle-one-does-not`).

The corresponding per-dimension contribution separation is not established. Repeated runs of the same two-block harness put the keyed block's removal cost between $0.45$ and $34$ times the null block's, crossing in both directions; one run put the null below zero and another at twice the keyed block. Fixing traffic leaves the reading as a function of the operational weight vector. The block weight mass is the surfaced functional of that vector and composes as a plain mean over equal-width blocks, but it cannot pin comparable convergence states: the same one-seed schedule began its measured stretch with family weight mass between $0.017$ and $0.12$, and five consecutive runs ended between 2,400 and 2,800 labels at $0.0072$, $0.0136$, $0.0189$, $0.0201$ and $0.0213$. A level reached in seconds in one run lay outside another run's range, and none had settled after runs lasting between one hundred and one hundred and fifty seconds.

At the closest comparable states, the null contribution ran from $-0.012$ to $+0.33$ and the keyed contribution from $0.05$ to $0.59$; one pair stood at $0.874$ and $0.752$, which would make a deployment act on both identically. At those same points association still separated in every run, keyed between $5.7$ and $18.6$ times the floor against null between $0.72$ and $1.53$, while coverage agreed to six decimals. A reproducible contribution pin therefore needs either a maintenance cadence driven from a harness clock, making competitive structure a function of the label schedule, or a surfaced effective sample size for the prequential window. Until then contribution confirms a sustained association alert per Sentinel and is context rather than a discrimination threshold per dimension.

**Entry (The identity convergence trackers are built, updated nowhere, and published empty)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`

**Locus** `src/owner/label_path.rs:1203`, where the published tracker map is a freshly constructed empty map, and `src/owner/label_path.rs:1156`, where the composite is computed over an empty tracker slice. The recording method that would fill a tracker is `src/health/identity_tracker.rs:110`. Both sites carry a comment conceding the placeholder; no labelled notice stands at either, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:monitoring:dimension-health`), which requires the competitive set size and the set change rate to be reported per registered identity dimension, and against (`dec:health:on-demand-composite`), which fixes the composite as derived from the individual trackers. Neither holds. A tracker is created at dimension registration (`src/owner/lifecycle.rs:644`) and removed at deregistration (`src/owner/lifecycle.rs:670`), and between those two events nothing ever calls the method that would record a change: every call site of `record_change_event` in the workspace is a test. The published summary's tracker map is not merely stale but never populated at all, and the composite receives an empty slice rather than the trackers.

The consequences compound in three directions, and the third is the reason this is the tranche's most serious finding.

Per-dimension health falls back to a derived default. Assembly looks each dimension up in the published map, finds nothing, and substitutes the default health value (`src/api/health.rs:253`), so every registered dimension reports a competitive set size of zero and a change rate of zero. Both leave the package as live exported metrics — `assayer_identity_cell_count` at `src/metrics/mapper.rs:408` and `assayer_identity_change_rate` at `src/metrics/mapper.rs:414` — which makes them two further instances of the shape the deferral pass was built to catch, neither of them registered as a deferral and neither previously recorded anywhere.

A specified stage becomes unreachable. The composite reaches interaction maturity only when some tracker is not yet stable (`src/health/composite.rs:195`), and the guard immediately above it treats an empty slice as universal stability (`src/health/composite.rs:190`). With the placeholder slice the branch can never be taken, so of the six stages (`tab:warmup:stages`) names, one is unreportable by construction.

And the composite overreports. This is the sharp consequence: an empty tracker slice does not read as *unknown*, it reads as *all stable*, so the composite advances to steady state on calibration evidence alone while the identity layer it is defined over is entirely unmeasured. The record's stated reason for computing on demand is that regression is thereby made possible — registering a new entity "genuinely returns part of the system to an earlier stage" (`dec:health:on-demand-composite`) — and with the placeholder no registration moves the composite at all, so the property the decision was taken to buy is not obtained.

**Severity** high, and the argument has to survive the report-only posture rather than ignore it. Nothing learned is corrupted and no path is gated (`inv:monitoring:report-only`), so on the campaign's usual reading this would be a medium. It is argued up because the corpus does not leave this number inside the package: the invariant's own reasoning is that reporting "puts the host at the point where it closes" the loop, and the catalogue prescribes alerting on this very gauge, naming an immature-calibration alert on `assayer_convergence_stage` (`src/metrics/catalog.rs:25`). A diagnostic that reports more convergence than it has evidence for, on the package's single most prominent health number, is a number a host acts on by the corpus's own design. The bound is that identity dimensions must exist for the overreport to be wrong, and the reference configuration specifies two, so this is live at the documented reference deployment rather than at an edge — the same reasoning the owner tranche used to raise (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`).

**What conformance would mean** the identity maintenance path calls `record_change_event` with the entries, exits and current set it already computes for the lifecycle events it already emits; the working copy's trackers are published instead of an empty map; and the composite receives them instead of the placeholder slice. The measurement machinery is written, exported and thoroughly tested — this is a call rather than an implementation, which makes it the third instance of the pattern the register warned about (`rem:assayer:extraction-census`).

**The first clause is corrected in place by the identity and signal tranche** (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-cross-cites`), which owns the maintenance path this line names, and the correction moves the repair out of that cluster entirely. The loop does compute all three arguments and does drop two of them into a log (`src/identity/maintenance_loop.rs:427`, `src/identity/maintenance_loop.rs:428`, `src/identity/maintenance_loop.rs:434`), so the line is right about where the quantities are. It cannot be right about the caller: the loop holds no handle on the trackers, which are created and destroyed on the owner's side (`src/owner/lifecycle.rs:644`, `src/owner/lifecycle.rs:670`), and threading one into it would put a health surface inside the cluster the memory record keeps single-owner. Nor is it necessary, because the owner already receives the entries and the exits as the lifecycle events it handles at `src/owner/lifecycle.rs:235` and `src/owner/lifecycle.rs:239`, and the current set is the union it is maintaining anyway. So conformance means a call on the owner's side over data already delivered to it, and no change to the identity cluster whatsoever. The severity, the disposition and the other two clauses are unchanged; the repair is the fourth to shrink once the owning cluster was read, and the first to shrink by moving to a different cluster than the one that was read.

**Coverage** none of the wiring, and abundant coverage of the part that works. The tracker's own module holds eleven tests exercising the change rate, the overlap measure and every stage transition, and the composite has its own suite driving stage boundaries with populated trackers. Not one test asserts that a production path ever calls the recorder, that the published map is non-empty, or that the composite is given the trackers rather than a placeholder — so a correct tracker and a caller that never uses it have coexisted, which is precisely the shape the ledger tranche registered at its uncalled sweep (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-sweep-unscheduled`).

**Sequencing** after the clock lane. Two wall-clock reads in the identity tracker are dormant today precisely because the composite is handed an empty tracker slice, and repairing this wiring turns both into live reads on the package's most prominent health number (`rem:assayer:health-clock-lane`). The clock repair is carved out of the freeze as roadmap infrastructure and can land first; this entry should follow it rather than lead it, or the wiring ships a non-deterministic stage.

**Repaired** in wave three at `b3521bf6`, after the clock carve-out as the sequencing requires, and on the owner's side as the corrected first clause directs: the owner records each lifecycle submission's competitive-set entries and exits on the per-dimension trackers, grouped per dimension against the current set the rebuilt map holds; the label path publishes the owner's tracker map instead of a fresh empty one and hands the composite the trackers instead of the placeholder slice. The two dormant clock reads the sequencing clause warned about became explicit parameters rather than live wall reads — the constructor takes its first-seen stamp and the stage computation takes the present, both fed from the injected clock — so the wiring ships a deterministic stage. Tests pin the owner-side recording against the rebuilt set and the published summary carrying the trackers; the identity cluster is untouched, as the correction promised.

**Entry (The drift accumulators answer to two of their four specified resets)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-drift-reset-triggers`

**Locus** `src/health/drift.rs:169`, whose only caller is the automatic path seven lines above it, and `src/owner/label_path.rs:837`, the calibration reset. No notice stands at either, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:monitoring:drift-resets`), which fixes four triggers. Two ship and two do not exist.

The automatic trigger ships as specified and is recorded as conformant above. The post-calibration trigger ships and departs from its row twice. The host-initiated trigger and the post-lifecycle trigger have no implementation at all: `reset_cusums` has exactly one call site in the workspace, inside the update method itself, and `reset_all` has exactly one, on the calibration path, so no command surface can reset the accumulators and no lifecycle event does.

| Trigger | Tabulated effect | Shipped |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Automatic | Reset the accumulators, report a drift event | as specified |
| Host-initiated | Reset the accumulators | no implementation |
| Post-lifecycle | Reset the accumulators for all models | no implementation |
| Post-calibration | Reset the accumulators for the affected regime | resets every model, and clears the smoothed diagnostics too |

The post-calibration departures need stating separately because they pull in opposite directions from the same call. In scope the reset is wider than tabulated: the row confines it to the affected regime and the code loops over every model in the drift map, under a comment calling the choice conservative (`src/owner/label_path.rs:838`). In depth it is deeper than any row asks: it calls `reset_all`, which restores the whole state to its default and so discards the mean absolute residual and the residual sign alongside the accumulators, where (`tab:monitoring:drift-diagnostics`) lists those two as running diagnostics distinct from the accumulators the resets act on.

The depth departure is the one the package itself argues against. The automatic path deliberately spares the smoothed pair (`src/health/drift.rs:169`), and the test covering that choice states the reason in its authored claim — that acting on a detected drift "should not also destroy the long-memory description of how the residuals behave, which takes many rounds to rebuild and is what makes the next detection interpretable" (`src/tests/outcome_drift.rs:18`). The corpus therefore holds, in its own test suite, the argument for a property that its other reset path breaks.

**Severity** medium, and the two missing triggers carry nearly all of it. The post-lifecycle reset is the consequential one: the specification's reasoning is that a lifecycle event alters the feature space the prediction was made in, so that carrying accumulated evidence across it "would report a drift that is really a change of coordinates" — and the owner tranche established that lifecycle events do extend and marginalise the models. Every Sentinel or axis registration therefore leaves every accumulator holding residuals measured in a feature space that no longer exists, and the automatic threshold then fires on that evidence and emits a drift event a host cannot distinguish from real drift. Nothing learned is corrupted, because the accumulators feed no update; what is corrupted is the one diagnostic this chapter builds the most machinery for.

**What conformance would mean** the lifecycle submission path resets every model's accumulators after applying its events, a command variant reaches `reset_cusums`, and the calibration reset narrows to the affected regime and calls `reset_cusums` rather than `reset_all` so the smoothed diagnostics survive it as they survive the automatic one.

**Coverage** none of the four triggers is covered as a trigger. Both reset methods are tested directly and thoroughly, including a test pinning exactly which fields each one spares; nothing asserts that a lifecycle event causes a reset, that a host can request one, or that the calibration path resets the regime rather than the fleet.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `2e3dbdaa`, all three departures of the conformance line. The host-initiated trigger exists: a public method crosses the command channel and the owner thread applies `reset_cusums` to every model. The post-lifecycle trigger exists: a submission carrying a Sentinel or axis event resets every model's accumulators after applying its events — identity events are left outside the trigger because the table's row names Sentinel and axis events, a reading recorded with the wave's findings. And the post-calibration trigger narrows both ways: to the affected regime, from per-regime deltas recomputed at the refit's call site, and to `reset_cusums`, so the smoothed diagnostics survive it as the suite's own claim argues they must. `reset_all` remains as the model-replacement semantics with no production caller, and says so at its definition. All four triggers are now covered as triggers: the narrowing unit test reads one regime reset and two spared with the event reporting the narrow kind (`test:unit:calibration-drift-reset-narrows-to-the-affected-regime`), and the two engine falsifiers ran red first, reading a step counter of one where eight would stand, the smoothed magnitude surviving both resets (`test:crate:host-initiated-drift-reset-reaches-the-accumulators`), (`test:crate:lifecycle-event-resets-drift-accumulators`).

**Entry (The instability flag compares the two regimes where the corpus compares recent against aggregate)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-instability-flag`

**Locus** `src/health/published.rs:291`, the comparison. No notice stands here, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:monitoring:auc`), whose recent-figure paragraph defines the flag in terms: the recent figure is computed over the most recent labels, and "when it departs from the aggregate figure by more than $0.1$ a **discrimination instability flag** is reported". The code computes the absolute difference between the sister-regime and anchor-regime figures against the same tolerance, and reports that. Both quantities are computed and available at the comparison — the aggregate two lines above at `src/health/published.rs:273` and the recent figure at `src/health/published.rs:280` — so the specified comparison was to hand and a different one was made.

The substitution is not a near miss of the specified quantity but an answer to a different question. The specified flag is temporal: it asks whether discrimination has moved recently, which is why the chapter pairs it with the lag caveat that names the recent window as "a faster and noisier signal" (`cav:limitation:auc-lag`). The shipped flag is structural: it asks whether the two blend regimes discriminate differently, which is a real quantity the corpus nowhere defines a flag on. A deployment whose discrimination is collapsing uniformly across both regimes raises nothing.

**Severity** medium. The flag is report-only and reaches no model, and it is not exported as a metric, so it is visible only to a host that fetches the full report and reads the field. What follows is that the corpus's own named early-warning for discrimination instability does not fire on the condition it names, and that the alert a host would build from it is not the alert the catalogue's guidance describes — the catalogue prescribes a discrimination collapse alert derived by the host from the recent and aggregate gauges (`src/metrics/catalog.rs:18`), which is the specified comparison, so a host following the catalogue reconstructs by hand the flag the package computed wrongly.

**What conformance would mean** the comparison becomes the recent figure against the aggregate one at the same tolerance, and the field's doc comment stops describing the regime comparison as the definition.

**Coverage** worse than none. The field's own doc comment at `src/health/published.rs:195` states the shipped behaviour as the design — "`true` when AUC metrics differ significantly across regimes" — so the divergence stands as this corpus's stated expectation in the place a reader would look to learn what the flag means. This is the same shape the extraction tranche found at its per-axis layout (`entry:assayer:wl-extraction-ledger-axis-grouping`), met here in a doc comment rather than in an authored test claim.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `0ac0b9ec`. The comparison became the temporal one the corpus defines — the recent figure against the aggregate at the same tolerance — and the field's doc comment stopped stating the regime comparison as the design. Two falsifiers ran red first, one in each direction the substitution failed: uniform collapse across both regimes now raises the flag (`test:crate:instability-flag-rises-on-recent-departure-from-aggregate`), and regime disagreement alone no longer does (`test:crate:instability-flag-stays-down-on-regime-disagreement-alone`).

**Entry (The synchronisation error is maintained, dropped at assembly, and exported as a zero)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-sync-error-constant`

**Locus** `src/api/health.rs:51`, the literal, and `src/health/summary.rs:97`, the field it fills. A notice stood at the adjacent precision-health type and a second at `src/health/published.rs:141`, each asking for three fields; the repair took the sync error and left both notices standing, narrowed, for the two fields that answer to the unbuilt shortening rule. Narrowing renamed them, because a notice's label is derived from its text.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:monitoring:synchronisation-error`), and the finding revises the chapter's own account of the gap rather than repeating it. The chapter says the shipped system computes a maximum over the diagonal in place of the specified Frobenius norm, and closes: "it is not surfaced in the health report, so neither the specified quantity nor the shipped substitute is visible to a host". The first half is right and the second is wrong in the direction that matters.

The substitute is computed, and it is maintained. The diagonal measure exists at `src/model/recompute.rs:353`, is taken before every recomputation at `src/model/recompute.rs:407`, is stored per model at `src/model/bayesian.rs:835`, is readable at `src/model/bayesian.rs:676`, and survives a checkpoint at `src/persistence/checkpoint.rs:167`. What the health path does with it is nothing: assembly binds a literal zero and never consults the models, and the per-model precision health type carries no field for it at all. The value is then exported as a live summary gauge, `assayer_max_sync_error` at `src/metrics/mapper.rs:296`, whose catalogue help text describes it as the maximum diagonal sync error across all models.

So a host is not deprived of the quantity, as the chapter has it. A host is told the quantity, and told it wrongly.

**Severity** medium, and it is the sharpest of the three surfaced constants for a reason none of the others has. A permanent zero is not a neutral placeholder on this field: zero is the reading that means the precision matrix and its maintained inverse agree exactly, which is the definition of perfect numerical health. The other two constants export an implausible value that an attentive operator might question; this one exports the most reassuring value the field can take, on the measure whose growth is the signal that two representations have drifted apart. The recomputation-interval shortening the definition attaches to the threshold is also unbuilt — the threshold parameter does not exist in the configuration, as three notices concede (`src/config/types.rs:362`, `src/model/recompute.rs:252`, `src/owner/label_path.rs:1554`) — so neither the report nor the mechanism answers to the quantity.

**The repair is smaller than this line states and the narrowing is made in place by the public surface tranche** (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-cross-cites`), which owns the drop site and found a second one beside it. The summary tier binds the literal one line beneath a fold of exactly the shape the sync error needs (`src/api/health.rs:47`), and the detailed tier iterates the same per-model records with each record in hand (`src/api/health.rs:137`) and declines to read the field under a second notice, ``todo:code:populate-sync-error``. The iteration and the fold pattern both already exist; what is missing is one field on one type.

**What conformance would mean** the per-model precision health carries the last synchronisation reading, assembly takes the maximum across the models it already iterates, and the reported quantity becomes the specified norm rather than the diagonal maximum — or, failing the last, the corpus records the diagonal substitute as the shipped measure under a name that does not claim to be the norm.

**Coverage** cannot distinguish the two. The one test touching the field asserts it is zero at cold start (`src/tests/health_infrastructure.rs:869`), where zero is also what a correct implementation would report, and its authored claim frames the value as a cold-start neutral rather than as a measurement — so the test passes under the constant and under the repair alike. The mapper's own tests drive the field with non-zero fixtures and observe it arrive, which establishes the export and says nothing about the source.

**Sequencing** after (`entry:assayer:wl-sync-error-diagonal`), which must land first: this entry's repair exports the quantity that entry establishes is the wrong one, and exporting a wrong number under a specified name is worse than exporting nothing. The repair itself is one field on one type (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-cross-cites`), so the ordering costs little.

**Repaired** in wave one at `0caddb45`, second of the pair and in that order: the computation became the norm first, and only then was it exported. One field on each of the two health types, filled from the per-model reading the label path already had in hand, folded across the models the summary tier already folds and read from the record the detailed tier already iterates. The coverage line's difficulty — that the one test touching the field asserts a cold-start zero, which a correct implementation reports too — is answered by a test rather than by amending that one: it publishes two models carrying different non-zero readings and requires the summary to report the larger and the detailed tier each model's own. Before the repair it read zero.

At that repair cut, the deferral this unblocked (`entry:posterior:sync-visibility`) was not closed by it. Its completion trigger also asked for clean high-error recomputations to shorten the interval. That remaining behavior has since landed: every live model reaches detailed health, their maximum reaches compact health, and a residual — the reading less the part the replenishment clamp put there — standing strictly above the specified threshold and above the reading's own resolution calls for a recomputation, whose adoption halves the effective interval down to its adaptive floor. The comparison reads the residual and not the whole value, because a rebuild removes rounding and cannot remove what the clamp adds. That comparison now reads the public monitoring configuration; equality and the smallest tested crossing are held by (`test:crate:needed-rebuild-shortens-interval`). The deferral is completed and retired.

**Entry (The composite stage-change event has no caller that is not a test)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-stage-event-dead`

**Locus** `src/health/events.rs:207`. Every call site of the emitter in the workspace is in `src/tests/health_convergence.rs`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong, and the corpus concedes it in advance: the health record carries this defect as a caveat (`cav:health:tracker-event-defect`), states the mechanism, and says the record's business is that "the next reader is not told the event works". It is registered here because a conceded defect still needs a disposition and a severity to be scheduled, and because the caveat expressly leaves the repair to another campaign without saying which. The mechanism was re-measured and the caveat's account is exact: the emitter sends only when the two stages differ, and the publication path computes the new stage alone with no earlier value to compare against (`src/owner/label_path.rs:1153`), so no production call exists to make.

**Severity** low. The stage is published on every label and is exported as a gauge, so a polling host loses nothing; what is lost is the push notification for a host that consumes events rather than scrapes, and the transition timestamp that a gauge sampled on an interval cannot recover. The severity would rise with (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`), since the transitions most worth pushing are the regressions that entry establishes never occur.

**What conformance would mean** the publication path holds the previous stage — it is on the summary it is about to replace — and calls the emitter with the pair.

**Coverage** inverted, and this is why the defect survived. The emitter is covered thoroughly at six call sites, including the case where the stages match and no event is expected; every one of those callers is a test. A function whose only callers are its tests is covered exactly to the extent that it is unused.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `d386ecfc`, exactly as the conformance line has it: publication reads the previous stage off the summary it is about to replace — the one production site holding both halves of the pair — and hands it to the emitter, on all three of the label path's exit paths. The conceding caveat (`cav:health:tracker-event-defect`) gains its dated repair paragraph and keeps the mechanism as history. The falsifier ran red first: the first label's cold-start-to-anchor-emerging transition now arrives in the drained event stream as the pair (`test:crate:stage-change-event-emitted-on-first-label`).

**Entry (A checkpoint restore discards all drift evidence, and no reset row admits it)** · `entry:assayer:wl-health-checkpoint-drops-drift`

**Locus** `src/snapshot/working.rs:534`, where the restored working copy binds an empty drift map under a comment naming the choice.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:monitoring:drift-resets`), which fixes the triggers on which accumulated drift evidence may be discarded and gives the table as the complete set of them. A restart from a checkpoint is not among the four, and it discards more than any of the four is permitted to: every model's accumulators, both smoothed diagnostics, and every step counter, for the whole fleet at once.

The finding is pointed rather than incidental because the same restore preserves the neighbouring numerical-health bookkeeping deliberately, and the suite says so. The checkpoint carries each model's conditioning and synchronisation readings, its recompute interval and its clean-recompute run, and the test covering that carries the reason in its claim: these "decide when the next recomputation is due, so losing them at a restart would reset the schedule" (`src/tests/persistence.rs:15`). The identical argument applies to drift evidence gathered over hundreds of labels — and the drift suite makes it, in a claim stating that a restart dropping that evidence "would grant every drifting model a fresh start it had not earned" (`src/tests/outcome_drift.rs:20`). The corpus states the principle twice and the restore path honours it for one kind of state and not the other, in the same constructor.

**Severity** medium. Nothing computed is wrong and nothing learned is lost, and the drift state is genuinely awkward to restore correctly, since residuals banked before a restart were measured against a model the restore may not reproduce exactly. What is lost is that every restart silently returns every drift diagnostic to cold, so a deployment that restarts on any regular cadence never accumulates enough evidence to reach the threshold, and the accumulators are dead in exactly the deployments that restart most.

**What conformance would mean** the drift state joins the checkpoint payload and is restored with the rest of the numerical-health bookkeeping, or (`tab:monitoring:drift-resets`) gains a fifth row admitting the restart and saying why the evidence cannot cross it. The audit's presumption (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) fixes the first as the default and the second is available on an argument nobody has yet made.

**Coverage** none. The persistence suite asserts field by field what crosses the checkpoint and never asserts what does not, so the one behaviour that would have caught this is the assertion the suite is shaped not to make.

**Settled from the persistence side** (`rem:assayer:persistence-cross-cites`). The restore is not choosing to discard evidence it holds: the checkpoint payload declares no drift field, so the empty map is the only thing the constructor could bind. That confirms this entry's reading and fixes the repair as the first of the two the entry offers — the state joins the payload, where there is no format obstacle and no capture problem, the accumulators sitting on the working copy the payload is already built from. Two facts are added. The payload's own notice enumerates the state the checkpoint is missing and does not mention the drift accumulators, so the disclosure a reader would rely on does not cover this one (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`). And the replay that follows a restore does not rebuild the evidence either, because the step that moves the accumulators is one of the two skipped on replay (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-stripped-context`) — so the evidence is lost at the restore and not recovered by the labels held for exactly that purpose.

**Sequencing** child of (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`). The restored working copy binds an empty drift map because the payload declares no field to bind from, so this entry closes when that payload gains the field and cannot close before.

**Repaired** in wave five at `2ceacb02`, the first of the entry's two options as the audit's presumption fixes: the per-model accumulators join the payload, the capture fills them from the working copy, and the restore binds the payload's field instead of the empty map. The falsifier ran red first — accumulators fed to distinct values on three models restored as an empty map, zero of three — and green after, both CUSUM sides, both smoothed diagnostics and the step counter value for value per model.

**Table (The health cluster's remaining divergences)** · `tab:assayer:health-remaining`

| Divergence | Authority | Locus | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One of the seven degradation categories increments the total but no category counter, so the six categories cannot sum to the total | (`cor:degradation:in-band-travel`) | `src/health/counters.rs:58` | low |
| The composite's own stage table states extents in eligible labels that the function beside it implements in total labels, at different boundaries | (`tab:warmup:stages`) | `src/health/composite.rs:39`, `src/health/composite.rs:104` | medium |
| A second type named for convergence health is declared, never constructed and never re-exported | (`rem:health:inline-versus-queried`) | `src/health/composite.rs:210` | low |
| The Platt tracker's own health snapshot is assembled only in its tests; the full report builds a separate calibration health type instead | (`dec:health:tiered-queries`) | `src/health/platt_tracker.rs:188` | low |

Three of these need their authority stated carefully, because in each the corpus is thin rather than contradicted.

The degradation-counter gap is **unspecified** as against the corpus, which fixes that degradation travels in band and never enumerates which categories earn an aggregate counter. It is recorded as a divergence rather than a gap because of an internal inconsistency the corpus does not need to reach: the degraded-report category satisfies the is-degraded predicate at `src/health/degradation.rs:62` and so increments the total, and the recording method at `src/health/counters.rs:58` handles the other six and not this one. A host subtracting the six from the total finds a remainder it has no name for.

The composite's stage table is a rustdoc defect rather than a specification divergence, and the distinction is the near-miss this tranche came closest to getting wrong. Both corpus tables that state stage extents disclaim binding force in terms — the stages "are approximate descriptions of a continuous process, not transitions the implementation performs" (`tab:warmup:stages`), and the milestones table "is a reading aid and not a source" (`tab:warmup:milestones`) — so the shipped boundaries are **unspecified** and the code is not divergent for using them. What is divergent is the table inside the file, which states extents in eligible labels that the function immediately below it implements in total labels and at different counts, so a reader consulting the code's own documentation is told the composite leaves cold start far later than it does. It is registered at medium because the stage leaves the package as a live gauge and the catalogue's alert guidance is written against it.

The two stale doc comments are one defect repeated: both say a field is populated where assembly still supplies an absent value or a default. They stop the next reader looking, which is the shape the ledger tranche registered at its absence cast (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-absence-cast`). The converse defect this table once carried — a signal-cache field documented as deferred after it had landed — was removed when (`entry:metrics:signal-cache`) completed.

**Remark (What the census turned up in this cluster, and the question it settles)** · `rem:assayer:health-census`

The census question was asked of every function in both clusters, in the generalised form the owner and risk tranches arrived at — who calls this, and does the corpus want anyone to (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`) — and this wave is the clearest evidence yet that the second half is load-bearing. Asked in its first half alone it would have produced one large false finding and missed the true one.

The false finding it would have produced is the metrics cluster. Both mapper entry points have no production caller anywhere in the workspace: every call site of `health_summary_to_samples` and `full_report_to_samples` is a test, and with them the whole of `src/metrics/mapper.rs` is unreached, since every private helper in that file is reached only through those two. On the first half of the question that is the largest block of uncalled machinery the campaign has met. On the second half it is a met requirement and not a defect at all: the record decides that the package renders nothing and exposes values for a host to render (`dec:metrics:passive-export`), so the caller is outside the package by construction, and a production call site inside it would be the violation. This is the same shape the risk tranche recorded at the Companion (`rem:assayer:risk-census`), met here at a larger scale, and it is why the metrics tranche records no census finding.

The true finding is the one the first half of the question would have found and misjudged. `record_change_event` at `src/health/identity_tracker.rs:110` is uncalled outside tests exactly as the mappers are, and here the corpus does want a caller: (`tab:monitoring:dimension-health`) requires the quantities it maintains to be reported per dimension. The two cases are indistinguishable by call-site census alone and opposite in disposition, and only the corpus separates them. That entry is (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`).

Beyond those, the cluster holds a modest amount of genuinely unreached surface and none of it implements a specified mechanism. Three stage-name accessors return the snake-case names of the three convergence enums and are called nowhere (`src/health/composite.rs:85`, `src/health/identity_tracker.rs:228`, `src/health/platt_tracker.rs:228`); they read as having been built to render enum values as metric label strings, which is a use the metrics record forecloses by encoding those enums as integer gauges instead (`src/metrics/catalog.rs:83`). Two convergence predicates, one hand-written default and two hand-written debug implementations are likewise unreached. All of it is accessor surface on public types in a library crate rather than machinery, and none is registered.

The cluster carries module-wide dead-code suppressions at `src/health/summary.rs:14`, `src/health/published.rs:15`, `src/health/events.rs:13`, `src/health/composite.rs:22` and `src/health/identity_tracker.rs:29`, so as in the owner, feature and extraction clusters the census had to be done by search. Five suppressions in one cluster is the highest count the campaign has met, and it is worth recording next to this cluster's headline finding: the one thing a compiler could have said about `record_change_event` is the thing five directives are configured to suppress.

**Remark (What this cluster's tests pin, and the two claims established twice)** · `rem:assayer:health-pins`

Every test driving either cluster was read against its authored claim. The corpus's discipline holds on one axis without exception and fails on another in a way the founding weak-test entry did not anticipate.

The axis that holds is authorship: every test in scope across both clusters carries a doc comment with a claim label. Not one is missing, which is a stronger result than the campaign has had elsewhere and is worth recording as evidence that the documentation campaign's carrier reaches this territory completely.

The axis that fails is duplication, and it is a fresh instance of a class the founding worklist recorded as holding a single pair (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`). Three pairs of tests here have effectively identical bodies split across the inline module in `src/health/drift.rs` and the external suite in `src/tests/outcome_drift.rs` — the mixed-residual decay pair at `src/health/drift.rs:248` and `src/tests/outcome_drift.rs:330`, the smoothed-measure convergence pair, and the serialisation round-trip pair — and a fourth pair is close enough to differ only in its loop count. The verification bar here is the one the earlier refutation set: that finding was withdrawn because a scanner had enumerated one file twice and matched its tests against themselves (`rem:assayer:worklist-refutations`), so each pair was confirmed by reading both files. They are genuinely two files.

What makes the duplication more than tidiness is that the pairs share their authored claims. The decay pair cites the same claim label from both sites, so the corpus's own accounting records one claim as established by two tests when it is established by one test written twice. A register counting claims against tests will read this corpus as better covered than it is, and the effect is concentrated exactly where the campaign least wants it — on the drift accumulators, which this tranche has just found answer to two of four specified resets with none of the four covered as a trigger.

Two further shapes are recorded without being counted as new classes. Four tests assert a lower bound that the production code makes unconditionally true — that an accumulator held at or above zero by a maximum is at or above zero — beside a second assertion that does establish half of the claim, so they are partial oracles rather than hollow ones. And two assert a disjunction that cannot fail, comparing a value against zero in both directions. Both shapes fall under the founding entry's no-oracle class and are handed to the authoring wave rather than registered separately.

**Remark (The items terminating in this cluster, cross-cited)** · `rem:assayer:health-cross-cites`

Four registered items have their locus in this cluster or are settled by it, and none is re-registered. All were re-verified at their recorded lines.

(`entry:assayer:wl-empirical-coverage`) stands at `src/health/published.rs:248` with its notice at `src/health/published.rs:240`, and the absence is confirmed: no standardised residual, neither coverage fraction and no inflation factor occurs in the cluster. **Its conformance line is wrong and is corrected here**, in the direction opposite to the campaign's usual one. The entry says the two fractions and the factor "are computed from the calibration rows the function already holds", following the chapter's own assurance that no additional per-request state is needed because the buffer "already stores the estimate, its uncertainty and the outcome per entry" (`alg:monitoring:empirical-coverage`). The buffer does not. A calibration entry carries the blended score, the blend weight, the binary outcome, the importance weight and the per-axis pairs (`src/risk/calibration.rs:133`), and no uncertainty; the row type the discrimination function unpacks carries four of those five (`src/health/published.rs:29`). The standardised residual divides by a quantity that is not there.

The repair is therefore larger than registered, though not by much, and the audit owes the exact size. The uncertainty is computed per request and is in scope at the construction site: the pending risk basis carries it (`src/pending/entry.rs:102`), and the label path reads two of that structure's other fields into the calibration entry two lines above where it would be added (`src/owner/label_path.rs:748`). So conformance means one field threaded from a structure already in hand, not a new measurement — but it does mean widening a persisted type, since the calibration entry is serialised into the checkpoint, and that is a schedule fact the entry as written conceals. This is the first time in the campaign that reading the owning cluster has made a registered repair bigger rather than smaller, and it qualifies the register's advice accordingly (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`).

**The last clause of that paragraph is wrong and is corrected by the persistence tranche** (`rem:assayer:persistence-cross-cites`), which owns the serialisation surface. `CalibrationEntry` derives the serialisation traits under the same feature gate the checkpoint sits behind, which is presumably what this reading rested on, and it is not serialised into the checkpoint: the payload declares no calibration field, no persistence surface in the workspace touches the entry or its buffer, and the payload's own notice lists the calibration buffer among the state it does *not* yet carry. Everything above that clause stands — the buffer genuinely carries no uncertainty and the repair is genuinely larger than the founding entry said — but the schedule fact is not a fact, and the repair is correspondingly smaller than this paragraph left it.

(`entry:assayer:wl-sentinel-informativeness`) has its locus at `src/api/health.rs:218` and its export half in this wave's other cluster, where it is measured at (`rem:assayer:metrics-surfaced-constants`). Its coverage line stands as written: the export test constructs the zero as its fixture input.

(`entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`) has three of its six departures resident here and all three were re-verified individually. The drift noise allowance ships at half a unit against the specified tenth (`src/health/drift.rs:74`). The minimum class count for the discrimination statistic ships at fifteen against the specified twenty (`src/health/published.rs:214`), still arguing its own deviation in a comment with no landed record behind it. The recent discrimination window ships as a quarter of the buffer against the specified fixed count (`src/health/published.rs:279`), and the coincidence the founding entry noted — that a quarter of the shipped buffer is exactly the specified figure — was re-checked and holds.

(`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`) is settled from this side and needs no correction. The drift flag the compact snapshot is missing would be derived from the accumulators in this cluster, which are computed and published (`src/owner/label_path.rs:1224`), so the entry's reading is confirmed: the inputs are present and the snapshot field is absent, rather than the quantity being unavailable.

**Remark (What the clock lane should take from this cluster)** · `rem:assayer:health-clock-lane`

Two notices in this cluster ask for the persistent-clock parameterisation carved out of the freeze, and this tranche adds facts rather than a disposition, as the extraction tranche did for the staleness item (`rem:assayer:extraction-cross-cites`). The lane should have three things.

The tracker's constructor reads the wall clock. Creating an identity tracker stamps the present into its first-seen field (`src/health/identity_tracker.rs:97`), and the notice above it asked for an explicit timestamp instead — the wave-three repair made the constructor take one, leaving the notice's other halves under a successor label (`todo:code:start-pessimistic-and-count-change-events`). This is the shape registered at (`entry:assayer:wl-timestamp-default-reads-clock`) met in a constructor rather than in a derived default, and it is reached from every dimension registration.

The stage computation reads the wall clock too, and that is the more consequential of the two. Deciding whether a dimension has matured takes the elapsed hours since first seen by reading the present at query time (`src/health/identity_tracker.rs:170`), under a notice that asked for the reading to be passed in "to keep this computation pure and deterministic in tests/checkpoint replay" — the wave-three repair took it. So a stage is not a function of the tracker's state: the same tracker returns different stages at different wall-clock moments, and the full health report is correspondingly non-deterministic.

And the third fact is that the lane's exposure here is currently smaller than it looks, for a reason that will reverse. Both readings feed the composite through the stability predicate, and the composite is handed an empty tracker slice (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`), so neither clock read reaches the published stage today. Repairing the wiring turns two dormant clock reads into live ones on the package's most prominent health number. The two repairs should therefore be sequenced together, and the clock repair should land first — which is the same conclusion the ledger tranche reached about its sweep and its cursor (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-positional-cursor`).

**Remark (Four defects in the corpus's own prose, and one understatement that changes a verdict)** · `rem:assayer:health-prose-defects`

Four are recorded for the record pipeline. Prose defects sit outside the disposition grammar by construction, but one of these did change a disposition and is marked.

At the audit cut, the monitoring chapter miscounted its own absent-quantity closings. That historical finding has been overtaken by delivery: empirical coverage, encoding effectiveness and the immature-cell count are now computed, and the chapter's census has been reduced accordingly (`chap:spec:health-monitoring`). A later health-surface delivery overtook the next census too: per-entity concordance, the alarm-outcome accumulators, maturity coverage and resolution utilisation now ship. Feedback latency and Ledger value realisation remain absent under their named owner STOPs. The row-by-row audit remains evidence for the original finding rather than a claim about the current count.

The dimension-health table understates what is missing, and this is the understatement that changed a verdict. Its closing says dimension health "is present in the package and carries the set size along with dropped-observation counts, but two of the six rows are absent" (`tab:monitoring:dimension-health`). Measured, the set size is a permanent zero rather than a carried value, so of the six tabulated rows none carries information: two are present as fields fed a default and four are unimplemented. The one quantity that is genuinely carried, the dropped-observation count, is not among the six rows at all. An audit trusting that sentence would have recorded this cluster's largest defect as a two-row gap already conceded. That historical defect is now overtaken, and completely: the set size and change readings are wired, both shape distributions reach full health under (`test:crate:identity-shape-distributions-reach-full-health`), and the two rows the table had been claiming without producing carry readings of their own — the coverage share under (`test:crate:identity-coverage-fraction-reads-the-competitive-hit-share`) and the cell weight mass under (`test:crate:dimension-cell-weight-mass-reads-the-competitive-indicator-positions`). Every one of the six rows now carries a reading taken from live producer state.

The empirical-coverage algorithm states a false fact about the code. Its assurance that "the calibration buffer already stores the estimate, its uncertainty and the outcome per entry" (`alg:monitoring:empirical-coverage`) is not true of the shipped buffer, as established at (`rem:assayer:health-cross-cites`). The substance of the diagnostic is unaffected and the sentence should be narrowed to say that the uncertainty is computed per request and is available at the point the entry is built.

And the synchronisation-error definition states a second false fact about the code, in the direction that matters most. Its closing that the shipped substitute "is not surfaced in the health report, so neither the specified quantity nor the shipped substitute is visible to a host" (`def:monitoring:synchronisation-error`) is wrong on the second clause: the field is on the summary, the metric is catalogued and emitted on every scrape, and what a host sees is a zero. A sentence telling a reader that a diagnostic is absent, where it is present and misreporting, is the more dangerous of the two errors, and it is argued at (`entry:assayer:wl-health-sync-error-constant`).

## Seventh tranche: the metrics surface · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-metrics`

The cluster is `src/metrics/` — the compile-time catalogue, the neutral sample type, the two encoding helpers and the two mappers — taken against the metrics record and the monitoring analysis chapters (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`).

It is the smallest cluster the audit has taken and the most nearly mechanical. The record makes three decisions and one convention, every one of them a property of the source rather than of a computation, and each is checkable by comparing two files against each other. The result is the second clean negative the campaign has had: this cluster's own machinery holds no divergence at all, and every finding recorded below is about a value arriving from outside it.

**Remark (The one cluster whose audit may not state what it counted)** · `rem:assayer:metrics-size-unstated`

The convention (`conv:metrics:size-unstated`) rules that no count of the exported metrics appears in the record, in the specification, "or in any other document", and that a reader wanting the number reads the catalogue. This audit is another document, and the rule therefore binds it: the tranche below verified the catalogue entry by entry and states no total anywhere, which is why its conformance rows are written as exhaustive correspondences rather than as matching counts.

The rule was worth obeying rather than arguing with, because this tranche is exactly the case it was written from. The convention's own evidence is that the number was carried in six places, given five different values across three documents, and agreed with the code in none of them. An audit that recorded the total here would become the seventh place, and would be stale on the next metric added — while the correspondence the rows do record stays true under any such addition, because it is a statement about the mapping rather than about its size.

**Table (What the metrics cluster conforms to)** · `tab:assayer:metrics-conformant`

Recorded first, because an audit that lists only its findings misrepresents what it read. Every row was established by a scripted comparison of `src/metrics/catalog.rs` against `src/metrics/mapper.rs` over the whole catalogue, not by sampling, and each holds without exception.

| Checked | Authority | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The package renders nothing, emits no formatted output, and depends on no monitoring crate | (`dec:metrics:passive-export`) | `src/metrics/mod.rs:10`, and the cluster by absence |
| The catalogue is a compile-time constant, not a runtime registry | (`dec:metrics:compile-time-catalogue`) | `src/metrics/catalog.rs:128` |
| Values leave as a neutral sample carrying a name, a value and its labels, committing to no exposition format | (`dec:metrics:neutral-sample`) | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:32` |
| The mapper reads the cheap summary, per the tiering the health record fixes | (`dec:health:tiered-queries`) | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:95` |
| Every catalogued entry outside the host surface is emitted by exactly one mapper site | (`dec:metrics:compile-time-catalogue`) | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:103`, `:192`, `:342`, `:373`, `:404`, `:434` |
| No sample is emitted under a name the catalogue does not carry | (`dec:metrics:compile-time-catalogue`) | the cluster, exhaustively |
| Every entry's emitted type is the type the catalogue declares for it | (`dec:metrics:neutral-sample`) | the cluster, exhaustively |
| Every entry's emitted label keys are the label dimensions the catalogue declares | (`cor:metrics:bounded-cardinality`) | the cluster, exhaustively |
| Each entry is emitted by the mapper matching the surface it declares, summary entries from the summary mapper and full-report entries from the report mapper | (`dec:health:tiered-queries`) | the cluster, exhaustively |
| The host-surface entries are emitted by no mapper, exactly as the catalogue's own note says | (`dec:metrics:passive-export`) | `src/metrics/catalog.rs:66` |
| No label value is minted at runtime; every label is an identifier rendered from a declared set | (`cor:metrics:bounded-cardinality`) | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:62`, `:375`, `:406` |
| The absent value encodes as a not-a-number rather than as a zero or a skipped sample | (`cav:metrics:encoding-absorbed`) | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:53` |
| The seven assessment degradation counters reach the export one for one | (`tab:monitoring:observability-summary`) | `src/health/counters.rs:25`, `src/metrics/mapper.rs:123` |

The fifth row is the one the record's central property rests on, and it is worth saying what was and was not established. The comparison confirms that catalogue and mappers agree today, name for name, type for type, label for label, surface for surface, with no entry emitted twice and none emitted under an uncatalogued name. What it does not establish is that they must: the property (`dec:metrics:compile-time-catalogue`) claims — that the catalogue "cannot drift from what the code emits" — is argued from the catalogue being a constant rather than a runtime registry, and that argument reaches registration order and skipped registration, which are indeed impossible here. It does not reach a source edit to one file and not the other. The agreement is real, complete, and maintained by care.

**Entry (Nothing checks the catalogue against the mappers)** · `entry:assayer:wl-metrics-unchecked-correspondence`

**Locus** `src/metrics/catalog.rs:128` against `src/metrics/mapper.rs:103`, `:192`, `:342`, `:373`, `:404` and `:434`. No notice stands here, and the item is new.

**Disposition** unspecified, and the corpus is the party with the gap. The record decides that the catalogue is a compile-time constant and argues from that choice to a property it states in strong terms — that the catalogue cannot drift from what the code emits (`dec:metrics:compile-time-catalogue`) — and the convention that the count is stated nowhere outside the code rests on the same confidence, since it makes the catalogue the single answer to a question no other document may answer (`conv:metrics:size-unstated`). Neither says who enforces the correspondence, and nothing does. The two files are ordinary source: a metric may be catalogued and never emitted, or emitted and never catalogued, and the compiler is indifferent to both.

**Severity** medium, as an amplifier rather than as a defect of its own, and this is the metrics analogue of the shape registered at (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-unchecked-extension`). Nothing is wrong today — the correspondence was verified exhaustively and holds — so the severity is entirely about what the absence permits. A catalogued metric that no mapper emits is a name a host registers at startup and then never receives a sample for, which reads to an operator as a dead series rather than as a missing emitter; the reverse is a sample a host cannot classify because it registered no such metric. The convention that no document may state the count makes the catalogue load-bearing in a way that a document-stated count would not be, so the one artefact a reader is directed to trust is the one with no mechanical guard.

**What conformance would mean** a test that walks the catalogue and the mappers' emission sites and asserts the correspondence in both directions — the check that would have made this tranche's central table a gate rather than a measurement. It is authoring work under the test documentation policy rather than repair work, and it belongs with the wave named at (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) rather than with the conformance fixes.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `1a0ef2cb`, and recomputation found the entry's premise half-stale first: the forward direction — every non-host catalogue entry produced by a mapper over richly populated fixtures — already stood as a test when the audit registered "nothing checks", so half of what the absence permitted was already caught. The wave authored the half that was genuinely absent: every sample either mapper emits must be catalogued, under its declared metric type and its declared label keys, and never from an entry reserved for the host (`test:crate:every-emitted-sample-is-catalogued`). The correspondence is a gate in both directions now, and the wave's own two catalogue additions landed against it.

**Entry (Two Companion metrics are catalogued as host-maintained on a reason that does not hold)** · `entry:assayer:wl-metrics-companion-surface`

**Locus** `src/metrics/catalog.rs:457` and `src/metrics/catalog.rs:464`, the two challenge entries, read against the surface's own definition at `src/metrics/catalog.rs:106`. No notice stands here, and the item is new.

**Disposition** unspecified, and the finding is against a justification rather than against a placement. The host surface is defined in the catalogue as naming metrics "sourced outside the Assayer", with a host-kept latency histogram as its worked example, and the reserved latency name fits that description exactly. The two challenge metrics do not: challenge effectiveness is a quantity this package implements and computes, and the reason no mapper can emit it is not that it is sourced elsewhere but that the Core deliberately holds no Companion state (`inv:guarantee:companion-independence`) — the property the risk tranche established is a met requirement rather than an omission (`rem:assayer:risk-census`). Two different reasons for not emitting a metric have been collapsed into one surface, and the corpus fixes neither, so the classification is a gap rather than a violation.

**Severity** low. Nothing computes a wrong number and no series is misnamed; the cost is that the surface a host filters on no longer partitions by anything a host can act on. A host reading the surface as documented concludes it must maintain the challenge metrics itself, in the same sense it maintains the latency histogram — where what is actually true is that it must obtain them from a Companion tracker this package supplies and does not own.

**What conformance would mean** either a surface that distinguishes host-sourced names from package-computed names the Core may not reach, or a sentence at the surface's definition naming the second reason alongside the first. Both are corpus and code changes together and neither is this campaign's to make; it is recorded so the boundary the risk tranche established has its metrics-side face on the record.

**Remark (The surfaced-constant audit and its current disposition)** · `rem:assayer:metrics-surfaced-constants`

The deferral pass originally marked three register entries as surfaced constants — the shape it was built to catch, a health field that exists, is exported as a live metric, and is fed a literal (`tab:assayer:deferral-pins`). All three terminated in this cluster at their export half and were measured here rather than transcribed. The live tree has since advanced all three projections: Sentinel informativeness is populated from operational slot weights, which is the whole of what its completion asks — the superseded sister-model wording is not owed and no sister-side or divergence figure replaces it — so its deferral is completed and retired; synchronisation health reads every live model, publishes their aggregate maximum and carries the clean high-error shortening cadence, so its deferral is completed and retired; and the Schur metric reads the checkpointed marginalisation ledger, so its deferral is completed and retired.

| Register entry | Catalogued as | Emitted at | Fed from now | Current standing |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| (`entry:assayer:defer-sentinel-informativeness`) | `src/metrics/catalog.rs:456` | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:407` | `src/api/health.rs:218` | completed and retired; a measured value, no longer a literal |
| (`entry:assayer:defer-sync-error-visibility`) | `src/metrics/catalog.rs:378` | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:308` | `src/api/health.rs:50` | completed and retired |
| (`entry:assayer:defer-schur-skip-counter`) | `src/metrics/catalog.rs:512` | `src/metrics/mapper.rs:503` | `src/api/health.rs:378` | completed and retired |

The mappers were not the defect in any of the three and should not be read as one: each read its field and passed the value through unaltered, which is what the neutral sample type requires of it (`dec:metrics:neutral-sample`), and the mapper's own tests drove it with non-zero fixtures and observed the value arrive. The defect at the audit cut was upstream assembly. The later repairs changed those upstream projections without changing the mapper contract.

At that cut the package already had an encoding for a quantity that was not available — the absent value maps to a not-a-number (`src/metrics/mapper.rs:53`) — and it is used elsewhere in the same mappers for exactly this purpose. That made the original literal values indistinguishable from genuine zero measurements. The live tree has removed that ambiguity for the three aggregate fields, and all three have since left the open register: none of the surfaced constants the pass caught is still a literal or still deferred.

Each is discharged, and the skipped-correction count is worth spelling out because it is the one where the encoding question was live. It is fed from the marginalisation ledger at `78d1f3b5` and is a measurement, so the distinction this passage says a host cannot draw is drawable on that field; the sample type is unchanged and needed no encoding of absence, the count being a genuine zero before any removal has happened rather than an unmeasured one.

## Eighth tranche: state persistence · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-persistence`

The cluster is `src/persistence/` — the atomic checkpoint writer and reader, the append-only label journal and its sequencing writer, the restore path with its structural check and its bulk time-decay, and the periodic checkpoint scheduler — taken against the durability record and against the guarantees appendix, for the reason recorded immediately below.

The cluster was taken because the extraction tranche named it one of the three lowest-fan-in clusters where the census question should be asked hardest (`rem:assayer:extraction-census`), and because two registered items terminate in it: the partial checkpoint's journal truncation, whose truncation site this cluster owns, and the restore that discards every drift accumulator, whose payload this cluster owns. Both are cross-cited below rather than re-registered.

**Remark (The scope map's specification half is wrong for this cluster)** · `rem:assayer:persistence-scope-map`

The scope map gives this cluster the durability record and *the runtime operations chapter* (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`). The record half is right and is the authority most of this tranche's dispositions rest on. The specification half is wrong, and it is wrong in the strongest available sense rather than by a shade: the words *checkpoint* and *journal* do not occur anywhere in `docs/spec.md` or in any chapter under `docs/spec/`, in any casing. The runtime operations chapter's only occurrences of *persist* at all are the signal chapter's two persistence modes — whether a declared signal persists with the entity (`chap:spec:host-signals`) — which is an unrelated sense of the word and governs `signal/` rather than this cluster.

What does govern the cluster on the specification side is the guarantees appendix. Two invariants there reach it: assessment writes only what is enumerated (`inv:guarantee:assess-only`), which is the guarantee the record's own assess-no-persistence decision realises, and the replay invariant (`inv:guarantee:replay`). The temporal operations chapter governs the restore's decay, because the record defers the domain question to it (`dec:operational:temporal-governance`). This tranche read those three and the durability record, and audited against them.

The correction matters beyond bookkeeping, and this is the second tranche to make it — the health tranche found the map wrong for two of its files and recorded the fix rather than working around it (`rem:assayer:health-scope-map`). Here the consequence is larger, because the map does not merely name the wrong chapter: it implies a specification chapter governs this cluster when the specification is silent about the mechanism entirely. A tranche trusting the row would have gone looking for checkable claims in a chapter that holds none, and an audit that found none there might have concluded the cluster was unspecified in bulk. It is not unspecified in bulk; it is specified almost wholly by one record. The audit does not amend the map, and records the correction here for the reader who checks one against the other.

**Table (What the persistence cluster conforms to)** · `tab:assayer:persistence-conformant`
