**Locus** `src/snapshot/shared.rs:56`, the comment on the health field, against the two independent swaps it stands between at `src/snapshot/shared.rs:50` and `src/snapshot/shared.rs:60`. No labelled notice stands there, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:ordering:publish-at-end`), which decides that both publications fall at the end of the same path and states the consequence unconditionally: a reader "never observes a model that has been updated alongside health that has not, and never the reverse". The record is explicit that this is a different question from independence — the retention record decides the two are separate swaps, and this record decides that neither is observable mid-flight.

Two separate stores of two separate atomic pointers cannot deliver that, and the type's own documentation says so: between the two stores a reader "may observe the *new* snapshot but the *old* health summary". That is precisely the first of the two states the record forbids, conceded in a comment and framed as an ordering note rather than as a gap. It is the shape (`rem:assayer:feature-flagged-snapshot`) recorded at this cluster's other constructor, met a second time in the same cluster and this time against a decision whose wording the comment negates clause for clause.

**What the comment misses is the part that makes it more than a race.** Read as written, the concession describes a window of two adjacent statements on the steward thread — microseconds, and easy to dismiss. Measured, the window is not the interesting case. The comment's premise is that the two publications travel together at step seventeen, and two of the four production snapshot publications carry no health publication at all: the lifecycle path stores a snapshot and returns (`src/owner/lifecycle.rs:151`), and the batch initialisation does the same (`src/owner/thread.rs:479`). After either, the published health summary describes a strictly older model version until the next label completes, which is the record's forbidden state held indefinitely rather than transiently. The comment's second clause — that the reverse is impossible — was checked and does hold, but it holds contingently rather than structurally: every health store in the workspace happens to follow a snapshot store within the same handler, including on the two revert paths, and nothing enforces that it must.

**Severity** medium. Nothing computed is corrupted and the health surface is report-only by construction, but the report-only posture does not cap this, for the reason the health tranche established: the corpus prescribes the alerts a host builds on these values (`inv:monitoring:report-only`), so a summary describing a model the reader is not looking at is a diagnostic acted on by design. It is held below high because the divergence is a skew rather than a wrong value, and because a host cannot currently detect it in either case — the published health summary carries no model version, which is the same missing join key (`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`) registers for the inline snapshot, met here on the polled surface. It is not registered as a second instance of that entry because the field it needs sits on a different type.

**What conformance would mean** either the two values are installed by one swap of a pair, which is what the record's wording asks for and what the monolithic decision already does for the model's own parts, or the health summary carries the snapshot version it was computed against so that a reader can tell the two apart and the record's claim is narrowed to say so. The second is the smaller repair and is the one the missing join key would want anyway; the first is what (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) presumes, because the record states the property rather than a bound on it.

**Coverage** none. Three tests exercise the model swap directly — `arcswap_store_load_new_version`, `arcswap_two_snapshots_alive` and `concurrent_readers_and_writer`, the last of which drives a thousand publications against four reading threads — and not one of them loads the health swap at all. The property the record states is the only one in this cluster with a concurrency suite beside it and no test.

**Entry (The pending entry carries eight of the twelve fields the corpus defines)** · `entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`

**Locus** `src/pending/entry.rs:152`, the entry type. No labelled notice stands over it, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:runtime:pending-entry`), which fixes twelve fields across eleven rows and says what each carries. Eight ship. The identifier, the timestamp, the entity key, the per-Sentinel extractions, the identity coordinates, the identity active cells, the signal features and the risk basis are all present and correct. Four are absent: the active Sentinel list, the reporting Sentinel list, the frozen per-dimension identity base features and the frozen per-dimension per-axis identity features. Three fields stand in the struct that the definition does not name — the wall-clock timestamp beside the monotonic one, the per-axis outcome predictions, and the degradation context.

The chapter concedes all of this in its own closing sentence, and the concession was re-measured rather than transcribed, as the health tranche's method requires. It is exact on every particular: four absences, and they are the four named; three unspecified additions, and there are three. It is a concession rather than an adjudication, and the disposition follows the reading the health tranche settled at the compact health snapshot (`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`) — a definition that fixes a field set is the requirement, and a sentence observing that part of it has not shipped is a status report against that requirement rather than a relaxation of it.

**What the four absences cost, which the corpus states and the audit confirms.** The chapter closes by saying that "the four absences are exactly the inputs the missing reconstruction steps would have read", and reading the reconstruction algorithm against the type confirms it precisely. Source one requires the stored identity base features and they are not stored. Source three requires the stored per-axis identity features "where the axis existed at assessment time" and they are not stored, so the distinction between an axis that existed and one registered afterwards cannot be drawn from the entry at all. Source four requires a slot per currently registered Sentinel filled three different ways — the stored extraction where it was reporting, occupancy with zero features where it was active but silent, and no slot where it is no longer registered — and telling the second case from the third needs the two Sentinel lists, which are the two other absences. The per-extraction occupancy flag the type does carry (`src/pending/entry.rs:66`) distinguishes a Sentinel that reported without valid cells from one that reported with them; it says nothing about a Sentinel that was registered and produced no extraction at all, because such a Sentinel has no entry in the map to carry a flag.

**Severity** high, and it is the storage half of a defect already registered at its consumption half. (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`) records that four of six reconstruction blocks stand at zero in every training vector, at high severity and by recomputation the founding worklist's highest-leverage item. This entry is registered separately rather than folded into it because the two have different loci, different repairs and different owners, and because folding them would hide the thing this cluster can see and that one could not: the repair registered there is not sufficient. That is argued at (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`).

**What conformance would mean** the entry gains the active and reporting Sentinel identifiers and the two frozen identity feature blocks, at which point the reconstruction's sources one, three and four have their inputs. The three unspecified additions are not part of this: two of them are read on live paths — the wall-clock timestamp by the guidance output and the outcome predictions by the calibration entry at label step fourteen — and the degradation context is carried and dropped, which is recorded below rather than here.

**Coverage** none, and none is reachable. Every test of this type constructs it field by field, so a missing field is invisible to all of them by construction; there is no site at which an entry is compared against what the assessment computed. Two of the tests build entries with empty identity maps, which is the absence the entry is charged with, asserted as an ordinary fixture.

**Sequencing** before (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`), whose repair reads three of the fields this entry establishes are missing. Widening the entry is a persisted-shape change and the reconstruction is a call; scheduling them in the other order would ship a call that reads what nothing stores.

**Repaired** in wave six at `5da9cbf3`, first of the ordered pair. The entry and its journal context gained the four fields in one stroke — the two Sentinel lists and the two frozen identity blocks, the base half stored as the five measurement values behind the structural triple the reconstruction re-derives — filled on the assessment path from values it already computed, and the journal format version stepped 1 → 2 exactly once, so a prior deployment's journal is refused and cold-starts by design. The coverage line's unreachable test now exists: the four fields cross the journal boundary in both directions with their values equal (`test:unit:pending-context-reconstruction-fields-round-trip`).

**Entry (The published snapshot's cold-start constructor discards the dimension it is given)** · `entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-cold-start-dimension`

**Locus** `src/snapshot/published.rs:192`, the map built from an empty configuration, and `src/snapshot/published.rs:202`, where the caller's dimension is discarded; the comment between them at `src/snapshot/published.rs:199` states that the two "may differ". No labelled notice stands there. The site was flagged for this tranche by the feature tranche (`rem:assayer:feature-flagged-snapshot`) and is registered here.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`inv:dimension:version-consistency`), which requires the map, the model parameters and the standardisation statistics to share one dimension and one index semantics at every published version, and which argues at length that the invariant is unconditional because there is no useful weaker form. A constructor of the published type that takes the model dimension, discards it, builds a map from a different source and documents the two as possibly disagreeing is that invariant conceded in a comment. The feature tranche's reading is confirmed on every particular.

**Severity** low, and the severity is this entry's whole contribution, because the flag could not carry one. By census the constructor has no production caller: the production cold start builds the working copy from the schema and projects it (`src/lib.rs:403`, into `src/snapshot/working.rs:213`), where the model dimension *is* the map's dimension and the invariant holds by construction. The constructor's only two call sites are test helpers, and both of them avoid the hazard the same way — each builds the dimension map first, takes its width, constructs parameters at that width, and then, having called the constructor, overwrites the standardisation vectors it just produced (`src/assessment.rs:1657`, `src/tests/assess_pipeline.rs:324`). Two independent callers working around a constructor's documented behaviour is evidence about the constructor rather than about them. So no snapshot violating the invariant is constructed anywhere in the workspace today, and the defect is a loaded surface rather than a live one.

**What conformance would mean** the constructor either derives its parameters' dimension from the map it builds, or takes the map from its caller instead of building one, or is deleted — the third being defensible on the census, since the production path does not use it and both test callers immediately correct it. Whichever is chosen, the comment stops asserting that a published snapshot may carry a map and parameters of different widths.

**Coverage** worse than none in the mild sense. Nothing asserts the constructor's output is self-consistent; what the two call sites do instead is repair it, so the suite's fixtures conceal the property rather than test it.

**Entry (The pending buffer's storage precision is fixed where the corpus makes it configurable)** · `entry:assayer:wl-pending-storage-precision`

**Locus** `src/pending/entry.rs:64` and `src/pending/entry.rs:168`, both feature vectors declared at single precision in the type. No notice stands at either, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`def:runtime:storage-precision`), which fixes single precision as the default and says in the same sentence that it is configurable, and against (`tab:config:pending-buffer`), whose third row tabulates the precision as a configuration parameter with a constraint of single or double. No such parameter exists: the width is a property of the two struct fields, no configuration type in the package carries a precision setting, and nothing in the workspace reads one.

**Severity** low, and it is low for a reason worth stating rather than assumed. The value that ships is the specified default and the reasoning behind that default is sound and is the corpus's own — single-precision quantisation is five orders of magnitude below the restandardisation error already present, so no deployment should want the alternative. What is lost is not accuracy but the ability to falsify that argument in a deployment where it fails, and the audit records it because the corpus states the trade as one a host may make.

**What conformance would mean** the precision becomes a configuration parameter the two vectors are declared against, or the corpus narrows both statements to say the precision is fixed at single and why. The second is the smaller change and the audit does not choose it, because (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) puts the burden on the party that would call the document stale and no landed record supersedes either statement.

**Coverage** none. Two tests measure the entry's footprint at the reference width and assert it against a range, which establishes the consequence of the shipped choice and says nothing about whether the choice is settable.

**Repaired** in wave six at `99a74e7d`, riding as a change to the same type, by the first of the conformance line's options: the precision is a configuration parameter on the infrastructure block, single by default and constrained to single-or-double by its type, and the entry's stored vectors are declared against a width-carrying type the parameter reaches by construction. The choice is now settable and testable both ways: a value single precision cannot represent survives double storage bit-for-bit and quantises to its nearest single under the default (`test:unit:storage-precision-double-keeps-what-single-quantises`), and a double-precision context crosses the journal boundary bit-for-bit in both directions (`test:unit:double-precision-context-serde-roundtrip`). What the parameter does not change: extraction hands over single-precision values by its own contract (`rem:extraction:single-precision`), so a widened store of extraction features carries the information the source held and no more.

**Table (The two clusters' remaining divergences)** · `tab:assayer:snapshot-pending-remaining`

| Divergence | Authority | Locus | Severity |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| The projection defaults the calibration buffer summary and relies on one call site to patch it, so the three publications that do not patch carry zeros where the snapshot's contents fix a summary | (`def:publication:model-snapshot`) | `src/snapshot/working.rs:276` | medium |
| The module's dead-code exemption states that its three modules are consumed only by tests and is suppressed until public consumers exist; all three are consumed from production, and the parent module is private so no public consumer can ever exist | (`dec:concurrency:snapshot-swap`) | `src/snapshot/mod.rs:24` | low |
| A legacy dimension-map placeholder type is declared and documented as retained for tests, and is constructed nowhere in the workspace including in tests | (`def:publication:model-snapshot`) | `src/snapshot/published.rs:55` | low |
| The buffer's own documentation states an eviction priority of expired entries before over-capacity ones; the pass has no priority, popping the same queue front under either trigger | (`dec:retention:lazy-eviction`) | `src/pending/buffer.rs:66` | low |
| The type documentation and the conversion's own documentation both state that the risk basis and the outcome predictions are recomputed at label time, and nothing recomputes them | (`cor:durability:replay-exactness`) | `src/pending/entry.rs:90`, `src/pending/entry.rs:184` | medium |

The calibration row needs its relation to the standardisation entry stated, because the two are the same construction fault at two fields and are registered at different weights. Both are defaulted by the projection under a notice asking for the default to be replaced by a real projection — (`todo:code:project-the-live-calibration-buffer-summary`), which stood beside the standardisation notice the wave-four repair took with its defect — and both are then patched by some call sites and not others. It is a medium rather than a high because the three regime counts the summary carries feed a reported field rather than a computation applied to a model, so a lifecycle publication carrying zeros understates a diagnostic where the standardisation vectors mis-scale every feature.

The two documentation rows about recomputation are tabled rather than registered because their locus is the entry the persistence tranche already holds, and they are the third and fourth sites of the same false assurance rather than a new defect; they are argued at (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`).

**Remark (The census in its third form, and the gaps the corpus leaves)** · `rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-census`

The persistence tranche asked the remaining tranches to put the census question in a third form wherever a cluster moves data between two others: not only who calls this and does the corpus want them to, but does anything ever put anything into it (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`). Both clusters here move data between two others, and the third form is what returned this wave's yield. The first two forms returned almost nothing.

By call-site census the two clusters are nearly clean, and the exceptions divide the way the health tranche's correction predicts. `SharedState::from_arc` at `src/snapshot/shared.rs:75` has no caller anywhere, not in production and not in a test; it is a second constructor for a holder the package builds once, and no corpus text asks for it. `WorkingCopy::cold_start` at `src/snapshot/working.rs:150` has no production caller — the construction path uses the schema-derived constructor beside it — and it is reached from a dozen tests. It is registered nowhere on its own account, but it is the constructor whose `p.max(dimension_map.p)` can build models wider than the map they are indexed against, which is the working-copy twin of the finding at (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-cold-start-dimension`) and is recorded there rather than here. `ModelSnapshot::cold_start` is the third and is that entry's locus. `PendingBuffer::is_empty` and the three-argument checkpoint wrapper at `src/snapshot/working.rs:385` are reached only by tests; both are accessor and convenience surface on types the health tranche's settled reading leaves unregistered (`rem:assayer:health-census`), and the second is cross-cited below because six of the tests reaching it are the hollow transfer the persistence tranche named.

The third form returned two results and they run in opposite directions, which is worth recording because the persistence tranche met only one of the two.

The first is the shape that tranche described: a field that is filled and never read. `PendingAssessment.degradation` at `src/pending/entry.rs:177` is populated on every assessment from the assessment's own degradation context (`src/assessment.rs:1168`) and is read by nothing. The label path's context accessor set offers no accessor for it, no owner path names it, and the degradation counters a host reads are recorded from the assessment result instead (`src/api/assess.rs:60`). So the buffer carries a degradation context per pending entry, at the buffer's capacity, for no consumer — and the entry's own conversion documents the field as "not needed for replay", which is true and is not the question. A call-site census sees a field that is written; only asking who reads it establishes this.

The second is its mirror and the campaign has not met it before: a field that is read on one path and is structurally absent on the other, so that the reader silently does nothing. `PendingAssessment.outcome_predictions` is filled on every assessment and is read at label step fourteen through an accessor that returns an option (`src/owner/label_path.rs:337`, read at `src/owner/label_path.rs:755`). The option is `None` for exactly one variant — the replay context — because the journal form has no such field to return. That accessor is where the two skipped steps the persistence tranche registered actually live (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-stripped-context`), and its shape is why they are skipped silently rather than loudly: the absence is expressed as an option a caller handles rather than as a case a caller must consider. The audit records this as the mechanism rather than as a second finding, and records the general form for the tranches still to come: where a cluster hands two differently-shaped payloads to one consumer, the question to ask of the narrower one is not what it omits but which of the consumer's branches its omission silently disables.

Neither cluster carries a per-file dead-code suppression, but `src/pending/mod.rs` carries a module-wide one at `src/pending/mod.rs:31`, justified by naming the cluster a layer-two data structure to be wired into the package at layer three and citing the legacy record for the buffer's design. The wiring has since happened — the buffer is constructed at `src/lib.rs:416` and is reached from four production surfaces. That is the stale-exemption shape the persistence tranche recorded twice (`rem:assayer:persistence-census`), met here in its third instance, and it is not registered for the same reason: stale rather than wrong. The `src/snapshot/mod.rs` exemption is worse than stale and is tabled above.

Four gaps the corpus leaves are recorded here rather than as entries.

The eviction cap's value is **unspecified**. The retention record decides that eviction is capped per insert and argues why a cap is necessary; it names no number and no corpus text does. Sixteen is the code's own choice (`src/pending/buffer.rs:51`) and is a reasonable one, so the audit records it as a choice rather than as a row. The same holds of the decision to count a stale identifier against the cap, which is what makes the pass terminate and is the code's own arrangement.

The guidance scan's selection is **unspecified**. A bounded scan takes the first entries the concurrent map happens to yield (`src/pending/buffer.rs:177`), which is neither insertion order nor uncertainty order nor any order the guidance chapter names. Whether a bounded scan of a deep buffer should be representative of it is a corpus question this audit does not answer; what is worth stating is that the current answer is a hash order, so the same buffer scanned twice may offer two disjoint candidate sets and neither is the oldest, the newest, or the most uncertain.

The published snapshot's version has **unspecified** overflow behaviour, and the gap is recorded only because the version is the invariant's own name for a publication. It is a sixty-four-bit counter incremented per publication and nothing in the corpus or the code says what a wrap would mean; at any plausible label rate it will not wrap, which is why this is a row and not a finding.

And the restore's reset of the working copy's last-label instant is **unspecified** on its own terms. Both restore paths set it to the present (`src/snapshot/working.rs:357`, `src/snapshot/working.rs:532`), which is what the suite's own claim argues for and which is right for the checkpoint path because the elapsed decay has already been applied by the recovery caller. Nothing in the corpus fixes it for the snapshot path, which is the revert rather than a restart, and where the reset discards a mark from a few milliseconds earlier rather than from a previous run.

**Remark (The items terminating in these clusters, cross-cited, and one prior repair that is not sufficient)** · `rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`

Five registered items terminate in or cross these clusters. None is re-registered; all were re-verified at their recorded lines and all stand, and two carry corrections that belong to these clusters to make.

(`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-stripped-context`) stands, and this tranche owns the type rather than the replay. The persistence tranche's account is exact on every particular this cluster can check: the context type is at `src/pending/entry.rs:223`, it omits the risk basis and the outcome predictions, and the conversion that strips them is at `src/pending/entry.rs:188`. Three facts are added.

The first is that the type's governing record condemns the omission independently, and the audit owes this because the entry rests on one record and a reader may reasonably ask whether the type's own record licenses what the durability record forbids. (`dec:retention:journal-subset`) decides that the journal form is a subset of the entry and that "the subset is defined by consumption", which read alone is exactly the code's justification and is the sentence the four documentation sites below reproduce. It does not stand alone. The same environment closes by saying that "replay's exactness rests on what the journal holds being sufficient", and subordinates itself to the durability corollary for that sufficiency. So the subset-by-consumption rule is licensed only where replay is exact, replay is not exact, and the record that a reader would cite in the code's defence is the record that makes the omission divergent. The code's justification is that record's first sentence read without its last.

The second is that the false assurance stands at four sites in this one file rather than at the two the persistence tranche found, and the two new ones are the more consequential because they are the type's own documentation rather than a test's. The registered pair are the type comment at `src/pending/entry.rs:208`, which lists the risk basis and the outcome predictions under "Recomputed at label time", and the test claim at `src/pending/entry.rs:307`. Beside them stand the risk basis type's own documentation at `src/pending/entry.rs:90` — "Replay contexts intentionally omit these values and recompute model-derived risk data from restored state" — and the conversion's documentation at `src/pending/entry.rs:184`, "Drops `risk_basis` (recomputed from model at label time)". Two of the four also assert the design the governing record rejects, and the risk-basis one asserts it as an intention. A reader consulting any of the four learns that a recomputation happens; none happens. The two documentation rows are tabled at (`tab:assayer:snapshot-pending-remaining`) and the coverage line of the registered entry stands as written.

The third is the mechanism, recorded at (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-census`): the skip is not a guard the label path takes on a replay flag alone but an option returning `None` from a context accessor, which is why it is silent.

(`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`) stands, and both of its conversions are in this cluster. The write conversion is at `src/snapshot/working.rs:413` and the read at `src/snapshot/working.rs:470`, and the enumeration of what the payload has no field for was re-derived from the working copy rather than transcribed and matches. One fact is added and it is a prerequisite rather than a correction. That entry's repair, and the repair of (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`) beside it, both require the running means and variances to join the payload. They cannot, in the shape the conversion has: the payload is built from a `WorkingCopy` and the working copy does not hold the standardisation statistics — they live on the owner (`src/owner/thread.rs:81`), which is the same structural fact that forces this tranche's own headline entry. So the three repairs share one prerequisite, the relocation of the statistics onto the working copy, and doing that relocation once serves all three. This is the register's advice — re-check a repair against the cluster that owns the mechanism, in both directions — returning neither bigger nor smaller but *shared*, which is a third direction and is worth carrying: two entries registered as independent are one relocation and two follow-ons.

(`entry:assayer:wl-health-checkpoint-drops-drift`) stands at `src/snapshot/working.rs:534`, and this tranche confirms both halves that the health and persistence tranches established between them: the restored working copy binds an empty drift map, and it does so because the payload declares no field to bind from. Nothing is added and nothing changes.

(`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-axis-fields`) stands at `src/snapshot/working.rs:511` and `src/snapshot/working.rs:516`, where the restore substitutes an empty name and the default eligibility, and this cluster owns those two lines. The persistence tranche's account is confirmed, and one fact sharpens it: the *snapshot* projection carries all three of the fields the checkpoint drops. The axis state published to readers has the name, the forgetting rate and the eligibility mode (`src/snapshot/published.rs:36`, filled at `src/snapshot/working.rs:245`), and the restore from a snapshot carries all three back (`src/snapshot/working.rs:336`). So the same package, in the same file, has one per-axis conversion that is complete and one that is not, and the incomplete one is the persisted one. That strengthens the entry's own argument that the omission was an oversight rather than a decision — the complete conversion is the model for the incomplete one, forty lines away.

(`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`) does not have its locus here and **its repair is corrected in place, in the direction that makes it larger.** The founding entry said the four zeroed blocks are re-derived from the current working copy; the feature tranche found that most of that work is already written and exported and concluded that "the repair is largely a call rather than an implementation" (`rem:assayer:feature-census`). The call is necessary and is not sufficient. The helper the label path should call re-derives the aggregate block, the identity dimensions' structural triple, the competitive indicators and the interactions from current state, and the corpus asks for exactly that in sources two, five and six of (`alg:runtime:reconstruction`). Sources one, three and four ask for something the helper cannot supply from any current state, because the corpus requires it to be *stored*: the frozen identity base features, the frozen per-axis identity features, and the distinction between a Sentinel that was reporting and one that was active and silent. Those are three of the four fields the pending entry does not carry, registered above at (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`). So the reconstruction cannot be made conformant by calling the helper, however correct the helper is: the buffer must first store what sources one, three and four read, and the two repairs are ordered rather than independent.

This is the third correction the campaign has made to a repair line after reading the cluster that owns the mechanism, and the second to run toward a larger repair rather than a smaller one. The first enlargement was the health tranche's (`rem:assayer:health-cross-cites`) and its added schedule fact did not survive the persistence tranche. This one is offered with that precedent in view and states its own falsifier: it is wrong if any of sources one, three or four can be satisfied from state the label path holds at label time, and it is not, because each of the three is by the algorithm's own wording a *frozen* value from assessment time and the assessment-time values exist nowhere but the buffer. The feature tranche's finding is untouched — the helper is written, exported, tested, and uncalled, and calling it is still most of the work — but the entry's repair is a call *and* a widening, and scheduling the call alone would close two of the four blocks and leave two at zero.

**Remark (What these clusters' tests pin, and the campaign's largest duplication)** · `rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-pins`

Every test driving either cluster was read against its authored claim. The authorship axis holds without exception in both clusters — every test carries a claim and both modules' test-index tables carry all of them — and two axes fail.

The hollow-transfer question the persistence tranche opened was asked of both clusters and both answer cleanly, which is worth recording because a class is only useful if it can come back negative. Every function the pending cluster's tests exercise is on a path a deployment takes: the buffer is constructed at `src/lib.rs:416`, inserted from the assessment path and the pre-seed path, removed from the label surface, read from the guidance surface and measured from the health surface, and the journal conversion is called at `src/api/label.rs:133`. The outcome-prediction transfer that `pending_assessment_carries_outcome_predictions` establishes is likewise real: production fills the field at `src/assessment.rs:1163` and step fourteen reads it. The snapshot cluster answers the same way for its swap and its projection.

It answers otherwise for one function, and it is the one the persistence tranche already named. The six tests that establish Ledger and identity state crossing the checkpoint faithfully all reach the payload through `WorkingCopy::to_checkpoint_payload` at `src/snapshot/working.rs:385`, the three-argument wrapper, passing populated vectors into arguments that production passes empty at `src/owner/thread.rs:350` through the four-argument variant. This tranche adds one fact to that finding: the wrapper the six tests use has no production caller at all, so the hollow transfer is not merely a populated payload on a path production takes differently — it is a populated payload handed to a function production never calls. That is the class at its sharpest and it belongs to (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`) rather than here.

The axis that fails largest is duplication, and this is the campaign's biggest instance by a wide margin. The health tranche found three pairs of effectively identical tests split between an inline module and an external suite, and recorded that the pairs share their authored claims so that the corpus counts one claim as established twice (`rem:assayer:health-pins`). The pending cluster holds nine such pairs. Every one of them was confirmed by reading both files rather than by matching names, which is the bar the earlier refutation set (`rem:assayer:worklist-refutations`): the inline modules are in `src/pending/buffer.rs` and `src/pending/entry.rs` and the external suite is `src/tests/pending.rs`, and they are genuinely three files. Two of the nine are verbatim but for the helper's name and the assertion messages. One claim — that exceeding capacity costs the oldest entry — is cited by three tests, two of them inline and one external, all three inserting past a capacity and asserting the same two identifiers.

What makes this more than tidiness here is the same effect the health tranche described, arriving at a cluster where the coverage that is genuinely missing is large. A register counting claims against tests reads the pending buffer as one of the better-covered corners of the package. Measured, its nine claims are established by nine tests written between one and three times each, and the entry type's twelve specified fields, four of which do not exist, are covered by nothing at all.

Two further pins are recorded. `pending_assessment_to_context_strips_transient_fields` at `src/tests/pending.rs` is named for stripping, cites the claim about *preservation*, and asserts one thing: that the entity key survives the conversion. It establishes nothing about stripping, and it is a strictly weaker duplicate of the test three declarations below it. And the pair that carries the campaign's sharpest claim defect — the two tests citing (`claim:pending:the-journal-context-preserves-everything-replay-depends-on`) — assert that the five carried fields arrive unchanged, which is true, under a claim whose own name asserts the sufficiency that (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-stripped-context`) establishes is false. The transfer is real and the sufficiency is the fiction, which is a different failure from a hollow transfer and should not be folded into it: a hollow transfer proves a mechanism that does not run, and this proves a mechanism that runs and is not enough.

**Remark (Three defects in the corpus's own prose, one of which would have inverted this tranche's central verdict)** · `rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-prose-defects`

Three are recorded for the record pipeline. The first is the largest the campaign has met, measured by what trusting it would have cost.

The concurrency chapter's snapshot definition describes an arrangement two records reject, and describes it as shipped. Its third paragraph states that "publication is componentised", that each parameter block, the map, the standardisation vectors and the calibration state "sit behind independent shared pointers", that structurally unchanged components are "shared across versions rather than copied, so an ordinary label never copies the lifecycle-stable state", and that the parameter components are double-buffered with resynchronisation charged to label processing (`def:publication:model-snapshot`). None of that is the shipped design and none of it is what the corpus decided. The retention record decides one snapshot, one allocation, one swap (`dec:retention:monolithic-snapshot`) and names the componentised design in its own discussion as the alternative it rejected, for a stated reason — a reader holding two parts that were never simultaneously true (`disc:retention:split-swap-alternative`). The concurrency record decides the same thing from the other side and states the division of authority that settles which governs: the specification defines the snapshot and states the guarantees, and the record decides the arrangement.

The consequence for this audit is why it is recorded first. An audit trusting the paragraph would have reported this cluster's most central function as a large performance divergence — every publication copying the whole dimension map and all three parameter sets, at the nine megabytes the code's own documentation names for the reference dimension, on a path the corpus says never copies lifecycle-stable state. It would have been a false finding of the first magnitude, against the one function in the cluster that is exactly right. This is the sixth instance of the class the feature tranche opened and the health tranche hardened, and it is the first in which the checkable sentence was wrong not about a status but about the design itself, and the first in which the corpus's two halves state incompatible arrangements. The rule should harden once more accordingly: where a specification chapter describes an arrangement rather than a requirement, the record that owns arrangements is the authority, and the chapter's description is evidence to be checked against it.

The second is the retention record's own, and it contradicts the specification it cites, in the direction that decides whether a host learns it has lost a label. (`cav:retention:evicted-label`) states that a label submitted after its entry has been evicted "has nothing to attach to and is lost: the submission does not fail, and the model does not learn from it", and adds that the specification "states this rather than promising otherwise". The specification states the opposite of the first clause: the caveat it names says that for an outcome reported against an evicted assessment "the call fails and the evidence is gone" (`cav:limitation:buffer-eviction`), and the label pipeline chapter says the same (`req:runtime:label-interface`). The code fails the call (`src/api/label.rs:109`), so the code agrees with the specification and the record disagrees with both. It is recorded rather than dispositioned because a record misdescribing the specification is a prose defect, and it is recorded with the first because the two together are the fifth and sixth instances of a corpus sentence reporting what another part of the corpus says being wrong — a class that has now been met between a chapter and the code, between a table and its own rows, between two records, and here between a record and the chapter it cites.

The third is smaller and is the configuration reference's. The publication-interval table closes by saying that its row "describes a surface the package does not offer", which is exact and is the concession that row deserves. The pending-buffer table immediately below it closes by saying that "the storage precision ships as tabulated" while its own third row tabulates the precision as a settable parameter, and no such parameter exists. So the same page applies the right concession to one row and the wrong assurance to another two tables later, which is argued at (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-storage-precision`).

## Tenth tranche: the public surface and label guidance · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-api-guidance`

The clusters are `src/api/` — the nine modules behind every call a host makes, from the builder that constructs an instance through assessment, labelling, reporting, the six lifecycle methods, pre-seeding and the three health queries — and `src/guidance/`, the scoring and selection behind the fourth of those calls. They were taken together because one record owns all four call surfaces and because the second cluster is reached only through the first: the public guidance method is three lines that delegate, so auditing either alone would have split one surface across two waves.

They were also taken because more items terminate here than in any cluster the campaign has met. Two of the three surfaced constants the deferral pass pinned are exported from this cluster; the buffer's health, the pending buffer's only read path and the journal context's only producer are all here; and the label surface is where the boundary order two records decide is either kept or broken. Every one of those is cross-cited below rather than re-registered.

The scale of what these two clusters move is the reason the census question was asked hardest here. Between them they touch every other cluster in the package, and the third and fourth forms of the question — does anything ever put anything into it, and does anything ever take anything out — returned this wave's second and third findings.

**Remark (The `api/` row names a chapter that does not govern the cluster, and the `guidance/` row names a record that is not corpus)** · `rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`

The scope map gives `api/` the surface and assessment-contract records and *the interfaces appendix*, and `guidance/` the label-guidance-api record and *the guidance landscape chapter* (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`). The tranche read its own rows before trusting them, as the register has now asked three times, and neither survives unamended. The two fail in different columns, which between them completes the demonstration the ninth tranche began: neither column of the map can be assumed checked.

The `api/` row is wrong on the specification side in the strong sense three tranches have now found. The interfaces appendix is the feed-forward boundary inventory: it enumerates what the Core consumes from a Sentinel, what it declines to consume, what crosses between a Sentinel and its graph, a symbol correspondence, and a six-row verification table over the system's boundaries (`app:spec:interface-map`). Not one of its rows concerns a call a host makes. The words *assess*, *label*, *builder* and *guidance* do not occur in it, and the one pointer it carries into this cluster's subject is a citation to report reception in a sentence about batch reports. A tranche reading the named chapter would have found nothing to measure against.

What governs `api/` on the specification side is the **host contract** chapter, which fixes what a host declares at construction (`req:host:construction`), the five fields a label carries (`tab:host:label-reporting`) and the pre-seeding path (`alg:host:pre-seeding`); the **label pipeline** chapter, which states the label interface and its error (`req:runtime:label-interface`); the **assessment operations** chapter for the assessment interface (`req:runtime:assessment-interface`); and the **concurrency** chapter, which carries the non-blocking invariant (`inv:guarantee:non-blocking`), the pending buffer's concurrency requirement (`req:publication:pending-buffer`), the label queue (`req:publication:label-queue`) and report reception (`req:publication:report-reception`). On the record side the row is right about surface and adds nothing about **construction**, which owns the builder and all six lifecycle methods and is cited by name in four of them; **durability**, which owns the boundary order the label method implements; **degradation**, which owns the error partition every one of this cluster's error types answers to; and **ordering**, which owns the batch timestamp at the assessment method's own head. Five records govern this cluster and the map names two.

The `guidance/` row is right about the chapter and wrong in a way the map has not been caught in before: its record column names a record that is not corpus. The label-guidance-api record is one of the three held out of this campaign, and a legacy record is not corpus in the sense (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) means. This tranche therefore audited `guidance/` against the specification alone — the guidance landscape chapter, which does govern it and governs it well, the surface record, which decides the four call surfaces including this one, and the configuration reference — and says so here, as the map's own preamble requires of a cluster in that position. The consequence is not a shortage of authority: the guidance chapter and the surface record between them state twelve checkable properties of this cluster, which is more than most rewritten records state of theirs.

That makes five of the map's rows now known wrong on the specification side and two known wanting on the record side, found by four tranches. The audit does not amend the map.

**Table (What the two clusters conform to)** · `tab:assayer:api-guidance-conformant`

Recorded first, because an audit that lists only its findings misrepresents what it read. Thirty rows were checked across the two clusters and hold, and the table is the whole of that set rather than a selection from it.

| Checked | Authority | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The assessment call takes a batch, returns one result per request positionally, and carries no error channel | (`dec:surface:batch-infallible`) | `src/api/assess.rs:33` |
| An empty batch returns an empty result rather than a complaint, with no snapshot load | (`dec:surface:batch-infallible`) | `src/api/assess.rs:36` |
| One monotonic reading is taken at the batch's head and shared across every request in it | (`dec:ordering:batch-timestamp`) | `src/api/assess.rs:44` |
| The snapshot is loaded per request, so a publication landing mid-batch is visible to the remainder | (`dec:concurrency:per-request-load`) | `src/api/assess.rs:53` |
| The assessment method performs no persistence work of any kind | (`dec:durability:assess-no-persistence`) | `src/api/assess.rs:48`, by absence |
| Degradation counted from the result is a distinct thing from the on-demand health query | (`dec:surface:inline-health`) | `src/api/assess.rs:60` against `src/api/health.rs:38` |
| The pending entry is removed at the boundary, before any journalling and irreversibly | (`dec:surface:consume-at-boundary`) | `src/api/label.rs:106`, against `src/api/label.rs:133` |
| A label naming an assessment the buffer no longer holds errors rather than acknowledging | (`req:runtime:label-interface`) | `src/api/label.rs:109` |
| A submission is sanitised before it is journalled, never after | (`conv:durability:sanitise-before-journal`) | `src/api/label.rs:112`, journalled at `src/api/label.rs:133` |
| The journal append and the channel hand-off happen inside one mutex, so journal order is channel order | (`cor:durability:replay-exactness`) | `src/api/label.rs:134`, released at `src/api/label.rs:145` |
| Non-finite values are repaired at the boundary rather than rejected, the valence to zero and a broken axis dropped alone | (`dec:surface:sanitise-not-reject`) | `src/api/label.rs:60`, `src/api/label.rs:65` |
| The five reported fields are the five the host contract tabulates, carried as one payload | (`tab:host:label-reporting`) | `src/api/label.rs:102` |
| Submission returns as soon as the label is accepted, acknowledging the boundary and not the model | (`dec:surface:async-label`) | `src/api/label.rs:148` |
| There is no idempotent resubmission, no batch variant and no completion variant | (`dec:surface:no-variants`) | `src/api/label.rs:102`, by shape |
| A full label queue returns an error to the submitter rather than absorbing the label silently | (`dec:concurrency:no-silent-drop`) | `src/api/label.rs:162`, `src/api/label.rs:149` |
| Every error the label surface can return is a structural failure, and no data-quality problem is one | (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) | `src/api/label.rs:109`, `src/api/label.rs:135`, `src/api/label.rs:162` |
| Reception returns what the report cost — cells carried, created, deleted and degraded — rather than a bare success | (`dec:surface:diagnostic-ack`) | `src/api/report.rs:38`, filled at `src/api/report.rs:100` |
| The reception method reads and writes no model, graph, cache or calibration state of its own | (`dec:surface:reception-isolation`) | `src/api/report.rs:86`, by delegation |
| The signal schema is required and its omission is an error rather than a default | (`dec:construction:eager-validation`) | `src/api/builder.rs:125` |
| The builder accepts Core configuration and has no place to put derivation or Companion configuration | (`dec:construction:core-only-config`) | `src/api/builder.rs:48`, by absence |
| Lifecycle is six operations over eight calls, each taking a shared receiver and each returning a result | (`dec:construction:six-methods`) | `src/api/lifecycle.rs`, the eight public methods |
| Infrastructure becomes visible synchronously and the model extension follows asynchronously | (`dec:construction:two-phase-visibility`) | `src/api/lifecycle.rs:79`, then `src/api/lifecycle.rs:89` |
| Identity deregistration waits on the steward, and only then removes the infrastructure and releases the maintenance state | (`dec:construction:blocking-deregistration`) | `src/api/lifecycle.rs:414`, `src/api/lifecycle.rs:422`, `src/api/lifecycle.rs:431` |
| Pre-seeding is a post-construction call that blocks until the steward has processed every entry | (`dec:construction:post-seeding`) | `src/api/preseed.rs:98`, blocking at `src/api/preseed.rs:116` |
| Pre-seed labels travel the ordinary label path rather than a shortcut | (`alg:host:pre-seeding`) | `src/api/preseed.rs:111` |
| Buffer health carries capacity and utilisation and nothing else, which is what the deferral records | (`entry:retention:eviction-visibility`) | `src/api/health.rs:271` |
| Guidance answers a budget and a parameter set with three ranked lists, without an error channel, and sends nothing to the steward | (`dec:surface:read-only-guidance`) | `src/api/guidance.rs:25`, `src/guidance/mod.rs:167` |
| The candidate carries the assessment identifier, the entity key, a timestamp, its score and its cross-membership | (`sig:guidance:interface`) | `src/guidance/mod.rs:130` |
| The three scores are the three the chapter defines: stored uncertainty, the anchor's blend weight, and the worst cell's divergence times its label shortfall, each factor clipped below at zero | (`def:guidance:risk-informative`), (`def:guidance:investigation`), (`def:guidance:starvation`) | `src/guidance/mod.rs:230`, `src/guidance/mod.rs:236`, `src/guidance/mod.rs:283`, `src/guidance/mod.rs:332` |
| The Ledger read decays to the present as a pure function and stores nothing | (`inv:publication:ledger-concurrency`) | `src/guidance/mod.rs:325` |
| Each list is sorted and truncated on its own score and budget, and a candidate in two categories appears in both naming the other and never itself | (`dec:guidance:no-deduplication`) | `src/guidance/mod.rs:350`, `src/guidance/mod.rs:445` |

Four of these deserve a sentence rather than a line, and three of the four are cases where reading one half of the corpus alone would have produced a finding against a function that is right.

The label-queue row is the largest of those and is argued at (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-prose-defects`), because the concurrency chapter requires the opposite of what the code does and the concurrency record decides what the code does. It is recorded as conformance on the division of authority the ninth tranche established from the corpus's own statement of it, and not as a spec-stale verdict, which it is not and does not claim to be.

The per-request snapshot load is the second. The assessment chapter's pipeline and the snapshot definition both say the published state is acquired "once, at the start of the call", and the method loads inside the per-request closure. What settles it is that the same chapter's interface requirement says each request sees "whatever state was current when it ran", and that the concurrency record decides the per-request load in terms and names the consequence — a publication landing mid-batch is visible to the remainder. The reading that makes the corpus consistent is that the call is the assessment and not the batch, and on that reading the code is exact.

The boundary-order row is recorded for the half the records agree on. That the entry is consumed before any journalling holds exactly, and it is the half both the surface record and the durability record's corollary depend on. The other half of the surface record's sentence — the position of sanitisation relative to consumption — is not the code's order and is tabled below.

And the read-time Ledger decay is recorded because the ordering record's rejected alternative reads like a prohibition of it. That record rejects lazy per-reader decay for the label path and concentrates it at the head (`dec:ordering:decay-at-head`); the concurrency chapter *requires* read-time decay of Ledger entries, on every reading path, for the reason that the alternative would turn every reader into a writer. The two are about different state and the guidance path implements the second. The check came back negative and is recorded as one.

**Entry (Pre-seeding synthesises no feature vector, so every seeded label trains on a bias and nothing else)** · `entry:assayer:wl-preseed-degenerate-vector`

**Locus** `src/api/preseed.rs:171` through `src/api/preseed.rs:174`, where the synthetic pending entry is built with an empty extraction map, empty identity coordinates, empty identity active cells and an empty signal vector. No labelled notice stands there, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:host:pre-seeding`), which states the two admissible arrangements and what the second costs: either the buffer is pre-seeded in step with real entries, or "the implementation offers a simplified path that synthesises a feature vector from the historical record — which in turn requires the signal schema and the Sentinel registry to be declared first". The code takes the second and synthesises nothing. The dependency the sentence names is the tell: the schema and the registry are prerequisites of a synthesis that reads them, and this helper is a free function that reads neither and takes nothing but the entry.

**What the emptiness costs, by recomputation against the reconstruction algorithm.** The label path rebuilds the training vector from the stored components, and what ships of that rebuild is "the bias, the frozen signal features and the frozen per-Sentinel slots and nothing else" (`alg:runtime:reconstruction`), which is the founding worklist's (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`) stated at its own algorithm. Of those three, the pre-seed entry supplies neither the signal features nor the extractions. Every position of every pre-seeded training vector is therefore zero but the bias, and the four blocks that stand at zero for an ordinary label stand at zero here too. A pre-seeded label moves the intercept, the two class-rate trackers, the valence compression scale and the calibration buffer, and moves no weight over any feature.

**The compounding that settles the severity, and it runs in the wrong direction.** Step fifteen of the label path updates the standardisation statistics from the vector it just trained on (`alg:standardisation:label-time-procedure`), so a batch of historical labels does not merely fail to teach the models: it drags the running means and variances of every position toward zero and unit, against a distribution no request will ever present. The corpus's own case for pre-seeding is that it "seats the importance-weight trackers at a real base rate instead of an assumed one" and "can carry a deployment through the first warm-up stages before a single live request arrives" (`alg:host:pre-seeding`), and by recomputation the first survives and the second does not — the trackers and the intercept are exactly the quantities that do move, and the warm-up stages the corpus names are the standardisation and dimension-convergence stages that the empty vector sets back.

**Severity** high. It is silent, it fires on the one operation the corpus recommends performing before traffic, and it corrupts learned state rather than a report. It is argued above the medium that a merely-ineffective seeding would deserve, because the emptiness is not neutral: a hundred historical labels at the reference configuration is exactly the batch-initialisation sample count (`tab:config:standardisation`), so a deployment following the corpus's advice can complete its standardisation batch initialisation entirely on synthetic zeros and publish those as its empirical statistics.

**The disclosure is self-refuting rather than absent.** The entry type's own documentation says "Sentinel features are zero (no reports at pre-seed time). Identity and signal features are computed from the current state" (`src/api/preseed.rs:36`); the helper that builds the entry says "Sentinel features are zero. Identity/signal features are empty" (`src/api/preseed.rs:163`). Two doc comments in one file, a hundred and twenty lines apart, give the same two blocks opposite contents, and the first is the one a host reads because it is on the public type. This is the shape the persistence tranche recorded inside one comment block (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`), met here across a module, and it is worse in one respect: there the two statements were adjacent and a reader met both, and here the false one stands on the public surface and the true one is private.

**What conformance would mean** the synthetic entry is built against the declared schema and the registered Sentinels — encoding the entry's own key on each identity dimension and filling an occupied, zero-featured slot per registered Sentinel, which is what source four of the reconstruction asks for of a Sentinel that was active and silent — or the corpus's first arrangement is taken instead and the host pre-seeds the buffer with real entries. The public type's documentation stops asserting a computation that does not happen either way.

**Coverage** none, and the suite argues the other way at three sites. `pre_seed_before_sentinel_registration` carries the claim that "the entry lands against zero Sentinel features", which pins one half of the emptiness as the expected behaviour and gives the corpus's own argument for it. `pre_seed_then_assess_reflects_learning` is the strongest test the surface has and it cannot see the defect: it seeds two engines with opposite valences and asserts that they assess the same entity differently, which holds because the intercepts differ and would hold identically if every feature in the package were removed. And `pre_seed_then_register_sentinel_then_assess` claims that "history loaded before a Sentinel is registered is still there when the assessment runs" — the history that is still there is a base rate.

**Re-verified at the crate glue** (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-glue`), which was asked whether the entry has an assessment-path half and reports that it does not. Nothing on the `assess_single` path reads or writes a pre-seeded vector; the pre-seeding helper and the assessment pipeline meet only at the pending buffer, which each writes independently. The entry is wholly the tenth tranche's and is not enlarged. **Falsifier** a read of a pre-seeded entry from `src/assessment.rs`, of which there is none.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `db19a802`, by the conformance line's first arrangement. The synthetic entry is built against the declared schema and the registered Sentinels, as the corpus's simplified path requires of a synthesis that reads them: each registered Sentinel holds an occupied, zero-featured slot — active and silent, what reconstruction source four reads — the entry's own key is encoded on every registered identity dimension with the current competitive state frozen beside it, and the signal block is encoded from the schema at the entity's cached persistent values. The self-refuting disclosure ends both ways: the public type's documentation states the computation that now happens, and the private helper that contradicted it is deleted for a synthesis beside the assessment path it mirrors. A pre-seeded label therefore trains the occupancy and identity structure it describes, and no longer completes a standardisation batch initialisation on synthetic zeros. With a declared signal, a registered Sentinel and a registered identity dimension, the synthesised entry carries every block the audit measured empty (`test:crate:pre-seed-entry-synthesised-against-schema-and-registry`).

**Entry (The batch initialisation's completion is discarded on a full channel and its accumulator is released anyway)** · `entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:1026`, where the completion command's send result is discarded, against `src/lib.rs:1032`, where the accumulator is deactivated unconditionally three lines later. No labelled notice stands there. The site was routed to this tranche as glue adjacent to the public surface, and it is the one `src/lib.rs` site this tranche adjudicates; the rest of that file's glue stays routed.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:standardisation:batch-initialisation`), whose third step is a conjunction: "**Apply** the empirical mean and variance through the model owner's command channel, and release the accumulator". The code performs the second half unconditionally and the first half best-effort, so on a full command channel the statistics are computed, thrown away, and the mechanism that would compute them again is switched off in the same statement group.

The degradation record is the authority that decides this is not licensed, and it decides it by the shape of what is dropped rather than by the channel it is dropped from. A silent drop is admissible on the observation path because "unit observations are exchangeable, so a dropped one slows the competitive set's adaptation and corrupts nothing" (`alg:publication:identity-draining`), and the queue face of the failure posture turns on exactly that reasoning (`rem:degradation:queue-face`). A batch-initialisation completion is not exchangeable and there is no second one: it is a one-shot carrying the whole of a hundred assessments' accumulation, and its loss is permanent for the life of the process rather than a slowed adaptation.

**The observable sequence.** Build an instance cold. Submit assessments until the hundredth. The accumulator fills, the completion command is constructed, the command channel is full because a lifecycle batch or a checkpoint request is queued ahead of it, the send fails, the result is discarded, and the flag is cleared. From that assessment onward the accumulator is never fed again and the statistics are never applied, so the instance standardises against class priors for its whole life and nothing reports that it is doing so.

**Severity** high, and the severity is carried by what it compounds with rather than by its own likelihood. The command channel is fixed-capacity — a literal at `src/lib.rs:408` and not, as this paragraph first claimed, a parameter of (`tab:construction:parameters`) — and the window is narrow, so this is rarer than the two registered standardisation defects. What makes it high is that batch initialisation is the fastest of the three mechanisms that would repair them, and that the one mitigation a reader of the original citation would reach for is unavailable: a host cannot raise the capacity (`rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`). (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`) disables the batch initialisation on every restart and (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-lifecycle-standardisation`) publishes neutral statistics over empirical ones on every lifecycle event; the mechanism left to a deployment that has met either is this one, and this is the path on which it can be lost silently.

**What conformance would mean** the two halves of step three move together: the accumulator is released where the send succeeded, and is retained for the next assessment where it did not, so that the completion is retried rather than lost. A blocking send is the alternative and is refused for the reason the whole posture is refused — the assessment path may not wait (`inv:guarantee:non-blocking`) — which makes retention rather than blocking the conformant repair.

**Coverage** none. Nothing in the workspace fills the command channel and then crosses the batch-initialisation threshold, and the flag's clearing is not observable from any public surface.

**Re-verified at the crate glue** (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-glue`), which confirms the entry entire and withdrew a draft correction to it. The locus is exact at the current tree — the discarded send at `src/lib.rs:1026`, the clear at `:1031`, the flag store at `:1032`, the last two outside the guard. The accumulator cannot return an empty completion, which removes the only competing explanation and leaves exactly one way for the statistics to be lost. One citation is refuted: the command channel is not a parameter of (`tab:construction:parameters`) but a literal at `src/lib.rs:408`, so the mitigation the severity paragraph implies is unavailable and the severity is higher than recorded (`rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`). **Falsifier** a configuration path reaching the command channel's capacity, which `src/lib.rs:408` does not admit.

**Repaired** in wave four at `7a4bd65a`, by retention as the conformance line specified: the accumulator is released where the send succeeded and retained where it did not, so the next assessment recomputes the completion and retries, and the flag stays set until the delivery lands. The falsifier ran the observable sequence with the owner parked and the command channel filled to its literal capacity: before the repair the statistics were computed, refused, and lost with the accumulator released — the published variances held the unit priors for the life of the process — and after it the retry one assessment later carried the empirical statistics into publication.

**Entry (The label boundary sanitises and counts nothing, on the one surface of three that counts nothing)** · `entry:assayer:wl-label-sanitisation-uncounted`

**Locus** `src/api/label.rs:58`, the sanitiser, which repairs and returns and touches no counter. No labelled notice stands there, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:surface:sanitise-not-reject`), whose sentence is a conjunction and whose second conjunct is unmet: "Non-finite and out-of-range values are sanitised at the boundary **and counted**, rather than rejected", with `src/api/label.rs` as its own cited evidence. It is divergent against (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) on the same clause from the other side, which fixes that a data-quality problem "degrades the affected cell, **reports the degradation**, and lets everything else proceed". Nothing here reports it: a repaired valence and a dropped axis leave no trace on any counter, any event or any health field.

**What makes this a finding rather than a missing nicety is that the package already does it twice.** The assessment path counts its own sanitisations — signals repaired before encoding and features repaired after standardisation both accumulate into the degradation counters (`src/health/counters.rs:29`, `src/health/counters.rs:37`) and reach a host through the inline summary and the health query alike. The reception path counts its own: degraded cells are the fourth field of the acknowledgement the report surface returns (`src/api/report.rs:104`). Three boundaries apply the same posture, two of them report it, and the one that does not is the boundary on the path that trains the models.

**Severity** medium, argued against the report-only posture rather than around it, in the manner the health tranche established. What is lost is not a number a model reads — a repaired valence is zero and that is the specified repair — but the ability to distinguish a clean label stream from one in which every valence arrived as a pathology and was silently zeroed. Zero valence is a legitimate and common value, so the repair is indistinguishable from ordinary traffic by inspection of the labels, and a host whose upstream computation has started producing non-finite valences learns nothing from any surface the package offers. It is held below high because nothing is computed wrongly and because the loss is of evidence about the host's own pipeline rather than about the package's state.

**What conformance would mean** the sanitiser returns what it repaired alongside the repaired payload, and the count joins the label-integrity health the report already assembles (`src/api/health.rs:290`), where the total and eligible label counts it would sit beside are already carried.

**Coverage** worse than none in the mild sense. Four unit tests establish the repair itself, each correctly, and their four authored claims between them state the whole of the sanitiser's behaviour without any of them naming a count — so the corpus's own account of this function, written under the test documentation policy, reproduces the record's first conjunct and omits its second.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `3eeb6c38`, as the conformance line has it: the sanitiser returns what it repaired beside the repaired payload, the surface accumulates the counts, and they join the label-integrity health beside the totals it already carries. The four unit tests are restated to assert the counts their claims now name (`test:unit:sanitise-nan-valence`), and the engine falsifier reads one repaired valence and one dropped outcome off the health report — where before no surface carried either — with a clean label moving neither count (`test:crate:label-sanitisation-counts-reach-label-integrity-health`).

**Entry (The builder's template validation is a constant, and the record decides that it validates)** · `entry:assayer:wl-builder-template-validation`

**Locus** `src/api/builder.rs:177`, a function that takes a template, ignores it, and returns success. The notice standing over it is (`todo:code:validate-the-canonical`).

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:construction:eager-validation`), which names three things the builder validates before it returns an instance — "the signal schema, the interaction templates and every numerical parameter" — and argues the decision from what late validation costs. The schema clause holds exactly. The templates clause does not: the validator is a constant function, its own comment defers the check "to runtime when templates are instantiated against a concrete dimension map", and the argument the record makes is precisely against that deferral, because the runtime in question is a path that "has already promised it cannot fail, so the only remaining options there are degradation or a panic".

**Severity** medium, and it is an amplifier in the same sense (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-unchecked-extension`) is, with one difference that matters for the disposition. There the corpus stated a property and did not say who enforced it, which made the absence a gap; here the corpus names the enforcer and the enforcer is empty, which makes it a divergence. What the missing check would have caught is the campaign's largest registered feature defect: the named-pair template that expands to a quadratic block (`entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`) is a template contract violation that a template validator is the natural place to detect, and the notice standing here asks in terms for "template shape, fixed-block offsets, Sentinel-slot offsets, signal names against the declared schema" — the last of which is the one check that cannot be made anywhere else, because the schema is fixed at construction and nowhere later (`cav:construction:schema-fixity`).

**The third clause of the same decision is already registered and this entry does not absorb it.** "Every numerical parameter" is false of at least the Ledger's absence threshold, which is (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-absence-cast`), and two notices in the same pass concede two more parameters that are not reached (`src/api/builder.rs:218`, `src/api/builder.rs:273`). The three clauses are kept apart because they have different repairs and one of them already has an entry.

The thirteenth tranche measured that clause rather than bounding it below, and the shortfall is larger than "at least" suggested: of the forty-three scalar parameters the configuration module owns, the pass reaches nineteen (`entry:assayer:wl-config-unreached-constraints`). That entry carries the clause from here on.

The same tranche refutes this entry's reading of the first clause. "The schema clause holds exactly" is not sustainable: the constructor the builder calls for it refuses one thing and one thing only, a repeated name (`src/signal/schema.rs:111`), and every shape parameter in a declaration reaches the assessment path unchecked — which is (`entry:assayer:wl-signal-declaration-unvalidated`), registered by the twelfth tranche against the same builder line this entry cites as sound. The two entries were written against the same call and reached opposite conclusions about it because this one read the clause as satisfied by the presence of a validating call and that one read what the call validates. All three clauses of the decision therefore fail, and the record should not be read as conceding two.

**What conformance would mean** the validator checks what the notice enumerates, at which point the record's second clause becomes true and the notice retires.

**Coverage** none, and none is possible against a function that cannot fail. The builder suite constructs templates and builds successfully, which is what this function guarantees for every input.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `e98f0f0a`, within this wave's remit. The validator validates: every offset a template carries is checked at build time against the block that resolves it — Sentinel-slot offsets against the base extraction width, aggregate offsets against the aggregate block, context offsets against the fixed context region the cold start lays out, which grows with the declared schema — so the record's templates clause is true and the refusal names the template's index (`test:crate:builder-refuses-out-of-range-template-offsets`), (`test:crate:builder-admits-boundary-template-offsets`). The notice does not retire whole: the checks the legacy offset-based enum cannot express — signal names against the declared schema and `CompetitiveIndicator` placement — need the canonical `FeatureSelector` operand migration, which is wave seven's (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-absolute-operands`), and the notice stands trimmed to exactly that residue.

**Entry (The journal failure is documented as safe to retry and the retry cannot succeed)** · `entry:assayer:wl-journal-failure-retry-claim`

**Locus** `src/api/label.rs:93`, the error's own documentation, against `src/api/label.rs:106`, the consumption three lines of control flow earlier. No labelled notice stands there, and the item is new.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong, and the party in the wrong is the doc comment rather than the ordering. The ordering is the corpus's own and is decided deliberately: the entry is removed at the boundary "before any journalling and irreversibly", the removal is the serialisation point, and the record states the cost without hedging it — "a failure after consumption loses the label" (`dec:surface:consume-at-boundary`). The doc comment tells a host the opposite, that a journal failure is "safe to retry". A retry submits the same identifier against a buffer that no longer holds it and returns not-found, which is the one outcome the surface guarantees (`req:runtime:label-interface`). A doc comment that contradicts the corpus is a finding against the comment (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`).

**Severity** medium. Nothing computed is wrong and the label is lost either way, so the cost is entirely to the host: the documentation prescribes the one recovery action that cannot work, on the error whose whole purpose is to tell a host that its durable write failed. A host following it retries, receives not-found, and has been told two incompatible things about the same submission by the same interface. The finding is the false assurance rather than the loss, which is the shape (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-absence-cast`) records at the other end of this cluster: a comment asserting a property the code does not have is worse than the absence, because it stops the next reader looking.

**What conformance would mean** the error's documentation says what the record says — that the journal write failed and the label is gone — and the doc comment carries the record's own reason for the ordering rather than a retry instruction.
