**Entry (The depth-overflow claim states a wrap that the narrowing it names cannot produce)** · `entry:assayer:wl-ingestion-depth-wrap-arithmetic`

**Locus** `src/report/ingestion.rs:304` in the test's authored claim and `src/report/ingestion.rs:9` in the module's test index, which carry the same sentence: "the narrowing to `u8` would otherwise wrap a depth of 200 round to 72 and silently file the cell against a real but wrong region of the space".

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong, and the divergence is arithmetic rather than editorial. A `u8` holds values to 255, so the narrowing the sentence names maps 200 to 200 and not to 72. Seventy-two is 200 reduced modulo 128, which is the operation the sentence's picture requires and which nothing in the code performs: `depth_to_u8_clamped` (`src/report/validation.rs:299`) branches on `depth > 128` before any cast, and the two functions a surviving out-of-range depth would reach both guard it rather than reducing it — `dyadic_ancestor_lo` returns the coordinate unchanged at `src/types.rs:504` and `is_dyadic` compares endpoints for equality at `src/types.rs:551`.

**Severity** low in consequence and worth registering anyway. The guard is correct, the test is correct, and the shipped behaviour is exactly what the claim says it should be; what is wrong is the falsifier the claim offers a reader for checking it. Under the testing-documentation policy a claim earns its place by stating the failure the test rules out, and a claim whose stated failure is arithmetically impossible cannot be checked by the reader it was written for. The class is new to this campaign: not a test without an oracle, but an oracle whose authored justification miscomputes.

**What conformance would mean** the sentence names the real hazard, which is that a depth past 128 is not a depth in this hierarchy at all and would file the cell against a region the dyadic scheme does not contain, rather than naming a modular wrap the types do not perform.

**Coverage** the behaviour is covered by `make_ledger_key_overflow` and the coverage is half-conditional: the assertions on the clamped value at `src/report/ingestion.rs:314` are reachable only in a release build, and the `should_panic` expectation is reachable only in a debug one. This is the debug-oracle class (`entry:assayer:wl-debug-oracle-release-reachable`) and is registered there rather than duplicated here.

**Entry (Every test in the validation module pins a function in another module)** · `entry:assayer:wl-validation-tests-pin-elsewhere`

**Locus** `src/report/validation.rs:308` and the three tests below it — `dyadic_full_range`, `dyadic_half_range` and `non_dyadic_misaligned` — together with the module's test index at `src/report/validation.rs:4`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against the testing plan's homing rule. All three tests call `is_dyadic`, which is `crate::types`' function and is audited with `types.rs`; not one of them calls `validate_structural`, `validate_and_extract_cell`, `validate_and_extract_coordination`, `extract_axis`, `extract_all_axes` or `depth_to_u8_clamped`, which are the six functions this module defines. The module's index table advertises three tests under the dossier area at the head of a file whose subject is report validation, and a reader taking the table at its word would conclude the validation surface is covered when what is covered is a geometry predicate it imports.

**Severity** medium. The uncovered surface is not idle: `validate_structural` is the sole realisation of (`pre:architecture:report-validation`) and the extraction functions carry the whole sanitisation posture for incoming reports. The mitigation is that the surface is covered elsewhere — `src/tests/ingestion.rs` exercises `validate_structural` and `validate_and_extract_cell` through the pipeline — so the repair is a re-homing and a corrected index rather than new coverage, and the register's count is what is wrong rather than the coverage itself. This is the mis-aimed-oracle class the twelfth tranche opened (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-observation-channel-unused`) in its second form: there the oracle was pointed at an artefact nothing runs, here it is pointed at a neighbouring module that another tranche already counts.

**What conformance would mean** the three tests move to the module that owns `is_dyadic`, and the validation module's index either stands empty or carries tests of the six functions it defines.

**Coverage** not applicable; the entry is about coverage.

**Entry (The degraded flag is documented as reporting every sanitisation and does not report the depth clamp)** · `entry:assayer:wl-report-degraded-omits-depth`

**Locus** `src/report/validation.rs:213`, whose returns clause promises "A `degraded` flag indicating if any value was sanitised", against `src/report/validation.rs:230`, where the depth is clamped without touching the flag.

**Disposition** unspecified rather than divergent, and the distinction is the one (`conv:assayer:audit-dispositions`) calls the hard one. The corpus places well-formed reports among the properties the Core "assumes and does not check", and says of a report violating one that "the Core's behaviour on such a report is unspecified" (`tab:architecture:sentinel-properties`). A depth past 128 is such a violation, so no corpus text requires the flag to rise for it, and the promise that is broken is the function's own rustdoc — which is not corpus (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`).

The same reasoning disposes the whole sanitisation surface of this module and is recorded once here. The zeroing of a non-finite axis, the negative-variance rejection, the noise-influence clamp and the energy-ratio substitution are all behaviour on reports outside the assumed contract, and all are therefore unspecified rather than conformant. It is worth saying plainly because the code reads as though it were realising a specification and is not: `degraded_cells` flows into the internal acknowledgement and out to health, so a number the corpus never asked for is published.

**Severity** low. The consequence is a diagnostic that undercounts by one class of sanitisation, on an input the contract already excludes.

**What conformance would mean** either the clamp sets the flag, so the rustdoc becomes true, or the rustdoc names the three sanitisations it actually reports.

**Coverage** none; no test asserts the flag's value for a clamped depth.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `0376e3fc`, by the conformance line's first option: the clamp sets the flag, so the rustdoc's promise becomes true, and the coordination extraction reports the same clamp the same way. The debug assertion inside the clamp went with it — an out-of-contract report is data-quality input this module sanitises and reports in every build profile, never a crash in one of them — and the two tests that pinned the assert-or-clamp split are restated to the uniform clamp. The falsifier reads the flag on a depth of two hundred and its absence on the boundary one-twenty-eight (`test:crate:quality-depth-over-128`).

**Entry (The configuration chapter's self-count disagrees with the chapter's own closing sentences)** · `entry:assayer:wl-config-chapter-self-count`

**Locus** `docs/spec/reference-configuration.md:27` — "Fifty-nine of these defaults were read against the code that implements them and fifty-three matched. Six did not, and each of the six is named where it lives: three are wrong values in tables that otherwise check out, and three are parameters tabulated here that the package exposes nowhere at all."

**Disposition** divergent-spec-stale, argued from the chapter's own arrangement. The Part's opening fixes where authority lies: a departure "is named in the closing sentence of the environment that tabulates it, at the value's own site rather than in a list of errata" (`part:spec:reference`). The closing sentences are therefore the record and the summary is a count of it, so where they disagree the summary is the party to correct. They disagree twice. Six closing sentences name a wrong value, not three — the pending buffer's capacity and its expiry horizon, the chain-length normaliser, the drift noise allowance, the minimum class count and the recent discrimination window. And the parameters exposed nowhere are not three: the publication interval is one, and the monitoring table's own closing sentence adds thirteen more in one clause, "The other thirteen have no configuration surface anywhere".

**Severity** low, and registered for a reason beyond tidiness. The summary is the sentence a reader trusts when deciding how much of the chapter to re-derive, and it currently reports the chapter as healthier than the chapter reports itself. The chapter says in the next breath that "the check is entirely mechanical" and that the hand count is not repeatable, which this entry is the evidence for.

**What conformance would mean** the summary's four numbers are recomputed from the closing sentences, or the paragraph gives way to the tool the chapter already says it needs.

**Coverage** none possible.

**Remark (What the cluster got right, and the checks that were run to find out)** · `rem:assayer:report-config-clean`

Six checks that a reading might have expected to fail did not, and the audit records them because a clean check that is not written down is a check that will be run again.

The structural validation matches its precondition exactly. The three enforced properties (`pre:architecture:report-validation`) are the root cell, the dyadic interval and the non-duplicate, and all three hold — but the first holds by a composition worth stating, because the code's own rustdoc misdescribes it. The root check at `src/report/validation.rs:126` tests only that some cell claims depth zero, and the precondition demands a root "covering the full domain". What enforces the full domain is the dyadic check: `is_dyadic` at `src/types.rs:548` returns true at depth zero only for the interval from zero to the maximum coordinate. The precondition's "exactly one" is enforced likewise, by the duplicate-key check rejecting a second cell at the same position and depth. The rustdoc at `src/report/validation.rs:46` attributes both to the root check.

The duplicate check spans both cell collections. It was checked because the opposite would have been a serious defect: index construction inserts competitive and ancestor cells into one map at `src/report/ingestion.rs:222` and `:235`, and a key collision across the two would silently overwrite an entry while `cells_ingested` counted the survivor and `degraded_cells` counted both. One `seen_keys` set is threaded through both loops at `src/report/validation.rs:125` and `:133`, so the collision is rejected before construction runs and the two counters cannot disagree.

The key and the depth are computed by one function. `make_ledger_key` and `validate_and_extract_cell` both reduce the reported depth through `depth_to_u8_clamped`, so the `max_depth` accumulated at `src/report/ingestion.rs:221` cannot exceed the depths the keys were built at, and the depth-walk in `route` cannot skip a populated level.

The Ledger's absence threshold is wired. It was checked as a candidate inert knob because `IngestionConfig` is not a field of `AssayerConfig` and carries its own default of three. It is reached: `src/api/report.rs:83` reads `config.ledger.n_absent` and hands it to the pipeline. The knob is live and the defect on it is the unvalidated narrowing, not inertness.

The numerics' delegated clamps hold. `decay_factor_since` and `decay_factor_elapsed` document guarantees they do not themselves implement — backward clock jumps returning unity and enormous gaps clamped — and both delegate to functions that provide them: `hours_since` returns zero for a non-positive delta and clamps at `src/types.rs:358` and `:367`, and `duration_to_hours` clamps at `src/types.rs:578`. The debug assertion in `decay_factor` that these feed cannot fire from either path.

Report staleness reads the clock the stamp was written by. The ingestion comment at `src/report/ingestion.rs:129` claims the arrival stamp and the assessment's batch reading "come from the same injected clock", and they do: `src/report/ingestion.rs:133` and `src/api/assess.rs:44` both call `now_monotonic` on the injected handle. The claim is not overstated for the production path. It is overstated for one constructor — `ReportIndex::from_components` at `src/report/index.rs:219` stamps `Instant::now()` directly — and that constructor's own rustdoc declares itself a test convenience and points callers at the threaded variant, so the exception is disclosed where it lives.

**Remark (The checkpoint architecture has no home in the corpus, and the report path is a sanitisation point outside it)** · `rem:assayer:report-config-checkpoints`

The tranche was asked to disposition `numerics.rs` against the NaN checkpoint architecture and reports first that the architecture is not where it was said to be. There is no `degradation.md` under `docs/`. The vocabulary survives entirely in source: `src/health/degradation.rs:3` fixes the counter set as "checkpoints CP1–CP4", `src/error.rs:430` and `:458` name CP5 and CP6 as the model-state spot checks, and `src/owner/label_path.rs:917` and `:958` are where those two run. No document in `docs/spec/` or `docs/layers/` contains the token at all. The architecture is therefore code that describes itself, and the retirement of the record that held it did not rehome it.

Against that, `numerics.rs` enforces no checkpoint, and this is the correct disposition rather than a gap. Every function in the file propagates a non-finite input: `stable_sigmoid` returns it, `decay_factor` raises to it, and `stable_logit` and `stable_atanh` debug-assert their domains and clamp or compute in release. The file is the shared arithmetic the checkpoints are computed *with*, not a checkpoint; the six sit at the signal boundary, the Sentinel occupancy, the standardised feature, the model fallback, and the two model-state guards, and none of those is here.

What the census does show is a seventh sanitisation point that the vocabulary does not name. `validate_and_extract_cell` zeroes a non-finite axis, rejects a negative baseline variance, clamps the noise influence and substitutes for a non-finite energy ratio (`src/report/validation.rs:213`), counts the result into `degraded_cells`, and publishes that count through the internal acknowledgement to health. It is a sanitisation checkpoint by every property except the name, and it is absent from the CP series. The consequence is small and specific: a reader auditing where the system replaces a number cannot find this one by following the vocabulary, which is how this tranche nearly missed it.

The one parameter `numerics.rs` shares with the configuration surface is conformant and was checked because it looked otherwise. `regime_transition` holds the transition steepness as a function-local constant at `src/numerics.rs:343` and the boundary at `:345`, while (`tab:config:calibration`) tabulates the steepness with a default of twenty and a constraint of positive — which reads as a configurable parameter shipped as a constant. The table's closing sentence settles it against the finding: the steepness "lives as a constant of the numerics rather than as a field of the calibration configuration, which means it ships at the specified value and is not settable". The corpus names the arrangement and accepts it, the value matches the regime definition (`def:platt:regimes`), and the boundary of three tenths is stated in that definition's prose rather than tabulated at all.

## Fourteenth tranche: the crate glue · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-glue`

The cluster is the five files at the package root that no directory tranche owned: `src/lib.rs`, the engine type and its construction, shutdown and shared-state implementation; `src/assessment.rs`, the assessment pipeline and the output types it returns; `src/error.rs`, the public error enums and the non-finite spot-check helpers; `src/types.rs`, the identifier newtypes, the persistent timestamp and the dyadic utilities; and `src/serde_codec.rs`, the encode-and-decode adapter the persistence paths share. `src/numerics.rs` is excluded: the thirteenth tranche took it, and the residual set is derived from the source tree rather than from the brief that named four files.

This is the fan-in cluster. Every other cluster reaches the host through it, and the consequence for the method is that findings here are rarely about a computation and usually about a promise: which surface is reachable, which write is enumerated, which call cannot fail. The tranche read the degradation record for the failure posture, the surface record for the four call surfaces and what none of them will show, the clock record — which names `src/types.rs` in three of its own decisions — the ordering record for the batch timestamp and the shape of the assessment function, the concurrency record for the label queue's exception, the construction record's parameter table, and from the specification the assessment operations chapter whole, its pipeline algorithm, its enumerated-write invariant and its two output schemas, together with the companion chapter for the challenge prior and the feature chapter for the batch initialisation.

**Remark (The map's rows were checked; its absences were not)** · `rem:assayer:glue-scope-map`

Six rows have been found wrong on the specification side and five on the record side, and every one of those corrections came from a tranche reading its own row. That method cannot find what this tranche found: the map had no row for this cluster at all. Five files, some three and a half thousand lines of production code excluding the tests they carry, the crate's entire public surface and the one function the specification writes a nine-step algorithm for, and no entry in the table the register performs its completeness check against (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`). The audit could have reached (`goal:assayer:audit-complete`) with the fan-in cluster unread and the check would have reported success, because a check that asks whether every row appears cannot ask whether every file has a row.

The row is added, and what it should carry is not obvious from any single source: the glue answers to six records and three specification chapters, which is more governing text than any other row names. Whoever rebuilds the map should derive its rows by enumerating `src/` and should treat the two columns as unverified even for rows a tranche has already amended, because an amended row is evidence about that row and about nothing else.

**Entry (Thirteen lock acquisitions panic on the one call the corpus says cannot fail)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-poisoned-lock-panics`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:885`, `:890`, `:895`, `:903`, `:911`, `:927`, `:941`, `:951` and `:967`, each `self.identity_dimensions.read().expect("identity_dimensions lock poisoned")`; `src/lib.rs:930`, `:944` and `:972`, each `mutex.lock().expect("cell state lock poisoned")`; and `src/assessment.rs:834`, `arc.read().expect("sentinel ledger poisoned")`. All thirteen are inside methods of the shared-state trait that `src/assessment.rs:793` calls, on the path from `Assayer::assess`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:degradation:infallible-core`), which states that the assessment call "returns an assessment, always — not a result that may be an error, and not an assessment carrying a sentinel value meaning failure", and against (`dec:degradation:retain-and-flag`), which requires that state which cannot be read as intended be "left at the best available value and flagged, rather than the call being failed". A panic is neither: it fails the call in the one manner the posture does not contemplate, by not returning at all, and it flags nothing because the degradation record's whole mechanism is in-band travel (`cor:degradation:in-band-travel`). The partition (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) does not rescue it either — a poisoned lock is not "a caller supplying something the contract does not admit"; it is the residue of some other thread's failure, which is the load-and-data side of the line and is required to degrade.

The audit did not have to construct the repair. The package already contains it, in the module the assessment path calls three lines earlier: `src/signal/cache.rs:166` acquires the same kind of lock as `self.cache.lock().unwrap_or_else(std::sync::PoisonError::into_inner)` under a comment that names the error-model record and then states the reason in five words: "assessment path never panics". One cluster read the posture and recovered; the glue read it and panicked, in thirteen places, over the same call. Where a record governs two things because they raise the same question the two implementations are each other's oracle, which is the rule the twelfth tranche drew, and it applies here between a cluster and the glue rather than between two clusters.

**Severity** high. It is the highest available, because the property destroyed is the one the corpus spends a whole record establishing and three other records cite as a premise. A host that has built on an infallible assessment has no catch site on the request path; the first poisoned identity lock takes down the request thread, and because the lock stays poisoned every subsequent assessment takes down its own.

**What conformance would mean** each of the thirteen recovers the guard through `PoisonError::into_inner`, as `src/signal/cache.rs:166` does. Nothing else is required: the state behind a poisoned lock is exactly (`dec:degradation:retain-and-flag`)'s "last state known good", so reading it is the conformant act rather than a concession. Whether the recovery should also raise a degradation counter is a second question and a smaller one, and it is the subject of (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-unflagged-degradations`).

**Coverage** none. No test in the package poisons a lock and then assesses. The absence is invisible by name — the identity and assessment suites are large — because a poisoning test has to be written on purpose and nothing prompts it.

**Repaired** in wave one at `ec1bc101`. The written-on-purpose test the coverage line describes was written: it poisons the identity-dimension map and a Sentinel's outcome ledger and then assesses, and before the repair it panicked at the first of the thirteen loci and, with its identity half disabled, at the last.

**Entry (The assessment path writes a fourth piece of shared state, and it is the only one that blocks)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-signal-cache-write`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:969`, `shared.signal_cache_get_and_merge(&req.entity, &req.signals)`, routed through `src/lib.rs:997` into `src/signal/cache.rs:151`, which takes `self.cache.lock()` at `:166` and calls `cache.put(...)` at `:176` and again in the miss branch below it, and increments three atomic counters besides.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`inv:runtime:enumerated-writes`). The invariant's opening sentence is "The assessment path writes exactly three pieces of shared state and nothing else", its table names those three — per-dimension measurement state, the concordance window, the pending entry — and its prose closes the set twice, once as "Nothing else moves" and once by enumerating the only permitted additions: "Two accumulators are additional structural writes on this path while they are active, at batch initialisation and per-Sentinel bootstrap, and the deferred identity observation is an enqueue rather than a graph mutation". The signal cache is in neither the table nor the exception, and the write is a genuine mutation of a shared, bounded, evicting structure rather than an enqueue.

What makes this more than an accounting gap is what the invariant is for. Its own closing paragraph says it "is what makes the concurrency chapter's non-blocking claim checkable rather than aspirational: a reader can enumerate three writes and confirm each is behind a bounded or structural budget". The unenumerated write is the only blocking one in the set. The two conceded accumulators take `try_lock` and skip on contention; the pending insertion goes into a concurrent map; the concordance window is a sharded accumulator. The signal cache takes a plain `Mutex::lock`, on every assessment that supplies an entity-persistent signal, and holds it across a clone of the feature vector. A reader performing exactly the confirmation the invariant invites would enumerate three bounded writes and conclude the path is non-blocking, and the conclusion would be false.

**Severity** high. The invariant is cited as a premise elsewhere and the claim it licenses is a latency claim on the request path, which is the kind of number a host acts on by design.

**What conformance would mean** either the cache write leaves the assessment path — the merge reads and the insertion is deferred to the same drainage the identity observation already uses (`alg:publication:identity-draining`) — or the invariant gains a fourth row and the concurrency chapter's non-blocking claim is restated against a blocking write. The first is the conformant direction under (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`); the second would need the argument that convention requires and this tranche found no text supplying it.

**Coverage** none against the invariant. The signal suite tests the cache's own behaviour thoroughly and no test asks what the assessment path writes, which is the shape a write-set invariant needs and no cluster-local suite produces.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `d43ef1c7`, in the conformant direction the entry names: the cache write leaves the assessment path. The merge reads — a peek under the lock, touching neither the stored vector nor the recency order — and the insertion is deferred to a bounded queue the identity maintenance thread drains on its cycle, the same drainage the identity observation uses; the recency refresh travels with the deferred write. The queue is bounded at the cache's own capacity, so it carries no figure of its own; an overflowed write is dropped and counted. The invariant's exception sentence now names both deferred enqueues and its closing conformance sentence is true again. The deferral is pinned where the old suite could not see it: the merging call returns the supplied value while the cached copy stays untouched until the drain applies it (`test:integration:cache-write-is-deferred-until-the-drain`), and the direct-cache suites are restated with explicit drains at their synchronisation points — the checkpoint barrier drains too, so a flush is also a cache synchronisation point.

**Remark (The batch initialisation's discard re-verified, and one of its citations refuted)** · `rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`

(`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`) is confirmed in full, at the current tree, and this tranche's first draft of it was wrong. The draft proposed to replace the entry's mechanism, having read the ninth tranche's finding as resting on the `None` arm of `complete()`; it does not. The entry names the `try_send` discard precisely — "the first half best-effort, so on a full command channel the statistics are computed, thrown away" — and the observable sequence it records is the one the code performs. Two of this tranche's own claims were refuted by its own checks and both are recorded rather than dropped (`rem:assayer:glue-cross-cites`); this is the first, and the twelfth tranche's rule that a tranche's own draft is a claim about code like any other is what caught it.

The re-verification returns three things the entry can absorb. The locus is exact at the current tree: the discarded send is `src/lib.rs:1026`, the unconditional clear `:1031`, and the flag store `:1032`, all outside the `if
let` that guards the send. The `None` arm the draft mistook for the mechanism is in fact unreachable, which strengthens rather than weakens the entry: `BatchInitAccumulator::observe` (`src/feature/bootstrap.rs:249`) resets on a width change at `:251` and then increments unconditionally at `:254`, so the count is at least one whenever `is_complete()` is consulted and `complete()` (`:273`) returns `Some` in every case the guard admits. There is therefore exactly one way for the statistics to be lost, and it is the one the entry names.

And one of the entry's citations is refuted. Its severity paragraph says "The command channel is fixed-capacity (`tab:construction:parameters`)", which reads as a host-settable parameter that happens to be small. The table's Channels row names "the bounded label and health channels, and the fixed command channel" and puts the group's values in `src/config/types.rs`; the label and health capacities are there and are read at `src/lib.rs:411` and `:424`, and the command channel's is not there at all — it is the literal `64` at `src/lib.rs:408`, restated in prose at `src/error.rs:213`, which is (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-command-channel-capacity`). The correction runs against this tranche's own interest, since a capacity a host could raise would make the compounded severity smaller: the citation is wrong in the direction that makes the standing entry *worse*, because the only mitigation its severity paragraph implies is unavailable.

**Entry (Four public accessors hand a host the steward's command channel and the internal state)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-steward-channel-public`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:610` `pub const fn shared()`, `:624` `pub const fn command_tx()`, `:631` `pub const fn label_tx()`, and `:637` `pub fn next_assessment_id()`, all inherent methods on the exported `Assayer`; together with `src/lib.rs:181`, which exports the shared-state trait, and `src/lib.rs:156` and `:165`, the two modules that re-export the private `numerics` and `signal` modules under `#[doc(hidden)]`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against the surface record's own statement of what it owns — "the four call surfaces the host uses at runtime: what each one takes, what each promises by the time it returns, and **what none of them will ever show**" — and specifically against (`dec:surface:guidance-opacity`), whose claim is that opacity is "enforced structurally rather than by review", so that "a future edit can[not] quietly remove it". A host holding `command_tx()` can send any `ModelOwnerCommand` to the steward, which is every mutation the four surfaces exist to mediate, reached without passing through any of them. The structural enforcement the record relies on is an import restriction between modules (`rule:ownership:import-allowlist`); it says nothing about what the crate root re-exports, and the crate root re-exports the channel.

`next_assessment_id()` fails a second decision on its own. (`dec:surface:assessment-identifier`) says "Every request reaching the assessment path is assigned an identifier as its pending entry is inserted, and that identifier is the label's only handle back to the assessment". A public allocator lets a host mint identifiers that reach no pending entry, so the correspondence the label surface depends on is breakable from outside; a label submitted against such an identifier returns `LabelError::AssessmentNotFound` for an identifier the package itself issued. The method is also a line-for-line duplicate of the trait implementation at `src/lib.rs:842`, which is the smaller half of the finding and the one a compiler could have prevented.

`#[doc(hidden)]` is not privacy and does not bear on the disposition. It removes a surface from the rendered documentation and leaves it in the public API, so `torrust_assayer::testing::VirtualClock`, `torrust_assayer::numerics_export` and the shared-state trait are all reachable by a host that reads the source. The three exist because the integration tests under `tests/` are a separate crate, which is a real constraint and not a licence: the conformant instrument is a feature gate, which the package already uses for `serde`.

**Severity** high. Nothing miscomputes and everything is bypassable, which is the worse of the two shapes for a package whose value rests on being the only writer of its own state.

**What conformance would mean** `shared()`, `command_tx()`, `label_tx()` and `next_assessment_id()` become `pub(crate)`; the trait export, the two export modules and the testing module move behind a feature the integration tests enable and a host does not. The four surfaces are then the four surfaces.

**Coverage** partial and in the wrong direction. `src/tests/api_surfaces.rs` and `src/tests/source_audit.rs` enumerate exported names, so the exports are pinned as present. No test asks whether an exported name should be exported, which is the question the record raises.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `ff455672`, under the surface ruling in (`dec:assayer:repair-waves-ratified-entire`). The four accessors seal at crate visibility, and the allocator's line-for-line duplicate collapses — the trait impl calls the inherent method, so the compiler-preventable half is prevented. The test-only exports take the conformant instrument the entry names: a `test-support` feature gates the testing module, the two export modules and the shared-state trait, enabled by the crate's own test builds through a self dev-dependency, so the integration tests enable it and a host build never carries it. A sealed featureless build compiles clean — the compile-side proof of unreachability — and the seal and the gates are held by a source scan against the merge that would quietly relax them (`test:crate:steward-channel-and-test-surface-stay-sealed`). The four surfaces are the four surfaces.

**Entry (The timestamp's public fields make the clamp the clock record calls unbypassable bypassable)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-timestamp-fields-public`

**Locus** `src/types.rs:270` and `:272`, the `pub seconds: i64` and `pub nanos: u32` fields of `PersistentTimestamp`, and `src/types.rs:330`, `pub fn to_system_time`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:clock:embedded-clamp`), whose operative clause is that "Neither domain exposes an unclamped reading, so a caller cannot obtain one without leaving the timestamp interface altogether", and against the corollary that rests on it, (`cor:clock:clamp-unbypassable`): "The clamp makes the unbounded interval unobtainable; the prohibition makes it unreconstructible."

The clamped accessor is conformant and was recomputed: `hours_since` (`src/types.rs:352`) returns zero on a backward jump at `:358` and applies the ceiling at `:367`, which is the double clamp the decision describes, against the same constant the monotonic side uses through `duration_to_hours` (`src/types.rs:576`). The defect is beside it. The fields are public, so a caller obtains the unbounded interval by subtracting two of them without leaving the type at all, and the corollary's first half fails on its own terms — the unbounded interval is obtainable, and the prohibition on direct exponentiation (`rule:clock:no-direct-exponentiation`) is then the only thing standing where the record says two things stand. `to_system_time` gives a second route for a caller who prefers standard-library arithmetic.

The falsifier that would overturn this is a corpus text requiring the fields to be readable — a published output schema tabulating seconds and nanoseconds as host-visible quantities. The tranche looked and found none; serialisation is not such a text, because the derived implementations reach private fields.

**Severity** medium. Nothing in the package computes an unclamped interval, so the defect is entirely about what a host can do and about what four other records are entitled to assume when they cite a bounded decay factor as a premise rather than re-establishing it.

**What conformance would mean** the fields become private with a constructor and clamped readers, or the decision's "cannot obtain one" is weakened to a statement about the package's own sites and the corollary is rewritten to rest on the prohibition alone. The first is the direction (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) presumes and the second would need an argument this tranche did not find.

**Coverage** the timestamp suite in `tests/types.rs` pins the clamp thoroughly and pins nothing about reachability, which is the expected shape: a test can show an accessor clamps and cannot show a field is unreadable.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `b24b21c2`, by the first of the conformance line's options — the direction the measure convention presumes. The two fields and the system-time conversion seal at crate visibility, so a caller cannot obtain the unbounded interval without leaving the timestamp interface: the clamped elapsed reader is the interface, and the corollary's first half holds on its own terms again. The derived serialisation still reaches the private fields, the route the entry's falsifier search already cleared. The integration tests that read the fields are restated against the surviving surface or moved to the crate suite where the fields are reachable; since a test cannot show a field is unreadable, the seal is held by a source scan instead (`test:crate:timestamp-fields-and-default-stay-sealed`), the arrangement the resonance allowlist uses for properties a merge could quietly relax.

**Entry (The one capacity a host cannot set is restated in prose, and both halves of the caveat forbid it)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-command-channel-capacity`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:408`, `crossbeam_channel::bounded::<ModelOwnerCommand>(64)`, a literal; and `src/error.rs:213`, the documentation of `LifecycleError::CommandChannelFull`, which reads "The command channel is full (capacity 64)".

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`cav:surface:no-local-figures`), which states that "Nothing here carries a capacity, a budget, an expiry, a threshold, or a count. Every figure the four surfaces reason about is a parameter of the construction contract and is named there once (`tab:construction:parameters`), with its value in the code rather than in prose." Both halves are broken by one number. The construction table's Channels row names "the bounded label and health channels, and the fixed command channel" and locates the group in `src/config/types.rs`; the label and health capacities are there and are read at `src/lib.rs:411` and `:424`, and the command channel's is not — the row's word "fixed" describes the code and disagrees with the table's own column heading, which is where the value lives. And the value is restated in prose, in a doc comment, which is the precise failure the caveat's second sentence exists to prevent and which the record attributes to the four collapsed contracts by name.

The finding compounds with (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`), whose severity paragraph cites this very table as though the capacity were settable. The channel that silently discards batch initialisation's only result is the one channel a host cannot widen, and it is shared with every lifecycle registration and every scheduled checkpoint.

**Severity** medium on its own and high in combination. A restated figure costs a reader; an untunable one costs an operator the only mitigation available for a high-severity silent loss.

**What conformance would mean** the capacity joins the infrastructure configuration beside the label and health capacities, the doc comment drops the figure, and the construction table's Channels row stops calling it fixed.

**Coverage** none, and the shape is familiar: `tests/error.rs` covers the error enums' construction and display and no test reads a capacity against the configuration that should own it.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `1e67718d`, under (`dec:assayer:repair-waves-ratified-entire`), which decided the entry's open question the third way: the capacity joins the infrastructure configuration beside the label and health capacities as a host-set value **with no default** — an undeclared capacity refuses to build, on the builder surface and the crate-internal construction path alike, so the value is a declared decision at every deployment rather than a constant nobody chose. The literal is gone from the construction site, the error doc drops its restated figure, and the construction table's Channels row stops calling the channel fixed, so both halves of the caveat hold again. Every fixture declares its capacity explicitly; the harness constant is 64, the figure the retired literal shipped, chosen so the behaviour under test is unchanged. An undeclared or zero capacity is refused naming the configuration path (`test:crate:builder-refuses-unset-command-channel-capacity`), and an engine declared at two slots with its steward parked admits exactly two commands and refuses the third as full (`test:crate:declared-command-channel-capacity-reaches-the-channel`).

**Entry (Construction panics where its own error variant is waiting)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-journal-open-panics`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:543`, `JournalWriter::open(&jp).unwrap_or_else(|e| panic!("failed to open journal at {}: {e}", jp.display()))`, against `src/error.rs:305`, `BuildError::PersistenceSetupFailed(io::Error)`, documented as "Persistence directory could not be created or is not writable".

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:construction:eager-validation`) and against the error partition (`dec:degradation:error-partition`). Construction is the package's fallible surface; an unwritable journal path is a caller-supplied configuration the contract does not admit, which is the hard-error side of the partition, and the variant that names it exists and is exported. The code panics instead, from inside `build_inner`, after the model-owner thread has already been spawned — so the panic unwinds through a partially constructed engine and the started thread outlives the failed construction.

Two neighbouring panics are the same shape and are recorded with it: `src/lib.rs:476`, the model-owner spawn, and `src/lib.rs:364` and `:732`, the schema construction, the last of which is defensible because the builder validated the declarations and the comment says so.

**Severity** medium. A host misconfiguring a path gets an abort where the package's own vocabulary has a value for it, and the started thread is a leak rather than a correctness fault.

**What conformance would mean** `build_inner` returns a result on the persistence path and the journal failure becomes `BuildError::PersistenceSetupFailed`, with the thread spawn moved after the fallible work or the spawned thread shut down on the error path.

**Coverage** none. `tests/error.rs` constructs `BuildError::PersistenceSetupFailed` and asserts its display, so the variant is pinned as a value and never as an outcome — a test that proves the vocabulary exists and not that anything says it.

**Repaired** in wave one at `5ae28394`. `build_inner`, and the two callers above it, return a result; the journal open moved ahead of every thread spawn rather than the spawns moving after it, which is the ordering the conformance line asks for with less to unwind. The two neighbouring panics the entry records with this one — the model-owner spawn and the schema construction — are untouched, being outside the conformance sentence. The variant is now pinned as an outcome: a directory standing where the journal file belongs refuses the build, where before it aborted the process.

**Entry (Two checkpoints are named in the code, defined nowhere in the corpus, and performed nowhere in production)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-checkpoints-five-six-uncalled`

**Locus** `src/error.rs:430` and `:458`, the documentation of `check_vec_nan` and `check_diagonal_nan`, each reading "Used by NaN checkpoints CP5–CP6 for spot-checking model state"; and the census of their callers, which finds `check_diagonal_nan` called from `tests/error.rs` alone and `check_vec_nan` from `tests/error.rs` and `src/testing/asserts.rs:53`, the test harness. Neither has a production caller anywhere in `packages/assayer/src`.

**Disposition** unspecified, on both halves and for one reason. The thirteenth tranche established that the checkpoint-numbered vocabulary has no home in `docs/spec/` or `docs/layers/` (`rem:assayer:report-config-checkpoints`), and this tranche confirms it holds for the records too: the token appears in no file under `packages/assayer/adr/`. The degradation record explains the absence rather than merely exhibiting it. (`cav:degradation:checkpoint-placement`) says the corpus "carries a placement of numeric checkpoints from the boundary inward", that this record does not own it because it "defines where the system takes its readings rather than choosing a posture about them", and that "the census marked it for absorption into the specification on that ground". The absorption did not happen. So the checkpoints are not a divergence — there is no requirement to diverge from — and they are not conformant either; they are a gap with a named owner, a named cause and a scheduled repair that was not made.

What this tranche adds to the thirteenth's finding is the second half. CP1 through CP4 are performed: `src/assessment.rs:951`, `:1011` and `:1073` take those readings on every assessment. CP5 and CP6 are performed nowhere. The two functions that carry the name in their documentation are a public utility and a one-line delegation to it, exercised by seven tests and called by no engine path. A vocabulary with no corpus home might still describe something the code does; this one describes something the code does not do.

**Severity** low as code and medium as corpus. Nothing is miscomputed because nothing runs. What it costs is the reader the caveat anticipates — one who looks for the checkpoint placement in the degradation record, is told where it went, and finds it did not arrive — and it costs the audit a spot-check on model state that the corpus intended to require.

**What conformance would mean** the placement lands in the assessment operations chapter as the caveat directs, defining all six checkpoints and where each is taken; and CP5 and CP6 then either acquire the production call sites the definition gives them or the two functions lose the claim from their documentation. Which of the two is right is the specification's to decide, and this is the rare entry whose repair begins in the corpus.

**Coverage** seven tests in `tests/error.rs` over two functions that production never calls, which is the inverse of the coverage failure the campaign usually meets and belongs in the same conversation.

**Repaired** in wave three at `44852e8d`, and the wave's recomputation corrects this entry's second half before repairing it: CP5 and CP6 *are* performed, and were when this entry was written — the label path's working-copy and pre-publish guards at `src/owner/label_path.rs:905` and `:946` take both readings on every label, revert on a finding, and count the reverts, exactly where the thirteenth tranche's remark said those two run. What was true is that the two functions carrying the name have no production caller. The repair therefore lands the absorption — the assessment operations chapter gains (`tab:runtime:numeric-checkpoints`), defining all six checkpoints and where each is taken, as the caveat directed — and takes the conformance clause's second branch for the code: the two utilities lose the production-use claim and cite the placement as host-and-harness spot-check conveniences, and the guards, like the degradation context, cite the record that now defines them.

**Entry (A Sentinel that reports nothing is counted as reporting)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-zero-sentinels-counts-silent`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:1066`, `let n_sentinels_reporting = ctx.extractions.values().filter(|e| e.occupancy).count();` against `src/assessment.rs:871`, where a registered Sentinel with a cached report but no coordinate in the request is inserted as `SentinelExtraction::new(0, Vec::new(), true)` — occupancy true, features empty. The count feeds `RiskBasis.n_sentinels_reporting` at `:1068` and `HealthSnapshot.zero_sentinels` at `:1142`.

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`schema:output:assessment`) as the two fields are described where the code defines them, and the code's own documentation is the evidence that the meaning intended is not the meaning shipped. `RiskBasis.n_sentinels_reporting` is documented at `src/assessment.rs:235` as "Number of Sentinels contributing to this assessment" and its constructor argument at `:258` as "Number of Sentinels with valid extraction"; `HealthSnapshot.zero_sentinels` at `:479` as "True when no Sentinel contributed data to this assessment", citing the prior-only operating mode. A Sentinel with no coordinate contributes no data by construction — its feature vector is empty and it is absent from `per_sentinel`, which the surrounding comment at `:1111` states as the rule for that map. The same Sentinel is nonetheless counted as contributing, so an assessment in which nothing whatever was measured reports a non-zero contributor count and denies that it was prior-only.

The occupancy flag is not the defect. Occupancy true for a coordinate-less Sentinel is required — the comment at `:867` cites the specification for "active, no data", and the flag means the Sentinel is online. What is wrong is reading an online flag as a contribution count.

**Severity** medium. Both fields are host-facing diagnostics and the second is the flag by which a host tells a designed prior-only estimate from a measured one. The report-only cap does not apply, on the argument the sixth tranche made and this entry inherits: a diagnostic that reports a reassuring value it has no evidence for is a number acted on by design.

**What conformance would mean** the count filters on contribution rather than on occupancy — the extractions with a non-empty feature vector, which is the same set `per_sentinel` is built from at `:1115` and is already computed.

**Coverage** the test index at `src/assessment.rs:18` carries `sentinel_registered_but_no_report`, whose claim says coverage "counts it as missing", and `:19` carries `sentinel_no_coordinate_in_request`, whose claim is about the per-Sentinel map only. The second names this case exactly and pins the map rather than the counter, so the divergence sits one assertion away from a test written to cover the situation that produces it.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `ada58816`. The count filters on the extractions that carry features — the same set the per-Sentinel map is built from — so an assessment in which nothing was measured reports zero contributors and admits through `zero_sentinels` that it was prior-only; occupancy stays the online flag it is. The family defending the divergence was larger than the coverage line saw: seven engine tests asserted the flag down for registered-but-silent Sentinels and ran red against the repair. Each is restated — where the scenario's intent was a functioning Sentinel, the fixture now routes a report and a coordinate through it and asserts the genuine contribution; where the Sentinel was merely online, the test asserts the flag rises, the fact it previously denied (`test:crate:assess-with-sentinel-has-sentinels-flag`). The one-assertion-away test the coverage line named now makes the assertion (`test:unit:sentinel-no-coordinate-in-request`).

**Entry (The two fallbacks of one checkpoint disagree about what a prior is)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-outcome-fallback-uncalibrated`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:190`, `impl Default for OutcomePrediction`, whose uncertainty is the literal `1.0`, reached from the guard at `:354` and from the CP4 outcome-axis fallback at `:1096`; against `src/assessment.rs:1209`, the CP4 risk-basis fallback in the same function, which computes `(time_correction / lambda_prior.max(1e-15)).sqrt()`.

**Disposition** unspecified, and recorded because the gap is where a specification would be expected rather than merely absent. The two fallbacks are the same checkpoint, taken eleven lines apart, over quantities the axis evaluation computes identically — `evaluate_outcome_axis` at `:340` applies the same time correction and the same calibration factor the blend does. One fallback derives the prior standard deviation from the prior precision and the snapshot's age; the other substitutes a constant that ignores both, and also ignores the axis's own calibration factor, so the substituted interval of plus-or-minus one point nine six is on a different scale from every interval the function returns when it succeeds. The tranche searched the axis inference outputs (`tab:axis:inference-outputs`) and the assessment chapter for a prior-only axis prediction and found none: the corpus fixes what a successful prediction is and says nothing about what a degraded one should carry.

**Severity** medium. A consumer reading a degraded axis prediction cannot tell it from a confident one of moderate width, because the width is a constant rather than a scaled prior — and (`dec:degradation:retain-and-flag`) is satisfied only in its flagging half, since the degraded model is pushed at `:1095` while the value it substitutes is not "the best available".

**What conformance would mean** first that the corpus states the prior-only axis prediction, and then that the fallback computes it. The second is small once the first exists: the constructor already receives `kappa_a` and `time_correction`.

**Coverage** `cp4_nan_from_blend_falls_back_to_prior` at `src/assessment.rs:25` covers the risk-basis half and its claim reasons carefully about why the substitution is "a deliberate, reported decision rather than a silent clamp". No test reaches the outcome-axis half, so the half with the reasoned claim is the half that computes the prior and the half with no test is the half that does not.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `f3f23ee7`, in the conformance line's order. The corpus first states the prior-only axis prediction — zero point estimate, the time-corrected prior standard deviation crossed into the host's units by the axis's own compression scale, the standard interval around them, the axis form of the risk basis's fallback at the same checkpoint (`def:axis:prior-only-prediction`). Then both reaching sites compute it: the dimension-mismatch guard and the CP4 axis fallback build the computed prior from the compression scale, the time correction and the prior precision, and the Default becomes the unit-parameter case of the same construction. The axis half now has the test the coverage line found missing — the guard, extracted so a falsifier reads it directly as the risk-basis half's does, returns the worked value and the flag (`test:unit:cp4-nan-axis-falls-back-to-computed-prior`) — and the mismatch test pins the scaling: a doubled compression scale and a quadrupled time correction read exactly four where the constant read one (`test:crate:dimension-mismatch-returns-prior`). One residue is recorded with the wave's findings: driving the CP4 axis fallback end to end from a poisoned mean is blocked in debug builds by the inverse transform's debug assertion, which fires before the guard can take the prediction.

**Entry (Two degradations on the assessment path are not reported in band)** · `entry:assayer:wl-glue-unflagged-degradations`

**Locus** `src/lib.rs:1020`, the `try_lock` whose failure skips a batch-init observation with no counter, and `src/lib.rs:1026`, the `try_send` whose failure is discarded; against `src/lib.rs:950` in the same file, where the identity observation's overflow is documented at `:955` as "Overflow increments a counter (degradation); observation is lost".

**Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`cor:degradation:in-band-travel`), which states that because degradation travels inline on every result "the absent error channel of (`dec:degradation:infallible-core`) costs a consumer no information. Everything an error channel would have carried is present." Two things an error channel would have carried are absent. `DegradationContext` carries no field for either, so the omission is structural rather than a missed increment.

The contrast is the argument. Three of the four lossy operations on this path are conformant — the identity observation counts its overflow, the signal sanitisation counts at `src/assessment.rs:953`, the feature sanitisation at `:1015` — and the two that are not sit in the same file as the one that is, within eighty lines. (`rem:degradation:queue-face`) reasons explicitly that a full observation queue "is a load condition rather than a contract violation, so by (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) it is not a hard error on the observing path"; a contended lock and a full command channel are load conditions by the same reasoning and inherit the same obligation to report.

**Severity** medium for the `try_lock` skip, whose cost is a slower and contention-biased initialisation sample, and high for the `try_send` discard, which is carried at (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`) and is recorded here only for the reporting half.

**What conformance would mean** two counters on the degradation context, raised where the identity overflow counter is raised, so that a host reading a batch of assessments can see that initialisation is being starved before it discovers the consequence from a standardisation that never improved.

**Coverage** none for either. The degradation suite covers the four counters that exist, which is the coverage shape that cannot find a counter that does not.

**Repaired** in wave nine at `5419890a`, the reporting half this entry carries — the discard half had already become a kept-and-retried deferral in wave eight. The observation attempt reports what became of it, and the assessment counts the two load conditions into its degradation context: a skip on the contended accumulator lock, and a completion deferred on a full command channel. Both aggregate into the degradation counters, reach the health summary, and export as two catalogue-declared counter series, with the catalogue's size band moved deliberately for the two new names. The mock-driven falsifier reads one skip, one deferral and a clean pass-through in band (`test:unit:batch-init-load-conditions-reported-in-band`), and the counters ledger accrues both kinds (`test:crate:degradation-counters-increment-monotonically`). The engine mapping from the contended lock itself is exercised by review rather than by a test — deterministic contention needs a harness hook the wave did not build, recorded with its findings.

**Remark (The four forms at the fan-in, asked outward, and what the crate root suppresses)** · `rem:assayer:glue-census`

The tenth tranche found that `api/`, the highest-fan-in cluster, held no uncalled machinery because every function on it is a public method or a helper of one, and predicted that the contract rather than the shape decides the yield. The glue is higher fan-in still and the prediction holds in the first form and inverts in the fourth.

Asked as *who calls this*, the cluster returns three: `hours_since_instant` (`src/types.rs:585`) has no caller anywhere in the package or its integration tests, is public through `pub mod types`, and reads elapsed monotonic time directly rather than through the injected clock; `check_diagonal_nan` (`src/error.rs:472`) has no production caller and is the subject of (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-checkpoints-five-six-uncalled`); and the type alias `ResonanceProfile` (`src/assessment.rs:543`), exported at the crate root and described as "Backward-compatible alias", appears in no type position anywhere — its only occurrences are a name in the export audit's list at `src/tests/source_audit.rs:276` and a documentation link at `tests/support/assertions.rs:610`. It is backward compatibility with nothing: the package has no released version, so the alias preserves a surface no host ever had.

Asked as *does the corpus want anyone to*, the answer separates the three. The first two the corpus wants called — the clock record's clamped monotonic accessor is the instrument its own decision describes, and the checkpoints were scheduled for definition. The alias the corpus does not mention at all.

Asked in the third and fourth forms — does anything put anything in, does anything take anything out — the cluster returns its sharpest single fact, and it is about a public surface rather than a field. `impl Default for PersistentTimestamp` (`src/types.rs:371`) reads the wall clock, and a full census of the package finds it reached from nowhere: no site spells `PersistentTimestamp::default()`, no type with a `PersistentTimestamp` field derives `Default`, and the one type whose `Default` could have reached it — `IdentityConvergenceTracker` (`src/health/identity_tracker.rs:76`) — implements `Default` by hand as a call to its own constructor. So the hidden read is unreached, which is the finding rather than its absence: it is a public `Default` that is never equal to itself on a type deriving `PartialEq`, `Eq`, `Ord` and `Hash`, waiting on a surface a host reaches and the package does not. The corpus settles nothing about it — the clock record's three decisions govern `src/types.rs` and none mentions `Default` — so the disposition is unspecified and the entry is a gap rather than a defect.

The same census, asked outward as the twelfth tranche requires, returns the other half: `PersistentTimestamp::now()` (`src/types.rs:298`) is public and has one production caller outside this cluster, `src/health/identity_tracker.rs:97`, which reads the wall clock directly where every other production path reads it through the injected clock. That is not a divergence either, and for a reason worth recording precisely: the clock record establishes two domains, a clamp and a funnel, and establishes no injection at all. The `Clock` trait the engine is built on is roadmap infrastructure the freeze carves out, not corpus, so a production wall-clock read outside it is measured against nothing. The gap is real and belongs to the record pipeline: the record that owns `src/types.rs` does not describe the mechanism the code uses to read time.

And the crate root suppresses the first form by construction. `src/assessment.rs:54` carries `#![allow(dead_code)] // wiring in progress` over the whole module, `src/lib.rs:244` carries the same attribute over the whole `Assayer` struct, and `src/lib.rs:342` over the whole inherent implementation. Two of the three uncalled items above are inside those scopes. The compiler's own answer to the census question is switched off across the cluster the question pays best in, which is worth a note to the repair wave: the attributes should come off before the census is re-run, because their removal is itself a census.

**Remark (Two oracles that cannot fail, and one flag production discards)** · `rem:assayer:glue-pins`

The cluster's coverage findings are all of one kind — a test that pins something adjacent to what its claim says — and two are worth stating in full.

The channel-freeness of the request context is the first contradiction the surface record collapses (`reg:surface:collapsed-contradictions`) and the subject of (`dec:surface:no-channel-input`). Its only oracle is a `compile_fail` doctest at `src/assessment.rs:105`, whose body reads `let _channel = request.channel;`. `RequestContext` has no field named `channel` and never had one; the field it does carry is `channel_hint` (`:125`). The doctest therefore passes because a non-existent field does not compile, which it would do whether `channel_hint` were private, public, or absent. The one instrument guarding the decision the record calls out as its most-collapsed contradiction cannot fail, and cannot fail for a reason invisible from its own text.

The second is a hollow transfer at the boundary between a test suite and its subject. `sanitise_f64` (`src/error.rs:424`) returns a pair, and eight tests in `tests/error.rs` pin the second element — the claim at `tests/error.rs:22` says the checkpoint "does not merely clean the value: it says that it did, so a NaN entering the model is a health event rather than a silently plausible zero". The function's one production caller is `src/assessment.rs:1013`, which binds that element as `_was_nan` and discards it, deriving its health event from a separate `is_finite` test in the enclosing `if`. The behaviour is correct and the transfer is hollow: the property eight tests establish is not the property production relies on, so the suite would go green if the flag were removed.

A third is smaller and worth the line. `src/assessment.rs:93` fixes `N_CAL_MIN: usize = 30` under the comment "Matches `PlattConfig::default().n_cal_min`", which is a shipped default with two homes joined by a sentence. Nothing checks the match, and the constant is the threshold `HealthSnapshot.calibration_mature` is computed from at `:1130`.

**Remark (Three sentences in which the corpus reports its own conformance, and all three are wrong)** · `rem:assayer:glue-prose-defects`

The campaign has recorded that a corpus sentence reporting what the code does is evidence to be checked and never evidence to be cited. This cluster supplies a subclass with a distinct grammar: the *shipped-claim*, a closing sentence that asserts the code realises the environment above it. The assessment operations chapter carries three and this tranche falsifies all three.

"The write set is shipped as enumerated" (`inv:runtime:enumerated-writes`) is false by a fourth write, which is (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-signal-cache-write`). "The pipeline is shipped step for step" (`alg:runtime:assessment-pipeline`) is false in its last two steps: the code labels the assembly of the result "Step 9" at `src/assessment.rs:1108`, the pending insertion "Step 8" at `:1146`, and the return "Step 9" again at `:1173`, so the numbering runs nine, eight, nine and one number is used twice. The order of effects is defensible — the entry is inserted before the assessment is returned — but the chapter's claim is about a correspondence, and the correspondence does not hold. "The signature and the context are shipped exactly, the absent channel field included" (`req:runtime:assessment-interface`) is false in both directions at once: the requirement's context "carries the entity key, a timestamp, the declared signals, and one coordinate per Sentinel", and `RequestContext` (`src/assessment.rs:112`) carries no timestamp and does carry a channel field, `channel_hint`, which is crate-private and present on the type.

Four smaller defects join them, none changing a disposition. The error module's header table at `src/error.rs:15` gives `ChannelError` six variants where the enum has seven — the table transcribes the derivation record's enumeration, which omits `InvalidGammaQt`, so the code grew a variant neither document followed. The module's sections run one, three, four, five, six, seven: the missing second section is where an assessment error type would have been, and its absence is (`dec:degradation:infallible-core`) realised as a scar, which is worth reading as evidence for the conformance rather than as an editing slip. `RiskAssessment.id` is documented at `src/assessment.rs:498` as "Assigned at pending buffer insertion", restating (`dec:surface:assessment-identifier`) faithfully and describing the code wrongly — the assignment is at `:1109` and the insertion at `:1171`. And the construction table's Channels row calls the command channel "fixed" in a table whose column asks where the value lives, which is (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-command-channel-capacity`).

**Remark (What the inherited items became)** · `rem:assayer:glue-cross-cites`

A prior tranche's cross-citation is a claim about code, and five entering this cluster were re-verified against the tree rather than transcribed.

(`entry:assayer:wl-batch-timestamp-split`) stands, and its locus has rotted by six lines: the per-request persistent read is at `src/assessment.rs:801`, not `:795`. The measurement is confirmed on both sides. `Assayer::assess` (`src/api/assess.rs:44`) takes one monotonic reading for the batch under a comment citing (`dec:ordering:batch-timestamp`) by label; `assess_single` then reads the persistent clock once per request at `src/assessment.rs:801`, and that reading is what reaches the Ledger decay at `src/assessment.rs:851` and the pending entry at `src/assessment.rs:1151`. The three are spelled with their file rather than as bare continuations, because the citation before them names a different file and a reader inheriting it would resolve all three into the wrong module — which the close-out's sweep found by resolving them itself (`rem:assayer:closeout-locus-sweep`). The record's phrasing anticipates and refuses the exculpation the chapter would otherwise supply: the chapter permits per-request state views, promising no consistency guarantee within the batch beyond each request seeing whatever state was current when it ran (`req:runtime:assessment-interface`), and the decision separates the two — "the batch shares a clock and does not share a view". The per-request snapshot load at `src/api/assess.rs:52` is the permitted half and the per-request clock read is not.

(`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`) stands entire, and the correction ran against this tranche rather than against it. The draft that would have replaced its mechanism was refuted by re-reading the entry, and what the re-verification actually returns — an exact locus, a strengthening unreachability argument, and a refuted citation to the construction table — is at (`rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`). The register has asked since the twelfth tranche that a tranche's own draft be treated as a claim about code; the cheapest form of that discipline turns out to be re-reading the entry one is about to correct, which costs less than the check it replaces.

(`entry:assayer:wl-identity-coverage-depth-divisor`) is confirmed and its locus is in this cluster rather than in `identity/`. The divisor is at `src/assessment.rs:911`, `let coverage_depth = f64::from(deepest_depth) / 128.0;`, inside the assessment pipeline's identity step. The identity tranche adjudicated it correctly from the feature table and registered it against the cluster that owns the feature's meaning; the line that must change is here, which is a distinction the repair wave needs and the entry does not currently state.

(`entry:assayer:wl-identity-measurement-rules`) is likewise confirmed with its locus in this cluster. The three departing rules are written at `src/lib.rs:970` through `:986` — the smoothing constant `ALPHA` at `:970`, the suspicion and volatility updates at `:983` and `:985`, the step count at `:986` — inside the shared-state implementation rather than in `identity/`. The twelfth tranche's factor of four hundred was recomputed from the same lines and stands.

(`entry:assayer:wl-preseed-degenerate-vector`) has no assessment-path half. The pre-seeding helper constructs its entry in `src/api/preseed.rs` and nothing on the `assess_single` path reads or writes a pre-seeded vector; the two paths meet only at the pending buffer, which each writes independently. The entry is wholly the tenth tranche's and is not enlarged here. The `extend_standardisation` post-condition the fifth tranche noted likewise does not live in these files: the function is in `src/feature/`, and the glue's only relationship to it is the batch-init command this tranche found dropped.

**Remark (What was cleared, and what would overturn it)** · `rem:assayer:glue-cleared`

Six properties came within a line of becoming findings and are conformant. Each is recorded with the falsifier, on the eleventh tranche's discipline.

The assessment call's shape meets (`dec:surface:batch-infallible`) exactly: `src/api/assess.rs:33` returns a vector with no error channel, `:37` returns an empty vector for an empty batch rather than a complaint, and the map at `:49` is positional. Overturned by any error channel, or by a keyed return.

The challenge estimate's default was recomputed rather than compared. `src/types.rs:192` ships a point estimate of one half and a variance of one twelfth; (`def:companion:prior`) fixes the prior as the uniform distribution on the unit interval, whose mean is one half and whose variance is one twelfth by the standard formula. Overturned by a corpus change to the prior's parameters.

The batch initialisation's sample size is conformant and correctly located. (`alg:standardisation:batch-initialisation`) says "After the first hundred assessments", `src/feature/standardisation.rs:199` ships a hundred, and the construction record's parameter table (`tab:construction:parameters`) puts the Standardisation group in exactly that file. Overturned by a configuration override reaching the value, which `src/lib.rs:560` reads and no default changes.

The persistent timestamp's clamp is conformant in both directions and the pre-epoch encoding round-trips. `hours_since` (`src/types.rs:352`) returns zero on a backward jump and applies the ceiling on the way out, which is the double clamp (`dec:clock:embedded-clamp`) describes; `from_system_time` (`:308`) and `to_system_time` (`:330`) invert each other across the borrow-a-second encoding, including at an exact negative second, where the complement of zero nanoseconds carries correctly through `Duration::new`. Overturned by a clamp applied at a consumption site instead, which the funnel forbids.

The Ledger is read and never written on the assessment path, which is the strongest clause of (`inv:runtime:enumerated-writes`) — "no Ledger entry is written by an assessment, ever". `src/assessment.rs:834` takes a read guard and `:840` constructs an empty Ledger for the rootless case rather than inserting one. Overturned by any write guard on this path.

And the assessment produces no derivation, which (`dec:ordering:core-boundary`) requires: nothing in `assess_single` calls the derivation, and `derive_reckoning` is reached only from the host. Overturned by any resonance import into `src/assessment.rs`, of which there are two — the exploration and tag types at `:83` and `:84` — both used as output types and neither invoked.

**Remark (A notice proposing what a record forbids)** · `rem:assayer:glue-notice-correction`

`src/assessment.rs:1020` carried a labelled notice reading "Parallelise step 6 and step 7 once profiling justifies it. They are data-independent after step 5 and dominate assessment cost." (`dec:ordering:assessment-function`) decides the opposite in terms: assessment "is one per-request function with factored helpers. It is not a pipeline of independently scheduled stages." Two steps scheduled independently is that pipeline, so the notice proposes a change that would take the code out of conformance with a landed decision, and a reader of the notice learns the opposite of what the corpus decides.

The freeze's standing exception permits a labelled notice to be improved as a notice, and this tranche exercised it once, on that authority and no other. The notice now records the profiling observation as an observation and names the decision that closes the option, so the measurement it carries — that these two steps dominate assessment cost — survives for whoever revisits the decision, without reading as a plan the corpus has already refused. No code changed.

A second notice of the same shape is in another tranche's file and was left alone: `src/api/assess.rs:46` proposes `rayon::par_iter()` over the batch, and (`dec:ordering:batch-timestamp`) says "A batch is processed sequentially". It is recorded here for the repair wave rather than edited across a boundary.

## Last tranche: the derivation layer · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`

The cluster is `src/resonance/` — the channel policy and its derived constants, the derivation function and its six-step kernel, the tag types and their kernel, and the exploration gauge — taken against the derivation record and the decision landscape Part (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`). It is the fifteenth tranche and the one the campaign could not open until today, and with it the scope map has no unaudited cluster.

The cluster is unlike every other the audit has read, and the difference decides how the rows below are written. Everywhere else the code implements a chapter and the audit asks whether it implements it correctly. Here the code implements the *appendix* — the rendering — with considerable fidelity, and the chapter the appendix is specified to be a layer over does not exist in the crate at all. So the tranche's conformance table is long and its findings are structural: what is built is built well, and it is built on nothing.

**Remark (Deriving the last row of the map)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-scope-map`

The row was marked underived in both columns because no tranche had gone to the derivation chapters and read them against this code (`rem:assayer:audit-scope-map-history`). It is derived here by the method the glue tranche prescribed, and both columns came out wider than a guess would have made them.

On the record side the governing text is the derivation record, and three others reach into the cluster rather than past it: the ordering record fixes the near side of the crossing this cluster receives (`dec:ordering:closed-crossing`), the surface record closes the guidance surface against everything computed here (`dec:surface:guidance-opacity`), and the ownership record carries the import check the derivation record scopes (`rule:ownership:import-allowlist`). The degradation record is cited by the derivation record for the error partition (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) and is read for that clause alone.

On the specification side the honest answer is the whole of Part IV and one appendix. The derivation-interface chapter fixes the signature, the output and the purity; the channel-policy chapter fixes the action set, the rewards, the exponents and the neutral zone; the crossover chapter fixes the mathematics and the computation order; the exploration chapter fixes the quantity this layer is supposed to produce. And the resonance-rendering appendix (`app:spec:resonance-rendering`) governs more of the shipped lines than any of them, because the shipped entry point is the rendering. A row naming only the derivation-interface chapter would have been the sixth wrong specification column rather than the first right one.

**Table (What the derivation layer conforms to)** · `tab:assayer:resonance-conformant`

Recorded first, and at length, because this cluster's conformance is its most surprising property and a findings-only reading would invert what it is. Every arithmetic row was established by recomputing the corpus's own stated numbers from the shipped formulae and comparing, not by reading the code and agreeing with it.

| Checked | Authority | Evidence |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The transform is a function of an assessment, a policy and a posterior, and reads the risk basis alone out of the first | (`sig:landscape:derivation-function`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:191`, `:192`, `:193` |
| The kernel's input is the risk probability, the effective uncertainty, the calibration parameter and the challenge variance, and nothing else | (`def:landscape:outcome-neutrality`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:55` |
| The probability uncertainty is recovered from the other three by the identity the bandwidth definition states, rather than carried | (`def:rendering:bandwidths`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:369` |
| The per-action cost tables are the specified ones, benign and adverse alike | (`tab:landscape:action-costs`) | `src/resonance/channel.rs:168`, `src/resonance/channel.rs:177` |
