| The differentials are adjacent differences of those tables, and the three-action set composes from the costs rather than by summing two four-action steps | (`def:landscape:reward-differentials`) | `src/resonance/channel.rs:200` |
| Every declared reward default is the tabulated value, and the two exponents carry theirs inside the reward structure | (`tab:channel:reward-parameters`) | `src/resonance/channel.rs:75` |
| The crossover is one logarithm algebraically identical to the decomposed form, and the two parts are never separated | (`eq:landscape:rigid-decomposition`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:282` |
| Each crossover's variance is exactly the specified two-source diagonal, and nothing off the diagonal is computed | (`thm:landscape:crossover-covariance`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:275`, `src/resonance/derivation.rs:313` |
| The catching-forfeiture condition is detected as a non-positive differential and emits an infinite crossover | (`thm:landscape:catching-forfeiture`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:283` |
| The four-action Challenge domination threshold falls at exactly the tabulated value | (`thm:landscape:dominance`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:379` |
| The six computation steps run in the fixed order, and step five's dependency on step four is honoured | (`alg:landscape:computation-order`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:241`, `:244`, `:249`, `:252`, `:255` |
| Classification bandwidths are the compression over the effective spread; action bandwidths are the independent-crossover form | (`def:rendering:bandwidths`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:336`, `src/resonance/derivation.rs:348` |
| The display floor is applied as a maximum against the raw bandwidth at the stated constant | (`def:rendering:bandwidths`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:358` |
| Magnitudes are relevance attenuated by the risk uncertainty, action relevance being the posture-axis regime width closed at both ends | (`def:rendering:magnitudes`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:370`, `src/resonance/derivation.rs:377` |
| Classification placement and the three relevances are the specified expressions | (`def:rendering:magnitudes`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:557`, `src/resonance/derivation.rs:561` |
| Interior actions locate at the logit midpoint of their bounding crossovers | (`alg:rendering:placement`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:413` |
| The crossover-matching offset is the specified expression, the half-width naming making the quarter factor explicit, and both stated reductions hold of it | (`alg:rendering:placement`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:506` |
| A two-action channel places both tags as boundary tags at the half-power offsets either side of the one crossover | (`alg:rendering:placement`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:441` |
| Boundary locations are constrained to the drawable intervals and no parameter is clamped to a domain endpoint | (`prop:rendering:no-clamping`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:407`, `src/resonance/derivation.rs:410` |
| The dominated treatment sets the magnitude, the bandwidth and the flag to the three specified values | (`alg:rendering:dominated-treatment`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:534` |
| The kernel is the Cauchy form on the logit scale and the tag record carries the specified five fields | (`eq:rendering:kernel`) | `src/resonance/tags.rs:131`, `src/resonance/tags.rs:80` |
| Every tag is strictly positive at every posture, the far tail returning a floor rather than a zero | (`prop:rendering:completeness`) | `src/resonance/tags.rs:135` |
| The channel constants are recomputed on every call and cached against no policy | (`dec:derivation:per-call-constants`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:189` |
| The action set accepts any ordered subset of two or more and the arithmetic is written over adjacent pairs without knowing how many | (`dec:derivation:ordered-actions`) | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:227`, `src/resonance/channel.rs:198` |
| The neutral zone is accepted on the policy and read by no formula in the derivation | (`def:channel:neutral-zone`) | `src/resonance/channel.rs:111`, and the cluster by absence |
| No production path in the Core reaches the derivation; the composition is the host's | (`dec:ordering:core-boundary`) | the cluster's callers, exhaustively |
| Guidance names no derivation quantity of any kind | (`dec:surface:guidance-opacity`) | `src/guidance/mod.rs`, by absence |

Three rows deserve their arithmetic stated, because they are the rows a reader would most reasonably doubt.

The crossover row is an algebraic identity rather than an approximation. The shipped expression takes one logarithm of the ratio of the complementary odds to the differential ratio and divides by the exponent sum; splitting that logarithm gives the shared term and the transition offset exactly, the first being the negative effective estimate over the exponent sum times the calibration parameter. Nothing is lost and nothing is separated, which is what the theorem's own closing sentence says of the shipped code and what this row confirms (`thm:landscape:rigid-translation`).

The placement row was checked against the appendix's own worked rendering. At the low-risk example's inputs the shipped formulae place the last action tag at the location the appendix tabulates, to every digit the appendix states, and the first action tag two thousandths away — a difference that vanishes when the recomputation is fed the appendix's own rounded intermediate magnitudes rather than the exact ones. The appendix says its examples cannot ship because they depend on landscapes the package does not produce (`ex:rendering:examples`); what this row establishes is the narrower and more useful thing, that the rendering half of those examples is reproduced by the shipped code.

The domination row is a threshold recomputed rather than transcribed. Setting the two innermost crossover offsets equal and solving for the challenge estimate gives the tabulated value exactly, and the shipped mechanism reaches it by a different route — an inverted pair of crossovers gives Challenge a non-positive regime width, the width becomes a zero magnitude, and the monotonicity pass converts a zero magnitude into the dominated treatment. Same threshold, and the code never names it.

**Remark (The six questions the record held for this tranche)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-held-questions`

The derivation record's own report held six code questions for the tranche that would follow it, and they are answered here in the order it asked them. Three confirm what the record supposed, two change what it says, and one is not a question about the code at all once the code is read.

*The exploration quantity.* The record supposed a substitution and the specification insists the substitution "is not a rename" (`inv:guarantee:exploration`). Measured: what ships is three Shannon entropies over the normalised rendered tag kernels at one posture, decomposed by renormalising within each family — which is, number for number, the ambiguity gauge the appendix specifies (`sig:rendering:ambiguity-gauge`). The arithmetic is the right arithmetic. What is wrong is everything around it, and it is worse than the record supposed: see (`entry:assayer:wl-exploration-gauge-named-information-value`).

*The purity clauses and the dedicated test.* The specification says the shipped derivation "satisfies all five clauses on its own arguments and carries a dedicated test that says so" (`pf:landscape:purity`). The five clauses hold, by construction and for the reason the proof gives — the argument list carries no mutable reference, no handle and no clock, and the return value is the only channel out. The dedicated test does not exist. The nearest candidate is (`claim:resonance:deriving-the-channel-constants-twice-from-one-policy-gives-the-same-numbers`), and it is a test of the channel-constants helper rather than of the derivation, whose oracle is two calls in one process on one argument pair. Repeat-call agreement cannot detect any of the five clauses: a function that read a clock, read a global, wrote a global or allocated a persistent resource would pass it unchanged. The disposition is **divergent-spec-stale** and the argument is the one the measure convention requires (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`): a later record supersedes the sentence. The derivation record rules that purity is guarded by the import allowlist and *not* by "a test that watches it behave purely" (`dec:derivation:allowlisted-imports`), and gives the reason — a behavioural purity test "exercises only the calls somebody thought to write". The specification is describing an artefact the corpus has since decided against having. The code is right; the sentence is stale.

*Per-call recomputation.* It fires. The constants are computed inside the derivation call from the policy and the challenge point estimate, and no structure in the crate holds a derived-constants value across calls (`dec:derivation:per-call-constants`). The record's reason is confirmed too: the differentials are functions of the estimate, so the same policy at two estimates gives two different constant sets, which the liveness test pins directly (`claim:resonance:reward-parameters-and-the-challenge-estimate-are-live-inputs-of-the-decision-layer`). One qualification the record could not have known: the channel-policy chapter says the shipped derivation "reads the point estimate and the variance into its derived constants" (`data:channel:challenge-interaction`), and it reads only the point estimate there; the variance travels into the kernel input instead.

*The allowlist against the module's imports.* They do not match, and the mismatch runs the permissive way. The allowlist permits eight crate roots and the cluster reaches five of them — the assessment, the numerics, the types, the error module and its own siblings. Three permitted roots are reached by no line under the cluster: the configuration, the health module and the linear-algebra module. Under the decision's own reasoning that is a real loosening rather than a tidiness question, because the guard's stated value is that it "fails on the first line that would break it" (`dec:derivation:allowlisted-imports`), and a line reaching into the health module or the configuration would not fail it. The scope, by contrast, is right: the check is drawn around the whole cluster rather than around the one file, which is the faithful reading of a record that puts the exploration quantity in the same layer (`dec:derivation:exploration-layer`). The larger finding is the guard's other half and it stands as (`entry:assayer:wl-resonance-allowlist-sidestep-partial`).

*Whether the shipped-surface gaps are still exactly four.* They are not, in both directions at once. See (`data:assayer:resonance-gap-count`).

*The neutral zone.* This is the question that dissolves. The record originally listed the declared-and-unread neutral zone as the fourth of its four gaps; its repaired caveat now records that item as conformance (`cav:derivation:shipped-surface`). The specification that governs the field says the opposite in terms: the neutral zone "is specified as carried and unread, and an implementation that begins reading it in the derivation has broken an invariant rather than fixed an oversight", closing with the sentence that it is unread in the shipped surface "exactly as specified" (`def:channel:neutral-zone`). Measured, it is carried and unread. So this is not a gap between the shipped surface and the decided one; it is conformance, and the audit's standing ruling puts the specification above the record here (`dec:assayer:spec-authoritative`). The row for it stands in the conformance table above.

**Data (What the shipped-surface caveat counted)** · `data:assayer:resonance-gap-count`

The record originally carried four gaps "carried rather than absorbed" and warned a reader not to read silence elsewhere as a claim that nothing further diverged. The warning was right and the count was not; the repaired caveat now records the three implementation repairs and the conformant fourth item (`cav:derivation:shipped-surface`).

| The record's gap | Measured | Standing |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The display configuration is a required argument of the primary call | holds | (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-display-config-argument`) |
| What returns is a rendered profile with no decision object beneath it | holds, and is short of the specified profile besides | (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-no-landscape`) |
| The challenge posterior arrives as a mean and a variance alone | holds | (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`) |
| The channel policy carries a neutral zone no formula reads | not a gap; the specification requires exactly this | conformant |

At this audit cut, three of the four stood. The fourth was a listing error rather than a divergence, and it is worth naming as such rather than quietly dropping, because the shape recurs: a record enumerating what the code does differently can catch a conformant property in the net when the property looks like an absence. A field nothing reads and a field nothing *should* read are indistinguishable from the code and are opposites in the corpus.

Against that, the tranche found five further divergences of the same shipped surface that the caveat does not reach, and one of them is the highest-severity finding in the cluster. The primary call silently substitutes a different channel for a malformed one (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-silent-policy-fallback`); it silently substitutes a prior for a malformed posterior (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-challenge-clamped`); the check the record assigns to the host is not reachable by a host (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`); the first of the three dominated-tag triggers is absent rather than starved (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-dominance-probability-absent`); and the returned profile carries neither of the two fields that make a stored profile self-describing, which is folded into the second entry above. So the shipped surface diverges in eight places, of which the record named three.

**Entry (The primary call substitutes a different channel for a malformed one)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-silent-policy-fallback`

**Locus** `src/resonance/derivation.rs:170`, the fallback assignment, reached whenever the declared action set holds fewer than two actions. No notice stands here. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:derivation:ordered-actions`), which states that the transform "assumes the declaration is well formed and does not check it", that the check is "the host's, at policy load rather than per call", and that "it fails hard rather than degrading" because a malformed action set is a caller's defect the caller can fix — the side of the partition the package already draws (`dec:degradation:error-partition`). The shipped call does all three of the things the decision rules out: it checks, it checks per call, and it degrades. What it degrades to is the aggravating fact. It does not shorten the landscape or return an empty one; it discards the host's policy entirely and derives against the default four-action channel, so the rewards, the exponents and the action set are all the package's rather than the caller's. **Severity** high. This corrupts a number a host acts on and does it silently. A host declaring a permit-only channel receives seven tags naming Challenge, Slow and Block, placed by reward parameters it never declared, with no flag, no error and no field of the result differing from a genuine four-action derivation. The result is not merely wrong, it is unfalsifiable at the call site. **What conformance would mean** the primary call takes the declaration as given and the well-formedness check moves to the host at policy load, which requires (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`) to land first so that a host has something to call. **Falsifier** overturned by any return path that reports the substitution, or by corpus text admitting a per-call repair of a malformed declaration. It is confirmed by deriving twice from one assessment, once under the default policy and once under a policy declaring the single Allow action, and observing the two results agree tag for tag. **Sequencing** blocked on (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`); shares a mechanism with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-challenge-clamped`). **Coverage** none. Every test in the cluster passes a well-formed policy, and the debug assertion that would fire on a short action set (`src/resonance/derivation.rs:228`) is removed by the release profile and is never reached in any case, because the fallback has already replaced the policy before the kernel sees it.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `1934d71f`, after its prerequisite. The fallback assignment is deleted: the primary call takes the declaration as given, states in its documentation that the well-formedness check is the host's at policy load, and a malformed set reaching the kernel fails hard rather than degrading. The entry's confirming experiment now fails as it should — a two-action declaration derives exactly its own three-plus-two tags where the default channel derives seven, so no two derivations across different declarations can agree tag for tag (`test:crate:derivation-answers-the-declared-action-set`) — and a single-action declaration panics at the call instead of being answered from a channel the host never declared (`test:crate:derivation-fails-hard-on-malformed-action-set`).

**Entry (The well-formedness check is not reachable by a host)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`

**Locus** `src/resonance/channel.rs:238`, and `src/lib.rs:107` where the module is declared private. No notice stands here. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:derivation:ordered-actions`), which assigns the well-formedness check to the host at policy load. The function implementing that check exists, is correct against the action-set definition and the reward table, and carries a doc comment saying it is "called eagerly by host-side channel registries". It is not exported. The crate declares the resonance module privately and re-exports eight named items from it; this function is not among them, so no host-side channel registry can call it and none does. **Severity** medium. Nothing computed is wrong, but the partition the degradation record draws is unenforceable from outside the crate: the caller's defect the caller can fix has no surface at which the caller can find it. The severity is medium rather than high only because the compounding item carries the consequence. **What conformance would mean** the validation function is re-exported beside the policy type it validates, or the policy type gains a checked constructor. **Falsifier** overturned by any export path reaching the function that this audit did not see — a public alias, a trait method, or a re-export from another module. **Sequencing** prerequisite of (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-silent-policy-fallback`). **Coverage** the function's own behaviour is untested from any surface a host shares; the cluster's tests construct policies directly.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `0e6e1f56`, first of the resonance trio as its sequencing requires. The validation function is re-exported from the crate root beside the policy type it validates, and the harness — the in-repo host — now runs it at channel declaration, so a malformed policy fails at load. The host-side reachability the falsifier asked for is pinned through the public surface: the default policy admitted, a single-action and an unordered declaration each refused by the name of its defect (`test:integration:channel-policy-validation-reachable-from-host`).

**Entry (The entropy gauge ships named as its own opposite)** · `entry:assayer:wl-exploration-gauge-named-information-value`

**Locus** `src/resonance/exploration.rs:35` and `src/assessment.rs:425`, the two types, and the six field and doc names between them. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`sig:rendering:ambiguity-gauge`), whose disclaimer is stated to be "part of the specification rather than a note about it": entropy of the rendered distribution weights uncertainty by nothing, a value of information weights it by decision sensitivity, the two disagree exactly where a host would act on the difference, and "the type is named for ambiguity precisely to sever the association". The shipped types assert the association instead, in every place they could: the module is named for exploration, its type for exploration metrics, its three fields for information value, and the assessment-level record's three fields carry the information-value suffix outright. The corresponding fragility definition names the same substitution and says the two "cannot be migrated between without changing what is being asked for" (`def:fragility:definition`). **Severity** high, and higher than the naming makes it look. This is not a rename pending: the appendix's own worked case is a posture where the distribution is broad but every tag leads to the same action, where the entropy is high and the information value is nil. A host ranking labelling candidates on this field buys exactly the observations that will teach it nothing, and every name it can see tells it that is what the field is for. **What conformance would mean** the two types and their six fields are renamed for ambiguity, and the disclaimer is written into the module documentation. **The specification's own conformance sentence is wrong here and this is the fourth instance of that subclass.** The signature closes by saying the shipped code "carries the disclaimer in its documentation in a different form". It carries no form of it. The module documentation states the arithmetic and a subadditivity note and says nothing about what the quantity is not, and the authored claim over one of its tests states the inverse — that the quantity is "a measure of what a label would buy" (`claim:resonance:a-tag-field-with-nothing-to-distinguish-carries-the-maximum-entropy-of-its-size`). A tranche that took the sentence as evidence would have recorded this cluster's highest finding as conformant, with the specification's own sentence as its citation, which is precisely the hazard the close-out named (`rem:assayer:closeout-completeness`). **Falsifier** overturned by any disclaiming sentence in the cluster's documentation that this audit did not find; the search was for the words the specification uses and for the word the type is supposed to be named after, and neither occurs anywhere under `src/resonance/` or in the assessment module's derivation types. **Sequencing** rides with (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`), because the claim that must be restated is the one asserting the association. **Coverage** worse than none. Two of the three authored claims over this module's tests describe the quantity as information value in the corpus's own vocabulary, so the suite is the document a maintainer would consult to conclude the rename is wrong.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `1eaf0d86`, with the fragility surface already standing beside it at `975afefe` as the wave's ordering intended, so the rename leaves a host something to use in the gauge's place. The module is named for ambiguity, its type is the specified `ProfileAmbiguity`, the per-call read over a profile is `profile_ambiguity` at a caller-chosen posture, and every field and doc name that called the three entropies information value now calls them what they are. The disclaimer the specification writes is in the module documentation in the specification's own terms, with the pointer to fragility beside it; and the claim that asserted the association is restated with the rename rather than after it — the maximum-entropy gloss now closes with why a field that has learned nothing scoring the most makes the quantity an ambiguity gauge and not a measure of what a label would buy (`claim:resonance:a-tag-field-with-nothing-to-distinguish-carries-the-maximum-entropy-of-its-size`). The falsifier's search terms now occur where they were absent: the module documentation carries the disclaiming sentences and the word the type is named after.

**Entry (The gauge's stated subadditivity is false and its own suite refutes it)** · `entry:assayer:wl-exploration-subadditivity-false`

**Locus** `src/resonance/exploration.rs:17`, the module documentation's stated inequality, and the test at `src/resonance/exploration.rs:183` that pins it. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`sig:rendering:ambiguity-gauge`), which defines the three figures as the joint entropy and the two within-family entropies and states no relation between them. The module documents the relation as an invariant — the total is at most the classification figure plus the action figure — and a test asserts it on one seven-tag fixture. The relation does not hold. Writing the joint entropy by the chain rule as the entropy of the family split plus each family's own entropy weighted by its share, the claimed inequality requires the split entropy to be no larger than the two families' entropies discounted by their shares, which nothing forces. **Severity** medium. Nothing in the derivation consumes the relation, so no number a host reads moves; what fails is a documented property a host may reason from, and a claim in the suite asserting it. **What conformance would mean** the module documentation states the chain-rule identity the hundred-configuration test already pins (`claim:resonance:total-information-value-is-the-chain-rule-sum-over-the-class-action-partition`) and drops the inequality, and the test and its claim are restated on that identity. **Falsifier, and it is already committed in the same file.** The first test of the module builds four tags identical in location, magnitude and bandwidth — three classification and one action — and pins the joint figure at the logarithm of four. On that same fixture the classification figure is the logarithm of three and the action figure is zero, because a single-member family has no uncertainty left in it. The logarithm of four exceeds the logarithm of three, so the fixture the module uses to demonstrate its ceiling refutes the inequality it documents two paragraphs above. Nothing new needs writing to see it; the two tests need only be read together. **The module already knows.** The hundred-configuration test carries a comment saying in terms that the originally-planned subadditivity property does not hold for independently renormalised subsets, and naming the reason — the total can exceed the sum when the family partition is itself informative, at `src/tests/resonance_exploration.rs:121`. So the refutation, the refuting fixture and the false invariant all stand in the same two files, and only the invariant is what a reader of the module documentation meets first. **Sequencing** rides with (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`). **Coverage** the inequality is pinned by one fixture on which it happens to hold, and refuted by another fixture in the same module which nothing compares it against.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `c64f7aaa`, as the wave's share of the tranche's second false claim. The module documentation states the chain-rule identity the hundred-configuration test pins and drops the inequality, naming its own refutation in place — the maximum-entropy fixture's total of the logarithm of four against its families' logarithm of three plus zero. The one-fixture test and its claim are restated on the identity, asserting the chain rule on the same seven-tag field the inequality happened to hold on (`test:unit:chain-rule-ties-the-three-figures`), so the module's in-file evidence and its documentation agree with the sweep that knew all along.

**Entry (The display configuration is a required argument of the derivation)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-display-config-argument`

**Locus** `src/resonance/derivation.rs:168`, the fourth parameter. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`sig:landscape:derivation-function`), which fixes the argument list at three and says that a fourth argument carrying display parameters "would make the returned object a function of presentation". The presentation-free invariant is stated unconditionally and does not hold of this call (`inv:landscape:presentation-free`), which the invariant itself records. The record carried it as the first of its four gaps (`cav:derivation:shipped-surface`). **Severity** high, and the severity is architectural rather than numerical. Two of the composition dividends rest on the invariant directly: an external service deriving under a different policy isolates the cost structure exactly only if the compared outputs share no display parameter, and a replayed assessment reproduces its landscape from three stored inputs only if three inputs were all there were. Neither property is available to a host of this crate. **What conformance would mean** the derivation returns a landscape from three arguments and the rendering becomes a second call taking the display configuration, which is the shape the rendering contract already specifies (`sig:rendering:contract`). **Falsifier** overturned by a call path producing decision content without the configuration; there is none, the configuration being a reference parameter with no default at the only entry point the crate exports. **Sequencing** the head of an ordered set with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-no-landscape`); neither closes without the other, since the landscape is what the rendering would take. **Coverage** none, and none is possible: no test can pin the absence of an argument.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `ebc053ee`, jointly with its ordered pair — the two entries are one repair and landed as one boundary change over the two commits `a5d73cab` and `ebc053ee`. The primary derivation takes exactly the three specified arguments and the display configuration moves to a second, optional rendering call (`sig:rendering:contract`), so the presentation-free invariant holds of the shipped surface and both composition dividends the severity argument rested on are available: compared outputs share no display parameter, and three stored inputs are all there were. The Core configuration's two derivation-layer fields — the reference posture and a display-configuration copy nothing read — left with the boundary, discharging both of their conceding notices.

**Entry (There is no landscape for the rendering to be a layer over)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-no-landscape`

**Locus** `src/assessment.rs:533`, the returned type, and `src/assessment.rs:543` where the specified profile name is bound to it as an alias. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`sig:landscape:output`), which fixes five field groups — the shared term, the shared uncertainty, the posterior as it crossed, one crossover record per adjacent pair and one regime record per declared action. None of the five is returned. The crossovers exist as a private structure carrying a logit, a posture and a variance, and are dropped at the end of the call, which the crossover definition records (`def:landscape:action-crossover`). Two consequences the corpus states separately are confirmed: the regime-width corollary is closed form in quantities the crossover computation already produces and is not surfaced (`prop:landscape:width-variance`), and neither landscape utility exists by name or by substance (`sig:landscape:utilities`). **A second divergence stands at the same locus and the record does not reach it.** The shipped surface does bind the specified profile name, as an alias to the returned type — and the specified profile carries three fields where the alias carries two different ones. The posture domain is absent and the echoed configuration is absent, and the exploration record stands in their place. The echoed configuration is not decoration: it is what makes a stored profile self-describing, so that "rendering replay needs no external state, because the profile carries the constants it was rendered under" (`sig:rendering:contract`). A stored profile from this crate carries none of its eight display constants, so replaying a rendering requires the configuration to have been stored separately and correctly associated, by machinery the crate does not provide. **Severity** high. The decision content the corpus defines every decision semantic on does not leave the package in any form, and what does leave it carries display constants baked into every field with no record of which ones. **What conformance would mean** the derivation returns the landscape and the rendering is a second call over it, its profile carrying the posture domain and the configuration echo. **Falsifier** overturned by any exported type carrying a crossover record or a regime record; the crate exports neither, and the private crossover structure is not re-exported. **Sequencing** ordered pair with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-display-config-argument`); prerequisite of (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-dominance-probability-absent`), which has nowhere to report a probability until a regime record exists. **Coverage** the tests establish properties of the rendered field thoroughly and establish nothing about the landscape, there being none.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `a5d73cab` with the rendering re-layered at `ebc053ee` and the two utilities at `975afefe`. The derivation returns the decision landscape with the five specified groups — the shared term, the shared uncertainty, the posterior as it crossed, one crossover record per adjacent pair with location on both scales, offset, signed sensitivity, two-source variance and the credible interval by exact quantile push-through, and one regime record per declared action — and the rendering is a layer over it, its profile carrying the posture domain and the configuration echo where the exploration record stood. The regime-width corollary is surfaced on the regime record and both landscape utilities exist by name and substance: the optimal action from the cost-curve envelope, correct under inverted crossovers, and fragility with its modal action, flip probability and two evidence shares. Two deliberate corpus additions ride the record and are flagged in the landing note: the exponent sum on the landscape and the two reward differentials on each crossover, without which the envelope is not computable from a landscape and a posture — the offsets carry only differential ratios, and the envelope's skipped-line breakpoints need the magnitudes. The falsifier is answered by export: the crate exports the crossover and regime records, and the worked chapter's tables are pinned figure for figure (`test:crate:low-risk-landscape-matches-the-worked-tables`), with rigid translation bit-for-bit across bases (`test:crate:regime-table-is-risk-independent`) and the envelope skipping a dominated action inside its inverted span (`test:crate:envelope-skips-a-dominated-action`).

**Entry (The challenge posterior crosses as moments alone)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`

**Locus** `src/types.rs:177`, the estimate type, carried into the kernel input at `src/resonance/derivation.rs:194`. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`sig:companion:posterior`), which fixes two variants and says the distinction "is not a convenience: it is the boundary at which the Companion's conjugate structure either survives into the derivation or does not". Only the moment-matched variant exists. The record carried it as the third gap (`cav:derivation:shipped-surface`) and stated the consequence precisely — the structure is discarded one call before the two consumers that need it. **Severity** medium to high, and the two consumers say which. Credible intervals are specified to push an exact quantile through the offset, which matters most where the offset is steepest, near the forfeiture threshold, and where a Gaussian linearisation "would understate the uncertainty badly" (`def:landscape:credible-intervals`). Dominance is specified as exact and closed form for the conjugate variant and approximate otherwise (`thm:landscape:dominance`). Neither consumer is built, so the severity is latent rather than realised — which is why it is not a high on its own, and why it rises to one the moment either is built. **What conformance would mean** the estimate type becomes a two-variant carrier, the conjugate variant holding the pseudo-count pair. **Falsifier** overturned by any conjugate carrier reaching the derivation; the Companion holds pseudo-counts and the boundary type holds two floating-point scalars, so the discard happens at the read rather than in the derivation. **Sequencing** shares its consumers with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-dominance-probability-absent`) and with the Companion's own read-decay item (`entry:assayer:wl-companion-read-decay`), whose staleness reaches this crossing. **Coverage** the variance is pinned as a live input (`claim:resonance:every-scalar-in-the-sufficient-statistic-actually-moves-the-tag-profile`), which establishes that the moment is read and says nothing about the variant that is not carried.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `5f772917`, after the landscape gave the crossing somewhere to land. The boundary type is the specified two-variant carrier: the conjugate variant holds the pseudo-count pair and the moment-matched variant holds a mean and variance from elsewhere, credible quantiles evaluating on the carrier's own shape — exact where structure crossed, a moment-matched Beta where only moments did, the uniform prior where no Beta carries the pair. The tracker's posterior converts into the conjugate variant through the replacement surface wave eight built, and the in-repo host feeds that crossing. The variant is now pinned as carried, not merely the moment as read: the tracker's Beta(2, 1) after one failure crosses whole and the intervals equal that shape's own push-through (`test:crate:conjugate-counts-cross-the-boundary-whole`), and a Beta-shaped moment pair reproduces its conjugate twin while an uncarryable pair falls back to the prior (`test:crate:moment-pair-crosses-at-matched-fidelity`).

**Entry (The first dominated-tag trigger is absent rather than starved)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-dominance-probability-absent`

**Locus** `src/resonance/derivation.rs:522`, the monotonicity pass, by what it does not test. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`alg:rendering:dominated-treatment`), which names three conditions that mark a tag dominated: a reported domination probability above a display threshold defaulting to one half, a crossover inversion, and a boundary existence failure. The shipped pass implements the second and the third — an inverted regime arrives as a non-positive magnitude, and the placement's negative discriminant arrives as a flag from the boundary computation — and implements nothing for the first. There is no domination probability anywhere in the crate, no display threshold, and no incomplete beta function, which the dominance theorem states of the shipped code and this audit confirms (`thm:landscape:dominance`). **Severity** medium. The two implemented triggers catch the point-estimate cases, so a tag that is dominated *now* is marked. What is lost is the evidential case the theorem is written for: an action at intermediate probability, Challenge at Companion cold start being the canonical one, which the corpus wants reported "with both its point-estimate regime and its probability, so the host sees an open evidential question rather than a settled fact". A host of this crate sees a settled fact either way. **What conformance would mean** the landscape reports a domination probability per regime and the display treatment reads it against a threshold. **The corpus's own phrasing overstates what ships and is corrected here.** The dominated-treatment algorithm closes by saying the first trigger is "present in form and starved of the quantity it tests". It is not present in any form: no branch, no threshold constant, and no field. What is present is the boolean the treatment *outputs*, which is not the trigger. **Falsifier** overturned by any threshold constant or probability field in the cluster; the display configuration carries eight constants and none of them is a domination threshold (`tab:config:rendering`). **Sequencing** blocked on (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-no-landscape`) and on (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`), the first for somewhere to report the probability and the second for the structure that makes it exact. **Coverage** the threshold is bracketed from both sides by tests that reach it through the inversion trigger rather than through a probability (`claim:resonance:a-dominated-action-is-pinned-to-epsilon-mono-magnitude-and-q-min`), so the suite would not notice the first trigger arriving or staying away.

**Repaired** in wave ten at `485ff060`, last of the ordered set as its sequencing requires. Each regime record reports its domination probability: every domination condition reduces to one affine threshold on the challenge effectiveness — the cross-multiplied width condition for interior actions, a benign-differential sign for the first, forfeiture for the last — and the probability is the posterior mass on the dominated side through the regularised incomplete beta on the crossed carrier's own shape, exact for the conjugate variant. The display treatment gains the first trigger, reading the reported probability against the display threshold; the threshold ships as a named constant at one half rather than a ninth configuration field, a reading the landing note records. The suite now notices exactly what it could not: a broad posterior above the threshold in mean but not in mass marks the tag through the first trigger alone while ten times the evidence at the same mean leaves it live (`test:crate:reported-domination-drives-the-display-treatment`), and the worked dominance columns — 0.375, 0.067, the forfeiture mass 0.294 — are pinned in the worked-table tests.

**Entry (A malformed posterior is silently replaced by the prior)** · `entry:assayer:wl-derivation-challenge-clamped`

**Locus** `src/resonance/derivation.rs:177` through `:187`, the three substitutions. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:degradation:error-partition`) as the derivation record applies it (`dec:derivation:ordered-actions`): a caller's defect is the caller's to fix and should fail hard rather than degrade. A non-finite point estimate is replaced by the default estimate, a non-finite or negative variance by the default variance, and an out-of-range estimate is clamped into the unit interval. All three are silent. **Severity** medium. The substituted value is at least a specified one — a host with no Companion supplies the uniform prior and its effect is conservative (`def:companion:prior`) — so the failure mode is a conservative landscape rather than a wrong one. What makes it a finding rather than a nicety is that the clamp's own endpoint is degenerate: clamping the estimate to zero makes the first differential zero, which the crossover computation reads as the catching-forfeiture condition and answers with an infinite crossover. A caller passing a slightly negative estimate therefore receives a landscape in which Challenge is dominated, for a reason that has nothing to do with its rewards. **What conformance would mean** the substitutions are removed and the estimate's own constructor refuses a malformed pair, alongside (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`). **Falsifier** overturned by corpus text admitting a per-call repair of a malformed posterior; the companion chapter specifies what a host supplies when it has no Companion and says nothing about what the derivation does when handed a value outside the domain. **Sequencing** shares a mechanism with (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-silent-policy-fallback`) and should land in the same wave. **Coverage** none. No test passes a non-finite or out-of-range estimate.

**Repaired** in wave eight at `a67abcf0`, beside its mechanism-mate. The three substitutions are removed from the primary call and the estimate's own constructor refuses the malformed pair where the caller can fix it: a mean that is not finite or outside the unit interval, a variance that is not finite or negative. The fields seal behind readers so the checked constructor is the only public route to a value; the crate's own producers — the Beta posterior's moments, the sanitised override — construct unchecked with the in-domain argument stated at each site. The slightly negative mean this entry measured being clamped to the degenerate zero endpoint is now refused by name, and an in-domain pair is admitted and echoed exactly (`test:crate:challenge-estimate-constructor-refuses-malformed-pairs`).

**Entry (The allowlist's sidestep guard covers ten of sixteen prohibited roots)** · `entry:assayer:wl-resonance-allowlist-sidestep-partial`

**Locus** `src/tests/source_audit.rs:231`, the forbidden-reference list, against `src/tests/source_audit.rs:172`, the permitted-import list. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:derivation:allowlisted-imports`), which makes the import guard the whole of how purity is guarded and prefers it to a behavioural test precisely because "an import guard is a statement about the whole module and fails on the first line that would break it". It is a statement about the module's `use` lines only. The second test exists to close that hole and closes part of it: the import guard permits eight roots and therefore prohibits sixteen, and the sidestep guard enumerates ten. Six roots the import guard prohibits are reachable by a fully qualified path with both tests green — the public interface module, the metrics module, the persistence module, the report module, the serialisation codec and the signal module. **Severity** medium, and the six are not an arbitrary residue. The signal module holds the shared cache the assessment path writes; the persistence module holds the journal and the checkpoint; the public interface module holds the steward's handle. A line in the derivation reaching any of the three by a fully qualified path would break the purity clauses the record's first decision states (`dec:derivation:pure-transform`) and would pass the whole source audit. **What conformance would mean** the forbidden-reference list is derived from the permitted-import list as its complement over the crate's module roots, rather than maintained beside it. **Falsifier, and the mechanism is already in use in the cluster.** The tag module reaches the numerics root by a fully qualified path written mid-expression rather than by an import (`src/resonance/tags.rs:103`). That line names a permitted root, so nothing is wrong today; what it demonstrates is that the construction the second test exists to catch is the cluster's ordinary idiom, and that the import guard cannot see it. Substituting any of the six roots at that line leaves both tests passing. **Sequencing** independent. **Coverage** the guard is its own coverage, and the gap is a gap in the guard. The claim over the second test asserts that no line under the cluster "names a core-model root anywhere" (`claim:audit:the-import-allowlist-cannot-be-sidestepped-by-a-fully-qualified-path`), which is true of the ten enumerated and is read by its first sentence as a statement about the allowlist as a whole.

**Repaired** in wave one at `7358abfc`. The forbidden list is derived: the crate's module roots are read off the source tree — each directory under `src/` and each module file beside them — and the allowlist's complement over them is what the guard checks. The claim now says what the test does, since a hand-kept complement has the same weakness the sidestep guard exists to close, one level up: it polices the roots somebody remembered.

Two departures from the entry's arithmetic, both deliberate. The derived set is seventeen rather than sixteen, because `testing` is a module root of this crate and a derivation reaching the harness would be as much a purity breach as one reaching the journal; the entry's count excluded it. And the test asserts that the allowlist and the forbidden set partition the tree exactly, so that a permitted root which is renamed or deleted fails the guard rather than silently shrinking it — the derivation fails closed in both directions, not only the one the entry asked for.

The committed falsifier the entry names is repaired with it: the tag module's fully qualified reach for the numerics root is routed through an allowlisted import, so the line the entry describes as the cluster's ordinary idiom is no longer written that way. Substituting `crate::signal` at that line was run both ways before this landed. Against the hand-kept list both tests passed, which is the hole exactly as the entry states it; against the derived complement the sidestep guard fails and names the line.

**Entry (The golden suite pins no action-tag placement)** · `entry:assayer:wl-resonance-golden-locations-unpinned`

**Locus** `src/tests/resonance_golden.rs:93` through `:103`, the four action-tag assertions. **Disposition** divergent-code-wrong against the file's own stated purpose and against what the placement algorithm needs from a golden suite (`alg:rendering:placement`). The module documentation says each test "verifies tag parameters (location, magnitude, Q) against spec tables". For the three classification tags it does: location, magnitude and bandwidth are each asserted. For the four action tags only the magnitude is asserted, and the appendix tabulates a location and a bandwidth for every one of them (`ex:rendering:examples`). **Severity** medium. Action magnitudes are regime widths, so the four assertions that do stand pin the crossovers; what they do not pin is the placement built on top of them, which is the appendix's most intricate arithmetic — the crossover-matching offset, its existence test, and the interior midpoint the boundary tags are measured from. A refactor that broke the offset while leaving the crossovers intact would pass the golden suite entirely. **What conformance would mean** the four action rows assert location and bandwidth alongside magnitude, at the appendix's tabulated values and its stated tolerance. **The placement is nonetheless correct today, and this audit verified it by recomputation rather than by the suite** — see the third paragraph after (`tab:assayer:resonance-conformant`). The finding is about what would be caught, not about what is wrong. **Falsifier** overturned by any other test in the package asserting an action tag's location against a corpus value; the crossover suite asserts an identity between two kernels rather than a location (`claim:resonance:adjacent-boundary-tags-meet-at-exactly-the-crossover-the-spec-places-them-around`), and the robustness suite asserts orderings and floors. **Sequencing** rides with (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`). **Coverage** this entry is a coverage finding.

**Remark (What the cluster's tests pin, and what they do not)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-pins`

Fifty-eight tests carry a claim of this area — twelve in the module's own `#[cfg(test)]` blocks, thirty-nine in the crate suite and seven in the integration suite — over forty-seven distinct claims, eleven of the tests citing a claim another test mints. The count is worth stating precisely because the number fifty-eight has been carried into this campaign as a count of claims, and it is a count of tests.

The family is the strongest the audit has met, and three things earn that.

The sufficiency suite compares tag profiles bit for bit rather than by tolerance (`src/tests/resonance_sufficiency.rs:331`), and it pairs every sufficiency assertion with a liveness companion that perturbs one input and requires the profile to move. Both directions are covered for all four kernel scalars and for both host levers. That is the shape the campaign has repeatedly wished for and found missing: an equality test whose passing could be explained by the code reading nothing is worthless, and this suite forecloses the explanation.

The crossover suite's oracle is a recomputation rather than a stored value (`src/tests/resonance_crossover.rs:63`): it derives the crossover logit from the reward differentials independently and asserts the two bounding kernels agree there, over ten thousand draws spanning the non-degenerate envelope. It also states honestly what it does not assert and why — interior crossovers are not matched by construction — and records that the scenario's original framing overstated the guarantee. A test that corrects its own commissioning document is rare enough to note.

The robustness suite brackets a corpus threshold from both sides rather than asserting it once, at the challenge estimate three tenths and four tenths around the tabulated three eighths.

Against that, four pins are weaker than their glosses.

Two glosses claim bit-for-bit agreement over oracles that are absolute tolerances of one machine epsilon — the neutral-zone claim (`claim:resonance:the-reserved-neutral-zone-is-accepted-but-reaches-nothing-in-the-derived-constants`) and the channel-constants claim (`claim:resonance:deriving-the-channel-constants-twice-from-one-policy-gives-the-same-numbers`). At the magnitudes those tests compare, one epsilon of absolute tolerance is tens of units in the last place for the smaller differential and tighter than one for the exponent sum, so the phrase is true of some assertions in the same test and false of others. The sufficiency suite in the same cluster shows what the phrase should mean, comparing raw bit patterns.

The second of those two also compares two of the three fields a differential carries, omitting the analytical derivative, while its gloss says "every reward differential". The omitted field is the one that scales every crossover's bandwidth.

The neutral-zone test pins inertness of the derived-constants helper rather than of the derivation. The transfer happens to hold — the policy reaches the kernel only through the derived constants and the action count — but the test does not establish it, and the property the claim states is about the derivation.

The cold-start floor test's oracle is a disjunction where the specification states a conjunction, and it admits a dominated tag as evidence of a floored one. Recomputed at the test's own inputs, the floor binds for Block and not for Slow, while the corpus says it binds "for Slow and Block" (`def:rendering:bandwidths`); the disjunctive oracle is what makes the test unable to tell the two readings apart. The escape clause is conceded by the notice standing over it (`todo:test:tighten-this-once-the-derivation-path`) and is latent rather than active, Block not being dominated at those inputs.

**Remark (The census: who calls this, and does the corpus want anyone to)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-census`

Asked in the generalised form the register recommends (`reg:assayer:audit-tranches`), the census returns the answer the corpus wants and one it does not.

The derivation function has no production caller anywhere in the package. It is reached from the crate's own documentation example, from the assessment module's test block, and from the test suites. That is a met requirement rather than dead machinery: composing an assessment with a policy is specified to be the host's explicit act, the Core is specified to stop at the risk assessment, and a source audit enforces the absence (`dec:ordering:core-boundary`). The scope of that enforcement is narrower than its claim — it covers the public interface directory, the guidance directory and the assessment module, and not the owner, the model or the health directories, where a call would pass unremarked — but no such call exists.

What the census did return is a public type nothing constructs. The composed assessment-and-derivation result is exported from the crate root and is built at exactly one site, a helper inside the assessment module's own test block. No production path constructs it, no corpus text requires it, and no host can obtain one from any call the crate exports. It is unspecified rather than divergent, and it is a low: a host reading the crate's surface finds a type that looks like the composition the layering describes and cannot get one.

Two smaller census results. The tag enumeration's action predicate has no caller in production, the exploration computation filtering on the negation of the classification predicate instead, under a module-wide dead-code suppression whose comment attributes the deadness to tests and to a layer (`src/resonance/tags.rs:23`). And five of the module's public items are public only within the crate, the module being declared privately at the crate root, which is what makes the validation entry above a finding rather than a preference.

**Data (The cluster's smaller departures)** · `data:assayer:resonance-small-departures`

Six items too small to carry an entry, recorded because the campaign's convention is that nothing is quietly dropped. Each is a low unless stated.

| Departure | Standing | Locus |
| --- | --- | --- |
| The monotonicity pass is written as a two-iteration loop that cannot iterate: the first pass sets every triggering tag to the target values, so the change flag is false on the second and the loop breaks | unspecified; a fixed point reached by construction, documented as an iteration bound | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:523` |
| The classification bandwidth's fallback branch is unreachable, the kernel having already floored the effective spread five orders of magnitude above the branch's threshold | unspecified | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:338` |
| The boundary placement's half-width fallback is unreachable, the only path that reaches it having at least three actions | unspecified | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:497` |
| The classification tag computation clamps the probability a second time, inside a value the kernel has already floored more tightly | unspecified | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:553` |
| The calibration parameter is the one kernel input with no guard; at zero the classification bandwidth becomes zero, which is a boundary of the domain the tag spectrum declares | unspecified, and the risk basis states no positivity constraint on the field | `src/resonance/derivation.rs:336` |
| The challenge-effectiveness decay rate is carried inside the reward structure under a comment calling it a per-channel reward parameter, where the reward table says in terms that it "is not a reward" | divergent-code-wrong against (`tab:channel:reward-parameters`), a comment defect | `src/resonance/channel.rs:70` |
| The derived-constants helper's action-count guard is a debug assertion, and a shorter set reaches an empty crossover vector and panics on the release profile | divergent-code-wrong against (`dec:derivation:pure-transform`), unreachable through the exported surface | `src/resonance/channel.rs:162` |

The cluster's legacy citations — the retired record number and the retired section numbering, carried in every file's cross-reference block — are not recorded as findings here. They stand on the burn registers the documentation campaign built for exactly this, and both registers already count occurrences under this cluster.

**Remark (Corpus prose defects met here)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-prose-defects`

Five, and three of them are the subclass the close-out singles out as the one that misleads rather than merely misinforms: a sentence in which the corpus reports its own conformance and is wrong about it (`rem:assayer:closeout-completeness`).

The purity proof says the shipped derivation "carries a dedicated test that says so" (`pf:landscape:purity`). It carries no test of the derivation's purity at all.

The ambiguity gauge says the shipped code "carries the disclaimer in its documentation in a different form" (`sig:rendering:ambiguity-gauge`). It carries no form of the disclaimer, and an authored claim in the suite asserts the disclaimer's negation.

The dominated-tag treatment says the first of its three triggers is "present in form and starved of the quantity it tests" (`alg:rendering:dominated-treatment`). It is present in no form.

Two further defects are ordinary inaccuracies rather than false conformance reports. The challenge-interaction data says the shipped derivation "reads the point estimate and the variance into its derived constants" (`data:channel:challenge-interaction`); the variance reaches the kernel input and not the constants. And the display floor's binding example says the floor binds "for Slow and Block" at the forfeiture extreme (`def:rendering:bandwidths`); recomputed at the corpus's own stated crossover uncertainty it binds for Block alone, the interior action's bandwidth being formed from two crossover variances of which only one is inflated.

The count deserves a note the campaign should carry forward. This cluster is one of nineteen and produced three of the false-conformance sentences by itself, where the whole read phase before it produced three. The reason is structural rather than accidental: this is the one Part written against a shipped surface the specification openly disagrees with, so nearly every environment closes with a sentence about what ships, and a sentence written to be honest about a divergence is exactly the kind of sentence that goes stale without anyone noticing.

**Remark (The guidance rider)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-guidance-rider`

The public surface tranche measured the guidance cluster against the guidance chapter and the surface record because its record column named a legacy record (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`). The derivation record has since landed and makes a claim about that cluster which no record then made, so its derivation-adjacent dispositions were re-checked here.

The claim is that the partition between the two exploration signals runs both ways: the derivation quantity is computed in this layer "and nowhere else", and "the Core does not consume it either — no Core surface accepts it, reports it, or ranks by it" (`dec:derivation:exploration-layer`). Measured against the guidance module, the claim holds absolutely: not one of the derivation's terms occurs in it — not the cluster's name, not the reckoning type, not the tag type, not exploration, not fragility, not a channel, not a posture, and not entropy. The surface record's opacity decision is confirmed against the new record's stronger phrasing as well as against its own (`dec:surface:guidance-opacity`).

**No disposition changes.** The tranche's three risk-informative rows and its opacity rows stand exactly as written, and the re-check is recorded as verification rather than as amendment. That is the expected outcome and is worth saying plainly: the earlier tranche measured against the specification alone and lost no authority doing it, which is what the scope map claimed for it at the time and what this rider now confirms from the record that was missing.

One thing the new record adds rather than changes, and it belongs to this tranche rather than to the guidance one. The two signals are complements and the record says a host treating them as one "buys the wrong evidence either way" (`rem:derivation:exploration-split`). The Core's half ships as specified. The derivation's half ships as a quantity that is not it, under a name that says it is — which is the entry above (`entry:assayer:wl-exploration-gauge-named-information-value`). So the guidance surface's opacity is intact and the hazard the opacity exists to prevent is live on the other side of the boundary, arriving by naming rather than by coupling.

**Data (What the tranche counted)** · `data:assayer:resonance-tally`

Fifty items dispositioned, and the split is the shape the opening paragraph promised: twenty-seven conformant, thirteen divergent, ten unspecified.

The twenty-seven stand in (`tab:assayer:resonance-conformant`) and are the cluster's arithmetic, its cost model, its placement, its bandwidths, its magnitudes, its per-call recomputation, its ordered-action genericity and the two boundary properties the census confirms.

The thirteen are the eleven entries above and the two comment-and-guard departures at (`data:assayer:resonance-small-departures`). By severity they stand at four high, six medium and three low. The four highs are the silent policy substitution, the display argument, the missing landscape and the gauge named as its own opposite, and they are not independent: the middle two are one architectural repair and the outer two are each a thing the package tells a host that is not true.

The ten unspecified are the five departures at (`data:assayer:resonance-small-departures`) that the corpus settles nothing about, and five more the audit met and could not adjudicate for want of corpus text rather than for want of reading: the allowlist permitting three roots the cluster never reaches, the composed assessment-and-derivation type that no production path constructs, the tag enumeration's action predicate with no production caller, the two input floors the kernel applies that no table fixes, and the kernel's far-tail floor, whose mechanism no environment prescribes while the property it serves is required (`prop:rendering:completeness`).

**Remark (What this tranche settles for entries already standing)** · `rem:assayer:resonance-cross-cites`

Three standing entries gain something from this cluster and none changes its severity.

The Companion's read-decay item argues its severity partly from the estimate being "a third input to the derivation a host acts on" (`entry:assayer:wl-companion-read-decay`). Confirmed at the crossing: the point estimate enters the derived constants on every call and the variance enters the kernel input, so a stale pair moves every crossover location and every crossover variance in one derivation. The direction the entry flags as the dangerous one is confirmed too — a stale variance that is too tight narrows every action bandwidth through the same denominator, and where it would have bound, releases the display floor early.

The builder's missing home for derivation configuration (`entry:assayer:wl-config-unreached-constraints`) has a companion this tranche can name: the Core's configuration structure carries the eight display constants and the reference posture outright, under two notices conceding that both belong on the host-owned derivation configuration — one over the display constants (``todo:code:move-derivation-layer-fields-out-of``) and one over the reference posture (``todo:code:reference-posture-belongs-on``); wave ten discharged both notices by removing the two fields. So the surface had the opposite defect from the one registered — the builder has no place to put derivation configuration, and the configuration structure it builds has a place for it that nothing reads.

The weak-tests census (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) gains four items from this cluster, listed at (`rem:assayer:resonance-pins`), and two of the three authored-claim restatements this cluster needs are ordered by entries above rather than by the census.

## Tranche register · `sec:assayer:audit-tranche-register`

**Register (Audit tranches)** · `reg:assayer:audit-tranches`

Each tranche is entered here when it closes, with the cluster it took, the records it read, and what it found. The register is the audit's own completeness check against the scope map (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`): the audit of (`goal:assayer:audit-complete`) is complete when every row of the scope map appears here.

That check has a second direction and it was never run, which is how the highest-fan-in cluster in the package stayed unmapped through thirteen tranches (`rem:assayer:glue-scope-map`). Asking whether every row appears here cannot ask whether every cluster has a row, and only an enumeration of the source tree can. The close-out ran that direction once by rebuilding the map from the tree, and recommends it be made a check rather than a wave (`rem:assayer:closeout-completeness`). Read in both directions against the rebuilt map, this register was short by exactly one row: `resonance/`, blocked on its record. That row is now entered, and the desideratum (`goal:assayer:audit-complete`) is met for every cluster of the scope map. The completion is a statement about coverage and not about conformance: the last tranche closed with four high findings, which is the desideratum's own point (`goal:assayer:audit-complete`).

The ledger tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-ledger`). Its shape is worth carrying forward as the method's first evidence: two of its five divergences were already labelled notices in the code, two were found by reading the cluster's call sites rather than the cluster itself, and one — the sweep with no caller — was found by asking who invokes a function rather than what the function does. The last is the question a reading of the module alone would never have raised, and every later tranche should ask it explicitly.

The owner and risk tranches are (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-owner`) and (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-risk`). Four things about their shape are worth carrying forward, and the first two qualify the ledger tranche's advice rather than repeating it.

The census question kept paying and stopped paying in the way the ledger tranche predicted. Neither cluster held a sweep with no caller: every function in the owner cluster is reached from production, and the risk cluster's uncalled half is uncalled because the corpus requires it to be. What the question found instead were two new shapes — a trigger with one caller for six specified events, and a surface whose absence of callers is a met requirement — so the question generalises to *who calls this, and does the corpus want anyone to*. Asked only in its first half it would have produced a false finding here, and the founding worklist had already made that mistake once.

The highest-severity findings came from asking what a function does to state another function established. The layout misalignment, the wrong-axis marginalisation, the revert's substituted configuration and the truncated journal are all of that shape: each function is defensible read alone, and each is wrong about what its neighbour left behind. Reading a cluster function by function would have found none of the four.

Corpus text argued the audit's case more often than it resisted it. The specification names the shipped importance weight's departure in its own prose, the eligibility table names the predicate's inversions, and two of the highest-severity owner findings stand over notices that concede them. That is the documentation campaign paying off, and it is also a caution: a corpus this self-aware makes it cheap to mistake a conceded defect for an adjudicated one, which is why every conceded item here was still measured rather than transcribed.

Coverage is now the campaign's structural finding rather than an incidental one. Between them these two tranches met four more tests asserting a divergence as their expected value and three standing hollow over properties they are named for, including both tests covering the owner cluster's central decision. Two founding entries' coverage lines were wrong in the same direction and are corrected. The consequence for sequencing is concrete: several conformance repairs cannot land without first rewriting a test and its authored claim, so the repair wave and the test-authoring wave (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) are not independent and should not be scheduled as though they were.

The feature and extraction tranches are (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-feature`) and (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-extraction`). They were taken together because the scope map assigns them the same record and because the second produces the block the first assembles, and four things about their shape are worth carrying.

The extension-order question is closed. The owner tranche's (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-layout-misalignment`) named two possible repairs and deferred the choice to whichever tranche owned the dimension map; that tranche has now read it and the answer is permutation, argued at (`rem:assayer:feature-extension-order`) from the corpus's own allocation sentence and from the compaction the corpus already requires. The severity and the locus are unchanged. What this settles is that the repair is a generalisation of machinery the package already has rather than a new allocation rule, which moves it from the largest of the outstanding repairs to something closer to the middle.

The census question's yield is now predictable, and the prediction is worth more than any single result it returned. These two clusters bracket the range: the feature cluster holds three public functions with no production caller, one of them the package's own implementation of a specified algorithm that the label path reimplements worse, and the extraction cluster holds none at all. The difference tracks fan-in rather than care — a cluster with one caller has no room for uncalled machinery, and a cluster that many paths reach has a great deal. That gives the remaining tranches a place to aim, recorded at (`rem:assayer:extraction-census`).

Two of the campaign's repairs are smaller than they were registered as being, and for the same reason. The ledger sweep needed a call rather than a cadence (`rem:assayer:owner-no-cadence`); the label-time reconstruction needs a call rather than an implementation (`rem:assayer:feature-census`). That is twice now that a registered entry's **What conformance would mean** has been written against the production path in isolation and has shrunk once the cluster that owns the mechanism was read. Every remaining entry whose repair reads as "implement X" should be re-checked against the cluster that would own X before it is scheduled.

And the corpus's prose is now the campaign's second structural finding. Three further defects join the three already recorded — a class count that disagrees with the table beneath it, a commentary that reasons from a formula its own table does not state, and four axes named two ways — and one earlier entry was itself overstated and is corrected. None of the six changes a disposition, because prose defects sit outside the grammar by construction. Together they say something the record pipeline should hear: the specification is accurate enough that the audit can lean on it, and inaccurate often enough in its *commentary* that leaning on a sentence rather than on a table has twice nearly produced a false finding.

The health and metrics tranches are (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-health`) and (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-metrics`). They were taken together because the second exports the first, and four things about their shape are worth carrying.

The census question's two halves came apart here, and the wave is the argument for keeping both. The metrics cluster holds the largest block of uncalled machinery the campaign has met — both mapper entry points and, through them, the whole of the mapper module — and it is not a defect, because the record requires the caller to be outside the package. The health cluster holds one uncalled method that is indistinguishable from those by call-site census and is this wave's most serious finding, because the corpus requires it to have a caller. Two clusters audited in one wave, the same evidence, opposite verdicts, and only the corpus separates them. The prediction the extraction tranche made from fan-in (`rem:assayer:extraction-census`) was right to aim here and wrong about why: the yield came from the clusters' *contracts*, not their shape.

The severity argument had to be made against the report-only posture rather than around it. Everything in these clusters is a diagnostic, and on the campaign's usual reading — a number a host acts on — that would cap the whole wave at medium. It does not, because the corpus is explicit that the loop closes at the host (`inv:monitoring:report-only`) and the catalogue itself prescribes the alerts a host should build. A diagnostic that reports a reassuring value it has no evidence for is therefore a number acted on by design, which is the argument at (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`) and at (`entry:assayer:wl-health-sync-error-constant`). Later tranches over report-only surfaces should expect to make this argument rather than assume the cap.

A registered repair got bigger for the first time. Twice now the campaign has found a registered entry's conformance line to be an overestimate once the owning cluster was read — the ledger sweep at (`rem:assayer:owner-no-cadence`) and the label-time reconstruction at (`rem:assayer:feature-census`) — and the register drew the general advice that every "implement X" repair should be re-checked against the cluster that would own X. This wave supplies the counter-instance: (`entry:assayer:wl-empirical-coverage`) says the diagnostic can be computed from the rows the function already holds, and the rows do not carry the uncertainty it divides by, so conformance also means widening a persisted type. The advice survives with its direction removed — re-check the repair against the owning cluster, in both directions.

Corpus prose is now implicated in a disposition rather than only recorded beside one. Four further defects join the six already registered, and one of them — the dimension-health table's claim that two of six rows are absent when none of the six carries information — would have caused this tranche to record its largest finding as a conceded two-row gap. Every previous prose defect changed nothing; this one would have changed a verdict. The rule the feature tranche drew from its near miss should harden accordingly: a corpus sentence reporting what the code does is evidence to be checked, never evidence to be cited, and this wave found four such sentences wrong in two chapters.

The persistence tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-persistence`). It is the first cluster the specification does not discuss at all, and four things about its shape are worth carrying.

A cluster can be governed almost wholly by one record, and the scope map did not say so. The map's specification column named a chapter that does not mention this cluster's subject in any casing, and the correction is at (`rem:assayer:persistence-scope-map`). Taken with the health tranche's two files, three of the map's rows are now known wrong on the specification side, and none was found by noticing a gap — each was found by going to the named chapter and finding nothing there. The remaining tranches should read their row's chapter before trusting it, and whoever amends the map should check the column entire rather than the three rows this campaign reached.

The census question found what it was aimed at and something it was not built to find. Aimed at fan-in it returned one uncalled constructor that the corpus wants called, which is the smallest yield of any low-fan-in cluster and confirms the health tranche's correction that contract rather than shape predicts the answer. What it was not built to find is a function that *is* called, on every restore, over input that is always empty — some thirty lines of restore-time decay whose collections the write site never populates and both readers discard. Neither a call-site census nor a compiler can see it, and only reading the write path does.
