Later tranches should ask the question in a third form wherever a cluster moves data between two others: not only who calls this and does the corpus want them to, but does anything ever put anything into it.

The highest-severity finding again came from a function being wrong about what its neighbour left behind, and this time the two neighbours are three lines apart. The warm-start branch disables the batch initialisation because the comment above it says the standardisation statistics were restored, and the comment below it says they are not checkpointed (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`). The register has recorded this shape between two files and between two clusters; recording it inside one comment block is a caution about how little distance the failure needs. It also means the disclosure a reader relies on can be self-refuting rather than merely absent, which is a worse thing to meet than silence.

And a correction ran toward a smaller repair for the first time. The register's advice after the health wave was to re-check an "implement X" repair against the cluster that owns X, in both directions; applied here to a *correction* rather than to a founding entry, it found that the calibration entry the health tranche called a persisted type is serialisable and not persisted (`rem:assayer:persistence-cross-cites`). Nothing is carried on faith from an earlier wave (`conv:assayer:audit-evidence`), and the audit has now had to apply that rule to its own corrections as well as to the items it inherited. The narrowing is worth stating for the tranches still to come: a prior tranche's cross-citation is a claim about code, checkable in the same way and to the same standard as any other.

The snapshot and pending tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-snapshot-pending`). Four things about its shape are worth carrying, and the first is a caution about this register itself.

The scope map is worse than the campaign had established, and its record column is now implicated as well as its specification column. The pending row names a chapter that does not mention the cluster's subject in any casing, which makes four such rows found by three tranches. The snapshot row names two records that do govern the cluster and omits the one that decides its central design question, which is the first time the map's *record* column has been found wanting and means the column cannot be assumed checked either. The correction is at (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-scope-map`). A tranche reading only the snapshot row's sources would not have been short of authority; it would have had the wrong authority, and would have adjudicated the cluster's most central function backwards.

The census question's third form is the one that paid, and it has a mirror the persistence tranche did not meet. Asked in its first two forms these clusters return almost nothing — one constructor with no caller anywhere and two convenience surfaces. Asked as *does anything ever put anything into it*, the pending cluster returns a field filled on every assessment and read by nothing. Asked as its mirror — does anything ever take anything out of it, and what happens on the path where the answer is no — it returns the mechanism by which the campaign's sharpest replay finding is silent: an accessor returning an option that is empty for exactly one variant, so the caller's branch is skipped rather than failed (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-census`). Later tranches should ask both.

Corpus prose has now been wrong about a *design* rather than about a status, and the two halves of the corpus have been found stating incompatible arrangements. The concurrency chapter describes the snapshot's publication as componentised, with independent pointers per block and unchanged components shared rather than copied; two records decide the opposite and one of them names the chapter's description as the alternative it rejected. Every previous prose defect would have added a false row or, once, changed a verdict; this one would have turned the cluster's one exactly-correct central function into the tranche's largest finding. What settles it is a division of authority the records state themselves — the specification defines the snapshot and states the guarantees, the record decides the arrangement — and that division is the rule the remaining tranches should apply wherever a chapter describes a mechanism rather than requiring a property (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-prose-defects`).

And a repair correction ran upward for the second time, with the first enlargement's fate in view. The register's advice after the persistence wave was that a prior tranche's cross-citation is a claim about code like any other; applied here to the feature tranche's finding that the label-time reconstruction needs a call rather than an implementation, it finds the call necessary and insufficient, because three of the algorithm's six sources read values the pending entry never stored (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`). The correction states its own falsifier rather than asserting itself, which is the discipline the first enlargement lacked. The same remark also returns a third direction the advice had not anticipated: two entries registered as independent repairs turn out to share one prerequisite, so re-checking a repair against the owning cluster can make repairs *fewer* as well as larger or smaller.

The public surface and guidance tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-api-guidance`). Four things about its shape are worth carrying, and the first closes the case against the scope map.

Both columns of the map are now established as unchecked, and the two rows this tranche took fail in one column each. The `api/` row names an appendix whose subject is the Sentinel and graph boundaries and which enumerates no call a host makes, which is the fifth row found wrong on the specification side; its record column names two records where five govern, which is the second found wanting on the record side. The `guidance/` row is the first whose record column names a record that is not corpus at all — one of the three held out of the campaign — so a tranche trusting it would have measured against a document the campaign's own measure convention excludes. The correction is at (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-scope-map`). Four tranches have now checked their own rows and four have amended them; whoever rebuilds the map should treat it as unverified rather than as partly verified.

The census question paid nothing in its first two forms and everything in its last two, and the split is explained by the clusters' shape in a way that completes the health tranche's correction. `api/` is the highest-fan-in cluster in the package and holds no uncalled machinery whatever, because every function on it is either a public method or a helper of one — so the prediction from fan-in the extraction tranche made is confirmed at the extreme, and the health tranche's correction that contract rather than shape predicts the *yield* is confirmed with it, since the contract here demands that everything be reachable. What the third and fourth forms returned instead were two entries the first two forms cannot see: a helper called on every seeded entry that constructs a value with nothing in it (`entry:assayer:wl-preseed-degenerate-vector`), and a public field written on every path and read on none (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-census`). The four forms should now be asked as a set rather than as a sequence, because at this cluster the first two would have reported a clean census.

A prior tranche's cross-citation is a claim about code, and this wave had to apply that rule to line numbers as well as to substance. Two of the ledger tranche's three citations for (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-absence-cast`) resolve to unrelated lines in the current tree, and one of the founding worklist's loci is a line below the value it names. In both cases the substantive finding was re-derived rather than transcribed and both stand, which is the outcome the evidence convention is built to produce; what the register should carry forward is that a line citation into a file a later tranche owns is the piece most likely to have rotted, and the owning tranche is the one positioned to repair it. Corrections of that kind belong in the owning tranche's cross-cites remark and state their own falsifier there.

And the class the ninth tranche opened has recurred at the same magnitude, which makes it a pattern rather than an incident. A specification chapter requires a label-queue overflow policy that two records forbid and argues explicitly against the policy the code has; trusting the chapter would have turned the label surface's error handling into a divergence against a requirement, when three records built the surface around exactly that handling (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-prose-defects`). Twice now the corpus's two halves have stated incompatible arrangements, and twice the chapter has been the half that was wrong. The division of authority the ninth tranche derived is now load-bearing for two of the campaign's largest verdicts, and the remaining tranches should apply it as a first step rather than as a tie-break: read the record before deciding that a chapter's description of a mechanism is a requirement about one.

The posterior and substrate tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-model-linalg`). Five things about its shape are worth carrying, and the first inverts a rule the register was about to treat as settled.

The division of authority is not a rule about chapters. Twice the corpus's two halves had stated incompatible arrangements and twice the chapter had been the wrong half, and the register's advice after the tenth tranche was to read the record first. Applied here that advice inverts: the record forbids what the specification's remedy permits and prices, and the record is the half whose own premise its counterpart falsifies — it calls a marginalisation rare where the chapter calls the same event frequent, and its prohibition would erase a row the cost table states as a distinct class (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-refactor-knot`). What is actually load-bearing is neither half's identity but the specification's tables: in all three collisions the half the tables support has been the right one, and in this one that half was the chapter. The advice should be restated as "find which half the tables support" before the next tranche applies it in the form the tenth tranche left it in, because in that form it would have produced a divergence here against code that is conformant.

Recomputation earned its cost and refuted as much as it found. Four claims turned on algebra; recomputing them confirmed the update and the half-solve exact, converted one apparent defect into a comment defect with the code conformant beneath it, and withdrew a registered divergence outright when the configuration reference's own note proved the absolute regularisation term is the specified product at the tabulated prior (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-prose-defects`). That withdrawal is the third near-miss from leaning on a sentence rather than a table and the first where the sentence was right and merely incomplete, which is a new subclass: a corpus statement that is true of the shipped defaults and silent about the tunable underneath them. Two of the tranche's conformant verdicts were likewise within a line of becoming findings and are recorded with the falsifiers that would overturn them (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-cleared`).

The corpus argued the audit's case at the two highest-severity findings and did so in its own unprompted prose. The positive-definiteness requirement states the spectral test, then explains why the diagonal test the code ships fails precisely in the regime the surrounding machinery exists to serve, and closes by saying that such a test passes the cases it was meant to catch. The synchronisation error's definition says it has one site deliberately because a norm and a diagonal maximum are quantities a restatement can silently interchange, and then reports that the shipped system interchanged them. Neither finding needed an argument this audit had to construct. The caution the owner tranche attached to this pattern applies undiminished: both were measured rather than transcribed, and the second turned out to be further along than the corpus said, since the specified norm is already written in this package as a test helper nine hundred lines from the substitute.

The census's fourth form returned the tranche's most consequential single fact, and it was about a deferral rather than about the code. A diagnostics type carries four fields, is written at seven sites, and has exactly one field read in production; the three unread ones include the distinction a deferred counter exists to count (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-cross-cites`). That makes the Schur skip counter the third registered repair to shrink once the owning cluster was read — after the ledger sweep and the label-time reconstruction — and the pattern is now regular enough to invert into a prediction: a deferral whose completion trigger reads "instrument path X" should be checked for whether X already computes the quantity and drops it, before it is scheduled as instrumentation. The same remark returns a hazard the register could not otherwise see, that closing the counter as its trigger is worded would aggregate two different events under one name.

And the coverage finding is a new kind. Alongside a third test pinning a divergence as its expected value and a fifth byte-identical pair, this tranche met a test with no assertion whose oracle is a debug assertion inside the code it calls — real under `cargo test`, absent under `cargo test --release`, and invisible from its name, its body and its authored claim (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-pins`). It also met the opposite of a weak test: a cascade test handed over as weak that proves exactly its own claim, beside a record decision — that regularisation stays exceptional because the recomputation policy reads the healthy-or-repaired distinction — that no test in either cluster pins at all. Both belong to the authoring wave rather than to the repair wave, and together they say the weak-test census should be re-run asking what each decision has a test for, not only what each test has an oracle for.

The identity and signal tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-identity-signal`). Five things about its shape are worth carrying, and the first is the campaign's sharpest single result.

A copy of a correct function, with one conditional inverted, has stood in the package's other spatial store since it was written. The identity cell's adverse rate averages the complement of the indicator the corpus names, and the Ledger's line-for-line twin — same name, same three steps, same predicate feeding it — averages the right one under a comment stating the convention (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-adverse-rate-inverted`). The audit did not have to construct the argument; the sister store adjudicated it, and the memory record's own reason for governing both stores at once (`dec:memory:key-types-distinct`) is what put the two functions side by side to be compared. The lesson for the remaining tranches is narrower than "read the neighbour": where a record governs two things because they raise the same questions, the two implementations are each other's oracle, and diffing them costs less than measuring either against the corpus.

The corpus's numbers are checkable and two of them were wrong in the code by factors nobody would have estimated. Recomputation cost a fifth of a second and returned the graph decay's shipped half-life at eleven and a half hours against a tabulated fortnight (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-decay-cadence`) and the shipped volatility inflated by four hundred against its tabulated rule (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-measurement-rules`). Both hid behind constants that look plausible read alone — a decay factor of 0.999 and a smoothing complement of one twentieth — and both are visible the moment the rate is composed with the period the code applies it over. Every remaining tranche meeting a rate should compose it with its cadence before believing it.

The census's fourth form has become the campaign's most productive question and its most treacherous. It returned two fields in one directory, written on every assessment and read in production by nothing: one is the divisor the corpus names for a feature and is a divergence (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-coverage-depth-divisor`), the other is a step count the corpus tabulates and gives no feature to, and is nothing at all (`rem:assayer:identity-census`). Only the feature table separates them. Asked in the third form the same wave produced its first false negative — the measurement state reads as complete and empty from inside its own directory, and is written from outside it — so the four forms must now be asked outward as well as as a set.

Two of this tranche's own claims were refuted by its own checks, and both are recorded rather than quietly dropped. A correction to the thresholds deferral was drafted and withdrawn when the record's completion trigger proved to enumerate exactly the four missing budget parameters and correctly omit the one that ships; and a scope-map claim that the signal cluster has no governing record was withdrawn when the retention record proved to hold two decisions about nothing else. The first withdrawal is recorded at (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-cross-cites`) and the second at (`rem:assayer:identity-signal-scope-map`). The register has asked since the persistence wave that a prior tranche's cross-citation be treated as a claim about code; what this wave adds is that a tranche's *own* draft is one too, and that the discipline is cheapest applied before the ink dries.

And the most conformant cluster the campaign has audited was audited anyway, which is the finding. `signal/` returns one divergence in nineteen checks, its shipped default agrees with its table where the founding worklist's six do not, and seven of its properties are cleared with falsifiers (`rem:assayer:signal-cleared`). The one divergence is the one nothing in the cluster could have caught, because it is about what the cluster declines to refuse rather than about what it computes: a structural violation the corpus assigns to the fallible surface passes it and surfaces as a panic on the call the corpus promises is infallible (`entry:assayer:wl-signal-declaration-unvalidated`). A cluster can be right about every value it produces and wrong about the inputs it accepts, and no amount of checking the first finds the second.

The report, configuration and numerics tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-report-config`). Four things about its shape are worth carrying, and the first is a warning about the map rather than the code.

A scope-map row can send a tranche to a document that is not prose. The `report/` row's specification column names "the assembly chapter", and the file of that name is the build manifest — a list of which parts assemble into the published document. Previous specification-column failures were chapters that said nothing, said half, or were miscounted; this one is not a chapter, and a tranche that trusted the row would have reported the cluster unspecified with no way of noticing it had been misdirected. The check that catches this is cheap and should now be standard: before measuring against a named chapter, confirm the document states a property of something. Six rows are now known wrong on the specification side and five on the record side, and one row — `config/` — has been positively confirmed correct in both, which is worth recording precisely because it is the first.

Where two corpus texts disagree, the audit's presumption needs a tie-break and the tie-break is specificity. The properties table says the Core normalises a coordinate's width on reception and the encoding chapter says the Core does not perform the lift at all (`entry:assayer:wl-report-width-lift-contradiction`). The code obeys the second, so (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`) cannot be applied mechanically: its presumption is that the code is wrong, and here the code is right and one of the two documents is. The discharge is that the table's own citation contradicts the table, which is the general form — when the corpus disagrees with itself, follow each text's citations and the stale party is usually the one whose authority does not support it.

Bounding a shortfall below is not measuring it. The tenth tranche left the third clause of the construction decision registered as false "of at least" one parameter; measuring it returned nineteen of forty-three (`entry:assayer:wl-config-unreached-constraints`), and the same measurement refuted that entry's finding that the decision's first clause holds exactly. Two tranches had read the same builder line and disagreed because one asked whether a validating call is present and the other asked what it validates. The second question is the one that decides a clause.

And a claim can carry a falsifier that does not compute. The depth-overflow test's authored claim offers a reader the wrong arithmetic — a narrowing to a byte does not reduce two hundred modulo one hundred and twenty-eight (`entry:assayer:wl-ingestion-depth-wrap-arithmetic`) — while the test, the guard and the shipped behaviour are all correct. The testing-documentation policy asks a claim to state the failure its test rules out, and this is a new way for that to fail: not a missing oracle, but a stated hazard the types make impossible. Every remaining wave reading a claim with a number in it should recompute the number, which for this one cost a moment and settled it.

The crate-glue tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-glue`). It is the last unblocked cluster and the highest fan-in one, and five things about its shape are worth carrying — the first of which is about this register rather than about the code.

The completeness check the register performs cannot detect a missing row. Six rows were known wrong on the specification side and five on the record side, and every one of those was found by a tranche reading its own row; the check that finds them all is "does every row appear here", and it returns success for a cluster with no row. This cluster had none. Five files, the crate's entire public surface and the one function the specification writes a nine-step algorithm for, and the audit could have declared itself complete without them (`rem:assayer:glue-scope-map`). The close-out should rebuild the map by enumerating the source tree rather than by re-reading the map.

The corpus reports its own conformance, and where it does it should be read as a claim rather than as a settlement. The assessment operations chapter closes three environments with a sentence asserting the code realises them — the write set "shipped as enumerated", the pipeline "shipped step for step", the signature and context "shipped exactly" — and all three are false (`rem:assayer:glue-prose-defects`). This is a new subclass of the campaign's oldest structural finding and a more dangerous one, because the previous defects were the corpus being wrong about a fact and these are the corpus being wrong about the code's agreement with itself. A tranche that took a shipped-claim as evidence would have recorded the cluster's two highest findings as conformant, with the chapter's own sentence as its citation.

Fan-in predicts the yield of the first census form and inverts the fourth. The tenth tranche predicted that a high-fan-in cluster holds no uncalled machinery because everything on it is a public method or a helper of one; the glue is higher fan-in still and returns three uncalled items rather than none, because its contract is to *be* the public surface, so an export is reachable by a host whether or not anything in the package reaches it. The fourth form then returned the cluster's most instructive fact and it is about a surface rather than a field: a `Default` implementation that reads the wall clock, on a type deriving four equality and ordering traits, reached from nowhere in the package and waiting on a public surface (`rem:assayer:glue-census`). At a library's edge the census question needs a fifth form — not only who calls this, but who *could*.

The discipline of treating a draft as a claim about code paid against this tranche's own work, and the cheapest form of it is not the one the register has been recommending. A draft correction to the batch initialisation's discard was written and withdrawn, not because a check refuted it but because re-reading the entry did: the entry already named the mechanism the draft proposed to supply (`rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`). What the re-verification did return is a refuted citation inside the standing entry, and it runs against this tranche's interest — the capacity the entry cites as a tunable parameter is a literal, so the only mitigation its severity paragraph implies does not exist. A cross-citation can be wrong in the direction that makes its own entry worse, and a wave that only looks for corrections favourable to its findings will miss those.

And the package contains the repair for its own largest finding, written by another cluster, in a comment citing the same record. Thirteen lock acquisitions on the infallible assessment path panic on poison (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-poisoned-lock-panics`); the signal cache, which that same path calls, recovers from poison under a comment reading "assessment path never panics". The twelfth tranche's rule was that where a record governs two things because they raise the same question, the two implementations are each other's oracle. This wave extends it across a different seam — between a cluster and the glue that calls it — and the extension is where the rule pays most, because the glue is what every cluster's discipline has to survive being called from.

The derivation tranche is (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`). It is the last cluster and the only one taken after the close-out, and four things about its shape are worth carrying — three of which qualify what the register has already learned rather than adding to it.

Holding a cluster for its record was the right call and the evidence is the row it produced. The map's own history says six specification columns were wrong and that each was found by a tranche going to the named chapter (`rem:assayer:audit-scope-map-history`); this row is the only one the campaign had the chance not to get wrong, and the reading found that the governing text is an appendix no earlier guess would have named, that three further records reach in, and that a whole Part is in scope. What the delay cost was one tranche's wait. What it bought was the campaign's one derivation performed with the record in hand rather than reconstructed afterwards.

The shipped-claim subclass is not the glue's alone and is worse where the corpus is most honest. The glue tranche found three sentences in which the corpus reports its own conformance and is wrong; this cluster found three more by itself (`rem:assayer:resonance-prose-defects`), and the reason is structural. The decision-landscape Part is the one Part written against a shipped surface the specification openly disagrees with, so nearly every environment closes with a sentence about what ships — and a sentence written to be honest about a divergence is exactly the sentence that goes stale unnoticed. The lesson for the record pipeline is sharper than "read shipped claims as claims": the density of false shipped claims is highest exactly where the corpus has taken most trouble to be candid, which is the opposite of where a reader would look for them.

A record can miscount a conformance as a divergence, and the census question has a sixth form. The derivation record listed four gaps between the shipped surface and the decided one and one of the four is conformance (`data:assayer:resonance-gap-count`) — a policy field the derivation does not read, which the specification requires the derivation not to read. From the code a field nothing reads and a field nothing *should* read are the same observation. So the census question, having generalised to *who calls this, and does the corpus want anyone to*, generalises once more for fields: *what reads this, and does the corpus want anything to*. Asked in its first half alone it produces a false finding, which is how the record produced one.

The strongest test family in the campaign is in this cluster, and what makes it strong is one habit worth naming. Every sufficiency assertion is paired with a liveness companion that perturbs one input and requires the output to move (`rem:assayer:resonance-pins`). An equality test whose passing could be explained by the code reading nothing establishes nothing, and the campaign has met that shape repeatedly; this suite forecloses the explanation for all four kernel scalars and both host levers. The habit costs one test per property and should be the pattern the test-authoring residue is written to.

**Register (What binds to what)** · `reg:assayer:audit-sequencing`

The read phase left its dependencies in the wrong place. A tranche that found one wrote it into its own prose — a cross-cites remark, a register paragraph — and the entries the dependency binds went on reading as independent repairs. A reader planning a campaign from the worklist alone would therefore have scheduled several repairs that cannot be scheduled apart, and would have found out by trying. The close-out recorded each relation on both entries it joins (`conv:assayer:worklist-grammar`) and consolidates them here, so the register performs a second completeness check beside its first: every relation named in a tranche's prose appears on the entries it binds.

| Relation | Binds | Why it is forced |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Parent and children | (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`) over (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-checkpoint-truncation`), (`entry:assayer:wl-health-checkpoint-drops-drift`), (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-axis-fields`), (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`) | Four clusters each found a different hole in one payload. Each child closes when the payload gains the field it names, so they are one structural repair seen four times. |
| One prerequisite, three dependants | The relocation of the standardisation statistics onto the working copy (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-cross-cites`), under (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`), (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`), (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-lifecycle-standardisation`) | The statistics live on the owner and the payload is built from the working copy. Nothing downstream can move until they do, and moving them once serves all three. |
| Ordered | (`entry:assayer:wl-sync-error-diagonal`) before (`entry:assayer:wl-health-sync-error-constant`), and before the deferral (`entry:posterior:sync-visibility`) | Exporting the quantity the code holds would publish the wrong number under the specified name. The wrong number must become the right one before anything publishes it. |
| Paired, and asymmetric | (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-checkpoint-truncation`) with (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-seq-restart`) | One defect seen at two distances. The sequence half's repair removes the consequence on its own and can land first; the truncation half removes the precondition. Closing only the truncation leaves the consequence reachable by the other route. |
| Ordered | (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`) after the clock lane's carve-out (`rem:assayer:health-clock-lane`) | Wiring the trackers turns two dormant wall-clock reads into live ones on the composite. Repairing the wiring first ships a non-deterministic stage. |
| Ordered | (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`) after (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`) | Three of the reconstruction's six sources read values the corpus requires to be stored, and they are three of the fields the pending entry does not carry. |

Two remarks about the shape of this table rather than its contents. The first is that every relation in it was discovered by a tranche reading a cluster *other* than the one that registered the entry — the dependency is visible from the mechanism's owner and invisible from the defect's site, which is the same asymmetry that made the census question pay. The second is that the relations run in both directions of size: the payload's four children make one wave out of four repairs, and the reconstruction's ordering makes two waves out of what was registered as one. Consolidating them is what lets the repair waves below be sequenced off mechanisms rather than off severity.

**Remark (What the consistency sweep found, and what it confirmed)** · `rem:assayer:closeout-consistency`

Every worklist entry was re-read against the tranches that came after it, asking one question: does the severity still match the facts, now that later clusters have corrected them? The answer is very largely yes, and the reason is a rule the campaign adopted early and kept — severity is read off the consequence and not off the effort (`conv:assayer:worklist-grammar`). Repairs were re-sized repeatedly during the read phase, in both directions and sometimes twice, and because severity never tracked repair size, none of that re-sizing disturbed a severity. A campaign that had graded by effort would have had to re-grade the worklist entire.

One severity argument had gone stale and is corrected in place. The batch initialisation's discard (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`) argued its severity partly from the command channel being a fixed-capacity *parameter*, and the glue tranche refuted that citation: the capacity is a literal a host cannot reach (`rem:assayer:glue-batch-init-reverified`). The level does not move — it was high and stays high — but the paragraph was inviting a reader toward a mitigation that does not exist, which is the kind of defect this sweep exists to catch. A refuted citation inside a standing entry is worth more attention than a refuted entry, because nothing about it looks wrong.

One entry's facts moved under it and the entry is amended rather than closed. Report staleness (`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`) is the item carved out of the freeze, and the carve-out lane has repaired one of its two ends. The entry now says which end, and says that the severity is unchanged on both counts. An entry half-repaired by a lane the freeze permits is a state the worklist had no way to express, and expressing it is cheaper than deciding whether the entry has become a different entry.

One entry does not carry a severity clause and is right not to. (`entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`) holds six departures that share a notice and a locus, and grades each in a column of its own table rather than grading the entry. That is the correct treatment and it is recorded here because a mechanical read of the worklist cannot see it: a reader counting severity clauses will find one fewer than there are entries, and the missing one is a table.


## Close-out · `sec:assayer:audit-closeout`

The read phase ended with the crate glue and every cluster but `resonance/` stood audited; that cluster has since been taken against the record it was waiting for (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`), so every cluster stands audited and this section's four pieces of work were done against a record one tranche shorter than the one that now stands. Where the last tranche moved something below, it says so in place. This section is what the glue tranche recommended in place of more reading: not more reading, but the work that turns a read record into an instrument a repair campaign can be planned from. Four things happen here. The scope map was rebuilt from the source tree (`tab:assayer:audit-scope-map`). Every locus in the record was re-resolved against the tree as it now stands. The worklist's severities and its sequencing relations were swept for consistency across the tranches that corrected each other. And the repairs are proposed as waves, sequenced off the mechanisms they share, each cut so that it can be ruled on whole.

**Remark (Every locus re-resolved against the tree the audit moved under)** · `rem:assayer:closeout-locus-sweep`

The tree moved under the audit twice while it ran, both times under the freeze's own standing exceptions: the clock wave threaded an injected clock through the engine, which is roadmap infrastructure the freeze carves out, and four notice corrections rewrote labelled to-do notices as notices. Every line below an insertion shifted, and a citation written before a shift now names a line the record did not mean. The audit's evidence convention makes a locus half of what a finding is (`conv:assayer:audit-evidence`), so a rotted locus is not a cosmetic defect: it is half a finding gone.

The sweep is mechanical and states its own method so a reader can re-run it. Every path-and-line citation in this record was extracted, including the bare continuations that inherit their file from the citation before them. For each one, the commit at which that sentence was written was read off the record's own history, the cited line was read as it stood in that commit, and the same line number was read at the tree's head. Identical text is a locus that still holds; different text is drift, and the original text was then searched for at the head to supply the corrected number.

Measured 2026-08-20 against the close-out branch: one thousand and seventy-one loci checked, one thousand and twenty-six still exact, forty-five drifted. Of the forty-five, forty-four are corrected in place above and the corrections are verified by recomputation — each new line was read and compared against the text its citation was written against, and all forty-four match exactly. The forty-fifth is not drift at all and is the sweep's one false positive, recorded because the shape will recur: `src/assessment.rs:1020` still holds the notice the glue tranche cites, and what changed is the notice's *text*, which that tranche changed itself and quotes in the past tense (`rem:assayer:glue-notice-correction`). A method that compares line content cannot tell a line that moved from a line that was deliberately rewritten in place, and both are corrections this campaign is entitled to make.

Two of the drifted loci carry more than a number, and neither changes a disposition. `src/report/ingestion.rs` now stamps report arrival from the injected clock rather than from the standard library's own call, which repairs one half of (`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`) and leaves the half the entry is actually about untouched — the staleness subtraction still leaves the timestamp interface and still reapplies the ceiling by hand. And `src/assessment.rs` now takes its per-request persistent reading through the shared state rather than through the timestamp type's own constructor, which changes how (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-timestamp-split`) is spelled and not what it says: the reading is still per-request, still inside a function that already receives the batch head's instant, and still what the Ledger decay and the pending entry consume. Both entries are re-stated at their corrected loci above.

The sweep found a second kind of rot the drift comparison could not have, and found it by resolving the citations rather than by comparing them. A bare continuation — a line number written alone, inheriting its file from the citation before it — can inherit the wrong file, and then it resolves silently into a module the record did not mean. Four such continuations stood in this record, three in the glue tranche's cross-cites where the sentence names the public entry point and then continues with three line numbers belonging to the assessment module, and one where the sentence names a test file and the nearest citation carrying a line names the source file beside it. All four are now spelled with their file. Only one of the four also carried a wrong number, and that is the point: three of them resolved to real lines in the wrong file, which no comparison of line content can catch and only resolution can.

The falsifier for this sweep, on the eleventh tranche's discipline: it is overturned by any commit touching `packages/assayer/src` after the measurement above, because a locus is a claim about a tree and this one names its tree. It is overturned in a second way by a citation the extraction did not see — a locus written outside a double-backtick span, or a range whose end is spelled in prose rather than as a number — and the reader who finds one has found a gap in the method rather than an exception to it. The freeze is what makes the first falsifier cheap: while it holds, the loci here can only rot from the two carved-out exceptions, and both are recorded.

**Remark (Every displayed label converted to a citation that resolves)** · `rem:assayer:closeout-conversion`

The audit ran against a calculus that could not yet reach the labels it most needed to point at. A notice is minted in Rust commentary and a claim is minted over a test, and while resolution ran over prose alone, a record that wanted to name one had to *display* it — a double-backtick span, inert, with a sentence beside it doing in words what a citation does mechanically. This record carried some fifty such spans and the testing plan carried one under an apology. Both are now citations.

The conversion is worth more than tidiness and the reason is what the audit itself kept finding. A displayed label is a transcription, and this campaign has now been wrong about transcriptions often enough to distrust them on principle: two of the ledger tranche's citations resolved to unrelated lines, four tranches in a row met the label grammar and lost a run to it, and the whole locus sweep above exists because a citation that names a line cannot notice that the line moved. A displayed notice label is the same hazard one level up — a notice can be renamed, reworded or retired, and a record displaying its old name says nothing false loudly enough to be caught. As a citation it is checked on every run.

Measured 2026-08-20 on the close-out branch: fifty-four spans converted across forty-five distinct labels in this record, and one in the testing plan, and every one of them resolves — the check's resolved-citation count rose by exactly the number converted and its failure count stayed at nothing. Forty-three of the labels are notices standing in code and two are claims standing over tests, which is the point of the capability rather than an incidental split: one resolution space, blind to kind and to surface.

No dead label was found, and the absence is evidence rather than luck. Every displayed label in this record was checked against the mints standing in the tree before any conversion ran, so a label naming a notice that had been renamed out from under the record would have been reported here and left displayed with a note. None was. That is a stronger result than it looks, because this campaign renamed a notice itself while it ran (`rem:assayer:glue-notice-correction`) and the record it renamed it in cites the notice by locus rather than by name.

The falsifier, and it is the acceptance the capability was built for: the check fails on a dangling notice citation. Demonstrated once against a scratch copy of this record with one notice label misspelled, which the check reported as an unresolved citation naming the label and the line. The failure is not committed here, because a record that carries a deliberate defect to prove a checker works is a record whose next reader cannot tell the deliberate defect from a real one.

The conversion also found the capability's own acceptance short, which is worth recording because it is the same defect one level further out. The check seeds four surfaces — the tests, the notices, the claims and the citations standing in Rust commentary — and the linter's integration test asserting that this repository's prose is in good standing seeded only the first, under a docstring saying it seeded "exactly as the check seeds it". That test passed for as long as no prose cited a notice, and failed the moment this record's fifty citations arrived: it was judging a smaller corpus than the gate judges, which is the precise divergence one resolution space exists to prevent. Its seeding is corrected and the suite is green. The finding belongs to the tooling rather than to this package, and it is the campaign's tools-over-discipline rule paying out a second time — the first document to use a capability is what tells you whether the capability is finished.

The grammar the conversion holds to is one label to a parenthesis, the label alone inside it. Where two notices disclose one finding they are two parentheses, not one; where a notice was already parenthesised in prose the parentheses are the citation's rather than a nesting of both. This is the rule that cost four consecutive tranches a check run, and it is written here so the fifth reader does not have to rediscover it.

**Proposal (The repair campaign as waves, sequenced off mechanisms)** · `proposal:assayer:repair-waves`

This is a proposal and is written to be ruled on. The audit's authority ends at saying what conformance would mean for each item; which of those repairs this project makes, and in what order, is a decision the audit does not carry. What the audit can supply is the structure the worklist has and an entry-by-entry reading cannot show: the same mechanism turning up in six clusters under six entry names, and the dependencies that make some orders work and others waste a wave. Nothing below lifts the freeze. The freeze lifts by entry, in the order the rulings take them.

The waves are cut by **mechanism**, not by severity and not by cluster. Cutting by severity would scatter one repair across five waves, because a mechanism's consequences are not uniform — the checkpoint payload holds a high and three mediums and they are one change to one structure. Cutting by cluster would repeat the read phase's own mistake, which the register spent fourteen tranches correcting: a defect's site and its mechanism's owner are usually different directories.

Three principles order the waves. Cheap-and-high first, where cheap means the package already contains the correct form and the repair is a substitution. Prerequisites before dependants, from the sequencing register (`reg:assayer:audit-sequencing`). And production-behaviour changes after everything that is not one, so that the waves needing a ruling are not blocking the waves that do not.

| Wave | Mechanism | Size | Needs a decision |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| One | Substitutions the package already contains | small, several files | no |
| Two | Values that disagree with the tables that fix them | medium, wide | **yes** |
| Three | Specified mechanisms written and never called | small to medium | no |
| Four | The standardisation statistics' relocation | small, structural | no |
| Five | The checkpoint payload made whole-state | large, one structure | no |
| Six | The pending entry and label-time reconstruction | medium, ordered pair | no |
| Seven | The dimension map and the feature layout | largest | **yes** |
| Eight | The public surface and what it declines to refuse | medium | **yes** |
| Nine | Diagnostics that report a value they have no evidence for | medium, wide | no |
| Ten | The derivation's shipped surface against the specified one | large, one boundary | **yes** |
| Riding | Tests and claims, per wave | see below | **yes**, once |

**Wave one — substitutions the package already contains.** The cheapest and highest-yield wave, and the one the audit is most confident about, because in every item the correct form is already written somewhere in this package or in this package's own suite. Thirteen lock acquisitions on the infallible assessment path panic on poison (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-poisoned-lock-panics`) while the signal cache, which that same path calls, recovers from poison under a comment reading "assessment path never panics" — so the repair is that pattern applied twelve more times. Construction panics where its own error variant is waiting (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-journal-open-panics`). Neither warm-up accumulator skips the non-finite entries both specified algorithms exclude (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-nonfinite-accumulation`), which is a guard in two places. The journal writer's construction calls the wrong constructor (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-seq-restart`): one argument at one call site, against a function that exists, is exported, is correct and is tested. And the synchronisation error is a diagonal maximum standing under the name of a norm (`entry:assayer:wl-sync-error-diagonal`) — the specified norm is already written in this package as a test helper — after which exporting it is one field on one type (`entry:assayer:wl-health-sync-error-constant`), in that order and not the other. Add the debug oracle's release reachability (`entry:assayer:wl-debug-oracle-release-reachable`), which is a guard rather than a redesign.

No ruling is needed. Nothing here changes a number a host reads except by making a diagnostic report the quantity it is named for, and nothing changes a public signature.

**Wave one has landed, all eight entries, each with its repair line.** Two things it learned are worth carrying into the waves that follow. The first is that the wave's own claim about signatures was not quite right: making construction fallible changed `build_inner`, `build_from_builder` and `build` to return a result, and although all three are crate-internal and the public builder already declared the error, "nothing changes a public signature" held only because the boundary happened to sit where it did. The second is a measurement rather than a defect: the persistence cluster is behind the non-default `serde` feature, so a default-feature run compiles none of it and neither builds nor tests the journal repairs. Later waves touching persistence must run with the feature on or they are testing a smaller package than they think.

One thing the wave found and did not repair, being outside every entry it carried: the `multi_channel` enrichment grid's same-policy channel-pair comparisons are load-sensitive. Under CPU contention the two aliased core assessments straddle a model-owner republish and drift some four orders of magnitude past the tolerance the harness allows them. Verified pre-existing by running the reproducer against both the repaired and the unrepaired synchronisation-error computation, which failed alike.

**Wave two — values that disagree with the tables that fix them.** Constants, rates and predicates, each wrong against a table the corpus states, each a small edit, and collectively the wave that most changes what this package computes. The six shipped defaults (`entry:assayer:wl-shipped-defaults`), the graph decay composed with the wrong period (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-decay-cadence`), the three measurement rules inflated by a factor of four hundred (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-measurement-rules`), the adverse rate averaging the complement of its indicator (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-adverse-rate-inverted`), the coverage divisor (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-coverage-depth-divisor`), the importance weight returning the odds ratio (`entry:assayer:wl-importance-weights`), the eligibility predicate's two inverted rows (`entry:assayer:wl-eligibility-predicate`), the shared class-rate tracker (`entry:assayer:wl-p-plus-tracker-rates`), the unweighted sample count (`entry:assayer:wl-risk-unweighted-samples`) and the discounted sample floor (`entry:assayer:wl-risk-recency-floor`).

**This wave needs a ruling and it is the campaign's most consequential.** Every item in it changes a learned quantity, and a deployment that upgrades across it gets different risk numbers from the same inputs and the same history. That is what conformance means here and the audit does not soften it. The ruling this wave asks for is not item by item — each item's argument is already made and each stands — but whether the wave lands as one release boundary with the change declared, or is spread so that a deployment can move one parameter at a time. The audit's view, offered as a view: one boundary, because these parameters interact through the same models and a deployment moving them singly sits in a state the corpus describes nowhere.

**The test-family restatement rides this wave and needs its own ruling.** Five tests in the campaign's count assert one of these divergences as their expected value, and several of their authored claims explain the wrong behaviour in the corpus's own vocabulary — the eligibility row pinned twice, the importance weight, the blend quantity that is tautological besides, the interaction arm pinned three times with the corpus's argument about one template type attached to another. Those tests do not merely fail when this wave lands: they are the document a maintainer would consult to decide the wave is wrong. So the family must be restated *with* the wave and cannot follow it, and restating a claim is authorship work that no sweep can do (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`). A ruling that takes the wave should cover the restatement with it, because a wave that lands against a suite pinning its old behaviour lands red for reasons that look like defects and are not.

**Wave two has landed, all ten entries, each with its repair line, and the mechanism-2 restatement rode it as ruled.** Three things it learned are worth carrying forward. The first is that the family of tests defending divergences was larger than the count above: beyond the pins the entries named, the convergence scenario's liveness bound encoded the retired tracker rate's horizon, the drift suites' fixtures sat inside the retired allowance rather than the tabulated one, and the challenge-without-result witness pinned the retired predicate under a notice already prescribing its restatement — every one was restated in the commit that changed its value, and the cross-cutting settlements the ruling reserved for the end of the branch turned out to be exactly these, no more. The second is a measurement from the restatement's own verification: with the pre-conditioned cells deriving once, forty full-suite instances of the `multi_channel` binary at forty-eight test threads — eight concurrent per round, five rounds — ran green, where a single contended round had reproduced the straddle before; what the load surfaced instead was a second, smaller seam — the derivation reads each live companion tracker at its own call instant and the estimate decays with wall-clock time, single-digit nanounits per scheduler-scale delay — which the suite now budgets by that mechanism under a named constant, retired when the clock seam closes. The third is that one repair widened a surface where the entry predicted a substitution: making the pending-buffer capacity the specified computation put the two factors the requirement computes from onto the configuration, so the infrastructure block's shape changed rather than a constant inside it.

**Wave three — specified mechanisms written and never called.** The campaign met this shape four times and predicted it a fifth; every instance is a call rather than an implementation, which is why they belong together and why the wave is cheap for what it closes. The Ledger's collection sweep needs a caller rather than a cadence (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-sweep-unscheduled`). The specified warm-up bootstrap ships complete and never runs (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-bootstrap-unrun`). The Companion's estimates never decay on read (`entry:assayer:wl-companion-read-decay`). The identity convergence trackers are built, updated nowhere and published empty (`entry:assayer:wl-health-identity-unwired`) — and that one is ordered after the clock lane's carve-out, for the reason its sequencing clause gives. Two named checkpoints are performed nowhere (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-checkpoints-five-six-uncalled`), which is a low and is here because it is the same mechanism. No ruling: each call makes the package do what the corpus already says it does.

**Wave three has landed, all five entries with their repair lines, and the positional cursor rode it** (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-positional-cursor`) — its own entry requires closing with the sweep's caller, and the unassigned residue says a low rides whichever wave touches its file. Three things the wave learned are worth carrying. The first is that one entry's premise did not survive recomputation: CP5 and CP6 are performed, and were before the audit began — what had no production caller was the pair of named utility functions — so the fifth repair was the corpus absorption and a documentation-claim correction, not a call. A wave built on "wired wrongly is the risk" caught exactly that by re-deriving each before-claim from the code. The second is live confirmation of a wave-four entry from a wave-three test: the bootstrap blend's engine-level falsifier initially read the published statistics through a lifecycle barrier and found the blend erased — any lifecycle publication overwrites the standardisation vectors with neutral state (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-lifecycle-standardisation`), so until wave four lands, a blend or batch initialisation survives in the owner's vectors but vanishes from publication at the next lifecycle event. The third is that making the Companion's reads clocked closed the harness-side wall-clock seam the batch suite's decay budget was named for: the World now reads and updates its tracker on its virtual clock, and the budget constant stands retirable by the harness lane that owns it.

**Wave four — the standardisation statistics' relocation.** One structural move, small, and a prerequisite for three registered repairs (`reg:assayer:audit-sequencing`). The statistics live on the owner and the checkpoint payload is built from the working copy, so nothing downstream of them can be repaired until they move. It is its own wave rather than the head of the next one because doing it once serves three dependants in two later waves, and because a wave that has to be done first is clearer as a wave than as a paragraph inside one. The lifecycle event that publishes neutral standardisation over the empirical statistics it has just maintained (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-lifecycle-standardisation`) closes with the move. So do the two extension defects beside it — the axis registration that under-extends (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-under-extension`) and the desynchronising extension accepted without complaint (`entry:assayer:wl-standardisation-unchecked-extension`) — and the batch initialisation's two discards, the completion thrown away on a full channel (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-init-discard`) and the command dropped at the owner's end (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-batch-init-drop`), which are the same statistics lost by a different route.

**Wave four has landed, all five entries with their repair lines, and the owner holds no second copy** — the relocation left zero standardisation fields on the model owner, verified by sweep. Three things the wave learned are worth carrying. The first is a boundary the relocation could not cross: the published snapshot carries the means and variances and no class vector (`def:publication:model-snapshot`), so `from_snapshot` restores the statistics and the CP5/CP6 revert preserves the live classes across the call — sound because no dimension change separates a publication from its revert — until the class assignment is derived from the map as the standing derive-classes notice asks; the two later repairs that need the relocation — (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`) and (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`) — inherit a working copy that holds all three vectors. The second is that the under-extension falsifier surfaced an unregistered sibling seam on its first run: two identity dimensions registered in one submission double-count the cross-dimension aggregate block, because each event's handler tests emptiness against a dimension map that is only rebuilt after all events, so the models extend by two aggregate blocks where the rebuild lays out one — caught only by a debug assertion, invisible in release, and belonging to the extension-versus-rebuild family wave seven owns. The third is that the sentinel-bootstrap completion still crosses the command channel by the discarded-send shape this wave repaired for batch initialisation: the wave-three wiring releases the slot accumulator whether or not its completion's send was accepted, the same one-shot loss by the same route, outside every entry this wave carried.

**Wave five — the checkpoint payload made whole-state.** The largest single structural repair the audit found and the one whose parts most clearly belong together: four clusters each found a different hole in one payload (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-partial-payload`) and each closes when the payload gains the field it names. The write site's empty Ledger and identity vectors (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-checkpoint-truncation`), the absent drift accumulators (`entry:assayer:wl-health-checkpoint-drops-drift`), the three missing per-axis fields (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-axis-fields`) and the warm start's falsified premise (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-warm-start-standardisation`) ride together. The journal-stripped context (`entry:assayer:wl-persistence-stripped-context`) belongs here too, being the same question asked of the journal.

The format version is already the guard that makes this safe: it has been bumped repeatedly and a mismatch cold-starts by design, so no ruling is needed for the compatibility question that would otherwise ask for one. What the wave costs is that its tests are inverted — the suite establishes that this state crosses the checkpoint faithfully, by constructing the payload by hand and passing it populated, while the production path passes empty vectors. Those tests do not fail today and will not fail when the wave lands; they will simply become true, and the wave must add the tests that exercise the path a deployment takes.

**Wave five has landed, every entry with its repair line** (recorded here by the orchestrator; the wave's own record commit restored the swallowed heading above and left this note unwritten). The payload is whole-state — axis metadata, drift evidence, standardisation statistics, the calibration row's uncertainty, and the owner-held state, captured at the write site and threaded back through recovery — the journal truncates at the checkpoint's mark under the append lock, both formats carry version guards that cold-start by design, and the production path the paragraph above demanded is a test that failed red first. The replay guards are deleted and replay runs all seventeen steps, so the exactness invariant now pins agreement. What remains of the cluster is bounded under its successor notice: restored identity payloads cross and return, and reattachment awaits host re-registration.

**Wave six — the pending entry and label-time reconstruction.** An ordered pair and the order is forced. The pending entry carries eight of its twelve specified fields (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-entry-fields`); three of the four missing ones are what the label-time reconstruction's sources one, three and four read (`entry:assayer:wl-phi-reconstruction`), and no call can supply them from state the label path holds. Widening the entry first, calling the helper second. The storage precision (`entry:assayer:wl-pending-storage-precision`) rides here because it is a change to the same type.

**Wave six has landed, the ordered pair in its forced order and the rider beside it.** The widening at `5da9cbf3` took the journal context and the journal version 1 → 2 in the same stroke, as wave five's landing note asked; the reconstruction at `2a2c60b2` follows it. Three things the wave settled are worth carrying. The first is a reading the covering invariant forces and no single record states: the stored "identity base features" are the five measurement values of each dimension block, because the structural triple ahead of them is exactly what source two re-derives from the current competitive set — the close-out's correction already implied this split and the wave implemented it. The second is that the cross-dimension block is named by no reconstruction source; the wave assigned it to source two's aggregates-recompute and re-derives it from current cell state, mirroring the assessment path value for value. The third is a boundary deliberately not crossed: the per-Sentinel slot guard inside the now-called helper still places stored rows by prefix copy, which is wave seven's registered defect (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-frozen-slot-truncation`) — but that entry's coverage argument has moved under it, because the production label path now reaches the guard on every label, so wave seven inherits a live locus where the audit measured a dormant one.

**Wave seven — the dimension map and the feature layout.** The largest wave and the one the audit would schedule last among the code repairs, because everything in it changes what a position in the feature vector *means*. Extension appends where the rebuild does not (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-layout-misalignment`), and the feature tranche settled that the repair is a permutation rather than a new allocation rule — a generalisation of machinery the package already has, which moves it from the largest outstanding repair to the middle. Axis deregistration finds its features by searching for a class (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-dereg-scan`). Template operands are absolute indices no rebuild re-resolves (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-absolute-operands`). The named-pair template is implemented nowhere (`entry:assayer:wl-interaction-templates`). The label-time slot guard truncates the position the corpus puts last (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-frozen-slot-truncation`), the anchor projection gathers what the corpus computes (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-anchor-gathered-pair`), every cold-start position is classed as a z-score (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-cold-start-classes`), the per-axis outcome memory is grouped by kind where the corpus pairs it by axis (`entry:assayer:wl-extraction-ledger-axis-grouping`), and the compilation pipeline's third stage does not exist (`entry:assayer:wl-feature-compiled-triples`).

**This wave needs a ruling.** Every model in a running deployment is fitted against the layout this wave changes, so landing it invalidates learned parameters in a way no format version guards. The audit has no view on whether that is acceptable and states only what it costs. Its tests ride the same way wave two's do: the interaction arm is pinned three times, and one claim carries the corpus's argument about one template type attached to another, in the gloss a reader consults to learn what the type is for.

**Wave eight — the public surface and what it declines to refuse.** Four public accessors hand a host the steward's command channel and the internal state (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-steward-channel-public`). The timestamp's public fields make the clamp the clock record calls unbypassable bypassable (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-timestamp-fields-public`), and its default is a hidden clock read (`entry:assayer:wl-timestamp-default-reads-clock`). A signal declaration's shape parameters are never validated and two reach the assessment path (`entry:assayer:wl-signal-declaration-unvalidated`) — a structural violation the corpus assigns to the fallible surface, passing it and surfacing as a panic on the call the corpus promises is infallible. The builder reaches nineteen of the forty-three tabulated parameters (`entry:assayer:wl-config-unreached-constraints`) and its template validation is a constant (`entry:assayer:wl-builder-template-validation`). Pre-seeding synthesises no feature vector (`entry:assayer:wl-preseed-degenerate-vector`). The assessment path writes a fourth piece of shared state and it is the only one that blocks (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-signal-cache-write`). Marginalisation hands a factorisation failure upstream as an error (`entry:assayer:wl-marginalise-error-channel`), and the replacement surface the companion chapter requires does not exist (`entry:assayer:wl-risk-replacement-trait`).

**This wave needs a ruling** because removing a public accessor is a breaking change to a library's surface, whatever the corpus says about it, and because the capacity a host cannot set (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-command-channel-capacity`) is a decision to make rather than a defect to fix.

**Wave eight has landed, all ten entries plus the resonance trio the wave-ten paragraph routes here, each with its repair line.** The ruling's two halves were taken as ruled: the surface broke where breaking was the repair, and the command channel's capacity is host-set with no default — every fixture now declares it, at 64, the figure the retired literal shipped. Three things the wave learned are worth carrying. The first is that the sealed test surface found its structural instrument in a `test-support` feature enabled through a self dev-dependency, so the crate's own test builds carry the harness and a host build compiles it sealed — and the compile of the sealed build is itself the unreachability proof for everything behind the gate. The second is that the newly reached constraint checks caught a live specimen of the entry's own prediction on their first full-suite run: a persistence fixture had declared its concordance percentile as eighty rather than 0.80, and nothing had ever refused it. The third is a boundary deliberately not crossed twice over: the template validator checks what the legacy offset-based enum can express and leaves the canonical-contract checks — signal names against the schema, `CompetitiveIndicator` placement — under the trimmed notice for wave seven's operand migration; and the replacement trait gives the challenge posterior its two-count carrier without touching the derivation's moment-only crossing, which stays wave ten's (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`).

**Wave nine — diagnostics that report a value they have no evidence for.** Every item here is report-only, and the health tranche's argument is why the wave is not therefore a low: the corpus closes the loop at the host and prescribes the alerts a host should build, so a diagnostic reporting a reassuring value it has no evidence for is a number acted on by design. Per-Sentinel informativeness exported as a constant (`entry:assayer:wl-sentinel-informativeness`), empirical coverage computed nowhere (`entry:assayer:wl-empirical-coverage`), the health snapshot's missing join key (`entry:assayer:wl-health-snapshot-fields`), the drift resets answering two of four (`entry:assayer:wl-health-drift-reset-triggers`), the instability flag comparing the wrong pair (`entry:assayer:wl-health-instability-flag`), the composite stage-change event with no caller that is not a test (`entry:assayer:wl-health-stage-event-dead`), a Sentinel reporting nothing counted as reporting (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-zero-sentinels-counts-silent`), the two fallbacks of one checkpoint disagreeing about what a prior is (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-outcome-fallback-uncalibrated`), two unflagged degradations (`entry:assayer:wl-glue-unflagged-degradations`), the label boundary counting nothing (`entry:assayer:wl-label-sanitisation-uncounted`), the degraded flag that omits the depth clamp (`entry:assayer:wl-report-degraded-omits-depth`), and the catalogue nothing checks against the mappers (`entry:assayer:wl-metrics-unchecked-correspondence`). Widening the persisted row that empirical coverage divides by is the one item here with a structural half, and it rides wave five rather than this one.

**Wave nine has landed, all twelve entries, each with its repair line.** Three things the wave learned are worth carrying. The first is that the report-only wave was the campaign's densest in tests defending divergences: seven engine tests pinned the occupancy reading of the contribution count and ran red against that one repair, a doc comment stated the wrong instability comparison as the design, an export fixture constructed the informativeness zero it should have caught, and a should-panic pinned the depth clamp's debug assertion — every one restated in the commit that changed its value. Diagnostics accrete tests that describe them, so a wave of diagnostics is a wave of restatements. The second is that recomputing before-claims kept paying, as wave three predicted: the correspondence entry's "nothing checks" was half-stale — the forward direction already stood as a test — and the repair was the reverse half plus the correction; the same discipline caught the batch-init discard already repaired by wave eight, leaving this wave exactly the reporting half its entry carried. The third is a boundary drawn twice and worth stating once: where the corpus's word underdetermined the computation, the wave wrote the reading into the artefact itself and flagged the choice — "near its threshold" became half the threshold, argued from observability, since an accumulator at the threshold has already reset; the coverage gate became the calibration minimum per hard regime partition over usable rows — and both readings are recorded as findings for the corpus to fix or overturn. One deliberate corpus addition rode the wave, as the conformance line required: the prior-only axis prediction is now a specified quantity rather than an implementation's improvisation.

**Wave ten — the derivation's shipped surface against the specified one.** Added after the proposal was first written, by the last tranche (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`). It is a wave rather than a set of items riding others because its mechanism is a boundary rather than a value: the derivation returns a rendering where the corpus specifies a landscape, and every item here is a consequence of that one substitution. The primary call takes the display configuration as a required argument (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-display-config-argument`) and returns no decision object for the rendering to be a layer over (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-no-landscape`); those two are one repair and the order between them is forced, since the landscape is what the rendering would take. The challenge posterior crosses as moments alone (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-moment-only-posterior`), which is what the two consumers the landscape would gain both need, and the first dominated-tag trigger has nowhere to report from until they exist (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-dominance-probability-absent`). The entropy gauge is renamed for what it is and gains the disclaimer the specification writes (`entry:assayer:wl-exploration-gauge-named-information-value`), which is cheap-and-high and could ride wave one instead — the audit's view is that it should not, because renaming it without the fragility surface beside it leaves a host with a gauge correctly labelled and nothing to use in its place.

**This wave needs a ruling and it is the campaign's largest surface change.** Every public item the crate exports from this cluster changes shape, and a host holding a stored profile holds one that cannot be replayed, since the profile carries none of the constants it was rendered under. The ruling asked for is not whether each item stands — each argument is made and each stands — but whether this package is to grow the landscape at all, which is a product decision the audit has no standing to make. If the answer is no, the honest alternative is that the specification's Part IV is amended to specify the surface that ships, and that amendment is a larger piece of corpus work than any repair in this proposal.

**Wave ten has landed, all five entries plus the subadditivity claim the cross-cutting paragraph routes here, each with its repair line.** The ruling was taken as ruled: the package grows the landscape, the rendering is a layer over it, and the exports changed shape on the pre-deployment ground the ruling names. Three things the wave learned are worth carrying. The first is that the specified landscape underdetermines its own utilities, and the gap is provable rather than felt: the crossover offsets carry only differential ratios, and the envelope's breakpoints between non-adjacent actions need the differential magnitudes, so the optimal action is not computable from the five specified groups at all. Two corpus additions rode the wave under the conformance-line exception — the exponent sum on the landscape and the two reward differentials on each crossover — and with them the envelope, the compound boundaries fragility reads under inversion, and the affine dominance thresholds are all closed form in what the landscape carries. The second is that recomputing before-claims caught the corpus's own arithmetic this time: the three-action worked interval tabulates [0.44, 2.51] where the definition it cites gives [0.79, 2.91] — every other interval row recomputes exactly, and the recomputed value also equals the Allow-to-Challenge interval rigidly shifted by the Challenge width, so the row is pinned at the recomputed pair and recorded as a figure defect; beside it, the high-risk fragility prose's "about 0.31" reads 0.343 under the Gaussian-marginal default the fragility definition prescribes, and the medium-risk "about 0.35" matches the exact quantile-integration substitute the definition permits rather than the default it prescribes. The third is the wave's readings where the corpus's word underdetermined the computation, written into the artefact and flagged: the flip probability is the capped sum of the two Gaussian-marginal boundary exceedances, validated against the low-risk worked figure of 0.72; the domination display threshold is a named constant at one half, because the rendering configuration is specified as exactly eight constants while the treatment algorithm says the threshold "defaults to" one half — the two sentences cannot both be honoured by a configuration field; and the interface chapter's "no iteration, no search, no convergence criterion" is read as no statistical iteration, since the exact incomplete beta and its quantile inverse the same Part specifies evaluate by fixed-cap recurrences. Two boundaries crossed in passing are recorded: the fail-hard refusal of a malformed action set now survives the release profile, hardening wave eight's repair whose should-panic witness had a release-stripped oracle; and the migration-inventory sentences Part IV concentrated at the output type, the exploration quantity and the optionality of rendering now describe a surface that no longer ships, which is prose-pass work the wave deliberately left to the record pipeline rather than edited from a lane.

**Two smaller items from that tranche do not ride wave ten and should ride earlier waves.** The silent policy substitution (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-silent-policy-fallback`) is the cluster's highest finding and is independent of the boundary question: it belongs in wave eight with the rest of what the public surface declines to refuse, together with its prerequisite (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-validate-unreachable`) and the posterior clamping beside it (`entry:assayer:wl-derivation-challenge-clamped`), all three being the same mechanism — a caller's defect repaired per call instead of refused.
