And the source audit's partial sidestep guard (`entry:assayer:wl-resonance-allowlist-sidestep-partial`) is a substitution the package already contains, the permitted list and the forbidden list standing in the same file, so it belongs in wave one.

**What rides every wave: the tests and their claims.** The campaign's structural coverage finding is that the suite is in several places the thing that would have to be rewritten before a conformance repair could land (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`). That is not a wave of its own and must not be scheduled as one. Each wave above carries its own share: the tests pinning its divergences, restated; the byte-identical pairs it touches, resolved; the claims whose numbers it changes, recomputed. The derivation tranche's four weak pins and two false claims ride waves eight and ten in the same way, and one of the two false claims must be restated with the rename rather than after it. What remains after every wave has taken its share is the residue that belongs to no repair — the thirty tests with no oracle, the six aimed at code no production path constructs, the claim whose falsifier does not compute (`entry:assayer:wl-ingestion-depth-wrap-arithmetic`), the validation module whose every test pins a function in another module (`entry:assayer:wl-validation-tests-pin-elsewhere`), the journal failure documented as safe to retry (`entry:assayer:wl-journal-failure-retry-claim`), and the test whose oracle is a debug assertion the release profile removes (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-pins`). That residue is authoring work under the test documentation policy and is the one thing here that could be scheduled independently of every wave.

**What is deliberately unassigned.** Two groups. The first is the corpus-side findings, which are not repairs to this package at all: a corpus that contradicts itself over who lifts a coordinate (`entry:assayer:wl-report-width-lift-contradiction`), a chapter whose self-count disagrees with its own closing sentences (`entry:assayer:wl-config-chapter-self-count`), two metrics catalogued on a reason that does not hold (`entry:assayer:wl-metrics-companion-surface`), and the prose defects the tranches recorded beside their findings. Those belong to the record pipeline and are listed at (`rem:assayer:closeout-completeness`). The second is the residue of lows whose mechanism is shared with nothing — the positional cursor (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-positional-cursor`), the tenth Ledger field (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-tenth-field`), the rootless disagreement (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-rootless-disagreement`), the starvation window (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-starvation-window`), the absence threshold's truncation (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-absence-cast`), the root-only Ledger read (`entry:assayer:wl-ledger-root-only-read`), the chain-length normaliser's form (`entry:assayer:wl-extraction-chain-length-form`), the no-report path's two flags (`entry:assayer:wl-extraction-no-report-flags`), the two inert knobs (`entry:assayer:wl-inert-knobs`), the low-rank exit (`entry:assayer:wl-low-rank-exit-unbuilt`), the two-swap health skew (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-health-skew`), the cold-start dimension discard (`entry:assayer:wl-snapshot-cold-start-dimension`), the checkpoint preemption (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-checkpoint-preemption`), the refit's three unanswered triggers (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-refit-triggers`), the absent class-assignment function (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-absent-class-assignment`), the revert's substituted configuration (`entry:assayer:wl-owner-revert-defaults`), the lifecycle drop (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-lifecycle-drop`), the non-deterministic event order (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-nondeterministic-event-order`), the unused observation channel (`entry:assayer:wl-identity-observation-channel-unused`), the Schur verification's diagonal test (`entry:assayer:wl-schur-diagonal-verification`), and the report staleness half-repaired by the carve-out (`entry:assayer:wl-staleness-raw-clock`) with the batch's two clock readings beside it (`entry:assayer:wl-batch-timestamp-split`). Each of those should ride whichever wave touches its file rather than justify one, and the three highs among them — the revert, the Ledger's root-only read and the lifecycle drop — should be re-read against this proposal before it is ruled on, because a high that no wave claims is more likely a wave the audit has not seen than a repair that is genuinely alone.

**Remark (What this audit deliberately did not reach)** · `rem:assayer:closeout-completeness`

An audit is complete when it says what it did not do as precisely as what it did. Three things are outside this record's reach, each for a different reason, and each with the condition that would bring it inside.

**The `resonance/` cluster was unaudited and no longer is.** It was the one row of the scope map with no tranche, and its columns were marked underived rather than filled. The reason was never scheduling: the record that governs it had not landed, measuring the cluster against the legacy record it replaced is what the audit's own measure convention forbids (`conv:assayer:audit-measure`), and measuring against the specification alone would have meant guessing which of the derivation chapters' statements the unwritten record intended to keep. **The unblocking condition was the derivation record's landing**, the record landed, and the tranche followed it (`sec:assayer:audit-tranche-resonance`). The completeness desideratum (`goal:assayer:audit-complete`) is now met by every cluster of the map.

The entry is kept rather than deleted, and not only by the campaign's convention that nothing is quietly dropped. The wait was the campaign's one controlled test of its own method, and it returned a result: six specification columns filled without going to the named chapter turned out wrong, and the one column the campaign refused to fill until it could go there came out naming an appendix, a whole Part and three records nobody had guessed (`rem:assayer:resonance-scope-map`). That is the case for the convention stated as evidence rather than as principle, and it is the reason a future campaign should hold a row open rather than fill it provisionally.

**The corpus's prose defects are recorded and not repaired.** The campaign's second structural finding, after the coverage one, is that the specification is accurate enough to lean on and inaccurate often enough in its *commentary* that leaning on a sentence rather than on a table has repeatedly come close to producing a false finding — and once would have inverted a tranche's central verdict, and once would have turned an exactly-correct function into its largest one. Some two dozen such defects stand, and each tranche that met any recorded its own: the founding pass (`rem:assayer:corpus-prose-defects`), the extraction tranche (`rem:assayer:extraction-prose-defects`), the health tranche (`rem:assayer:health-prose-defects`), the persistence tranche (`rem:assayer:persistence-prose-defects`), the snapshot and pending tranche (`rem:assayer:snapshot-pending-prose-defects`), the public surface tranche (`rem:assayer:api-guidance-prose-defects`), the posterior and substrate tranche (`rem:assayer:model-linalg-prose-defects`), the identity tranche for the cluster's own comments (`rem:assayer:identity-prose-defects`), and the glue tranche (`rem:assayer:glue-prose-defects`). None is repaired here. That is deliberate and follows from what this campaign is: it measures the code against the corpus, so a defect in the corpus is outside its remit by construction, and a campaign that quietly edited the measure it was measuring against would have destroyed its own evidence. **The unblocking condition is the record pipeline taking them as a queue.** Each is already located and stated; what none of them has is an owner and a wave. The sharpest subclass should go first, because it is the one that misleads rather than merely misinforms: the three sentences in which the corpus reports its own conformance and is wrong about it (`rem:assayer:glue-prose-defects`). A tranche that took a shipped-claim sentence as evidence would have recorded the glue's two highest findings as conformant, with the chapter's own sentence as its citation.

**The scope map's own history is recorded and its lesson is not yet a tool.** The rebuilt map is derived from the source tree and its failure history is written down (`rem:assayer:audit-scope-map-history`), but nothing checks it. The register's completeness check asks whether every row of the map has been audited; nothing asks whether every cluster of `src/` has a row, which is the failure that let the highest-fan-in cluster in the package go unmapped through thirteen tranches. Both directions are cheap and only one is run. **The unblocking condition is a check that enumerates the source tree and compares it to the map** — the same shape as the register machinery the campaign already built for the deferral register, where an item can neither vanish nor linger falsely. This belongs to the campaign's standing tools-over-discipline rule: the map was held correct by care for the whole read phase, and care is what failed.

Two smaller absences are worth naming so that a reader does not mistake them for oversights. The audit did not read the test corpus as a corpus — it read what tests establish about the code under audit, which is how the coverage lines and the pin findings arose, and the weak-test census (`entry:assayer:wl-weak-tests`) is a report on a scripted pass rather than a tranche of its own. And the audit did not measure performance, anywhere; where a notice proposed a speed-up the audit adjudicated the proposal against the corpus and said nothing about whether the speed-up would work (`rem:assayer:glue-notice-correction`).
